# Security review: Low and Info findings

The Low and Info findings of the [security review](Security-Review.md), by area, in short form: what happens, where, and the suggested fix. The evidence each reviewer quoted is in the reports (`plans/security-review-evidence/slice-N.md`).


## Web authentication and controls

- **SEC-WEB-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Several sign-ins keep the session id; the forced password change flow never changes it.** `login_user` rotates only when `password_login` returns `Ok`; an account with `requirePasswordChange` or an expired password returns the error before the rotation, and `Login/changePassword` then signs the user in with `internalLoginUser`, which does not rotate. The id the attacker planted is then a signed-in session. The cookie name has no `__Host-` prefix, and the first `moqui.session` pair of the header wins. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:54-61`, `:403-416`, `:514-516`; `Login.xml:105-114`. Fix: Rotate the session id in `finish_login` whenever a bound web session's user changes from none or from another user, whatever the caller. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 8a271bf02): every sign-in moves a session the client brought to a new id when it held another user or none (`login.rs` `internal_login`: the forced password change's `internalLoginUser`, a second factor's code, a key, an app password, an identity provider; `rotate_on_password_login` unless the session was made for this request), so a planted id is never the signed-in one; an SSO sign-in for the user the session already holds keeps the id. Test `user::session` `every_sign_in_moves_a_brought_session_to_a_new_id`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (the rest; commits 23a478718 and 833f6b5ad, mr-pass 6a23ff8), the owner's call: rename the cookie over https, keep the name over plain http, and have users sign in once again at the upgrade. Over https the session cookie is `__Host-moqui.session` (`Secure`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`), so no other host of the domain and no plain-http page can plant or shadow it, and an https request reads only that name, a plain `moqui.session` there being no session (`user::session_cookie_name`, `format_session_cookie`, `web::session_cookie_of`, the host's `notws::session_cookie` for `/notws`, `/termws`, the Assist event stream and the upload check); plain http keeps `moqui.session`, and the edge's sticky cookie reads `__Host-moqui.session` first. PASS keeps and counts the authority's https cookie by its new name (`Transport.js`, `Headers.js`, `ShopCart.js`). Tests `user::session` `a_bad_mac_is_an_unknown_session`, `web` `an_https_request_reads_only_the_host_prefixed_session_cookie`, `edge_link::web` `the_sticky_cookie_is_read_from_every_cookie_header`.
- **SEC-WEB-06 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Logout keeps the session and its attributes; a password change ends nothing.** `logout` clears the user but keeps the session id and every attribute, and the next sign-in's new session is a copy of it, so attributes of the earlier user (the map's one-time values such as `newAccessToken`, `newAppPassword`, `singleUseCodes`, and application state) are in the next user's session. The `hasLoggedOut` flag that ends other sessions is cleared by the next person sign-in, including any request with the user's `api_key`, so a session that made no request in between stays signed in. `change_password` and `update#PasswordInternal` end no session and revoke no key. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1042-1071`, `:1227-1245`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/session.rs:1490-1506`, `:2763-2781`. Fix: At logout drop the session (retire the id and its attributes) and send a new anonymous one. At a password change end the user's other sessions and offer to end the keys. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 8a271bf02): logout ends the bound session with its attributes (`session::renew_after_logout`: the id retired, its row deleted and the end published to the cluster, a new empty session of no user and no visit), and `update#PasswordInternal` ends the account's other sessions on every server (`UserSecurityChecks.endUserSessions`; the current one stays). Tests `user::session` `a_logout_ends_the_session_and_its_attributes`, `PasswordChangeGate.spec.js` "a new password ends the account's other sessions". Fixed 2026-11-16 (the rest; commit 806142bca, mr-boss e32064e3), the owner's call: only an interactive sign-in clears `hasLoggedOut`, so a key or app password request never undoes a logout; logout is not changed, and the account gets "sign out everywhere and end my keys and app passwords". A login key, `api_key` header or app password signs its request in and leaves `UserAccount.hasLoggedOut` set; a password, a second factor's code, SSO or an SSH key sign-in at the host clears it (`login.rs` `recorded_login`). `IdentityServices.revoke#OwnAccess` (the caller's own account, no permission but `require#RecentAuth`) ends every session of the account on every server (the current one at its next request), its login keys and app passwords, through `revoke#AccountAccess`'s path with its evidence row, and leaves SSH keys and phones; Commons Account's "Sign out everywhere and end my keys and app passwords" calls it. Tests `user::login` `a_key_or_app_password_login_leaves_has_logged_out_set`, AdminSessions "revoke#OwnAccess signs the account out everywhere and ends its keys and app passwords after a recent sign-in", mr-boss CommonsSettings "Sign out everywhere ends my own keys and app passwords, after a recent sign-in".
- **SEC-WEB-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The `login` rate limit covers part of the password checks and is passed by a dot segment.** The limit's globs are matched against the path as sent; the screen path drops `.`, `..` and `~` segments, so the same transition is reached on a path the glob does not match. The same holds for any `path-rule` deny and any path-scoped limit a component adds. The listed other paths check a password or code and are not in the limit at all; only `all` (50 a second, burst 500) and the `auth-failure` ban apply to them. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:295-300`, `:1010-1035`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/url.rs:645-672`; `MoquiDefaultConf.xml:869-870`. Fix: Normalize the path in `Guard::check` the way `cleanup_path_names` does before matching, or refuse a request path with `.`, `..` or `~` segments at the listener. Fixed 2026-11-16: each `rate-limit` and `path-rule` applies when it matches the path as sent (escapes decoded) or the screen path it resolves to (`guard.rs` `screen_path`, in `check` and `check_served_path`); test `guard_tests.rs` `a_limit_and_a_path_rule_match_the_screen_path_a_request_reaches`.
- **SEC-WEB-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `reset#Password` rewrites `requirePasswordChange` for any account.** With the default `email-require-change="false"` the service sets `requirePasswordChange = N`, which removes an administrator's "must change at first sign-in" from a new account. With `true` it sets `Y`, which makes any user's next sign-in fail until they change their password. The unknown-user branch logs the caller's text and returns before the hash, so timing tells whether the account exists. Where: `UserServices.xml:404-462`. Fix: Leave `requirePasswordChange` alone at reset; set it when the reset password is used in `update#Password`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `reset#Password` leaves `requirePasswordChange` alone and makes its random password and hash before the lookup, so an unknown, closed or recently reset account answers after the same work; test `framework/test/PasswordChangeGate.spec.js`.
- **SEC-WEB-10 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Dev conf: every origin is allowed with credentials.** With `*` the origin check passes every `Origin`, and the response echoes it with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` and exposes `X-CSRF-Token`, so that page reads responses and the session token and can post as the signed-in developer. A dev instance with Dev AIDE runs code as the server's user for such a session. Where: `runtime/conf/MoquiDevConf.xml:9`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/origin.rs` `origin_allowed_by`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:2318-2327`. Fix: In the dev conf allow `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` origins of the server's own port only, or do not send `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` when the match was `*`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 4591ccf8c), the owner's call: the dev conf allows only this machine's origins, and `*` never comes with credentials on any conf. `MoquiDevConf.xml` allows only `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` origins, any port and scheme (`webapp_allow_origins=localhost,127.0.0.1`; an IP address entry no longer allows hosts under it), and `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` is sent only for an origin the allow list names other than by `*` (`web::origin::origin_credentialed_by`, in `apply_cors` and the sockets' origin check `notws::origin_allowed` for `/notws`, `/termws` and the Assist stream), so a conf on `*` gives cross-site reads without cookies only. Tests `web::origin` `a_wildcard_origin_carries_no_credentials_and_the_dev_conf_allows_only_this_machine`, `screen::tests` `redirect_json_list_security_headers_and_cors`.
- **SEC-WEB-11 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `/health` and `/metrics` trust a loopback TCP peer.** Both routes allow a peer that is loopback or in `status-ips`, by the TCP peer, so every request the local proxy forwards is allowed. `/metrics` answers pool, queue, statement and web protection counts. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:957-963`, `:1679-1684`. Fix: When `client-ip-header` is believed for the peer, test the resolved client address as well as the peer. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit b490313fb, beside SEC-CLU-01's edge rule): `/health` and `/metrics` answer only when the peer passes `status_allowed` and, when a believed `client-ip-header` names another client, that client is listed in `status-ips`; a loopback address a header names is not this machine (`http::status_request_allowed`). Test `http_tests::status_routes_check_the_client_a_proxy_names`. A monitor that probes through such a proxy must be listed in `webapp_status_ips`.
- **SEC-WEB-12 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Second factor details: a TOTP code works more than once; code sends are not limited in the login flow.** Nothing records a TOTP step as used, so a code is accepted again for its step and the one after. Each `sendOtp` creates a new single-use row and sends an email or SMS; only the re-authentication path has the 30 second gap. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/totp.rs:89-106`; `UserServices.xml:688-694`, `:772-814`. Fix: Store the last accepted TOTP step on the factor and refuse a step not after it. Apply the send gap in `send#ExternalAuthcCode`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `validate#UserAuthcFactorCode` takes an authenticator code only for a step after the factor's last one (`UserAuthcFactor.lastTotpStep`, `user::totp_code_step`), and `send#ExternalAuthcCode` refuses a second code to a factor within 30 seconds; tests `user::totp` `a_code_is_taken_for_a_step_after_the_last_one`, `framework/test/CredentialGuards.spec.js`.
- **SEC-WEB-13 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A `session_cookie_key` set by the operator is not checked.** A value from the environment is never replaced. `CHANGEME` is valid base64 and decodes to six known bytes, and any length is accepted as the HMAC key. A forged MAC still needs a live session id (160 random bits), which is in `HttpSession`, `Visit.sessionId` and the telemetry attribute `session.id`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/session.rs:3057-3065`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/factory.rs:1812-1816`. Fix: Refuse to start with a decoded key under 32 bytes or one ending in `CHANGEME`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 68a63d939): the server does not start with a `session_cookie_key` that still ends in `CHANGEME`, is not standard base64 or decodes to under 32 bytes (`context/factory.rs` `check_session_cookie_key`, at boot and on a conf reload); the message names the property, not the value. Test `context::tests` `a_weak_session_cookie_key_stops_the_boot`.
- **SEC-WEB-14 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An anonymous tarpit lock is everyone's.** The count key and the lock row use an empty user id for every caller with nobody signed in, so one client that passes the limit on `/Login` locks the screen for every signed-out visitor for the lock time, and can repeat it. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/authz.rs:703-704`, `:753-758`. Fix: Key the anonymous count by the client address or the visitor. Fixed 2026-11-16: with nobody signed in the tarpit's count and lock are keyed by `_ANON_` and a digest of the client address (an IPv6 one by its /64; `authz.rs` `anonymous_tarpit_subject`); test `authz_tests.rs` `an_anonymous_tarpit_lock_is_the_client_addresss`.
- **SEC-WEB-81 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A path-scoped rate limit or path rule is passed by another path to the same transition or screen (SEC-WEB-07's fix does not cover these).** The guard matches a limit's globs against the decoded request path and against `screen_path(path)`, which only drops empty, `.` and `~` names and pops `..`. The screens reach the same transition by paths neither form matches: an extra path after the transition (`POST /r/<token>/submit/x` runs `submit`, and `/r/*/submit` does not match four segments; the same for `/call/<token>/joinGuest/x` and `/i/<token>/<step>/x`), the FOP servlet (`POST /fop/Login/login`, `/fop/rest/login`, `/fop/SecondFactor/...`, `/fop/ChangePassword/...`, and `/fop/apps/tools/...` past an operator's `deny /apps/tools/**`), a `//name` search path (`//tools/x` is `/apps/tools/x`; this passes only a rule that names a path below the root), a `ScreenPathAlias` row, which rewrites the path after the guard has run, and a render-mode extension (`POST /rest/login.<mode>`; this form is likely, not confirmed). The limits are `login`, mr-boss `request-form` and `invitation-accept`, basic-comms `call-guest`, and any `path-rule` or path limit an operator adds. The `all` limit, the `auth-failure` ban and the account lockout still apply, so for the login paths this is the residue SEC-WEB-07 recorded; for `request-form` (5 a minute) what is left is `all` (50 a second) and whatever the intake service counts itself. `/ReAuth/password` checks a password and is in no path limit. Not confirmed: which `screen-text-output` types the conf declares, whether `create#IntakeRequest`, the invitation steps and `joinGuest` have limits of their own, and who may write `ScreenPathAlias` rows. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:338-344`, `:1015-1019`, `:1034-1035`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/public_site.rs:123-136`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/url.rs:272-275`, `:301-307`, `:548`, `:567-585`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/mod.rs:585-598`, `:739`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:868`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/MoquiConf.xml:30`, `:32`; `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/MoquiConf.xml:14`. Fix: Refuse extra path names after a transition that declares no `path-parameter` for them (404), and a transition through the FOP servlet. For the guard, match a limit against the path with a leading servlet prefix (`/fop`) removed and with trailing names dropped one at a time, or let a limit name a transition (screen location and name) and count it in the worker where the path is resolved. Add `/ReAuth/password` to the `login` limit.
- **SEC-WEB-82 (Low; reported (likely)). A per-host web protection rule is passed by a `Host` header with a port that is not a number.** The guard cuts a port from `Host` only when it is all digits, so `Host: shop.example:x` is `shop.example:x` to it and no `rate-limit`, `path-rule`, `user-agent-rule` or deny `client-rule` with `host="shop.example"` applies. The screens cut the header at its first colon, so the root screen and the `public-site` of `shop.example` serve the request. A stricter per-site limit or deny rule is passed by an anonymous client that comes straight to a listener (not through an edge node's forward); the rules without `host` still apply. Not confirmed: that hyper passes a `Host` value with a non-numeric port to the service on HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2; no shipped conf has a rule with `host`. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:162-173`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:2532-2537`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/public_site.rs:196-203`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/info.rs:148-150`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http/serve.rs:619-627`, `:642-650`. Fix: One function for the host name of a request, used by the guard, `root_for_host` and the public-site routing; answer 400 for a `Host` whose port is not digits.
- **SEC-WEB-83 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Once 100,000 clients are tracked, a new client is not rate limited, banned or connection-capped.** `with_client` returns `None` for an address not in the table when `max_tracked` (`max-tracked-clients`, 100,000) entries exist; `admit` then admits the connection uncounted, `check` returns `Ok` with no rate limit applied, and `record_key` counts no event, so no ban starts. An entry stays while it has a connection, a ban, or was seen within the longest ban window (600 s by the default conf), so 100,000 addresses (an IPv4 botnet, or IPv6 /64s out of a routed /47 or larger) that each send one request every ten minutes keep the table full, and every other client, the attacker's fresh addresses included, has only the global limits. Nothing evicts an entry but the 30 second `sweep`, and only the counter `untracked` shows it. The account lockout and the tarpit still apply. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:863-872`, `:935-944`, `:1029`, `:1054-1056`, `:1117`, `:1336-1345`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:915`. Fix: When the table is full, drop the entry seen longest ago that has no connection and no ban, and log once a minute while `untracked` rises. Consider counting an untracked IPv6 client under a shorter prefix (/48).
- **SEC-WEB-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). With an OAuth 2 provider the recent sign-in check is met by a redirect any site can start and the provider may answer without asking.** `/sso/login` is `read-only` with `require-session-token="false"`, so `GET /sso/login?authFlowId=<an OAuth flow>&reauth=true` runs for whoever moves a signed-in browser to it: another site by a top-level navigation or a link, a person at an unattended browser, a script in the page. For an OpenID flow the callback asks for an `auth_time` after the request; for an OAuth 2 flow (`AftOauth`) there is no such proof and the callback takes `now`. A provider that already has the user's session and consent redirects straight back, so the session's `LAST_AUTH` is fresh with no credential typed, and the five-minute gate of `require#RecentAuth` (new keys and tokens, factor and code changes, the email address, Login as) is open to whatever holds the session. A stolen cookie alone does not get this: the thief's browser has no provider session. Not confirmed: which providers answer a repeated authorization without a prompt. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/sso.xml:19-27`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:309-318`, `:522-530`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:603-610`. Fix: Start a re-authentication only by a POST with the session token (keep the plain login a GET), and do not count an OAuth 2 round trip as a recent sign-in unless the flow's row says its provider prompts; otherwise ask for the account's password or second factor.
- **SEC-WEB-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The new-device notice is not sent for a sign-in that passes a second factor, or one made within a minute of any other login attempt on the account.** `notify#NewDeviceSignIn` runs only when a `UserLoginHistory` row with `successfulLogin` Y and a visit is created. The password step of an account with a second factor is recorded as not logged in and the step that finishes the factor (`internal_login_user`) records nothing, so such an account never gets a Y row for a password sign-in and never a notice. One row a minute is written for a user, failed or successful, so a wrong password sent first claims the minute and the right one within 60 seconds writes no row; for the same reason an account whose key or automation logs in every minute never has a free minute. `user/factor.rs` was read only by a grep for `save_history`, and `Identity.js` `clientSummary` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:158-163`, `:307-336`, `:359-364`, `:486-498`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/Security.eecas.xml:154-155`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/admin/IdentityNewDevice.js:63-99`. Fix: Write the successful row when the second factor finishes, and keep the minute's claim apart for failed and successful logins (or let a successful login always write when the last row of the minute was a failure or from another visit).
- **SEC-WEB-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). User info claims are merged into the profile without comparing the user info `sub` with the ID token's.** After the ID token is validated its claims are the profile; every user info member the ID token lacks is then added (`email`, `email_verified`, `preferred_username`, `roles`, `groups`), and these decide the username, the verified-email link and the role maps. OpenID Connect Core 5.3.2 has the client refuse a user info answer whose `sub` differs from the ID token's; here the ID token's `sub` wins silently and the other user's claims are used. It needs a provider that answers user info for another subject; none is known. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/sso.rs:557-565`. Fix: For an OIDC flow refuse a user info answer with no `sub` or one unequal to the ID token's.
- **SEC-WEB-88 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The HTTPS listener takes the client's order of cipher suites.** `server_config` builds the listener's `rustls::ServerConfig` and leaves `ignore_client_order` at rustls's default, false, so the suite is the first of the client's list that the server has: a client that lists AES-128-GCM before AES-256-GCM gets AES-128-GCM. Only the provider's default suites are on, each an AEAD with an ephemeral key exchange, so nothing weak can be chosen; ASVS asks that the server prefer the strongest, and `tls_tests.rs` records that it does not. Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-12.1.2); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/tls.rs:154-163`; `crates/moqui-host/src/tls_tests.rs:538-545`. Fix: `config.ignore_client_order = true;` in `server_config`, with the provider's suites in the order wanted (AES-256-GCM and ChaCha20-Poly1305 before AES-128-GCM).
- **SEC-WEB-89 (Low; confirmed (second check)). A screen, and a transition of method `any`, runs for any HTTP method: TRACE renders the screen, a PUT of the sign-in form passes the `login` limit, and the default `methods` list refuses WebDAV's.** The screens look at the method only to pick a transition (`ScreenDef::transition`): a request for a screen runs its actions and renders it whatever the method, so `TRACE`, `TRACK` or a made-up name answers 200 with the page as for a GET (nothing of the request is echoed, so this is not cross-site tracing), and a transition with no `method` (`any`, the XSD's default) runs for any method; a REST resource and a static file answer 405. The body is parsed as a form for any method but OPTIONS. Web protection's `requests` `methods` list (GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS) refuses another method with 405, but only when the per-client rules are on (`trusted-proxies` set or `enabled="true"`; off on a default install) and never for an exempt client (the `loopback` client rule). Within that list, the `login` limit counts POST only while `Login/login` (`require-session-token="false"`) takes any method, so a password sent by PUT, PATCH or DELETE to `/Login/login` is checked and not counted by `login` (`SecondFactor/verifyUserAuthcFactor` and `ChangePassword/changePassword` take any method too); `all`, the `auth-failure` ban and the account lockout still apply, the residue SEC-WEB-81 records for other paths to the same transitions. The default list also leaves out the WebDAV, CalDAV and CardDAV methods `/dav` serves (PROPFIND, PROPPATCH, MKCOL, COPY, MOVE, LOCK, UNLOCK, REPORT), and the guard runs before `/dav` is routed, so turning web protection on, the conf's way to refuse TRACE, answers every DAV client 405 unless the operator adds them. The PUT is from reading the code, not run; the TRACE answer and the DAV 405 are the proof's. Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-13.4.4); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:868-872`, `:1774-1786`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/definition.rs:1191-1200`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/mod.rs:233-236`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:2137-2139`; `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:107-112`, `:973-975`, `:1002-1008`, `:1027-1029`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:583`, `:592-593`, `:607`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:41`. Fix: Answer 405 for a request to a screen whose method is not GET, HEAD, POST or OPTIONS, and for a transition of method `any` the same, whatever web protection says; count the `login` limit on every method but GET and HEAD (or give the sign-in transitions `method="post"`); add the DAV methods to the default list, or let a `requests` rule name methods by path (`/dav/**`).
- **SEC-WEB-90 (Low; confirmed (second check)). An `ipAllowed` refusal comes after the password check and says so, so a client outside the list learns the password is right.** `check_ip_allowed` runs in `finish_login`, which `authenticate` reaches only after `password_ok` (for an account with a second factor, after the code, through `internal_login_user`). Its error, `Authenticate failed for user X because client IP ... is not in allowed list for user or group.`, is not the invalid-login error, and `/rest/login` answers it as it is with 401, so a client outside the account's or a group's `ipAllowed` tells a right password (this message) from a wrong one (the invalid-login error, counted as a failure), and sees the address the server took for it (behind a proxy, which hop it believed). The refusal is not counted toward the lockout and is not noted as an `auth-failure`. A password learned this way works from inside the list, or wherever else the user reused it. Report: the security proofs, phase P5; lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:118-125`, `:266-299`, `:1641`, `:1717-1742`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/dispatch.rs:210-213`. Fix: Check `ipAllowed` before the password compare, in `gate_account` with the other refusals (an argon2 burn, the invalid-login error), or answer the invalid-login error after it and put the address and the rule only in the server's log.
- **SEC-WEB-91 (Low; confirmed (second check)). Behind a trusted proxy, `X-Forwarded-Proto`, `-Host` and `-Port` are read from their first value, the one a client may have sent.** For a peer `ClientAddressing` believes, the client address is the rightmost hop of `client-ip-header` that is not a trusted proxy, but the scheme, host and port are each header's first comma-separated value: in `forwarded_https` (whether the request is https: the session cookie's form, the https redirect and the refusal of a writing transition over plain http, the credential rule of `secure_transport`), `request_site` (the request's own origin, which CORS, the socket and event-stream `Origin` checks and the MCP transport compare with) and the LLM sandbox's `frame_host` (the host its frame document names). A proxy that appends to these headers instead of replacing them puts the client's value first, and one that sets no `X-Forwarded-Host` passes the client's header through, so the client's value wins: a plain-http client is taken as https, and a client chooses the host its own request counts as. A browser cannot add these headers to a cross-site request without a preflight, so another site cannot turn this on a signed-in user; what is left is a client misdescribing its own request, and whatever a shared cache keeps of an answer built from that host. With `client-ip-header` set and no `trusted-proxies`, every peer is believed and the client address is the first hop as well (the Web map says so). Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-4.1.3); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1795-1814`, `:1113`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/origin.rs:74-99`, `:127-141`; `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/sandbox.rs:61-88`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/address.rs:193-220`. Fix: Read the scheme, host and port from the same hop as the client address (the value at the rightmost untrusted hop's index when the header has as many values, else the last value, which the nearest trusted proxy wrote), or document that each trusted proxy must replace these three headers.
- **SEC-WEB-92 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The password change interval has a default in three places: 104 weeks in the shipped conf, 12 in the code and the XSD.** `policy::change_weeks` falls back to 12 when `user-facade/password@change-weeks` is missing, the XSD's default is 12 (its documentation says months), and `MoquiDefaultConf.xml` ships 104. The shipped conf always gives the attribute, so 104 applies; the fallback is a default written again beside it, which the conf rule of AGENTS.md refuses, and would expire passwords after 12 weeks instead of two years if the attribute were ever missing. Not a weakness. Report: the security proofs, phase P5; lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/policy.rs:65-68`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:633`; `runtime/components/base/framework/xsd/moqui-conf-3.xsd:1021-1022`. Fix: Read the attribute with no fallback of its own (`conf_env::setting`), make the XSD's default 104 and its documentation weeks, and check the other fallbacks of `policy.rs` against the conf the same way.
- **SEC-WEB-93 (Low; confirmed (second check)). The OIDC sign-in takes its ID token algorithms from the provider's discovery document, and nothing requires https for that document or its `jwks_uri`.** The grant of an OIDC flow is built with huskarl's `builder_from_metadata`, which takes the accepted ID token algorithms from the document's `id_token_signing_alg_values_supported` (less `none`, which huskarl always refuses); the flow's `preferredJwsAlgorithmEnumId` is read only to refuse ES512, so a deployer cannot pin the algorithm the provider uses, and whoever controls the document also chooses the algorithms. The document is fetched from the flow's `discoveryUri` (a fixed https one for Google, Apple and Azure AD, `baseUri` for Keycloak) with no scheme test, and huskarl's `EndpointUrl` takes `http` for the `jwks_uri` and every other endpoint the document names, so an `http://` document, or an https one that names an `http://` `jwks_uri`, has the keys that verify ID tokens fetched in clear, where a network attacker can replace them and sign an ID token for any account the flow links. The Admin provider screen refuses an `http` `discoveryUri` or `baseUri` but for this machine on an instance that is not production (`urlText`), so the first case needs a row written another way (a data file or an entity tool; which may write `OidcFlow` was not traced); the endpoints inside the document are checked by nothing. Report: the security proofs, phase P4 (ASVS v5.0.0-9.1.2, 9.1.3); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/sso.rs:292-309`, `:376-428`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:188-207`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/admin/ConfigurationLib.js:85-97`; huskarl 0.11.10 `src/grant/authorization_code/grant.rs:418-441`; huskarl-core 0.10.10 `src/endpoint_url.rs:70-78`. Fix: Refuse a discovery URL, and a `jwks_uri`, token or user info endpoint in the document, that is not https (this machine on an instance that is not production aside, as `urlText` does); when `preferredJwsAlgorithmEnumId` is set, give it as the only allowed algorithm (`allowed_id_token_signed_response_algs`), and otherwise hold the document's list to a fixed set.

## Routes, screens and REST

- **SEC-RTE-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). State changes on `GET` with no token.** A `GET` is never token-checked. A `read-only` transition takes URL parameters, and a transition that is not `read-only` still runs on a `GET` with no query, its `path-parameter` values included. So these change state from a link: `/apps/setPrefGoLast?preferenceKey=..&preferenceValue=..` (any user preference; `mantle.content.root` is the default root of `/resources`), `/Login/logout`, `/rest/logout`, `/sso/logout`, `/Login/removePreAuth`, and any application transition without `method="post"` that takes only path parameters. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2667` (`csrf_rejected`), `:2070` (`insecure_transition`). Fix: Refuse a `GET` for a transition that is not `read-only` and has actions; make `setPrefGoLast` and the logouts `POST`. Partly fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-04: a GET another site started is refused for a transition that runs actions and is not `read-only` (`/Login/logout`, `/rest/logout`, `/Login/removePreAuth`, path-parameter transitions); the `read-only` ones (`setPrefGoLast`, `/sso/logout`, SEC-ADM-09) still change state on such a GET. Fixed 2026-11-16 (rest): `apps/setPrefGoLast` is `method="post"`, `/sso/logout` is neither `read-only` nor without the session token (a cross-site GET is 403), and Tools' and Marble's `createFieldDynamic` are not `read-only`: the field tree sends it as a POST with the token (`screen/tree.rs` `loadMethod` "post", qvt `loadContainer`, html `postThenLoad`), and a GET with its parameters in the URL is refused. Tests `forms.rs` `a_tree_link_that_loads_through_a_writing_transition_is_a_post`, `SecurityScreens.spec.js` "a GET of sso/logout, setPrefGoLast or createFieldDynamic changes nothing". The terminal client posts such a tree link too (`moqui-tui` `follow_tree_row`; test `a_tree_link_that_changes_data_is_posted_with_its_parameters`).
- **SEC-RTE-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `lastStandalone` takes the parents out of the user check.** A screen marked `require-authentication="false"` or `anonymous-*` under a parent that requires a user is reachable with no user, and so are its transitions, which then need no authorization record. The always-actions of every parent still run. In the base and dev components the only such screen is `ScreenTreeNested`, whose actions fail without `ScreenTree`'s; an application screen written on the assumption that its parent protects it has no such luck. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/url.rs:373`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:1513`. Fix: Check authentication against every screen of the path (`path_defs`), as the transition path already pushes them all, and keep `render_defs` for what is drawn. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commits 19ae280ce, 94ae325b4): `check_authentication` and the login redirect ask `path_requires_authentication` of every screen of the path, so a screen under a parent that requires a user needs one as a `lastStandalone` fragment and for its transitions (`ScreenTreeNested` now needs a user); a parent's `allow-web-request="false"` refuses a fragment below it (`check_web_settings` reads `path_defs`), and a device's route check exempts a file only when no screen of the whole path needs a user (`device.rs` `check_route`). Tests `a_screen_under_a_parent_that_needs_a_user_needs_one_as_a_fragment`, `a_parent_that_allows_no_web_request_refuses_a_fragment_below_it`, `a_file_below_a_parent_that_needs_a_user_is_in_the_device_route_check`. The `require-encryption` redirect to https still reads the rendered screens only.
- **SEC-RTE-10 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). `/health` and `/metrics` trust the TCP peer.** Both routes allow any loopback peer and do not use the client address a trusted proxy names. The map says the server is meant to run behind a proxy; with the proxy on the same host every request has a loopback peer, so `/metrics` (pool, guard, telemetry counters, Prometheus text) and `/health` answer the internet unless the proxy blocks the two paths. `/status` uses the proxy-named client and does not have this. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:957`, `:977`, `:1679`. Fix: When `trusted-proxies` names the peer, decide by the resolved client address, as `/status` does; say so in the map row either way. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-11 (commit b490313fb), of which this is a second report: `/health` and `/metrics` answer only when the peer passes `status_allowed` and, when a believed `client-ip-header` names another client, that client is listed in `status-ips` (`http::status_request_allowed`). Test `http_tests::status_routes_check_the_client_a_proxy_names`. Checked against the code 2026-11-17.
- **SEC-RTE-11 (Low; reported (likely)). A restricted host still serves some framework paths.** The refusal is by the reserved first-segment list. The token routes `i` (staff invitation) and `call` are not in it and are dispatched before the site's own routing, so they run on the restricted host. Where the site has no mount at `/`, the root transitions (`/menuData`, `/shellData`, `/resources`, `/find/rank`, `/email/..`) and a `//name` search path (`//tools` is `/apps/tools`) also reach the framework there; a request with a key or Basic header is signed in for them. The map says nobody reaches the apps or the token routes on such a host. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/public_site.rs:96`-`102`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/reserved.rs:114`. Fix: On a restricted host refuse every path the site's own mounts do not take (and every token route), instead of refusing a list.
- **SEC-RTE-12 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Anonymous reads through `/listViewColumns` and `/statusBar`.** With no user and no authorization the caller reads any `StatusItem` and `Enumeration` description by id (500 ids a request for `values`; `stages` is not capped), every status of a type, and the flow transitions. Enumerations include what applications and customers define. Each id is a cached find, so unknown ids also add finds and cache entries. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot.xml:83`, `:104`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/ui/StatusServices.xml:62`, `:171`. Fix: Answer 401 without a user, as the neighbouring transitions do; a public page that needs a board can name its values server-side. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 21e1edfc5): `listViewColumns` and `statusBar` answer 401 without a user, as `shellCounts` does, and `statusBar` takes at most 500 ids of each list. Test `screen::tests::shell` `the_board_and_status_bar_transitions_answer_only_a_user`.
- **SEC-RTE-13 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Answers that tell an anonymous caller what exists.** A missing screen path is 404 with the component location of the last screen found, while an existing protected one is a redirect or 401, so the screen tree under `/apps` can be mapped. `/rest/s1/<root>/<bad>` lists the resource names at that level before any user check. `/rest/sm/<type>/<remote>` says whether a message type and a remote exist, names the remote's HMAC header, and on a failure returns the error text (`Error receiving message: {err}`) whatever the instance purpose. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/url.rs:499`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:480`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/message.rs:231`, `:248`, `:252`, `:290`. Fix: Answer an anonymous caller a plain 404 (no location, no names), one 403 text for every `/rest/sm` refusal, and an error id for its 500. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 441476173): for nobody signed in a missing screen path is `screen path not found` with no name or location (`url.rs` `not_found`) and an unknown Service REST resource is `Resource not found`; every `/rest/sm` refusal (unknown type or remote, missing or wrong signature, token or login, stale, replayed or unparseable timestamp) is 403 `System message not accepted` with the reason logged, and a 500 is `Error receiving message, id <n>` with the detail logged under that id. Tests `a_missing_screen_is_a_plain_not_found_for_nobody`, `service::system_sync` `a_refused_receive_tells_nothing_of_what_exists`, the /rest/sm features of `SecurityAccess.spec.js`. The redirect-or-401 against 404 of an existing protected path is unchanged, as in Java.
- **SEC-RTE-14 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Service REST entity lists have no upper bound.** `pageNoLimit=true` removes the 100-row default and `rest-max-page-size` is not applied here, so one request reads a whole table into memory and one response. Generic entity REST removes the parameter for this reason. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:731`. Fix: Apply the same removal and `rest-max-page-size` cap in `find_entity`. Fixed 2026-11-16: Service REST `find_entity` drops `pageNoLimit` from the request and caps a page at `rest-max-page-size`, 100 when no page is asked for; test `rest_tests.rs` `entity_resources_answer_encrypted_fields_as_set_and_read_a_page_at_most`.
- **SEC-RTE-15 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). `getPreferences` builds a JavaScript regular expression from the request.** The pattern is compiled by the script engine and tested against each of the user's preference keys, which the same user sets. A backtracking pattern against a long key of their own holds a request worker. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/user.rs:120`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/apps.xml:58`. Fix: Match with a prefix or a fixed set of keys, or cap the pattern's length and the script's time. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): `getPreferences(keyRegexp)` matches in the host with the `regex` crate, whose time is linear in the key, and takes a pattern of at most 256 characters (`script/host/user.rs` `key_matcher`); the script no longer builds a `RegExp`. The pattern syntax is the common one (no back-references or look-around), which every caller's pattern is. Test `script::host::user::tests::a_preference_key_pattern_matches_whole_keys_in_linear_time_and_is_capped`.
- **SEC-RTE-16 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). A refused token-route token is not counted.** The dispatch answers 404 and does not call `web::note_auth_failure`, which is what per-client web protection counts for sign-ins, device codes, git and CI tokens. Guess limits are then whatever each token service does itself. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/token_route.rs:300`-`365`. Fix: Call `note_auth_failure` in `dispatch` when `check_token` refuses a well-formed token. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit b547c4559): a token the route's service refuses is noted as a failed credential (`token_route.rs` `check_token` calls `web::note_auth_failure`), so the web protection counts it against the client as it does a wrong password; an accepted token and a token service that fails are not counted. Test `screen::token_route::tests::a_refused_token_is_noted_as_a_failed_credential`.
- **SEC-RTE-17 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The Login page verifies a password on every render and offers a one-click admin sign-in.** When `EX_JOHN_DOE` exists the page runs a password verification for each anonymous render (the stored hash is rehashed at login, so it may be the configured slow hash), and when the password is still `moqui` it shows a form that signs in as that `ADMIN` user. Nothing ties this to the instance purpose; it depends only on whether demo data was loaded. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:191`-`193`, `:249`-`256`. Fix: Run the section only when `instance_purpose` is `dev` or `test`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit b6437d069), the owner's call: the section only on a dev or test instance or with `demo_test_login=true`, which the demo component sets, and the `moqui` check cached a minute. The Login screen's Test Login (john.doe / moqui) and its password check run only when `instance_purpose` is `dev` or `test` or `demo_test_login` is true (the demo component's `MoquiConf.xml`); the check's answer is kept a minute in the local cache `webroot.login.test`, keyed by a digest of the stored hash (`UserServices.get#TestLoginOffered`, called by `Login.xml`). Tests LoginSetup "the Test Login is offered only on a dev or test instance or a demo, and its password check is cached", Demo "the demo's conf turns on the Login screen's Test Login".
- **SEC-RTE-18 (Info; reported (to check)). `/PublicShell` takes `tokenRoute` from a JSON body.** The map says no request parameter sets the token route's context. For a request to `/PublicShell` itself the screen reads `tokenRoute` from the context, where a JSON object parameter of that name lands, and builds the page's config (base path, title, context, the session token) from it. The page then loads `<basePath>.qvt` as a template with the compiler build of Vue. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/PublicShell.xml:24`. Fix: Have the token-route dispatch put `tokenRoute` in a request attribute the screen reads, and ignore a parameter of that name.
- **SEC-RTE-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An anonymous request to `/search/*` or `/dashboard/*` may send a 100 MiB body that is held in memory before any check.** The host takes the body limit from the servlet the path maps to (`max-body="100m"` on both proxies, which a default install declares whatever the search backend), reads the whole body into memory and copies it once more, and only then queues the request; the session, the sign-in and the `AuthFilter` run in the worker afterwards. So the 10 MiB cap of an anonymous body is 100 MiB on these two paths, within `body-timeout` (60 s) each: with per-client rules off (the default) the bound is the 4,096 connections, with them on 64 connections a client. SEC-RTE-07 closed the same gap for multipart bodies only (`anonymous_upload_limit`). The conf's comment "The bodies stream through" was not checked against `ProxyServlet.js`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1975-1976`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/servlet.rs:514-516`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1130`, `:1155-1159`, `:1432`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:840`, `:848`. Fix: Apply the SEC-RTE-07 rule to any body: a request whose limit is above `webapp_max_body` (a servlet's `max-body`) gets it only when `upload_has_user` holds, else `webapp_max_body`.
- **SEC-RTE-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A git access token mints a key for the LLM gateway at `POST /rest/s1/moqui/hosting/gatewayKey` (SEC-PFS-61's scope does not stop it).** A scoped key is taken only on the paths of its scope, and the `git` scope covers `/rest/s1/moqui/git/...` and all of `/rest/s1/moqui/hosting/...`, which holds `gatewayKey`; the path grant is `ALL_USERS`. `create#UserScopedKey` checks only that there is a user and makes a `gateway` key good for `device-expire-hours` (90 days by default). So a token "for git alone" yields a credential for another service, which outlives the token's own revoke or expiry and can be made again each time. A session with no recent sign-in makes one too, though the other lasting credentials ask for one (SEC-ADM-03, SEC-ADM-07), and nothing counts the keys made. What a `gateway` key may spend or reach at `/llm/v1` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1235-1240`, `:1042-1060`, `:799`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/user_keys.rs:762-778`; `runtime/components/base/tools/service/moqui.rest.xml:357`; `runtime/components/base/tools/data/ToolsSecurityData.xml:80`. Fix: Take the `git` scope only on `/rest/s1/moqui/git/...` and the read resources of `hosting` (`repository`, `mergeRequests`, `agreement`), or refuse `create#UserScopedKey` when the request signed in by a scoped key; ask for a recent sign-in when the caller is a session.
- **SEC-RTE-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `/rest/sm/{type}/{remote}` takes any message type on any remote's check.** The route checks that the type and the remote exist, authenticates by the remote's `messageAuthEnumId`, and calls `receive#IncomingSystemMessage` with the type from the URL; neither the route nor the service compares the type with the remote, and `SystemMessageRemote.systemMessageTypeId` ("this remote is for one type") is read by neither. The message is stored and consumed by the type's `consumeServiceName`, so whoever passes one remote's check (a partner with its HMAC secret or URL token, a `SYSMSG_RECEIVE` user for a `SmatLogin` remote, anyone for a `SmatNone` one) delivers a message of another type (an order import, an EDI interchange, a git webhook). The reviewer rates it Medium where a type's consume service trusts the type and does not look at the remote; the shipped receive and consume services were not read (Shippo's says it works on the gateways on the message's remote). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/message.rs:257-264`, `:397-408`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/SystemMessageServices.xml:334-365`. Fix: When the remote has a `systemMessageTypeId`, refuse another type in `receive_checked`; refuse a remote with none on the HMAC, token and `SmatNone` paths, or add a list of the types a remote may send.
- **SEC-RTE-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `get#ServerPrinters` connects to any host and port with the internal-address guard off, on a `GET`.** A member of `ADMIN` sends `GET /rest/s1/moqui/print/server?serverHost=<host>&serverPort=<port>`; the service posts an IPP request to that host and port with `deny_internal: false` whatever the instance, where a stored printer follows `allowPrivateNetwork`, then creates a `NetworkPrinter` row for each printer the server names, with that server's `description` and `location` texts, and answers the transport error. As a `GET` it carries no session token, so a link an administrator follows runs it. Not read: whether the `OPEN` client refuses a link-local or metadata address (`ipp.rs:98-106`), and the cookie's `SameSite` value for the cross-site case. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/tools.rs:518-562`; `runtime/components/base/tools/service/moqui.rest.xml:175-176`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ipp.rs:209`. Fix: Use `deny_private_network("")` (on in production) for the lookup, make the resource a `POST`, and create the rows disabled.
- **SEC-RTE-88 (Low; reported (to check)). The `//` screen path search keeps every miss in a map with no limit.** `ScreenUrlInfo::parse` passes the request path to `prepare_screen_path`, which takes a path that starts with `//` as a search. For a `//name` path not in the map, `find_subscreen_path` walks the subscreen tree (loading screen definitions), and the result, a miss too, is stored under the root location and the path in a plain `HashMap`, cleared only when the screen configuration generation moves or a definition is reloaded. Each distinct path costs a tree walk and one entry kept. The reviewer did not trace whether the HTTP layer hands a leading `//` through unchanged, or whether the per-source rate limits bound it; `A1.md` reads that hyper and axum do not normalize a path and the fallback gives the raw path to the core (`crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1118`), and that `//tools/x` resolves to `/apps/tools/x`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/url.rs:567-577`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/cache.rs:308-311`, `:607-618`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/mod.rs:315`. Fix: Do not take `//` from a request path (only from a screen's own URL), and cap the map (or do not store misses).
- **SEC-RTE-89 (Info; confirmed (second check)). `/echopath/<anything>`, a sample screen kept from Moqui Java, answers 500 to anyone on every instance.** `echopath.xml` (`require-authentication="false"`, standalone, `allow-extra-path`) renders the label `${sri.screenUrlInfo.extraPathNameList}`. The Rust `sri` has the method `getScreenUrlInfo()` and no `screenUrlInfo` property, which is likely why the expression fails: every request, anonymous ones included, is answered 500 (the proof's run). It echoes nothing; what is left is a route no application uses, which logs an error for each request. Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-15.2.3); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/echopath.xml:15-22`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/sri.rs:120-142`. Fix: Delete the screen and its line in the Routes map; if it is kept for testing, have it read `sri.getScreenUrlInfo().extraPathNameList` and require a user.
- **SEC-RTE-90 (Info; confirmed (second check)). A service's validation error in a screen transition answered as JSON is a 500.** A transition asked for JSON (`Accept: application/json`, a `.json` extension, the tui mode) answers through `send_json_redirect` and `send_json_response`, which, as Java's `sendJsonResponseInternal`, make any error in the message facade a 500, validation errors included. So a client's mistake is answered 500 with the right validation message in `errors`: `POST /Login/changePassword` without `newPasswordVerify`, refused by `update#Password`'s required parameter, is the case the proofs met; a transition's own missing `<parameter required>` is a 400 (`missing_parameter`). Every JSON transition answer with an error in the facade has this status, not only the sign-in page's. A monitor counts these as server faults, and a client cannot tell its error from the server's. Report: the security proofs, phase P4; lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1120-1166`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2026-2030`, `:2247-2254`, `:2472-2475`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:105-115`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/UserServices.xml:201`. Fix: Answer a JSON transition response whose errors are all validation errors with 400, and keep 500 for errors that are not the client's, as the REST servlet maps its error kinds (`web/mod.rs` `ErrorKind`).

## Administration, identity and sign-in

- **SEC-ADM-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Long-lived credentials are minted from a session with no recent sign-in.** A session holder gets an API key (no second factor at use, A2), an app password or an SSH key for the account without the password. `expireDays` has no upper bound. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/user_keys.rs:216-229, 382-401, 137-176`. Fix: Ask `check#RecentAuth` in the three create services and cap `expireDays`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `create#UserSshKey`, `create#UserAccessToken` and `create#UserAppPassword` need a sign-in in the last five minutes (`require_recent_auth`), and a token's `expireDays` is at most 366; `create#UserScopedKey` (the gateway key, narrower than the key that makes it) is left without the rule; test `service::user_keys_tests` `credentials_need_a_recent_sign_in_and_an_access_token_is_for_git_alone`. Follow-up 2026-11-16 (commit 67c0856f3): an administrator acting on another account needs a recent sign-in too, for its password, codes, factors, email and provider link, Login as, and the revoke of its keys, tokens, app passwords, SSH keys and phones (`revoke#AccountAccess`, every kind, and `revoke#Sessions` for another account's sessions); test `framework/test/AdminSessions.spec.js`.
- **SEC-ADM-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Login-as leaves no evidence and has no bound.** `internal_login_user` calls `internal_login` and not `recorded_login`, so no `UserLoginHistory` row is written, no audit row is written by the transition, and the session id is kept. The target is not compared with the caller (`heldBeyond`), and may be the caller's own or any account that is not a display account. Where: `base/tools/screen/System/Security/UserAccount/UserAccountDetail.xml:31-43`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:403-415`. Fix: Write an audit row (actor, target, visit) and apply `heldBeyond`. Fixed 2026-11-16: Tools' `loginAs` refuses the caller's own account and an account that holds access the caller lacks, and writes an `EntityAuditLog` row (`changedFieldName` `loginAs`, the administrator's user and visit) and a warning before the login; `UserLoginHistory` is still not written. Follow-up 2026-11-16 (commit 67c0856f3, and mr-boss Admin's `UserDetail.xml` `loginAs`): Login as, in Tools and in mr-boss Admin, also needs the administrator's sign-in in the last five minutes (`require#RecentAuth`), and logs in only when it is recent.
- **SEC-ADM-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `/sso/logout` signs the browser out on a GET.** The transition is `read-only` with no session token and `logout#User` is `anonymous-all`; it calls `crate::user::logout()` for any session, SSO or not. Where: `base/webroot/screen/webroot/sso.xml:40-45`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:551-580`. Fix: Take the logout by POST with the session token, as `/Login/logout`. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-RTE-08: `/sso/logout` is neither `read-only` nor `require-session-token="false"`, as `/Login/logout`; a cross-site GET is 403.
- **SEC-ADM-10 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Owner services run below `OperationServices` with inherited authorization.** See B4. An owner's registered service that relies on its artifact authorization, as the map warns for import targets below an app screen, is open to every holder of the operation permission. The transfer, audit and readiness families have a non-inheritable call step; this one does not. Where: `admin/OperationLib.js:59-72`; `base/framework/data/AdminConfigurationData.xml:204, 353, 367`. Fix: Call owners through a non-inheritable `call#OwnerService`, as `call#TargetService`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 1f756d031, mr-mantle 1ef16f53), the owner's call: a non-inheritable `call#OwnerService` step, and the mr-mantle owner services keep their own permission checks with `ALL_USERS` ALLOW grants, as `DataTransferServices` targets have. An owner's registered service is called only through `OperationServices.call#OwnerService` (`allow-remote` false; only a service a `RecoveryDescriptor` or `MailOutcomeOwner` row names) in the `AdminOperationCall` group (`inheritAuthz` N, `ALL_USERS` ALLOW), so the `AdminOperationServices` grant does not carry into it and it is authorized for the caller as a direct call is: `OperationLib.js` `callOwner` at the five owner sites (the framework's own steps keep `callRegistered`), and `find#MessageJobRunItems` joins `AdminOperationServices`. mr-mantle's five owner services have their own `ALL_USERS` ALLOW (`MantleOperationOwners`, `PartyAccessData.xml`) and each checks its own permission: the two `find#MailOutcomeOwners`, which had none, refuse (`PERMISSION_DENIED`) a caller who reads no mail queue (`ADMIN_OPERATION_VIEW` or `_MANAGE`, `ADMIN_CONNECTION_VIEW` or `_MANAGE`). Tests AdminOwnerCall "an owner service the operator holds no grant to is refused through execute#Recovery, a granted one runs", mr-mantle `OperationOwners.spec.js`.
- **SEC-ADM-11 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). The SSO sync writes an unverified `email` claim to `UserAccount.emailAddress`.** `map_fields` copies the claim whatever `email_verified` says, and the account is created or updated with it, authorization off. Anything that treats `UserAccount.emailAddress` as proof of a mailbox (`reset#Password` finds an account by it, `UserServices.xml:420`) reads an address its owner never confirmed. For an existing account `update#UserAccount` refuses a duplicate with "Email X is already in use. Login with username Y", which names another account and fails the sign-in. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:601-650, 762-777`. Fix: Map `email` only when `email_verified` is true, and give one generic refusal. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 2a53b8a29): the sync drops a mapped `emailAddress` unless the profile's `email_verified` claim is true (`sso.rs` `email_verified`, which the account link uses too): an existing account keeps its address, a new one gets none, logged. A provider that sends no `email_verified` claim no longer sets the address. Test `service::sso` `an_email_address_is_taken_only_when_the_provider_verified_it`.
- **SEC-ADM-12 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An anonymous reset can force a password change.** The reset sets `requirePasswordChange` Y on the account named. Every login of it, by password, key, app password or provider, then fails in `post_password` until the password is changed. Where: `UserServices.xml:404-462`. Fix: Require the change only when the reset password is used, not when it is requested. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-09: `reset#Password` leaves `requirePasswordChange` alone.
- **SEC-ADM-13 (Info; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `create#InitialAdminAccount` on a fresh instance.** The first caller becomes `ADMIN`. The check and the create are not under one lock, so two requests at once can both pass. An account made first by a provider sign-in or a registration turns the path off. Where: `UserServices.xml:464-505`. Fix: None needed beyond the documented rule; a unique marker row would close the race. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-08: the first administrator is made only from this machine or with the startup log's setup secret, under a row lock.
- **SEC-ADM-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A sign-in provider an instance administrator allowed on the private network keeps that when a delegated administrator repoints it.** The connection engine sets `allowPrivateNetwork` N when an endpoint changes on a production instance; `store#IdentityProvider` sets it only when it creates a flow, and its retest branch clears only the test mark. For a flow with `allowPrivateNetwork` Y (set in Tools), a holder of `ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE` changes `discoveryUri` to any internal `https` address through `saveConfiguration` and runs `test#IdentityProvider` (`testProvider`, `System/Security/IdentityProviderDetail.xml:35`, `:103`), and the server fetches it from inside the network; the test's text says whether it answered and how its document failed to parse. Confirmed for the service; the enforcement in the Rust client, and what `check#Flow` answers for an internal address that is not a discovery document, were not read. Where: `admin/ConfigurationProviders.js:481-484`, `:588-611`, against `admin/ConfigurationConnections.js:400-403`. Fix: on an endpoint change of a production instance set `flowChanges.allowPrivateNetwork = "N"`, as the connections do.
- **SEC-ADM-85 (Low; reported (likely)). A field map writes any claim to the account's email address when the provider verified another one (SEC-ADM-11's fix does not ask which claim was mapped).** An identity mapping is any claim name onto `emailAddress`, and a change of the mappings neither disables the provider nor asks for a test. SEC-ADM-11's fix drops the mapped address unless the `email_verified` claim is true, but does not ask that the mapped claim is `email`. With `nickname` (or another claim a user sets at the provider) mapped onto `emailAddress` by a holder of `ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE` (`saveFieldMap`, `System/Security/IdentityProviderDetail.xml:74`) on an enabled provider, a user whose own `email` the provider verified gets an address of their choosing written to their account, unverified, and `reset#Password` finds an account by that address (`UserServices.xml:472-474`). The account link and the verified address row use the `email` claim (`sso.rs:997-999`, `:1035-1051`), so neither is widened; `update#UserAccount`'s refusal of an address another account holds was not read. Where: `admin/ConfigurationProviders.js:405-420`, `:529-544`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:723-730`. Fix: in the sync take `emailAddress` only from the `email` claim, or only when the mapped value equals the `email` claim; or refuse another source for `emailAddress` in `fieldMapRows`.
- **SEC-ADM-86 (Low; reported (likely)). A translation with markup is saved through the import, though the single save refuses it.** `store#LocalizedMessage` refuses a `<` because its `localized` parameter is plain text. The row services of the `messageTranslations` import target take `rowValues`, a `Map` (`DataTransferServices.xml:144`), and call `storeLocalizedMessage` as a function; the parameter check looks for HTML only in a text or a list of texts, never inside a `Map`, and `translationProblem` checks length, control characters, links and placeholders, not markup. So a holder of `ADMIN_SETTINGS_MANAGE` who may run the import stores `<img src=x onerror=...>` as a translation; whether it runs depends on each place that shows a translated text without escaping it. The staging code of the import (whether a file's cell with `<` is refused there) was not read. Where: `admin/ConfigurationLocalization.js:389-432`, `:95-108`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/validate.rs:629-647`. Fix: refuse `<` in `translationProblem`, so every path holds it.
- **SEC-ADM-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). No configuration change asks for a recent sign-in.** Enabling a sign-in provider, changing its link rule or role mappings (`ADMIN_ACCESS_MANAGE`), replacing a connection's credentials, and repointing a relay and enabling it by a test are done by a holder of the manage permission from a session of any age, a taken-over one included. `grep RecentAuth` over the configuration, readiness and audit services finds nothing, nor do the screens' transitions ask (`IdentityProviderDetail.xml:124`, `ConnectionParts.xml:42`, `:73`), where the neighbouring identity actions do (`Identity.js:349`, `:542`, `:604`). Where: `admin/ConfigurationProviders.js:611-628`; `admin/ConfigurationConnections.js:288` (`lib.requireManage(MANAGE)`, the whole gate of `store`). Fix: call `require#RecentAuth` when a provider is enabled or its access parts change, and when a connection's endpoint or secret changes.
- **SEC-ADM-88 (Low; reported (to check)). A connection key that names no record starts a new connection of a kind marked not creatable.** `store#Connection` checks the adapter row's `creatable` flag only when no `connectionKey` is given; with a key that loads nothing, the engine treats the save as a creation and runs the kind's `newParts`. Reached by a holder of `ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE`. No framework kind is affected (`mailOAuth`, not creatable, has no `newParts` and is refused; `remote`, `relay` and `printer` are creatable); Mantle's `taxGateway` is `creatable="N"` (`mr-mantle/data/PaymentGatewayConnectionData.xml:26`) and its definition was not read. Where: `admin/ConfigurationConnections.js:1388-1389`, `:292-300`. Fix: in `storeConnection` refuse when the adapter is not creatable and `get` answers no connection for the key, or pass `creatable` into the engine.

## Tools, System, Monitor and Dashboards

- **SEC-TLS-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `export#EntityDataSnapshot` joins `baseFilename` into a path.** The zip is written to `runtime/db/snapshot/<baseFilename>.zip` with parent directories made, so a snapshot (every field of the chosen entities) lands in any directory the server can write, for example one a static route serves. The other snapshot transitions check `isSafeSinglePathSegment`; this one does not. The file is never replaced (`create_new`). Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/EntityServices.xml:276-284`. Fix: Refuse a `baseFilename` that fails `isSafeSinglePathSegment` in the service. Fixed 2026-11-16: `export#EntityDataSnapshot` refuses a `baseFilename` that is not one safe path segment (`WebUtilities.isSafeSinglePathSegment`); test `tools/test/EntityExport.spec.js`.
- **SEC-TLS-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The production internal-address check of a deploy URL is bypassed by user info, and fails open.** `check_url` takes the authority as the text before the first `/` or `:`, so with `user:password@host` it tests `user`, which does not resolve; a resolution error counts as not internal. The fetch then goes to the internal host. The check also resolves once, before the fetch resolves again. Where: `crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/git.rs:210-234`. Fix: Parse the URL with the `url` crate, treat a host that does not resolve as refused, and pass `deny_internal` to the fetch so the address used is the address checked. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part: `check_url` parses with the `url` crate, and on production a host that does not resolve, or has any internal address, is refused (test `deploy::git::tests::on_production_an_internal_or_unknown_deploy_host_is_refused`); left: the fetch resolves the host again (gix resolves itself and offers no hook to connect to the checked address). Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 6c112f7e4): on a production instance every deploy fetch connects only to an address it checked, the stored URL's at an update too. The in-process fetch runs under `moqui_git::deny_internal()` (`fs::DenyInternal`, a guard of the thread): over https its reqwest client (gix's `configure_client`) resolves through `moqui_lib::http::PublicOnly` with no proxy, at every connection and redirect, and refuses a request to an internal address written out (`configure_request`, `internal_literal`); over ssh `core.sshCommand` is pinned to a public address checked there, `ssh -o HostName=<address> -o HostKeyAlias=<host>`, so the host key known for the name still holds (`fs::pin_ssh`, `pinned_ssh_command`, which refuses a host a shell would read as more than a word). The clone storage's system `git` gets the same pin in its environment beside the token: curl's `http.curloptResolve` over https, the pinned `core.sshCommand` over ssh (`deploy::git` `network_allowed`, `address_pins`, `git_process_with`). Tests `deploy::git::tests::a_clone_fetch_is_pinned_to_the_checked_address`, moqui-git `fs::tests::the_guard_holds_for_its_life_on_its_thread`, `an_https_fetch_gets_the_public_only_client_and_an_ssh_one_a_pinned_command`.
- **SEC-TLS-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Secrets written to the server log by Tools and Instance.** Service Run logs every result value at INFO. `init#InstanceDocker` logs the whole container request, with each `Env` value (`entity_ds_password` among them, stored encrypted in `AppInstanceEnv.envValue`). The log is read in Monitor by any `SYSTEM_APP` holder and kept by retention. Where: `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Service/ServiceRun.xml:26-29`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/dev/InstanceServices.xml:439`. Fix: Drop both log lines, or log the names only. Fixed 2026-11-16: `init#InstanceDocker` logs the image and the names of the env and host settings, and Service Run logs the result names only.
- **SEC-TLS-10 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). `create#DatabaseMySQL` quotes the password for standard SQL only.** The password is put in a string literal with `'` doubled. MySQL's default mode also takes `\` as an escape, so a value with `\'` ends the literal early and the rest is read as SQL, with the database host's admin login. The seed's claim of interpolated identifiers is refuted: names must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$` (`:812-814`). Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/dev/InstanceServices.xml:816`, `:842`. Fix: Refuse a password with `\` or a quote, or send it as a bound parameter. Fixed 2026-11-16: `create#DatabaseMySQL` refuses a password holding `'`, `"` or `\`.
- **SEC-TLS-14 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). (telemetry reader) An ingest key's sender can pose as this server's own service and move the regression detector and recovery.** The receiver fixes only `source.id` and `source.name`. A sender sets `service.name`, `service.version` and `service.instance.id` to this server's own, and may post a `release` annotation for a version. `detect#Regressions` (every five minutes) and the release list select by `service.name` and never by source, so forged request metrics open a regression and an incident for the local service, which the seed `DEFAULT` policy escalates to the `ADMIN` group. Where an `automatic` policy and `allow-automatic="true"` are set, the incident can run the ladder (pause a job, deny an artifact group, drain an instance). Forged healthy data can equally hide a regression or let a verification pass. Spans can also carry any `user.id`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/telemetry/ingest.rs:878-891`, `crates/moqui-report/src/regression/mod.rs:144-157`, `:276-281`, `crates/moqui-report/src/annotations.rs:60-75`. Fix: Have the detector, the release list and verification read the local service only from records with no `source.id`, and treat a received service of the same name as another service (or refuse the local `service.name` from a key). Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): a receiver's decoder (`Decoder::receiving`, `crates/moqui-core/src/telemetry/ingest.rs`) keeps a received record that names this server's own `service.name`, in its resource or in a record or data point attribute, as another service's: its `service.name` becomes `<name> (received)` (`RECEIVED_SUFFIX`), so the regression detector, the release list and verification never read it as the local service (the resource map keeps what the sender sent). `POST /v1/annotations` refuses a `release` of the local service from an ingest key (`annotations.rs` `ingest_refusal`); this server's releases are the ones it records at start. Tests `telemetry::ingest::tests::a_received_record_that_names_this_servers_own_service_is_kept_as_another_service` and `annotations::tests::an_ingest_key_writes_no_business_event_and_no_release_of_this_servers_own_service`.
- **SEC-TLS-15 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). (telemetry reader) An ingest key owned by an administrator writes business annotations for any organization.** The receiver calls `create_annotation` as the key's owner. For `business` the check is the owner's own grant of `org.moqui.report.annotation.business`, which `ADMIN` holds, so a telemetry credential placed on another machine writes a business event marker (255 bytes of text and a path on this server as its link) into the dashboards of any organization. The code's comment says the technical sources write no business event. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/annotations.rs:19-31`, `crates/moqui-host/src/monitor/receiver.rs:224-243`. Fix: In the receiver path refuse `source` `business` (the shared map already carries `monitor.ingest.source`). Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): `create_annotation` refuses `source` `business` when it is called through the OTLP receiver (the shared map's `monitor.ingest.source`), whoever owns the key (`annotations.rs` `ingest_refusal`). Tests `annotations::tests::an_ingest_key_writes_no_business_event_and_no_release_of_this_servers_own_service` and, over HTTP, `monitor_edge::otlp_keys_partial_success_compression_and_request_context` (400).
- **SEC-TLS-16 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). (telemetry reader) `Settings/revertAll` runs on a GET with no token.** The transition has no `method`, so it accepts GET; a GET is never token-checked, and a transition with actions is refused on GET only when the URL carries parameters, which this one does not need. Every runtime override on every instance is reverted (a raised log level, a receiver turned off for an incident, a sample ratio). The change is logged with the victim as the user. Where: `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/Monitor/Settings.xml:17-20`. Fix: `method="post"` on every writing transition of `Settings.xml`, `Recovery.xml`, `IncidentDetail.xml`, `Incidents.xml` and `ReleaseDetail.xml`, as `Checks.xml` and `Sources.xml` have. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-04: `revertAll` and the other writing transitions run actions and are not `read-only`, so a GET another site started is refused.
- **SEC-TLS-17 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). (telemetry reader) The ingest routes do not count failed keys, and gRPC decodes before it authenticates.** A well-formed unknown token takes one of the eight `concurrent-requests` permits and a blocking thread for a database read, and its refusal is not counted anywhere (the rate limit is per accepted key; the deploy attestation by contrast counts a bad signature as a failed sign-in). A flood keeps the eight permits busy and real senders get 503. On gRPC the message, up to `body-limit` unpacked, is decoded before the key is looked at and outside the permits. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/monitor/receiver.rs:53-78`, `crates/moqui-host/src/monitor/grpc.rs:95-109`. Fix: Count a refused key against the client address as a failed sign-in, and check the key in a tonic interceptor before the message is decoded. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit d40982257): a refused ingest key (401 with a key given) is a web protection `AuthFailure` of its client on both transports (`monitor::receiver::note_refusal`, from the HTTP `ingest` and from the gRPC `Otlp::receive` with the listener's `grpc_listen::Caller`). Test `monitor::receiver::tests::a_refused_ingest_key_is_a_failed_sign_in_of_its_client`. Left: checking the key before the gRPC message is decoded (tonic's interceptors are synchronous and the check reads the database; proposed an async tower layer on the OTLP routes that authorizes the metadata's key); decoding stays bounded by `body-limit`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 6c112f7e4): each collector service is wrapped in `monitor::grpc::KeyFirst`, an async service that reads the key from the request's headers and awaits `receiver::authorize` (a permit, then the key and its rate) before the inner service reads and decodes the message; a refusal is the matching gRPC status and, for a key given, a failed sign-in of the caller (`note_refusal`), and the `Authorized` with its permit goes to `Otlp::receive` in the request's extensions, held until the request is done. Both mount points, the gRPC port and h2 on the HTTPS port, take it from `add_routes`. Tests `monitor::grpc::tests::a_request_without_a_key_is_refused_before_its_message_is_read`, `tests/it/monitor_grpc.rs` (an oversized message with a made-up key is `Unauthenticated`, not `OutOfRange`).
- **SEC-TLS-18 (Info; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). (telemetry reader) Request spans keep the raw session id and unredacted query values for every `SYSTEM_APP` reader.** Each request span stores `session.id`, the bound session's id, and `url.query` with every value whose parameter name is not on the secret-name list. The id is the first half of the session cookie (`moqui.session={id}.{mac}`), and query values hold search terms, record ids and any secret a screen passes under an unlisted name. The spans are kept seven days and, with `otlp-endpoint` set, sent to the outside collector. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1398`, `:1456`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/session.rs:1210-1221`. Fix: Store a hash of the session id, as a correlation key only. Fixed 2026-11-17 for the session id (commit 2a53b8a29): a request span's `session.id` is 32 hex characters of the id's SHA-256 (`http::session_tag`), the same for each request of a session, never the id that signs it in. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 892c810df): `url.query` and the Referer's query keep every parameter's name and, by default, the value only of `pageIndex`, `pageSize`, `orderByField`, `renderMode`, `lastStandalone` and `_uiType` (`http::KEPT_QUERY_VALUES`), the others `[…]`; the Referer loses its fragment.
