The new `<telemetry query-values>` is `names` by default, and `all` keeps every value for debugging, a secret's still redacted (`redacted_query`, `redacted_referer`, read through `RequestTelemetry.all_query_values` so a conf reload takes effect). Tests `http::tests::a_screen_request_has_a_root_span_with_identity_and_health_is_independent` (a search term and a Referer's query dropped, paging kept), `a_span_keeps_query_values_only_when_the_conf_says_all`.
- **SEC-TLS-19 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). (telemetry reader) The recovery callback compares token hashes with `!=` and tells an unknown action from a wrong token.** The stored SHA-256 of the callback token is compared with the hash of the presented one by string inequality, not in constant time, and the answers `no recovery action <id>`, `the callback token does not match the action` and `... is <status>, not active` are returned to the caller (HTTP 400 with the text), so action ids and states can be probed. The token is 32 random characters, so the comparison leak is not practical. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/recovery/execute.rs:598-612`. Fix: One generic refusal for all three cases and a constant-time compare.
- **SEC-TLS-96 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The backup mirror reads neither `disabled` nor `allowPrivateNetwork` of its remote, and takes an `http` S3 endpoint.** `mirror` reads the remote's `sendUrl`, `username`, `password`, `privateKey`, `publicKey`, `remoteAttributes` and `systemMessageTypeId` and nothing else, so a remote marked `disabled` still receives every archive, and one with `allowPrivateNetwork` `N` on a production instance is still reached at a loopback or private address; the other senders of the same record kind enforce `disabled`. An S3 `endpoint` may be `http://`, so a plain archive and the signed requests cross the network in clear, and encryption of the archive is opt-in (`mirrorEncrypt`). Not confirmed: whether `S3Client` or `SftpClient` applies an internal-address guard of its own (not read). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/backup.rs:200`, `:213-271`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/s3.rs:91-95`. Fix: Refuse a mirror to a `disabled` remote; apply the remote's `allowPrivateNetwork` as the other senders do; take an `http` endpoint only for this machine on an instance that is not production; log a warning, or refuse on production, when the mirror is not encrypted.
- **SEC-TLS-97 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `moqui save-db` and the desktop app's backups write the dump with the process umask (SEC-DAT-06's fix covers the backup job's dump only).** SEC-DAT-06's fix makes the job's dump `0600` before `pg_dump` writes it (`crates/moqui-drivers/src/backup.rs:194` `private_partial`). `save_database` hands `pg_dump` a path it creates itself, and the desktop app makes its folder with `create_dir_all`, so the file and the folder get the umask's modes (`0644` and `0755` with the usual `022`). After Back up now, a restore's `-before-restore` dump or `moqui save-db`, the whole database is readable by any local account that can reach the folder (by default `MR-Moqui Backups` in the user's Documents). Not confirmed: the modes of the Documents directory on each platform (macOS keeps it `0700`). Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:163-169`, `:348-362`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/backup.rs:381-405`. Fix: Create the dump (and each sibling database's) with `private_fs::file_options()` before `pg_dump` runs, as `dump_one` does, and make a new backup folder with `private_fs::create_dir_all`.
- **SEC-TLS-98 (Low; reported (likely)). A restore also loads every file beside the chosen dump that shares its suffix, into a database the file's name names.** `load_save` restores the chosen dump into `moqui`, then, for each other file in the same directory named `<name><suffix>` with `<name>` letters, digits and `_`, creates a database `<name>` if missing and runs `pg_restore --clean` of that file as the superuser. Only `moqui` and `postgres` are left out (`template1` is not), and the desktop app checks only the chosen file's name. So whoever can write a file into the backup folder (a shared or synced Documents folder) has it run by the cluster's superuser when the operator later restores a dump from that folder. Not confirmed: what a custom-format archive can make `pg_restore` run as a superuser (Postgres, not this code); the chosen dump is trusted the same way, so this widens it only to files the operator did not pick. Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:398-431`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/backup.rs:213-215`, `:537-548`. Fix: Restore only siblings named `moqui_<name>` (the prefix `save_db` and the job write), refuse `template0`, `template1` and `postgres`, and name each sibling in the confirmation.
- **SEC-TLS-99 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The table check runs DDL without `DATABASE_ADMIN` and writes no audit row.** `Schema/Entities` `checkTables` and `Schema/Entity` `checkTable` (`method="post"`, `authz-action="all"`) call `ec.entity.checkTables`, which forgets what is known of the tables and creates or extends the tables, indexes and keys of every entity, in the blocking forms, on the request's thread. Nothing asks `DATABASE_ADMIN`, which the planned jobs of the same screens need (`crates/moqui-tools/src/schema.rs:191`), and no `DatabaseAudit` row is written. The statements are the model's, so the effect is a schema brought to the model and the locks that takes. Where: `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Schema/Entities.xml:23-36`, `Schema/Entity.xml:25-33`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1633-1684`. Fix: Ask `DATABASE_ADMIN` in both transitions and write a `DbaJob` or new audit action row.
- **SEC-TLS-100 (Low; reported (likely)). A Tools holder without the SQL permission plants SQL in a grid view or saved query that another user runs.** `store#GridView` refuses a SQL source from a user who may not run SQL, and both own services write only the caller's own rows, but `moqui.database.GridView` and `moqui.database.SavedQuery` are in no list of `sensitive.rs`, so the grid's own save (or Browse, entity REST, a load) takes them: a row with `isShared` Y, or another user's row (`ownerUserId` is a field like any other), with a `sourceJson` of type `sql`. `find#GridViews` answers it to every Tools user, and when a SQL holder opens it the query runs read only as that user; with SEC-TLS-86 a read-only query is not harmless. The run checks only the runner's own permission (`source_of`). Not confirmed: whether `Grid.qvue` shows another user's SQL before it runs, and a view whose owner was forged to be the victim; `generic_access` (`sensitive.rs:405-415`) was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sensitive.rs:72-82`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/grid_service.rs:57-71`, `:356-369`, `:428-431`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/grid.rs:1853-1866`. Fix: Add `gridview` and `savedquery` to the service-written keys.
- **SEC-TLS-101 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An external database connection reaches internal addresses, and other cluster members keep its old pool.** On an instance that is not production a `DATABASE_EXTERNAL` holder (`ADMIN_ADV`) stores a connection whose host `check_host` takes as given (loopback, private and link-local included, any port; it refuses only a socket path, a pipe and white space), so `test#DatabaseConnection` is a TCP probe of the server's network that answers connected or not. The Postgres driver answers a server's request for the password in clear text (`pgwire.rs:577-581`), so with `sslMode` `disable` a stored password goes in the clear to whatever answers. The pools are a process map: `forget` after a store or delete clears this process only, so on another member a pool to the old host, with the old password and the old `readOnly`, serves `db=ext:<id>` while it is used, a deleted connection's too. Where: `crates/moqui-tools/src/ext.rs:93-96`, `:146-155`, `:198-207`, `:515-562`. Fix: Refuse loopback, private and link-local addresses unless a conf entry allows them; key the pool by the row's `lastUpdatedStamp` so a changed row is never served from an old pool.
- **SEC-TLS-102 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The database audit understates what changed and leaves several actions out.** `isChange` is `!read_only && statement.changes`, and `changes` is the classifier's guess: a `SELECT` of a function that writes, or `EXPLAIN (COSTS, VERBOSE, BUFFERS, TIMING, ANALYZE) DELETE ...` (`plain_read` looks for `analyze` in the first four words only), is confirmed and audited as no change in a run with changes allowed. A refused attempt is audited only in the allow-changes branch, not a refusal for a parameter, an offset or a run id; `store#`, `delete#` and `test#DatabaseConnection` write no row; an export or read of an entity source writes none (only a SQL source's query does); and a SQL run with changes can change or delete the `DatabaseAudit` table itself, or put a rule on it, in the same database. Where: `crates/moqui-tools/src/sql.rs:174-193`, `:879-898`, `:1112`, `:1184`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/ext.rs`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/grid.rs:1382-1534`. Fix: Audit every statement of a run with changes allowed as a change; search every word for `analyze`; write `DbaConnection` rows in `ext.rs`; say in the map that the audit is not proof against a SQL holder.
- **SEC-TLS-103 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder changes another user's ETL mapping and it still runs as its owner.** `store#EtlMapping` lets a `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder replace every field of another user's mapping and keeps `ownerUserId`. A cron run of `run#EtlMapping` whose job has the owner as a `ServiceJobUser` (`System/Operations/JobDetail.xml` `createJobUser`) then signs in as that owner and runs the changed mapping: a service target of the editor's choice (any service but a `delete#`) is called with constants or with rows of an entity source, under the owner's permissions, sealed ones the editor lacks included, and the run and its audit row are the owner's. The editor cannot add an expression or a SQL source without holding their permissions (`service.rs:1074-1075`). Not confirmed: the `JobDetail.xml` transitions that add the job's user were read as definitions only; which sealed permissions a `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder typically lacks (both are `ADMIN_ADV`'s in the seed). Where: `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/service.rs:121-154`, `:175-218`, `:463-481`, `:963-1011`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/mapping.rs:362`. Fix: When a user who is not the owner stores a mapping, make that user the owner, or keep `lastChangedByUserId` and have `sign_in_owner` refuse a mapping its owner did not save last.
- **SEC-TLS-104 (Low; reported (likely)). `remove#Component` moves aside the directory a `DeployedComponent` row names.** `remove#Component` needs the `ADMIN` group only; it takes `location` from the row and renames that directory to `.removed/<name>-<millis>` beside it, whatever directory it is (the runtime's `conf`, `db`, another component). `deploy#Component` and `discard#ComponentDeploy` (`COMPONENT_DEPLOY`) likewise rename or delete `.staging-<name>` beside the row's `location`, with `componentName` from the request and no `check_name`. So an `ADMIN` with no sealed permission who writes `moqui.server.DeployedComponent.location` (the map's threat model says a record editor writes it) and then uses `System/Components.xml` `removeComponent` moves any directory the server can rename. Not confirmed: the generic write of the row. Where: `crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/load.rs:673-703`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/git.rs:404-434`, `:458-467`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/services.rs:275-294`. Fix: Resolve the directory from the component name and the conf's component directories, never from the row; call `check_name` in `remove`, `deploy` and `discard`; add `deployedcomponent` and `deployedcomponentattestation` to the service-written list.
- **SEC-TLS-105 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A report or alert over a service dataset counts as technical, and `create#Schedule` sends it to any user or group.** `create#Schedule` stores `userId` and `userGroupId` as sent and asks no group-delivery artifact; at a run a schedule that is not "business" is rendered as its owner and mailed in one message to the account named and every member of the group named, with no scope comparison and no occurrence record. A schedule is business only when `store#Schedule` saved it or its definition reads a business dataset or one with a `rowFilterService` or `contextFilters`, so a service dataset is not (though the module comment says a service source "is never comparable, so it is delivered to its owner alone"), nor a dataset restricted only by its artifact and `DataDocumentUserGroup` rows, nor a telemetry dataset: the owner's rendering of a service report goes to recipients who could not run it. In the same way `get#AlertState` answers the owner's labels and last values of a technical rule over a service dataset to any reader of the rule who may call the service. The owner decides the recipients and already reads the data, so this is a control the map claims that an owner can pass, not a read by an outsider; reached by `ADMIN` through `Dashboards/Reports.xml` `createSchedule` and by `BOSS_INSIGHTS_DISTRIBUTE` in mr-boss. Not confirmed: whether mr-boss ships a service dataset its Insights groups read. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/schedule.rs:63-118`, `:1461-1528`; `crates/moqui-report/src/delivery.rs:579-616`; `crates/moqui-report/src/alert_delivery.rs:835-842`. Fix: Make `viewer_scoped` true for a body with `serviceName`; ask `org.moqui.report.delivery.group` in `create#Schedule` when `userId` is not the owner or `userGroupId` is set, and at the run of an owner-mode schedule as `send_each` does (`delivery.rs:1426`).
- **SEC-TLS-106 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A technical alert rule notifies any user or group, under any topic, with the author's title.** A `notification` contact's `userId`, `userGroupId` and `topic` are taken from the rule, and a persistent notification goes to that user or every member of that group with the rule's title and its `detailLink` (a plain server path) with `?definitionId=`, again every `repeatSeconds` (60 at least) while the rule fires. Nothing asks whether the author may notify that user or group or use that topic, so any author sends an in-app message that looks like the system's own; reached by anyone who may call `save#Definition` for an `alert` or a `contactPoint` (`DASHBOARDS_APP`; `BOSS_INSIGHTS_AUTHOR` and `BOSS_INSIGHTS_DATA` in mr-boss, whose screens refuse it by policy but the service does not). The title is not run as script and the link cannot leave the server. Not confirmed: what a subscriber of another topic does with a message of this shape. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/alert.rs:564-599`, `:908-923`. Fix: Take a `userGroupId` only as `may_share_with` allows, a `userId` other than the owner only with the group delivery artifact, and a topic only from the alert topics.
- **SEC-TLS-107 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An alert rule's `incident` label opens a Monitor incident with no `MONITOR_INCIDENT`, and recovery decides it at full confidence.** `save#Definition` asks nothing but a signed-in user for an `alert`; when a series goes to Firing and the rule or the series has the label `incident="true"`, `open_from_alert` creates a `MonitorIncident` with authorization off, its service the `service.name` or `service` label (else the rule id) and its severity the `severity` label, unchecked. The recovery job builds its facts with `confidence` 1.0 because the incident has no regression, so a policy's `minConfidence` never refuses a step: under the seed's advisory `DEFAULT` the result is an escalation to the `ADMIN` group, and under an `automatic` policy with `allow-automatic="true"` a step with a named target (`pause-job`, `pause-alert-rule`, `disable-artifact`, a webhook `restart`) is approved and started with no `MONITOR_RECOVER` holder (the reviewer rates that case Medium, where Dashboards is granted to someone without `MONITOR_RECOVER`). The same holds for an incident row written through Tools › Data, since `MonitorIncident` is not a service-written entity; the rule's owner reaches it through `Dashboards/AlertRule.xml` `storeAlert` or `Definition.xml` `save` (`DASHBOARDS_APP`, `ADMIN` in the seed) and the jobs `evaluate_ReportAlerts_minute` and `run_Recovery_minute`. Not confirmed: `approved_labels` (`alert.rs:1217`) was not read, so whether a rule's own labels are narrowed first; `execute::start` was not read again; `isolate-instance` and `rollback` are refused for such an incident by their own rules. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/alert.rs:1392-1397`; `crates/moqui-report/src/incident.rs:328-384`; `crates/moqui-report/src/recovery/mod.rs:205-212`, `:405-407`; `crates/moqui-report/src/recovery/policy.rs:312-314`, `:365`. Fix: Ask `MONITOR_INCIDENT` of the rule's owner (their own permission, at the evaluation) before `open_from_alert`; take the severity only from `minor`, `major`, `critical`; give an incident with no regression a confidence below every automatic threshold, or refuse automatic steps for a `source` of `alert` and `user`.
- **SEC-TLS-108 (Low; reported (likely)). A git sync reads and writes any hosted repository with no check of that repository's access.** A sync row takes any `repositoryId` and `path`; push writes `<path>/<definitionId>.json` into that repository as a commit and removes the files of tracked definitions that are gone, and pull lists the directory and reads every `*.json` file. All of it goes through `ec.factory().resource()` on a `git://` location, which the git provider does not check against `AT_GIT_REPOSITORY` (its comment leaves that to the service that uses the location), and `git_sync.rs` asks only `org.moqui.report.gitSync`, so that grant is also write access to every hosted repository (definition-shaped JSON at a path of the holder's choice, on the configured branch) and read access to every `*.json` file that parses as a definition. In the seed `ADMIN` holds all of `AT_GIT_REPOSITORY` and the demo takes both away together; it matters where git sync is granted to a report administrator with no repository rights, and for a repository a component is deployed from. Not confirmed: the effect (the missing check is confirmed in code): the `git://` provider's write path was read only at `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:785-812`; whether a git ECA `pre_check` applies branch rules to these writes; what a component deploy does with such a file. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/git_sync.rs:245-282`, `:285-374`, `:469-476`, `:545-595`; `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:305-307`, `:460-466`. Fix: In `store_sync`, `push` and `pull` ask `AT_GIT_REPOSITORY` of the repository for the sync's user (`view` to pull, `update` to push) and, at `store_sync`, for the caller too.
- **SEC-TLS-109 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A git sync runs as any user its creator names.** `store_sync` stores the `userId` given (the transition's `service-call` has no `in-map`, and the form offers the field), and push and pull sign that account in with `internal_login_user`. A pull saves, as that user, whatever definition files the repository holds (it replaces any definition that user owns, shares with any group that user may share with, and saves a `dataset` if that user holds `org.moqui.report.dataset`), and a push writes every definition of the folder that user may read, private ones included, into the repository. No check ties the named user to the caller: not `ADMIN_LOGIN_AS`, not the `heldBeyond` rule `loginAs` applies, not a recent sign-in; an alert rule already active keeps running after a pulled change, as its owner. Not confirmed: whether `internal_login_user` refuses a disabled account (read only as a call). Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/git_sync.rs:80-99`, `:190-191`, `:313`, `:329`, `:549-595`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Dashboards/GitSync.xml:7`, `:45`. Fix: Let `store_sync` take only the caller's own `userId` unless the caller holds `ADMIN_LOGIN_AS` and the `heldBeyond` rule passes; audit the choice on `moqui.monitor.audit`.
- **SEC-TLS-110 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). One session can fill the replay index, and a replay's chunks are keyed by an id the page chooses.** When `telemetry/browser` `enabled` and `replay-enabled` are true (both off by default) any signed-in user posts to `/rum/replay`; the size and time caps are for one `replayId`, which the sender picks (16 to 64 letters, digits and dashes), so with 120 posts a minute and a 1 MiB body one session stores about 120 MiB a minute under fresh ids, and `replay-sample-ratio` is applied only by the page. The budget map is one process's memory and holds 4,096 ids: after an eviction, a restart or on another member `charge` makes a new entry with whoever posts first as the owner, so a session that knows another replay's id can add chunks to it, and a chunk with the same `seq` has the same document `_id`. The id is 8 characters of the session's id and 24 random hex characters, listed only to `MONITOR_REPLAY` holders, so that attacker already reads replays. Not confirmed (to check): whether the index replaces a document of the same `_id` or refuses it. Where: `crates/moqui-report/src/browser.rs:106-129`, `:211-251`, `:264-336`. Fix: Cap replay bytes for a user and visit per hour; keep the owner in the first chunk's document and check it from the index when the budget has no entry.
- **SEC-TLS-111 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A co-editing join with a bad state vector is counted as a member and never leaves, so the room is never saved on its last editor's leave and never closed.** `join` adds one to `room.members` and records the user's edit right before it decodes the client's state vector, and returns a refusal when that does not decode; the host adds no member for a refused join, so nothing calls `leave` for it. `members` stays above zero for the life of the process: the room and its document stay in memory and the tick's save and close never runs, and for a page kept in git that is the only automatic save of a version, so its co-edited text stays in the `CollabDocument` row until someone presses Save. Any signed-in user who may view the page does it with `{collab: "join", doc, client, sv: <bytes that are not a state vector>}`. Where: `crates/moqui-collab/src/lib.rs:587-595`, `:718-733`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/collab.rs:153-158`. Fix: Decode the state vector before `room.members += 1`, or count the member only when `join` returns `Ok`.
- **SEC-TLS-112 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A co-editor's right to change a page is checked at the join only.** `join` asks `get#WikiPageAccess` once and keeps the answer in `room.can_edit` by user id; `apply` reads that entry for every later update, `leave` does not remove it, nothing refreshes it, and the socket's own 60-second check is of the session and the account, not the page. So a user whose update or view access to a wiki page is taken away goes on changing the document, and reading the others' updates and awareness, until their socket closes; the tick's save as them fails the wiki's own check, but the next save by another editor writes their changes into a version. Not confirmed: that `update#WikiPage` refuses the revoked editor when the tick saves as them (the service was not read). Where: `crates/moqui-collab/src/lib.rs:579-590`, `:632-639`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/collab.rs:204-207`, `:230-237`. Fix: Ask `access` again in `apply` when the cached answer is older than a minute, and in the tick for each member; drop a member who may no longer view.
- **SEC-TLS-113 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Presence and membership on the notification socket have no count or size limit, and `get#CalcCells` stores any JSON as a selection.** A socket may join one CalcGrid room or co-edited page under any number of client ids (100 characters each): each join adds a member, sets a presence entry, sends a `peer` frame to every viewer and publishes it to the cluster, and a collab join also costs a request worker and the wiki access call; no count or rate is kept per socket or user. `get#CalcCells` stores the caller's `selection` as given, of any shape and size, for 60 seconds, and the other viewers get it in `peers` (the socket's own `select` keeps four numbers); a collab `awareness` frame's `update` and a join's `awareness` are any JSON up to the socket's 16 MiB message and go on to every editor, the join's kept for the socket's life. A co-edited document has no size limit: every update up to 16 MiB is merged and the whole state is encoded and written again at each flush. Not confirmed: whether the browser clients draw a peer's area or an awareness field as markup. Where: `crates/moqui-calc/src/service.rs:807-820`; `crates/moqui-calc/src/together.rs:394-405`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/calc.rs:116`, `:147-169`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/collab.rs:129`, `:147`, `:159-193`, `:230-237`; `crates/moqui-collab/src/lib.rs:511`. Fix: Keep the service path's selection to the four numbers, as the socket does; cap the clients of a socket for one room or page (4), an awareness (4 KiB) and a co-edited document's state (a few MiB), and count joins a minute.
- **SEC-TLS-114 (Low; reported (likely)). A workbook opened as a CalcGrid document is unpacked and parsed with no limit on its expanded size.** `open#CalcDocument` with a `workbookLocation` of the caller's uploads (under the Tools app's own access; refused under a profile) holds the file to 32 MiB as stored, then IronCalc's xlsx reader builds an in-memory XML tree of each part (`XmlNode::parse(BufReader::new(file))`) with no bound on the part's expanded size or the tree's nodes. Deflate expands about a thousand times, so a 32 MiB file can ask for tens of gigabytes; the same reader is behind `calculate#Spreadsheet` (Service Run or a script only). There is no XML entity expansion (the reader drops `DocType`). Not confirmed: the `zip` crate's own behaviour; the fallback reader's limits beyond `MAX_SHEET_CELLS`. Where: `crates/moqui-calc/src/service.rs:695-711`; `crates/moqui-core/src/workbook.rs:270-273`; IronCalc `xlsx/src/import/shared_strings.rs:49-50`, `worksheets.rs:343-344`. Fix: Before the engine reads it, walk the zip's entries and refuse a workbook whose declared expanded sizes pass a limit (256 MiB), and read each part through a `take`.
- **SEC-TLS-115 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The `DbaEtlRun` audit row's detail is not JSON and takes the target name unescaped.** The format string has `"mappingId"`, `"target"` and `"jobName"` in that order and the arguments are the mapping id, the job name as JSON and the target name, so `target` holds `"null"` or the quoted job name inside quotes, and `jobName` the raw target name, which the caller chose, unquoted and unescaped. A reader of the audit detail of any ETL run gets text that does not parse, and a target name can add keys to it. Not confirmed: who reads `detailJson`. Where: `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/service.rs:635-642`. Fix: Build it with `serde_json::json!`.

## LLM, A2A, MCP and Assist

- **SEC-AI-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Assistant Markdown keeps same-site image paths, which the browser fetches with the session.** External images become placeholders, but an image whose `src` is a path of this site stays an `<img>`. The browser then GETs that path, with any query string, with the user's cookies and no click. Any GET on the site that has an effect or a cost (an export, a report, a read that marks something) can be triggered this way. Where: `js/WebrootAssist.js:300-305`, `:326-329`, `:335`. Fix: keep an image only under static prefixes (`/images/`, `/libs/`, an attachment route), as the canvas image rule at `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:340-342` already does. Fixed 2026-11-16: `WebrootAssist.js` `siteImage` keeps an image only of a static image path (no query, not under `/apps`, `/rest`, `/assist` and the like) or `/richTextImage/<id>`; test `WebrootAssist.page.js`.
- **SEC-AI-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An external link in an answer carries whatever the model put in its URL.** A Markdown link to any `https://` or `http://` URL is kept, with the host as its title, and opens in a new tab. An injected instruction can have the model write a link whose query holds data from the conversation. The "invented" list drops only links the server marked. Where: `js/WebrootAssist.js:371-376`, `:351-353`. Fix: show the full URL on hover or in a confirm for an external link with a query string, or allow external links only to hosts a tool result returned. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit adb7b60a0): `WebrootAssist.js` `renderMarkdown` marks an external link whose URL carries data past its host and path (a query, a fragment, or a user and password; `assist.externalCarries`) with its whole address as its title, and a click opens it only after the user OKs a dialog that shows the whole URL and its host (`assist.confirmExternal`), then `noopener,noreferrer`; `assist.hostOf` names the host after any `user:password@`. Client test `WebrootAssist.page.js` "an external link that carries data shows its whole address and asks before it opens (SEC-AI-04)".
- **SEC-AI-05 (Low; reported (likely)). With "other sites" on, a Vue SFC canvas can send data out in an image URL.** `x=1` adds `https:` to the frame's `img-src`. The read proxy is then refused, so the component cannot read screens. The component's source and its initial values are still the model's, and the model has the conversation, so an injected instruction can have it write `<img src="https://host/?d=...">` with that data. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/sandbox.rs:43-45`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/assist/canvas.rs:545-549`. Fix: say in the preference's label that the canvas's own content can leave; or allow listed image hosts.
- **SEC-AI-06 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). One user can hold every Assist event stream.** The stream count is one node-wide counter with no per-user part (A2A and MCP waits have 16 per user). One client fills it, and every other drawer gets 503 until the streams end. Each stream also holds a thread. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/sse.rs:47`, `:55-63`, `:169`. Fix: count streams per user as `host/a2a.rs` `Open::take(user_id)` does. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit e2c5bcd0b): `GET /assist/runs/{runId}/events` holds at most 16 streams of one user on a node (`MAX_STREAMS_PER_USER`, `Open::take(user_id)` as A2A's), the next 429 with `Retry-After`; the node's 256 stay (503). Test `llm::sse::tests::one_user_holds_at_most_their_share_of_the_event_streams`.
- **SEC-AI-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `/find/rank` sends caller text to the embedding model with no quota or external-user check.** One post may carry 5000 texts of 2000 characters. They go to the provider in batches of 64. The path checks no user quota, counts no tarpit hit (`track-artifact-hit="false"`), and does not refuse an `EXTERNAL_USER`, though `complete` does. With a hosted embedding model that is spend and egress of arbitrary text by any account. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/find.xml:9-20`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/embed.rs:206-240`. Fix: check `is_external_user` and the `api` quota in `embed_batches`, and count an `AT_LLM` hit. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 092541ccd): `LlmFacade::embed_batches`, for a call with a current user, refuses an `EXTERNAL_USER`, checks the user's `api` quota and counts an AT_LLM tarpit hit `embed:<modelId>` (artifact group `LlmEmbedCalls`, ALL_USERS 120 a minute); work with no user and a call with nothing left to embed make no check. Test `members_tests::an_embedding_call_for_an_external_or_over_quota_user_is_refused`. Release note: an external user's hybrid or semantic search on a hosted embedding model now fails.
- **SEC-AI-08 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). `search-form-inputs` on the audit lists lets an `AgentAdmin` test content without `AgentAudit`.** The lists select metadata only, and content needs `AgentAudit` with a logged read. The find's search form takes a condition on any column of the entity, so the row count answers whether a user's prompt, answer, title or approval arguments contain a string. Nothing is logged. Where: `System/Llm/Calls/CallList.xml:27-30`, `Conversations/ConversationList.xml:26-28`, `Runs/RunList.xml:25-28`, `Approvals/ApprovalList.xml:25-28`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/search_form.rs:125-155`, `:240-280`. Fix: add `skip-fields` for the content fields on these four finds. Fixed 2026-11-16: the four audit lists name their content fields in `skip-fields`, which a request can then neither search nor order by.
- **SEC-AI-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Three agent services check nothing in their bodies.** `rollup#LlmUsage` and `clean#LlmCallContent` are `authenticate="false"` and turn authorization off. `clean#LlmCallContent` takes `retentionDays`; with 0 it clears the stored content of every call. `record#AgentRunUsage` writes steps and cost to any run with an `externalHarnessId` and can request its cancel; it compares no user. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/services.rs:761-762`, `:802-810`, `:1285-1345`; `AgentAdminServices.xml:73-74`, `:85-89`. Fix: require `AgentAdmin` (or no user at all, as a job has) in the first two; check the run's owner or an internal marker in the third. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 092541ccd): `rollup#LlmUsage` and `clean#LlmCallContent` run only with authorization disabled (the jobs) or for an AgentAdmin, and `record#AgentRunUsage` records a run's usage only with authorization disabled or for the run's own user; anyone else is told the run is not found. Test `members_tests::the_usage_services_take_internal_work_their_admin_or_the_runs_user`.
- **SEC-AI-10 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The MCP public documents are cacheable without `Vary: Host` though their URLs come from `Host`.** The body names `{base_url}/mcp`, built from the request's `Host`. The answer is `public, max-age=3600` with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` and no `Vary`. A shared cache that keys on the path alone would serve an attacker's host as the server's endpoint, and a client configured from it would send its login key there. The A2A card sets `Vary: Host`. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/server.rs:596-615`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/mod.rs:107-115`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/card.rs:101-116`. Fix: add `Vary: Host`, or advertise only `mcp_public_url` / the webroot's configured host. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 8c03adf04): the MCP public documents (server card, `mcp.json`, `ai-catalog.json`, setup) send `Vary: Host, X-Forwarded-Host, X-Forwarded-Proto` (`mcp::server::public_reply`). Test `mcp::tests::the_documents_name_the_endpoint`.
- **SEC-AI-11 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Loopback counts as a secure transport for credentials.** The test uses the socket peer. Behind a same-host proxy every peer is loopback, so a login key sent over plain HTTP to the proxy is accepted, and the 403 "credentials over plain HTTP are refused" never fires. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/mod.rs:106-108`; `host/a2a.rs:207`; `host/mcp.rs:84`. Fix: when a client-address header is configured, use the resolved client address for the loopback test. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 49984767c, with SEC-GIT-09): one host rule, `http::secure_transport` (TLS, a believed proxy's `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, or a client on this machine, which behind a believed proxy is the address the proxy names in `client-ip-header`, never the socket peer; a request the proxy passes without naming a client is not local), decides a secure request for `/a2a`, `/mcp` and `/llm/v1`; `moqui_llm::a2a::secure` is gone. Test `http::tests::a_loopback_peer_is_secure_only_when_it_is_the_client`.
- **SEC-AI-12 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The external agent adapter inherits the server's environment.** The child is started without `env_clear`, so it sees every environment variable of the server (database password, `session_cookie_key`, API keys set by environment). The call's parameters are passed as strings on stdin with no schema, and the function checks no permission; the defining service's own `authenticate` is the only gate. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/external_agent.rs:46-51`. Fix: clear the environment and pass a named allowlist; document the permission the defining service needs. Fixed 2026-11-16: `moqui_lib::env::limit_child_env` gives the adapter only `PATH`, `HOME`, the locale and the like, plus the names `MOQUI_EXTERNAL_AGENT_ENV` lists; test `external_agent.rs` `the_adapter_does_not_see_the_servers_environment`.
- **SEC-AI-13 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Exposing a service that needs no authentication gives it to every agent user.** `user_permitted` authorizes the user only when the service needs authentication. For the others every `AgentUse` user's agent may run the tool; a role group on the agent is the only bound. `store#AgentToolExposure` says nothing about it. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/tools/catalog.rs:157-160`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call.rs:1308-1310`. Fix: refuse, or flag on the screen, an exposure of a service that is not `authenticate="true"`.
- **SEC-AI-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An A2A context made earlier still starts tasks of an agent whose `exposeA2a` was turned off.** With a `contextId` of a conversation of theirs, a caller's `SendMessage` takes the conversation's agent, and `agent_of`, the only place that checks the exposed agents, is skipped. `start_run` then checks that the agent is not disabled and serves the surface, not `exposeA2a`. An admin who withdraws an agent from A2A by its flag leaves it reachable for every caller who has a context with it. The agent's role group and the user's permissions still bound what it does; `start_run` was read by grep, not whole. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/ops.rs:639-661`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/mod.rs:525-530`. Fix: in `start_task`, check the conversation's agent against `exposed_agents` as `agent_of` does.
- **SEC-AI-85 (Low; reported (likely)). An A2A message is up to 2 MiB of text, and messages to a working task are not counted.** A message may hold 32 parts of 65,536 bytes each; the drawer takes 16,000 characters. A message to a working task is stored as an input (`add_input_with`) with no tarpit hit and no count: the `agent:<id>` tarpit counts a run's start only. `tasks` then reads every user and assistant message of a page of up to 100 tasks with no limit and cuts to `historyLength` afterwards, in memory. Not confirmed: whether a step folds every queued input into the prompt or caps them, whether `start_run` cuts the text, and the web guard's rate for one client. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/types.rs:300-349`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/ops.rs:732-795`, `:381-390`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/conf.rs:840-844`. Fix: cap a message's whole text (the drawer's limit, or one of the conf); count a message to a task against a tarpit; limit the message read of `tasks` per task.
- **SEC-AI-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An `LlmApiClient` limits nothing on the A2A and MCP servers.** `store#LlmApiClient` takes the scopes `a2a` and `mcp`, `allowedAgents` and `disabled`, the screen offers them, and the entity says a client "calls the A2A server, the MCP server or the gateway". Only the gateway reads the row (`client_of`). `log_in` of A2A and MCP reads none, so a client that is disabled, has no `a2a` scope, or lists two agents still calls every exposed agent and every MCP tool of its user with the same credential. `docs/ai/A2A.md:250` and `docs/ai/MCP.md:268` list it under "Not yet"; the screen and the entity do not. A key made for the gateway alone is refused by A2A and MCP (`login.rs:798-799`). Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/a2a/mod.rs:206-258`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/server.rs:422-455`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/gateway.rs:1610-1743`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/Llm/Gateway/ClientDetail.xml:45-46`. Fix: enforce the row in `log_in` (disabled, scope, `allowedAgents`), or take `a2a`, `mcp` and `allowedAgents` out of the service and the screen until it is.
- **SEC-AI-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). When the MCP agent's tool policy cannot be read, the server offers every tool but the destructive ones.** The MCP agent's policy is the server's allowlist. On a read error of `AgentToolPolicy` (a database error) the code logs and uses `ToolPolicy::default()`, which has no rules, and with no rule a tool is offered unless it is destructive. A `never` rule, or a cap of the agent at READ, is then gone for that request. The `exposeMcp` flag, the caller's own permissions, the agent's role group and the approval of a write still hold. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/tools.rs:104-110`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/tools/lazy.rs:61-67`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/tools/mod.rs:587-592`. Fix: answer an internal error when the policy cannot be read, as the code does when the agent cannot be read (`tools.rs:87-91`).
- **SEC-AI-88 (Low; reported (likely)). The gateway buffers 16 MiB of a request before it looks at credentials.** An anonymous `POST /llm/v1/...` while the gateway is on is read whole into memory and copied (`body.to_vec()`), and only then is the job queued that authenticates. `/llm/v1` is also exempt from the request deadline (`serve.rs:547`), though `read_body` has the body timeout. `/a2a` and `/mcp` do the same at 4 MiB, and on `/mcp` the body is parsed as JSON before the login. The web guard's limits on connections and requests of one client were not read. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/gateway.rs:43`, `:163`, `:192`; `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/a2a.rs:187`; `crates/moqui-host/src/llm/mcp.rs:67`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/server.rs:465`, `:486`. Fix: before the read, answer 404 while the protocol is off and 401 when no credential header is there; for the gateway, a small anonymous cap as SEC-RTE-07 has for uploads.
- **SEC-AI-89 (Low; reported (likely)). One user can fill the node's agent pool, so other users' runs wait.** `start_run` bounds a new run by the AT_LLM tarpit `agent:<agentId>` (30 a minute for each agent id) and the user's quota. It does not count the user's runs that are Submitted or Working, and conversations are not limited. Each run is a chain of step jobs on one pool of 8 workers and 64 queued jobs for the whole node, and a step holds its worker for a whole model call. Thirty runs of one user take turns with everyone else's steps, and past 64 queued jobs a step is refused and left to the recovery sweep after its lease lapses. Not confirmed: whether the drawer's `turn` limits a user's open runs before `start_run`, and whether a default quota is seeded. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/mod.rs:498-670`, `:738-924`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/pool.rs:36-52`, `:70-84`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/request_pool.rs:141-142`. Fix: refuse (429) a new run while the user has more than N runs Submitted or Working (a conf limit, say 4), counted in `begin_run` under the same transaction.
- **SEC-AI-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A run keeps working after its user loses `AgentUse` or its agent is disabled.** `start_run` checks the agent is not disabled and the user is not external; its callers check `AgentUse`. A step checks none of the three again: it loads the agent without the `disabled` filter and logs the user in by name, which refuses only a disabled or terminated account. So the steps left of a run (up to its `maxSteps`) still read as the user and call the model, and `resume` does not look at the agent either. The tool policy is read again each step, and a user without `AgentUse` cannot resume a paused run from the drawer. Not confirmed: that `provider::call` does not refuse an external user itself. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/step.rs:852-868`, `:1013-1017`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/mod.rs:1170-1219`. Fix: in `step`, after the login, end the run (`Rejected` or `Failed`) when the agent is disabled, the user is external, or the user lacks `AgentUse` (not for a trigger's service user, whose check is the trigger's).
- **SEC-AI-91 (Low; reported (likely)). The text of a step's internal error is shown to the user and posted in the chat room.** `run_step` takes the step's `Error` as text (a database error, a row that cannot be read, a failed login), with no redaction, into the stored `error` event, `AgentRun.errorText` and `statusReason`, and the `run.state` payload and signal. A provider's error is redacted on its own path (`run/step.rs:1745`); this one is not. For a run started in a room the chat adapter posts the first 300 characters of `errorText` as the agent's reply, which every member reads. Not confirmed: what an entity or SQL error's text holds, and what the SSE and A2A readers send of the `error` event and `reason`. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/step.rs:749-767`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/mod.rs:1510-1513`, `:1531-1540`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/chat.rs:1050-1060`. Fix: log the error with the run id, and store and send a fixed text ("the step failed; see the server log") with the code.
- **SEC-AI-92 (Low; reported (likely)). The server's check of invented links covers only a run's last text, and its marks are lost on reload.** `check_answer` runs only where a step has no tool call, so a text the model writes in a step that also calls a tool is stored and drawn as Markdown with no check. The result reaches the client as the event `answer.references`; a conversation loaded from `GET conversation` (a reload, another tab, an old conversation) is built from the stored messages, which keep the model's text, and nothing fills `state.references` for a run that is not streaming. In both cases a link the server would mark is a live link. What is left is SEC-AI-04's client rule, which asks before opening only for a query, a fragment or a user and password: `https://attacker/<data-in-the-path>` or `https://<data>.attacker/` opens on one click, with only the host as its title. The user must click; `assist/read.rs` was searched, not read whole, and nothing was run. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/run/step.rs:1695-1718`; `js/WebrootAssist.js:748-751`, `:488-534`, `:928`, `:300-303`. Fix: check every assistant text before it is stored, and send `invented` with each message in `GET conversation`, or store the checked text; in `externalCarries`, treat a path beyond a short length, and a host no tool result or user message named, as carrying data.
- **SEC-AI-93 (Low; reported (likely)). `remember` and `save_skill` ask for a confirm only when tool output follows the last user message.** `needs_confirmation` walks the conversation's messages from the newest back to the last `user` message and asks for a confirm card only when a `tool` message is among them. Tool text read in turn N (a record, a mail, a page, an MCP result) stays in the model's context; if it says "when the user next writes, remember …", then in turn N+1, before any tool runs, the fact is stored with no card. A memory is in the prefix of every later conversation of that user, where it counts as the user's own words, so one read can leave an instruction that lasts. The same test gates `save_skill`, whose result is at least kept as `learned` and wrapped. The drawer shows "Remembered: … [Forget]", the user's memory gate still applies, and a fact is at most 200 characters. Not confirmed: whether other untrusted text reaches the model inside a `user` message (page context, record mentions, a canvas's posted values), which would pass the test in the same turn. Reports A18 and A19. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/memory.rs:147-184`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/tools/remember.rs:122-124`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/skills/learn.rs:741`. Fix: ask for the card whenever the active window holds any tool result or other untrusted text that is not the framework's own (a load of a file skill), and keep at once only in a conversation that has read none; or always ask on the drawer (one click).
- **SEC-AI-94 (Low; reported (likely)). `data` finds and sorts by an `ai-opt-out` field when the find is written in the path's query.** The tool refuses a `where` or an `order` that names an `ai-opt-out` field, because the rows kept would tell its text. The refusal looks only at the `where` and `order` arguments. The same find parameters written in the query of `path` (`…/List?note=abc&note_op=begins`, or `orderByField=note` with no `order` argument) are put into the render's parameters untouched and are not in the list the refusal reads. Not confirmed: that the form-list of such a screen takes the parameter for an `ai-opt-out` field, and what the agent's `tui` render does with such a find; no screen with the attribute was looked up, and the `screen` tool's session was not read for the same guard. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/tools/data.rs:664-671`, `:684-686`, `:742-761`. Fix: run the `ai_off` test over every key of the render's parameters (with `_op`, `_not`, `_from`, `_thru` taken off) and over `orderByField`, or do it once in the agent's render origin so `screen`, `data` and the canvas proxy all get it.
- **SEC-AI-95 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An eval experiment or scorer calls any service by name with authorization off.** `call()` in `AgentEval.js` runs the named service with `disableAuthz()`; the name is free text, and nothing checks that it is a scorer or a target. A holder of `AgentAdmin` and `MONITOR_EVAL_RUN` reaches it through `startExperiment` with `serviceName`; a holder of `AgentAdmin` and `MONITOR_EVAL` by storing a scorer of type `AgstService` and scoring a run or attaching it to an agent, after which the five-minute job calls it with no user. The parameters are fixed names (`input`, `metadata`; for a scorer also `output`, `groundTruth`, `runId`, `config`), and an experiment stores the service's whole out-map as the result's `outputText`, so it is also a read. Many framework services treat "authorization disabled" as the mark of internal work and skip their own check. `advance#Experiments` and `score#LiveRuns` check no permission, so any `AgentAdmin` can press Check now. The call is confirmed in code; what it gains is to check: no service was found that gives an `ADMIN` holder something sealed with only these parameter names, and whether an `AgentScorer` row can be written by a route that skips `MONITOR_EVAL` was not traced. Where: `AgentEval.js:7`, `:44`, `:101-104`; `System/Llm/Evals/Experiments.xml:5-8`, `:53`; `Scorers.xml:4-11`. Fix: call the target and the scorer service as the experiment's or the scorer's author with authorization on (keep `disableAuthz` for the eval entities only), and take only a service that implements a scorer or target interface; give `advance#Experiments` the `MONITOR_EVAL_RUN` check when a user calls it.
- **SEC-AI-96 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A prompt saved from another user's run is later run as the administrator who starts the experiment.** Any `AgentUse` user writes the text, as a message to an agent. An `AgentAdmin` with `MONITOR_EVAL` saves that run as a dataset item, and one with `MONITOR_EVAL_RUN` starts an experiment of the dataset. Each item's text becomes the user message of a new run on the job surface as the starter, with the starter's permissions. On an agent that serves only `job`, a policy rule approves a write by itself (`is_background`), so no person sees a card; the item list shows the input cut to 300 characters. The write it leads to depends on the agent's policy: which seed agents serve only `job` and have approving policy rules was not checked. Where: `AgentEval.js:30-31`, `:72-83`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/ai_task.rs:476-499`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/approval/mod.rs:457-461`. Fix: say on Save as a dataset item whose run it is; run experiments with policy auto-approval off (every write refused or held) unless the starter opts in for the experiment.
- **SEC-AI-97 (Low; reported (likely)). The `hosting` tool gives the model a remote server's error text outside the untrusted wrapper.** A non-2xx answer of a connected group server becomes an error whose text holds the first 300 characters of the answer's `errors` or `message`. `service()` returns it as `ToolError::new("hosting", …)`, which the model reads as the tool's own words; only successful answers go through `as_data`. Whoever controls what the server answers for a failed request can write it: the server's operator, or a contributor only if the server echoes their text in an error. Not confirmed: whether mr-moqui's own REST errors ever carry a contributor's text, and how `ToolError` is rendered. Where: `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/agent/hosting.rs:177-190`; `src/hosting/client.rs:306-318`, `:325-332`. Fix: wrap the remote part of the error with `untrusted("group-server", …)` and keep only the status line outside.
- **SEC-AI-98 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An `AgentAdmin` reads any user's personal skill.** `get#AgentSkill` loads the row by id with authorization off and returns its Markdown; it does not look at `ownerUserId`. `get#SkillRetrieval` takes a `userId` and answers that user's personal skills by name, title and the `skill find` text. `learn.rs:23` calls a learned skill "the owner's alone", and a draft from a run holds the values the user's run set on screens. Writing is guarded: a personal skill is changed only by its owner. Whether `SkillList.xml` lists personal rows was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/skills/services.rs:398-447`, `:296-318`. Fix: answer a personal skill only to its owner or to `AgentAudit` with an audit row, as for content.
- **SEC-AI-99 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The MCP tarpit counts by tool name, and the name is the caller's.** The hit of a `tools/call` is `mcp:<name>` and the counter's key holds the name, so the 120 a minute are for each name. A call with an unknown name is counted as any other, so it never reaches a limit when the name changes each time; each such call still loads the agent and the policy. With n direct tools the caller has n times 120. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/mcp/tools.rs:433-436`, `:482`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/authz.rs:730`. Fix: count one name for the method (`mcp:tools/call`), or count an unknown name under one name.
- **SEC-AI-100 (Info; reported (to check)). The gateway passes URLs in a prompt to the model server, which may fetch them from where it runs.** `messages` and `input` pass through as they are, so an `image_url` or a file URL part of a caller with `LlmGatewayUse` and a client reaches the model server; `prepare` checks the field names, the tools and `include` only. A hosted provider fetches from its own network. A model server on the private network (an endpoint with `local` or `allow-private-network`) that fetches media by URL would fetch an internal address for the caller. Which model servers fetch was not checked; nothing in this repository does. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/gateway.rs:160-204`, `:512-543`. Fix: for an endpoint on a private network, refuse content parts that name a URL other than `data:`.
- **SEC-AI-101 (Info; reported (to check)). The `save_skill` confirm card shows the skill's name and description, not its text.** When `needs_confirmation` is true, the card's summary and its one change row are `name: description`; the body (the steps) is only in the approval's `args`. The terminal pane draws the summary and the change rows only. The skill is stored as `learned`, so its text is wrapped when loaded, which bounds the effect. Whether the drawer's card shows `args` for a `TargetKind::Tool` approval was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-llm/src/skills/learn.rs:745-759`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/assist/draw.rs:264-282`. Fix: add the body as a change row (cut to a few thousand characters).

## Git hosting and pipelines

- **SEC-GIT-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Secret masking misses what is not one log batch.** a secret is masked only inside the text of one `POST jobs/<id>/log` call. A value that falls across two batches is stored whole in two chunks. The `message` of `POST jobs/<id>/result` (500 characters) and artifact and report content are not masked. When the job's secrets cannot be read for masking, the error is logged and the log is stored with nothing masked. A value shorter than 4 characters is never masked. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/log.rs:98-121`, `:217`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/runner_http.rs:413-417`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/trust.rs:291`. Fix: refuse to store log text while the mask patterns cannot be read, keep a tail of the last batch to match across the boundary, and mask the result message. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 982c70d82): `ci::log::append` stores nothing while the job's secrets cannot be read (the runner gets an error and sends again); each batch is masked with `trust::mask_batch`, which also masks a start of a secret at the batch's end and an end of one at its start when the batch goes on the line the last stored chunk left open (each part at least 4 bytes); `POST jobs/<id>/result`'s message is masked, or left out when the secrets cannot be read. Test `ci::trust::tests::a_secret_cut_between_two_batches_is_masked_on_both_sides`. Not masked still: artifacts and reports (files the job made), a secret cut across three batches, a value under 4 bytes.
- **SEC-GIT-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A loopback socket peer counts as a secure transport.** credentials and CI tokens are taken when the socket peer is loopback. Behind a proxy on the same host every request's peer is loopback, so `allow-http="false"` and the `X-Forwarded-Proto` test refuse nothing, and a password, an access token, a job token or a runner token sent over plain HTTP to the proxy is accepted. The refusal is also only advice: git sends the credential after the 401 challenge, which the server gives over plain HTTP too. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/git_http.rs:293-298`. Fix: when a proxy is believed, test the client address the proxy names and its `X-Forwarded-Proto`, not the socket peer; send no `WWW-Authenticate` challenge on a request that is not secure. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 49984767c): `git_http::secure` uses the host's `http::secure_transport` (SEC-AI-11) with `allow-http` on top, for git, LFS and the runner REST, and a request that is not secure gets no login challenge: what would be a 401 is 403 "this needs a sign-in, which is taken only over HTTPS; use HTTPS" with no `WWW-Authenticate` or `LFS-Authenticate`. Test `server::tests::credentials_and_rights_are_checked`. An anonymous clone of a public repository over plain HTTP still works.
- **SEC-GIT-10 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `fork#GitRepository` uses the new `repositoryId` as a directory name without checking it.** `localPath` is refused without authorization disabled, but the fork's `repositoryId` is taken as given and joined onto `runtime/git/`. An id with `..`, or an absolute one, makes the bare repository directory outside `runtime/git`. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/service/review.rs:81-97`; `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:479`; `crates/moqui-git/src/fork.rs:181-188`. Fix: require the new id to pass `server::path::check_path` as one segment, in `fork_repository`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `fork_repository` takes the new id only as one segment that passes `server::path::check_path`; test `trust_tests::a_fork_id_is_one_safe_segment_and_only_a_branch_or_tag_is_deleted`.
- **SEC-GIT-11 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). `delete#GitRef` deletes any ref, a merge request's head included.** a `refName` that starts with `refs/` is used as given, and `write_refs` checks that a name is a branch or tag only when the ref gets a new value. A delete of `refs/merge-requests/<n>/head` (a hidden merge request's too, by its number) or of any other ref of the store passes. The merge request then cannot merge and its commits become unreachable for gc. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:631-665`; `crates/moqui-git/src/hosting.rs:406`. Fix: apply `valid_ref_name` to deletes too in `write_refs`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `write_refs` applies `valid_ref_name` to deletes too (`GitBadRefName`), so only a branch or tag is deleted.
- **SEC-GIT-12 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). A revision that is a full ref name reads any ref of the store but a hidden merge request's head.** `resolve_commit` reads any `refs/...` name and leaves out only hidden merge request heads. A mirror's fetch stages the remote's branches under `refs/moqui-incoming/` before its gate (the gitECA pre events of source `fetch`) passes them, and they stay when a fetch is cut short. During that time a viewer reads a branch the gate has not passed, or refused. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/browse.rs:140-150`, `:102-106`; `crates/moqui-git/src/fs.rs:258`, `:340-386`. Fix: resolve by name only `refs/heads/`, `refs/tags/` and readable `refs/merge-requests/`, and list only those. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): `resolve_commit` reads a full ref name only under `refs/heads/`, `refs/tags/` and `refs/merge-requests/` (still not a hidden one's head), and `refs` lists only branches and tags (`crates/moqui-git/src/browse.rs`), so a mirror's staged `refs/moqui-incoming/` is neither read nor listed. Test `tests::refs_logs_commits_and_diffs_read_any_revision`.
- **SEC-GIT-13 (Low; confirmed in code; fixed). Approvals stay when a merge request's target changes.** the author moves an approved merge request to another branch. Approvals are rows of the merge request, counted against the new target's rules; with `dismissStaleApprovals` they are compared with the head, which did not change. Reviews given for a change into an unprotected branch count for a protected one. A user with the update right must still call merge. Where: `GitReviewServices.xml:508`, `:520-535`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:1905-1917`, `:2070-2084`. Fix: set approvals back to requested when `targetRef` changes. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 8892e5e4e): `update#MergeRequest` sets each approval back to requested (its reviewed commit and date cleared) when the target branch changes, before it checks the merge request again. Test moqui-git `server::hosting::tests::merge_requests_follow_reviews_checks_and_pushes` (moved to another branch and back, the approval is asked again).
- **SEC-GIT-14 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The services' repository checks pass when `AT_GIT_REPOSITORY` authorization is off.** the git server refuses to run in that state "or every check would pass" (`server/mod.rs:476-486`), but the services have no such guard, so every read and write service allows every caller. The default is on. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/authz.rs:502-504`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/mod.rs:143-153`. Fix: make `provider_and_id` refuse as `server::provider` does. Fixed 2026-11-16: `provider_and_id` refuses a call with authorization on when `AT_GIT_REPOSITORY` authorization is off, as `server::provider` does; test `trust_tests::with_repository_authorization_off_the_services_refuse`.
- **SEC-GIT-15 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). `delete#GitRepository` leaves the repository's push mirror rows.** the list of hosting tables deleted with a repository names `GitWebhook` and not `GitRepositoryMirror`. Either the mirror rows, with their encrypted credentials, outlive the repository and attach to a later repository of the same id, or the entity's relationship to `GitRepository` makes the delete fail. The map (old lines 696, 717) says the repository's rows go with it. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/storage.rs:160-171`. Fix: add `moqui.resource.GitRepositoryMirror` to the deleted tables, and cancel the repository's queued `GitWebhook` system messages there too. Fixed 2026-11-16: `storage::delete_rows_of` deletes the `GitRepositoryMirror` rows and cancels the queued deliveries of the repository's webhooks; test `trust_tests::a_deleted_repository_takes_its_mirrors_and_queued_deliveries`.
- **SEC-GIT-16 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A webhook URL may be plain http and carry a user and password; `remoteUrl` is shown to every viewer.** a webhook to `http://public-host/` is accepted, so commit messages, author e-mail addresses and the signature go in clear. A URL with `user:password@` is stored in the unencrypted `url` field, answered by `find#GitWebhooks`, and sent as basic authorization. A mirror URL refuses both. `find#GitRepositories` answers a mirror's `remoteUrl` to every viewer; nothing refuses a `remoteUrl` with a user and token in it, and an internal host name is shown too. Where: `GitServices.xml:1123`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:1670`, `:322`. Fix: use `smart_http::check_url` for a webhook URL; answer `remoteUrl` only with the delete right, redacted, and refuse one with a user or password where it is stored. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commits 4fb0e7208, 2c9ca9934): a webhook URL is checked as a mirror's (`smart_http::check_url`: https, http only with `webhook-private-network`, no user or password) by `store#GitWebhook` (`check#GitWebhookUrl`) and by each delivery (`webhook_url_allowed`), so an existing webhook that breaks the rule fails each delivery with the reason; `find#GitRepositories` answers `remoteUrl` only to who may delete the repository, its user, password and query left out; `FileServices.create#Drive` (mirror) and `WikiServices.make#WikiSpaceStorage` (remote) refuse a `remoteUrl` with a password, or with any user for http(s). Tests `hosting_tests::webhooks_are_signed_logged_and_retried`, `files_tests::a_mirror_url_with_a_user_or_password_is_refused`, `wiki_tests::spaces_are_made_and_cloned_on_a_git_repository_with_or_without_history`. Not covered: Tools' `GitRepositoryDetail` entity form (ADMIN's) still stores a `remoteUrl` as given, and `FileServices.get#Drive` answers a mirror drive's `remoteUrl` to its members.
- **SEC-GIT-17 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The run services put their parameters into the Docker API path and remove a container by any id.** `instanceRef` is formatted into `/containers/{id}/json` and `/containers/{id}?force=true&v=true` without encoding or a check that it is a container id, so a value with `/`, `..` or `?` names another path or query of the Docker API. `remove#RunDocker` force-removes the container `instanceRef` names with its volumes, whatever its labels; only the label listing is held to this server's label. The services check no permission in their bodies. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/driver/docker.rs:732`, `:769-792`, `:273-279`. Fix: accept only hex container ids, encode path segments, and remove a container only when it carries this server's label. Fixed 2026-11-16: `inspect#RunDocker` and `remove#RunDocker` take `instanceRef` only as 1 to 64 hex digits, `remove#RunDocker` removes only a container that carries this server's `moqui.ci.server` label, and every label listing carries that label; test `docker::tests::the_run_services_speak_the_docker_api`.
- **SEC-GIT-18 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Unauthenticated requests take a git worker, and one call can walk every repository.** git, LFS and the runner REST share one pool (`max-concurrent` 4, `max-queued` 16) and a request takes a worker before its credentials are looked at, so a burst of requests with no or bad credentials makes signed-in clones and runner calls wait or be answered 503; the per-client rate limits are the only bound. `find#MergeRequests` calls `find#GitRepositories` with `pageNoLimit`, which reads every `GitRepository` row and checks view on each, with a `ResourceDrive` lookup for each one the user has no record for. Nothing caps a repository's webhooks or mirrors, and the retry job and the mirror queue each send one after another, so one repository's slow endpoints delay the others'. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/git_http.rs:251`, `:345`, `:432`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/mod.rs:262-279`; `GitReviewServices.xml:766-767`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:179-181`, `:267`. Fix: answer a request with no or malformed credentials before it is given a pool worker where the answer needs no repository; bound `find#MergeRequests` for REST callers; cap webhooks and mirrors per repository and bound each queue pass by time. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commits d1a622f26 and 958ccc492), the owner's call: the proposed limits as `git-server` settings, both a length and a gap for the queue passes, and no repository required. (a) git and LFS requests with credentials over plain HTTP (403), an `Authorization` header in no form the server takes, or an empty `api_key` (401, a failed sign-in) are answered by the host before they take a git pool worker (`moqui_git::server::early_refusal`, `git_http.rs` `early_answer`); (b) the REST `find#MergeRequests` pages at most `merge-requests-max-page` (100, was 200) and, naming no repository, looks in the first `merge-requests-max-repositories` (500) the caller may view, by id (`rest::first_visible`, the walk stopping there); (c) a repository has at most `max-webhooks` (20) webhooks and `max-mirrors` (10) push mirrors, one more refused by `store#GitWebhook` (through `get#GitWebhookLimits`) and `service::mirror::store_mirror`, those past a cap already kept; (d) a pass of `retry#GitWebhookDeliveries` and of `run#GitRepositoryMirrors` (`mirror::push_due_mirrors_until`) starts no send after `queue-pass-seconds` (300), the rest left due for the next run, and starts at least `queue-pass-gap-seconds` (300; 0 no gap) after the last pass on the node (`moqui_git::queue_pass_may_start`); a push's own mirror is pushed at once. Tests `server::access_tests::credentials_that_cannot_be_taken_are_answered_before_a_git_worker`, `service::rest::tests::a_call_that_names_no_repository_looks_in_so_many`, `server::hosting_tests::webhooks_are_capped_and_a_pass_of_the_retries_is_bounded_by_time`, `mirror::tests::mirrors_are_capped_and_a_pass_of_the_queue_is_bounded_by_time`.
- **SEC-GIT-19 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Runner tokens never expire, and a pool of registered runners may take untrusted runs.** `runner_of` accepts a runner token while its row is `active`; there is no expiry, rotation or source binding. `store#RunnerPool` accepts `poolKind` `registered` with `trusted` false, so an approved run of a fork's code runs on a machine nothing cleans between jobs and reads the runner token as in C2. The documentation says to use such a pool for trusted runs; the code does not hold it to that. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/token.rs:150-160`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/service/pools.rs:162-193`. Fix: refuse a registered pool that takes untrusted runs, and expire runner tokens. Refuted in part 2026-11-16: runner tokens expire since SEC-GIT-02 (`git-ci` `runner-token-days`, 90, rotated halfway; `token::runner_token` answers `Expired`), and a pool can name its allowed addresses. Fixed 2026-11-16 (the rest; commit 347fd4290), the owner's call: an untrusted run goes to a registered pool only when the claiming runner reports `--step-user`, and otherwise waits for such a runner. `ci-run poll` started with `--step-user` says `{"stepUser": true}` in each claim, and `registered::claim_for` gives a job of an untrusted run from a pool of registered runners to no other runner, so the run stays queued; the claim's answer carries a `notice` (`registered::needs_step_user`), which `poll` prints once. The step user is the runner's own report. Test `ci::claim_tests::an_untrusted_run_goes_only_to_a_runner_that_runs_its_steps_as_another_user`.
- **SEC-GIT-20 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Pushed trees are not checked for unsafe names, and `write_commit` has no limits.** a tree with entries named `.git`, `..`, or with `/` is stored and served to clones. `write_commit` skips `.git`, `.`, `..`, names with `/`, links and submodules, so nothing is written outside the target; it has no entry count, depth or byte limit and no duplicate-name check, where `archive.rs` has all four. `.GIT` and `\` are not skipped. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/server/pack_in.rs:269-277`; `crates/moqui-git/src/checkout.rs:196-226`. Fix: share `archive.rs` `entry_name` and its limits with `write_commit`; consider refusing unsafe tree entry names at push.
- **SEC-GIT-21 (Info; reported (to check)). `get#AuthorSigned` answers for any user id or e-mail address.** the caller's `authorUserId` or `authorEmail` goes to `agreement-check-service` with authorization disabled, and `signed`, `message` and `signUrl` come back. For a public repository any account learns whether an address signed its agreement. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/service/rest.rs:546-565`. Fix: answer for another author only to a caller with the update right.
- **SEC-GIT-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). LFS uploads and locks are taken for an archived repository, and for one that is being deleted.** a push to an archived repository is refused in `open` only for receive-pack. Every LFS request opens the repository as an upload-pack does, and `may_write` tests the token, the mirror kind and the push right, not `archived`, so an archived ("read only") repository still takes LFS objects and locks from a user with a push right. The upload writes its row with no `still_writable` call, where a push reads the row again under the lock, so an upload that runs beside `delete#GitRepository` can leave a `GitLfsObject` row for an id whose rows were just deleted, which a repository made later with that id would have. Whether the second delete of the id's rows after the commit (`storage::delete_rows_of`, not read) closes the window was not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/server/lfs.rs:406`, `:485-502`, `:701-761`, `:862-899`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/mod.rs:784-792`. Fix: in `Lfs::may_write` refuse an archived repository, and call `still_writable` before the row is created.
- **SEC-GIT-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A user with a push right creates LFS locks without limit, and each locks request reads them all.** `create_lock` has no count limit for a repository or a user, and a lock's path is any text that is not empty. Each `locks`, `locks/verify`, `unlock` and create request lists every lock row of the repository into memory. `list_locks`, which any signed-in viewer calls, takes the page size from the caller with no ceiling and makes one `UserAccount` read for each lock it answers. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/server/lfs.rs:803-815`, `:818-831`, `:776-801`, `:862-899`. Fix: a `git-server` cap on a repository's locks (as `max-webhooks`), a ceiling on `limit`, and the owners' names read once per page.
- **SEC-GIT-86 (Low; reported (likely)). An import from a caller's `source` offers the repository's stored token to that host.** `import` passes the repository's own `definition.credentials` (`GitRepository.username`, `accessToken`) to `fs::fetch` also when the URL is the caller's `source`, not the repository's `remoteUrl`. The credential callback answers any request for a credential without looking at the URL it is asked for, so the host of `source` receives the token the administrator stored for `remoteUrl` once it answers 401. Today the callers that reach it with a repository that has a token are ADMIN's (the mr-boss `RepositoryDetail/Settings.xml` import transition, the System `GitRepositoryDetail` screen); `ironstone.projects.CodeServices.create#Repository` (a project manager) imports into a repository it has just made, which has no token. Not run; whether any non-administrator path passes `source` for a repository that has an `accessToken` was not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:1579-1587`; `crates/moqui-git/src/fs.rs:438-456`. Fix: in `import`, give `fs::Credentials::default()` when the source is the caller's; in the callback, answer only when the asking URL's scheme, host and port are the remote's.
- **SEC-GIT-87 (Low; reported (to check)). gitoxide's text of a failed fetch is stored and shown as it is, and can name the URL with its password.** `fs::fetch` redacts the URL it prints itself and appends gitoxide's error with `{err}`; the text becomes `GitRepository.lastFetchError` and the service's error. Two gitoxide messages print the transport's URL ("Credentials provided for ... were not accepted by the remote", "Failed to configure the transport before connecting to ..."), which for HTTP is `url.to_bstring()`, the form that writes the password. It needs a `remote` repository whose `remoteUrl` was stored with a user and password (the ADMIN Tools form stores one as given). Whether either message is the frame `{err}` prints was not confirmed (`gix-error` prints the top frame without `{:#}`), and the first needs a URL password and an `accessToken` that the server both refuses. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/fs.rs:427-433`, `:461`; `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:845`, `:1250`. Fix: pass the whole message through a redactor that removes `://user:password@` and the token before it is stored or returned (as `smart_http::redact` does for a mirror's `lastError`); refuse a `remoteUrl` with a password in `fetch` itself.
- **SEC-GIT-88 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A scheduled run is trusted whoever pushed the branch it runs on.** the schedule row keeps the first plain branch the default branch's schedule pipeline names. When it is due the engine reads the pipeline file at that branch's head and queues it with no user, and `is_trusted` answers true for every schedule run. An account with the create right and no update right, whose own push event is untrusted, pushes that branch when it is not there (or is one such an account may make): the run does not wait for approval, runs on a trusted pool, writes caches other trusted runs read, and gets an environment's secrets when the environment's `allowedRefs` names the branch. `plan::applies` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/trust.rs:59-60`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/queue.rs:644-651`, `:737-744`. Fix: trust a schedule run only when its ref is the default branch or `is_protected`; otherwise decide trust by the pusher of the head (`GitCommitPush`) as a push is decided.
- **SEC-GIT-89 (Low; reported (likely)). The pipeline file is read whole before its size is checked, on each event and for any viewer.** `read_at` asks for the blob with no limit (`file_at` with `u64::MAX`) and copies it to a string; `max-file-bytes` (256 KiB) is tested on the string afterwards. An account that may push (or push for review, within `review-max-object-size`) plants a large `ci.xml` or `component.xml`, up to `max-object-size` (512 MiB) for a pushed blob; each push and merge request event, and any viewer through `GET /rest/s1/moqui/git/{repositoryId}/pipelines/definitions` (`get#PipelineDefinitions`), then holds the bytes twice. `file_at` was not read past its signature; whether it reads an LFS pointer's content here, which would raise the size to `lfs-max-object-size`, was not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/queue.rs:127-133`, `:139-140`; `crates/moqui-git/src/browse.rs:319`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/file.rs:696`. Fix: call `file_at` with `limits.max_bytes` and treat `TooLarge` as the "over this server's limit" error.
- **SEC-GIT-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A job's uploads have no storage cap by default, and the count of a job's uploads is not held.** `max-repository-upload-bytes` and `max-owner-upload-bytes` are 0 in the default conf, and with a cap of 0 the loop skips it, so the code of any run, an approved untrusted one included, stores up to `max-artifacts` (100) uploads of `max-artifact-bytes` (1 GiB) each with its job token on `PUT /ci/runner/jobs/<id>/artifacts`. The count is read before the body and not again after, so uploads sent at once each pass it; the byte caps are counted again after the row is written, the count is not.
