Whether mr-mantle's conf sets the caps was not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/artifact.rs:207-216`, `:219-247`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:1456`. Fix: give `max-repository-upload-bytes` a default, count the job's rows again after the insert, and hold an untrusted run to a smaller total.
- **SEC-GIT-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An environment's `allowedRefs` and `approvalRequired` change without its secrets being given again.** `store#CiSecret` refuses to move a secret to another scope or environment without a new value, so that who edits does not read a value another maintainer stored. The environment a secret is scoped to can be edited to the same effect by a holder of the delete right (`store#CiEnvironment`): set `allowedRefs` to a branch the editor pushes and `approvalRequired` to false, push a `ci.xml` whose job names the environment and the secret, and the next run is given the value. The `protected` scope has the same limit by another road (a maintainer changes the branch rule). It is a limit of the design, recorded so the "new value" rule is not read as more than it is. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/service/secrets.rs:184-190`, `:358-369`, `:402-404`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/trust.rs:114-125`. Fix: when `allowedRefs` widens or `approvalRequired` goes to false, clear `secretValue` of the environment's secrets (they show as needing a value), or record who widened it and require a second maintainer.
- **SEC-GIT-92 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Quarantine and scratch directories a killed process left are never removed.** a refused push removes its quarantine directory, and gc's scratch directory (`objects/gc-<pid>-<millis>`) is removed on drop. Nothing removes one a dead process left: gc lists only `objects/pack` and the two-hex directories, a search of `crates/moqui-git/src` for `incoming` finds only the three sites that make the name, and the only start-up sweep is the archives' (`archive.rs:334` `sweep_at_start`). An account that may push, by pushes cut short at the right moment (a server restart, an out-of-memory kill during a large push), leaves up to `max-push-size` (2 GiB) on disk each time. `server/mod.rs` around lines 342 and 1071 was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/server/mod.rs:342`, `:1071`, `:1103`; `crates/moqui-git/src/lib.rs:1569`; `crates/moqui-git/src/gc/local.rs:116-121`, `:245`. Fix: at start and in `gc#GitRepositories`, remove `objects/incoming-<pid>-*`, `objects/gc-<pid>-*`, `runtime/tmp/git-*-incoming-<pid>-*` and `git-import-*` whose pid is not a live process of this server and whose age is over an hour.
- **SEC-GIT-93 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A pool's `egressRule` refuses only `host` and `container:`, and an image's `VOLUME` is outside the disk limit.** the rule is a deny list of two. On Podman a network mode `ns:<path>` joins the network namespace at that path, the host's included (`ns:/proc/1/ns/net`), and passes the check; so do `slirp4netns:...` and `pasta:...` with their options. The rule is a `GIT_CI_ADMIN` holder's to store. The container body sets `Binds: []` and tmpfs mounts and nothing about the image's declared volumes, so the engine makes an anonymous volume at each `VOLUME` path of an image the pool allows: writable, on the host's disk, not sized by `StorageOpt` nor by a tmpfs, and there with `ReadonlyRootfs`. Confirmed in code for the rule and likely for the volume (the engine's behaviour); neither was tried against an engine. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/driver/docker.rs:560-563`, `:613-627`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/service/pools.rs:212`. Fix: accept `bridge`, `none` and a network name of `valid_name` letters only (no colon); inspect the image and refuse one with `Config.Volumes`, or mount a sized tmpfs over each.
- **SEC-GIT-94 (Low; reported (to check)). `process#GitUpdate` runs the rest of the caller's request as the pusher if the way back fails.** the service signs the context in as the update's user (`internal_login_user`), runs the post-receive rules, and signs the caller back in. Between the two there is no guard, so a panic in a rule unwinds past the restore; when the pusher's account does not pass the gate the rules run as the caller instead, with only a warning. `login.rs:576-579` rotates a bound web session's id when the user changes, twice here. It is reached by a holder of the delete right on a repository through mr-boss "Run again", by ADMIN through the System screen, and by the pending job. Whether a panic in a rule is caught below `run_post_events`, and whether the session row is written before the request ends, were not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/events.rs:638-666`. Fix: use `moqui_core::user::as_user` (as `trust::has_update` does) or a guard that restores on drop.
- **SEC-GIT-95 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The CI runner unpacks a cache with no limit on expanded size or entries.** `unpack` inflates the `tar.gz` into the workspace entry by entry, as the runner's user, with `set_overwrite(true)`. Nothing bounds the bytes or the count, so a small archive can fill the runner's disk. Paths are held inside the workspace by `unpack_in`. The archive is what the server serves for the cache key: a trusted run's for a protected ref, and an untrusted run reads only what no protected run wrote (`ci/artifact.rs:18-19`). Report: round 3 `A4.md`. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/runner/archive.rs:59-76`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/runner/mod.rs:634`. Fix: count bytes (`entry.size()`) and entries and stop past the limits the script host's `extractArchive` already has; skip entries that are not regular files, directories or links inside the workspace.
- **SEC-GIT-96 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The `value` of a CI secret is not a secret name to the request log's rule.** `secret_parameter` knows `password`, `secret`, `token`, `credential`, `code` and names ending in `key`. `value`, the field of Smithy's secret form (`store#CiSecret`), is none of them, so `ec.web.getRequestDetails()` lists it. Its one user is the System home's "Request details" dialog of the viewer's own request (`System/Home.xml:106`), so nothing leaks today. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:4-26`, `:32-49`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:549`. Fix: name the parameter `secretValue` in the service and the form.
- **SEC-GIT-97 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A commit made by `commit#GitFiles` keeps a `Moqui-User-Id` trailer the caller wrote.** the trailer is added only when the message has none, so a message that ends with `Moqui-User-Id: <another user>` is stored as written and `get#GitTree` answers that id as `moquiUserId`. A pushed commit can say the same; the author and the push log are the caller's. Where: `crates/moqui-git/src/merge.rs:420-428`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:502-503`. Fix: for a commit a service makes, replace a `Moqui-User-Id` trailer in the message with the caller's.

## Mail

- **SEC-MAIL-05 (Low; confirmed in code; fixed). Out-of-office replies and rule forwards have no total limit and trust the From header.** The reply goes to the message's `From` address once per address in four days, with no count per account and no look at the stored authentication verdict, so a run of messages with different forged senders makes the account send one reply to each (backscatter), each with the attacker's subject after "Automatic reply: " and a new `EmailOutOfOfficeSent` row. A `forwardTo` rule forwards every match, automatic mail and bounces included, with copies of its files, and is skipped only when `From` is the account's own address; two accounts that forward to each other have no loop stop here, and the listener counts no `Received` fields. Where: `EmailClientServices.xml` `send#OutOfOfficeReplies` (5197-5412), `run#EmailRules` `forward` (4532-4571). Fix: Cap automatic replies and forwards per account per hour, skip a reply when the trusted `Authentication-Results` says DMARC failed, do not forward mail with `Auto-Submitted` other than `no`, and refuse a message with more than a fixed number of `Received` fields. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 8892e5e4e): `send#OutOfOfficeReplies` sends at most 30 replies an hour from an account (`record#Attempt`) and none to a sender the kept `Authentication-Results` say failed DMARC; a rule's forward is skipped for automatic mail (`Auto-Submitted` other than `no`), for mail with more than 25 `Received` fields, and past 50 forwards an hour from the account, and carries `Auto-Submitted: auto-generated`, so another account's rule does not send it back. Tests moqui-mail `client_tests` (a DMARC failure, the hourly cap) and `delivery_tests` (the forward's mark, automatic mail not forwarded).
- **SEC-MAIL-06 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The "delivered" notice is backscatter with the sender's headers.** After the store the server mails a report to the envelope sender through an account's outgoing server. The report holds the message's whole header block, which the peer wrote and which may be megabytes, so in internet mode with `dsn` on anyone can make the server send chosen text to a chosen address, once per message, limited only by the per-address connection limits. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/delivery.rs:601-725`. Fix: Send the notice only when SPF or DMARC passed for the envelope sender, and cut the returned header block to a few kilobytes. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 6710609a2): a delivered notice returns only the whole header fields that fit in 4 KiB (`delivery::delivered_report`, `MAX_RETURNED_HEAD`); test `delivery_tests::text::a_delivered_report_names_each_recipient_and_returns_the_header`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 451c9d781), the owner's call: a delivered notice only when the sender passes SPF or DMARC alignment. `delivery::send_delivered_notice` sends one only when `notice_authenticated` holds: `smtp_authserv_id` is set (so the kept `Authentication-Results` are only this server's or its gateway's) and they show `spf=pass`, or `dmarc=pass` whose `header.from` is aligned with the envelope sender's domain (`relaxed_aligned`: the same domain or one a subdomain of the other, without DNS, so sibling subdomains count as not aligned); a comment in a result is not read as a property. A sender with no SPF or with forwarded mail gets no notice, nor does any sender on a node with no authserv-id (logged at info). Tests `delivery_tests::a_delivered_report_goes_to_the_sender_for_notify_success`, `delivery_tests::text::a_delivered_notice_needs_an_authenticated_sender`.
- **SEC-MAIL-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Internet mode has no cap on messages a session, refused recipients, or stored bytes.** A connection may send any number of 25 MiB messages for 30 minutes and an address may open 30 connections a minute, each stored in the database and under `dbresource://email/` with no quota per account or in total. An unknown recipient is answered `550` and not counted as an error, so one connection can test local addresses without end (`RSET` clears the 100-recipient count). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp.rs` session loop (1169-1206), `rcpt` (1354-1450); `crates/moqui-mail/src/delivery.rs` `deliver_one`. Fix: Count refused recipients toward the error limit in internet mode, cap messages and bytes per connection and per address per hour, and add a per-account storage quota that answers `452`. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 02250ac58): in internet mode an unknown recipient's 550 counts as an error, so 20 of them close the connection (421; `RSET` does not clear the count), and a connection brings at most 100 messages and 256 MiB, after which the next `MAIL` is 421 "connect again" (`MAX_INTERNET_MESSAGES`, `MAX_INTERNET_BYTES`); behind a gateway nothing changes. Test `smtp::tests::internet_mode_counts_unknown_recipients_and_messages_a_connection`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commits bd52bf5de and d8e340b0e), the owner's call: 1000 messages and 1 GiB an hour per sender, and 10 GiB stored per mail account (an `EmailAccount` field with a conf default), 452 over it. In internet mode the listener keeps the last hour of stored mail, by the minute, per client address (IPv6 by its /64) and per envelope sender (lower case; a bounce only by its address), counting messages, not recipients: a `MAIL` that with one more message and its `SIZE` would pass `smtp-server` `internet-messages-per-hour` (1000) or `internet-bytes-per-hour` (1 GiB) on either key is `451 4.7.1` (`hourly_refusal`, `charge_hourly`); behind a gateway nothing changes. An account at or over its `EmailAccount.quotaBytes`, else `email-client` `mailbox-quota` (10 GiB), of the sum of its messages' `messageSize` (view `EmailAccountBytes`, `delivery::over_quota`) is answered `452 4.2.2 Mailbox full` at `RCPT TO` (not an error count), for hosted, shared and the server's own accounts, and `deliver_one` holds its message with a temporary error (an edge-queued item is retried); a warning names the account the first time it is found over. Tests `smtp::tests::internet_mode_limits_the_mail_of_an_hour_by_address_and_by_sender`, `smtp::tests::an_hourly_window_forgets_mail_older_than_an_hour`, `delivery_tests::a_full_account_takes_no_more_mail_until_it_has_room`, `smtp::tests::a_recipient_over_its_quota_is_answered_452_and_not_counted_an_error`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (what was outside the call; commit 6c112f7e4), the owner's call: the sum stays, with no kept counter (later work if it is slow). Each quota check keeps `EmailAccount.quotaFullDate` (`delivery::over_quota_of`, `mark_full` in a transaction of its own), set when the account is found over and cleared when found under; it is in the replicated `mailRecipients`, so the edge node's listener answers `452 4.2.2` for a full account (`smtp_queue` `Lookup::Found { full }`, `RecipientMap::is_full`), on an all-in-one node for the paired side too. The account's owner and members get an `EmailMailboxFull` notice when it fills (`notify_full`). The job `check_FullMailboxes_frequent` (`check#FullMailboxes`, every 15 minutes) checks the marked accounts again, since a full account gets no mail that would. `update#EmailAccount` takes `quotaBytes` from a mail administrator only (`EMAIL_ACCOUNT_HOSTED`), which clears the mark; the account page (BasicComms Settings, Mail accounts) shows the used and allowed bytes and "Mailbox full", and a quota form to such an administrator. Tests `delivery_tests::a_full_account_takes_no_more_mail_until_it_has_room` (the mark, one notice, the job), `smtp_queue::tests::an_account_its_local_node_marked_full_is_answered_452`, basic-comms `HostedMailAddress.spec.js` "only a mail administrator sets a hosted account's quota".
- **SEC-MAIL-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A message is dropped when its Message-ID is already in the account.** The second message with the same `Message-ID` is answered `250` and not stored, whatever its content or sender. The peer's own message with that id takes the place of the real one. The same holds for a Sent row whose `receivedDate` C3 set. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/delivery.rs` `deliver_one` (753-772). Fix: Treat a message as a duplicate only when its envelope sender and a hash of its content also match, or only within the retry window of the same session id. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 6710609a2): a message is taken as one already stored only when a received message of the account with its Message-ID also has the same envelope sender (its `Return-Path`) and the same subject, From address, body and text (`delivery::same_message`); another message with a known id is stored beside the first. Test `delivery_tests::a_message_is_stored_once_per_account_with_files_rules_and_ecas`.
- **SEC-MAIL-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The mail capture can be on in production.** With it on, every send is recorded as sent, nothing is delivered, and the last 500 messages (password resets, invitations, with their links) stay in memory for any script that asks the tool. The tool factory is in the default conf and nothing checks `instance_purpose`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/mail_capture.rs` (`decide`, `enabled`); `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:617-619`; `crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:1582-1616`. Fix: Refuse to enable the capture when `instance_purpose` is production, and log a warning at startup and on each `enable()`. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-DEV-05: `MailCapture` captures only in a `moqui test` run, and the `mail_capture_sends` property is ignored with a warning elsewhere.
- **SEC-MAIL-65 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Reset passwords and verification tokens are kept in clear in EmailMessage.body.** The reset password is hashed in `UserAccount` and then mailed as text; the send stores the rendered body in an `EmailMessage` row because the call leaves `createEmailMessage` at its default of true. The same holds for the registration `verificationToken` (one hour) and the single-use sign-in code. The stored copy stays after the secret is used or expires, and for 48 hours a stored reset password signs in. Where: runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/UserServices.xml:441-459; runtime/components/base/framework/screen/PasswordReset.xml:18; runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:173-190; crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:710-718 and `update_ready`. Fix: Pass `createEmailMessage: false` for mail that carries a secret (as Mantle's invitations do), or store such a message with its body left out. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 98b9d160f): `reset#Password`, `send#AuthcCode`, the sign-in code send and the registration verification mail (`public/Account.js`) call `send#EmailTemplate` with `createEmailMessage: false`, so no `EmailMessage` row keeps the secret (such a mail is not retried by the queue and does not show in the mail screens). Tests `senders_tests::password_reset_mails_a_reset_password_that_changes_the_password`, `rest_login_with_an_email_code_sends_it_and_verify_otp_takes_it_once`. Rows stored before stay.
- **SEC-MAIL-66 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Edge MX delivery connects to whatever address a recipient domain's DNS names, and falls back to clear text.** The MX host's addresses are used as DNS answers them; no test for a loopback, private or link-local address was found in `mx.rs`, and the MTA-STS policy fetch has none either, so a domain with an MX of `127.0.0.1` or an internal address makes the node open an SMTP session to port 25 there, deliver the message and return that host's replies in the status report. With no `enforce` policy a failed STARTTLS is followed by a reconnect and a send without TLS, and any certificate is accepted, so stripping STARTTLS or breaking the handshake gets the message in clear (the usual opportunistic model, no DANE). Where: crates/moqui-mail/src/mx.rs:977-1010 (`hosts`), 1053-1075, 1138-1160 (`session`), 1313-1326 (`sts::fetch`). Fix: Refuse MX and policy-host addresses that are not public unless `mail_mx_override` names them, and offer a setting (or DANE and TLS reporting) for domains that must not be downgraded. Fixed 2026-11-16, with SEC-OUT-04: internal MX addresses are dropped and the MTA-STS fetch goes to public addresses with no proxy. The downgrade half is fixed by the new property `mail_mx_require_tls` (commit 3a4acebf2): recipient domains, comma-separated, `*` for all, whose MX gets mail only over STARTTLS with a certificate valid for the host (as an MTA-STS `enforce` policy, whatever the domain publishes); no STARTTLS, a failed handshake or an invalid certificate leaves the message to wait and bounce after `mail_delivery_days`, never a clear-text send; it applies with `mail_mx_override` too. The default stays opportunistic (an unlisted domain without an `enforce` policy as before); DANE and TLS-RPT are later work. Test `mx::tests` `a_domain_that_requires_tls_gets_no_clear_text_and_another_still_does`.
- **SEC-MAIL-67 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). A mail user's hosted domain is one the node signs for and then verifies as its own.** With no domain list the node signs a message for its From domain when that domain has a hosted or shared account, and any mail user can make one at any domain. On arrival at the listener, `verify_internal` checks signatures of the node's selector against the node's own key with no DNS query and writes a passing `Authentication-Results` under the node's authserv-id. A user may so get mail with a From of a domain they do not own marked `dkim=pass` for local readers, and signed with the node's key for outside ones (which fails there, as the outside domain has no such key record). Where: crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:405-418 (`signing_domain`), 493-512 (`sign_for`), 763-790 (`verify_internal`); crates/moqui-mail/src/delivery.rs:563. Fix: Sign and internally verify only for domains in `mail_dkim_domains` or domains an administrator approved; fix C1. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-MAIL-01 (commit 15ed46936): only a mail administrator (`EMAIL_ACCOUNT_HOSTED`) or a call with authorization off makes a hosted or shared account or gives one its address, so with no `mail_dkim_domains` the domains the node signs for and verifies as its own are the ones an administrator gave an account. A deployment that wants a fixed list names it in `mail_dkim_domains`. Checked against the code 2026-11-17.
- **SEC-MAIL-10 (Info; reported (to check)). Mail forged as from the reader loads remote images without asking.** Remote images load at once when the `From` address is a trusted sender's or the reader's own (`message.from.isMe`). `From` is the sender's to write, so such a message reports the open and the reader's address to the image host. The stored authentication verdict is not seen in that test. Where: `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Inbox/Mail.qvue:1880`. Fix: Allow automatic remote images only when the message's trusted `Authentication-Results` shows a DMARC pass for the `From` domain.
- **SEC-MAIL-68 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). An identity's address is any text with an @.** A draft's From is its identity's address, and an identity is made with any address; nothing ties it to the account's address or domain. What stops a forged From is only the account's own SMTP server, which the user also chose. The same address is matched second for inbound delivery to a hosted or shared account (the first report's C1). Where: runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/mail/EmailClientServices.xml:5002-5034 (`create#EmailIdentity`), 3357-3359 (`save#Draft`). Fix: For a hosted or shared account, let only a mail administrator add an identity or limit it to the account's domain.
- **SEC-MAIL-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Mail the edge node answered 250 for is failed, oldest first, when a pairing's queue passes its size cap.** The listener queues each message for its local node and answers `250` once the item commits; `enqueue` tests the size of one item only (1 MiB payload, 64 MiB content), never the pairing's waiting total. The retention pass (every 40th maintenance round of 15 seconds) sums each pairing's pending bytes and, over `queue-max-megabytes` (1024), fails the oldest pending items of that pairing, of every kind, until it is under. The listener's hourly limit is 1 GiB for each client address and each sender, so while the local node is not linked (or drains slower than mail arrives) one address reaches the cap in an hour, and what fails is the mail accepted first: the real mail of the outage, and older `mailStatus` or chat items to the same node. The kind `mail` has no failed-service, so nothing tells the sender or the recipient; an error is logged. Not confirmed: whether the hourly limits hold on the edge backend as on the store backend (taken from the map); how fast a linked local node drains the queue. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/queue.rs:273-397` (`enqueue`), 1234-1266 (`fail_over_cap`), 1528-1549 (`clean`); `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp_queue.rs:418-439`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:1505-1510`. Fix: Refuse new mail instead of failing old: in `QueueBackend::recipient` (and the paired side of `SplitBackend`) answer `452 4.3.1` when the pairing's pending bytes (`EdgeQueueCount`, read at most once a few seconds) are over a share of the cap, and have `deliver_queued` answer `451` when the item would pass it. Let the cap fail kinds other than `mail` first, or fail the newest `mail` items, and add a failed-service for `mail` that logs the sender and recipients.
- **SEC-MAIL-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). On an edge node a second message with a known Message-ID and the same recipients is answered 250 and dropped.** This is SEC-MAIL-08 again: its fix (`delivery::same_message`) does not cover the edge node's queue. The item's idempotency key is a hash of the `Message-ID` field and the sorted recipient addresses (`edge_mail.rs:303`); `enqueue` answers an item whose id exists as queued before, without reading the content, and the listener answers each recipient stored. The row stays for `queue-retention-days` (14) after delivery, so for 14 days a message with a `Message-ID` already queued for the same recipients of that node is dropped, whoever sent it and whatever it says; a sender who knows an id before the real message arrives (a list post, a message deferred by its own `451`) takes its place. Reached by an SMTP client of an edge node's listener, or of an all-in-one node for a paired node's accounts. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:284-308` (`inbound_item_trusting`); `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/queue.rs:308-329`; `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp_queue.rs:423-431`. Fix: Put the envelope sender and a SHA-256 of the message in the key (as the branch for a message with no `Message-ID` already hashes it), so only the same message from the same sender is one item; the local node's `same_message` then decides as on a normal node.
- **SEC-MAIL-88 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A trusted ARC sealer overrides DMARC whatever that sealer recorded about the message.** Off unless `mail_arc_trusted_sealers` is set. When DMARC fails under `reject` or `quarantine` and the ARC chain verifies, the action becomes `Accept` if the `d=` of the last seal is listed. The sealer's `ARC-Authentication-Results` is not read, so a message that already failed DMARC when the sealer took it (which the sealer records as `dmarc=fail` and still seals) is accepted here. Listing a large provider so that its lists work lets any customer of that provider send mail with a `From` of a `p=reject` domain to the Inbox. Not confirmed: which link `arc.chain().last()` is (taken as the newest, as the doc comment at 562-564 says). Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:666-677`. Fix: Override only when the trusted sealer's own `ARC-Authentication-Results` (the instance of that seal) shows `dmarc=pass` for the message's `From` domain, and say so in the property's description. Write `(overridden)` into the listener's `dmarc=` result as a comment so readers of the stored field see it.
- **SEC-MAIL-89 (Low; reported (likely)). On an all-in-one node the sender rule and the mailbox read different From fields.** `refused_sender` stops a paired node sending as the server's own users, and `internal` decides the signature, both from `from_address`: the first `From` field, and in it the text after the last `<`. The mailbox stores the first mailbox of the last `From` field as `mail-parser` reads it. A `mailOut` message of a paired node with `From: a@paired.example` and then `From: ceo@server.example`, or with `From: ceo@server.example (<a@paired.example>)`, passes the rule, is signed for `paired.example`, and is delivered to the server's own accounts by `deliver_own` with a trusted `Authentication-Results` of `dkim=pass` on top (`verify_internal`), shown as from `ceo@server.example`. Not run; not confirmed: that the DKIM signature verifies with two `From` fields (`From` is named twice in `SIGNED_FIELDS`, `auth.rs:51-53`). Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:385-403` (`from_address`), 376-381; `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:618-634`, 810-835 (`refused_sender`), 908-960 (`deliver_own`); `crates/moqui-mail/src/store.rs:128-130`. Fix: Read the sender for these rules with the same parser the store uses (`mail-parser`'s `from()`), refuse a `mailOut` item with more than one `From` field or mailbox, and write a `dmarc`-like alignment into the internal result only when the signature's `d=` is the domain of the stored `fromAddress`.
- **SEC-MAIL-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). With `mail_dkim_domains` set, an edge node signs one pairing's mail for another pairing's domain.** Needs the property set on a node with more than one pairing. The comment at `edge_mail.rs:619` says a paired node never sends signed as the server or another node, and the closure given to `sign_for` tests the paired node's domains, but `signing_domain` calls the closure only when `mail_dkim_domains` is empty; with a list it signs for any listed domain. On a dedicated edge node `internal` is always true and `refused_sender` is not called, so node A's message `From: x@domain-of-B` (an identity with the other domain's address, SEC-MAIL-68) is signed with the edge node's key for B's domain and delivered by MX. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:408-417`; `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:618-634`. Fix: For a paired node's mail sign only when the domain is the paired node's and, when a list is set, also in the list (`paired.contains(domain) && (configured.is_empty() || configured.contains(domain))`); call `refused_sender`'s domain rule on a dedicated edge node too, against the other pairings' domains.
- **SEC-MAIL-91 (Low; reported (likely)). Anyone who has seen a relayed message's Return-Path can report it bounced.** The edge relay's VERP address is `bounce+<item id>@<domain>` with nothing but the `mailOut` item id (a UUID), which is in every copy of the message; Mantle's own bounce address carries a random key per delivery. Any mail to it that has the text `message/delivery-status` and an `Action: failed` field becomes a `bounced` status. The local node then puts a client message that is still `ES_SENT` back in the Outbox with `sendError` set to the report's text (up to 4,000 characters the sender chose), or marks a template's message `ES_BOUNCED`, which the map says Mantle reads as a hard bounce that suppresses the delivery's address for every organization. One recipient of a message to several so reports it for all; the report need not come from the MX the message went to, nor follow a delivery that did not end `accepted`. Not confirmed: what Mantle does with `ES_BOUNCED` set this way (not read); a client message is not sent again by a job (`send#DueDrafts` sends only `ES_SCHEDULED`), the user resends it. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:92-106`, 121-180, 1035-1072, 1111-1142; `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp_queue.rs:177-194`, 394-417. Fix: Add a keyed tag to the address (`bounce+<id>.<HMAC of the id, 80 bits>@`, as `web::email_view_key` does for the pixel) and refuse an address without it; count a late report only for a recipient the delivery row does not show as sent with a 2xx more than a few days ago, and never turn it into a suppression for a recipient other than the one named in `Final-Recipient`.
- **SEC-MAIL-92 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A DMARC check that ends in a DNS error accepts the message.** Only a timeout of the whole check or a panic is a temporary error (451). A DMARC result of `TempError` or `PermError` is not `Fail`, so the action is `Accept` and the message is delivered with `dmarc=temperror`; with no resolver at all the message is accepted with `none (no resolver)`. A sender does not control the lookup of someone else's `_dmarc` record, so this is a gap of robustness. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:599-608`, 660-665. Fix: Set `temp_error` (451) when `dmarc.result()` is `TempError` and when there is no resolver.
- **SEC-MAIL-93 (Low; reported (likely)). A slow mail server holds the one sync job, and a sync stores without any size or quota limit.** The job `sync_EmailAccounts_frequent` syncs the due accounts one after another on its own thread. A session has no deadline: each socket read may take up to 60 seconds with no limit on how many there are, and the session renews its own 15 minute account lease as it goes, so a server a mail user names keeps one account's session, and the job's pass over every other account, for as long as it likes; mail of other accounts then arrives only through the 8 IDLE workers. Separately, every message a sync fetches is stored with its files: the mailbox quota the SMTP listener applies is not read by the sync, and `sync-batch` (500 a folder a session, then again a minute later) is the only count limit, so a user's own server fills the database and the `dbresource://email/` store. Not confirmed: what the scheduler does with a run that outlives its job lease (`job_lease.rs` was read in its header comment only). Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/sync.rs:3292-3324` (`sync_due_accounts`), 1045-1119 (`run_sync`), 2793-2844 (`store_new`), 3122-3192 (`pop_sync`); `crates/moqui-mail/src/inbound.rs:1070-1071`. Fix: A deadline per session (for example the lease time), accounts synced on the service pool with a bound instead of in the job's loop, and the mailbox quota (or a separate synced-mail limit per account) checked before a store.
- **SEC-MAIL-94 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A sync shows the remote server's own text, which the test was changed to hide.** SEC-MAIL-02's fix made `test#EmailServer` answer a fixed kind of failure; the sync of the same server row does not. `sync#EmailAccount` (level 1 on the account, the Inbox's "Sync now") answers as a service error the first line a non-TLS port sent (up to 16 KiB), the tagged answer of a command, the operating system's connect error with host and port, and the IMAP or POP3 server's refusal, and stores it in `EmailAccount.lastSyncError` (4,000 characters), which `get#EmailAccountAccess` answers to every member. On a production instance the address guard keeps this to public hosts. On an instance that is not production an account-owned server has `allowPrivateNetwork` empty, which `deny_private_network` allows (`outbound.rs:108`), so the banner and port oracle of SEC-MAIL-02 remains there through the sync. Not confirmed: how the Inbox screen shows the message; `is_production` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/sync.rs:486-492`, 444-457, 905-906, 1155-1176; `crates/moqui-mail/src/inbound.rs:1067`; `EmailClientServices.xml:133`. Fix: Pass a sync's failure through the same kind mapping the test uses (`implementation::test_failure`) before it is answered or stored, and log the text.
- **SEC-MAIL-95 (Info; reported (to check)). Mail queued back to the local node keeps any Authentication-Results that claims the edge node's name.** `deliver_own` passes the message through `rewrite_head`, which drops fields claiming the authserv-id. `queue_back` only puts the internal field on top (`edge_mail.rs:986` `raw.extend_from_slice(signed);`) and names the edge node's host name as the authserv-id the local node trusts (1003-1006), so a field `Authentication-Results: <edge host>; dmarc=pass ...` already in the message is trusted by the local node's delivery (`delivery.rs:710-714`). Reached by a sender on the paired node that can put a header field in an outgoing message (`extraHeaders` of `send#Email`, which excludes only the letter's own fields; a mail client message cannot, `CLIENT_HEADERS`). Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:967-1010` (`queue_back`), against 908-931 (`deliver_own`). Fix: Build `queue_back`'s message with `rewrite_head(signed, &hostname, &[], &fields)` as `deliver_own` does.
- **SEC-MAIL-96 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Every RCPT and every message of an edge node's listener waits on one lock held across database work.** `recipient` and `deliver` take the `Copies` mutex, then read the paired nodes and the set's applied count (two queries for each `RCPT`), and `deliver` keeps it while `deliver_queued` writes the item and up to 25 MiB of content. One slow write holds every other session's `RCPT` (20 connections, anonymous clients); after a replication batch one `RCPT` rebuilds the map from up to 1,000,000 copies under the lock. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp_queue.rs:490-496`, 508-519, 108-131. Fix: Refresh under the lock, clone an `Arc` of the built maps, and release the lock before the look-up and the enqueue; read `active_nodes` and `applied_count` at most once a second.
- **SEC-MAIL-97 (Info; reported (to check)). The OAuth callback makes an account with authorization disabled, so a shared account needs no mail administrator when the provider row has no IMAP host.** `finish_pending` disables authorization before it reads the provider and keeps it disabled through `store_new_account`, which calls `create#EmailAccount`; that service lets a hosted or shared account with no `storeHost` through when authorization is disabled (the SEC-MAIL-01 rule). `provider_server_fields` leaves `storeHost` out when the provider row has none, and only the POP3 case is refused for an empty host. With such a provider row, any mail client user (`start#MailSignIn` to `start#OAuthSignIn` with `accountKindEnumId=EakShared`, then the callback) makes a shared account delivered by the SMTP listener at the signed-in address without `EMAIL_ACCOUNT_HOSTED`. The address is the one the provider's ID token named, so the user holds that mailbox elsewhere; what they gain is local delivery for it. Not confirmed: whether any seeded or documented provider row has an empty `storeHost` (the seed rows were not found; `Provider::configured`, `oauth.rs:223-230`, does not require one). Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/oauth.rs:1360`, 1079, 1191, 1799-1803, 1817; `EmailClientServices.xml:263-267`. Fix: Refuse a new-account sign-in when the provider has no store host for the protocol asked, and call `create#EmailAccount` as the user (drop the guard before `store_new_account`, or check the hosted rule there).
- **SEC-MAIL-98 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The mail OAuth sign-in reads the ID token's claims without checking its signature, and nothing holds the token endpoint to https.** `complete#OAuthSignIn` takes the account's address from the ID token in the token endpoint's answer: `jwt_claims` decodes the payload and `check_id_token` checks the audience, issuer, expiry and nonce, with no JWS verification. OpenID Connect Core 3.1.3.7 lets a client skip the signature of an ID token it got straight from the token endpoint over TLS, and the module's comment says so; ASVS asks for it always. The premise holds for the seed providers, whose `tokenUrl` is https, but nothing requires it: `post_form` sends to the `EmailOAuthProvider` row's `tokenUrl` with no scheme test (the Outbound map says so), and the user info fallback is the same, so a row pointed at an `http` URL gets its tokens, and the claims the sign-in trusts, in clear. Whoever can change that row already receives the code and the client secret. Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-6.8.2); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-mail/src/oauth.rs:22-28`, `:451-475`, `:1517-1525`, `:1548-1570`, `:1592-1600`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/BasicEntities.xml:853`, `:866`. Fix: Refuse a `tokenUrl` or `userInfoUrl` that is not https; verify the ID token's signature against the provider's JWKS (both seed providers publish one), or take the address only from the user info answer.

## Files, DAV, SSH and the terminal

- **SEC-FIL-05 (Low; confirmed in code; fixed). No attempt limit on a public link's password.** `open#PublicShare` checks the password with an argon2 comparison on every call and keeps no count of failures; a wrong password adds an error and nothing else. A holder of the link can guess passwords at the server's speed, and each guess costs the server an argon2 computation. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/FileServices.xml:2669-2702`. Fix: Count failed openings per share and per address, and refuse for a time after a few. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 8892e5e4e): `open#PublicShare` counts each try of a link's password before it checks it, 10 in ten minutes for the link and 30 from one requester (`AccountServices.record#Attempt`, the requester as the public account limits know it), past which it answers "too many tries" and computes no hash. Test example `Files.spec.js` (the eleventh try, the right password, refused).
- **SEC-FIL-06 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). Anonymous file drop has no size, count or type limit of its own.** `upload#PublicShare` passes the files to `upload#Resource` with authorization off. The only bound is the drive's `quotaBytes`, which is null unless an owner sets it, so uploads are limited only by the request body limit, per request, with no limit on the number of requests. A file's own name may hold folders, so an upload-only link writes into new subfolders, and on a whole-drive link a name starting `.trash/` is not refused (only the `path` parameter is checked). Nothing scans the content. Where: `FileServices.xml:2834-2870`, `:1250`. Fix: Give link uploads a per-request and per-link byte and file cap, require a quota on a drive before it takes an upload link, and use only the last segment of an anonymous file name. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit b547c4559): `upload#PublicShare` names each file by the last segment of its own name (no folders; a name that is empty or `.trash` becomes `upload`), takes at most 20 files and 100 MiB in one call, 30 calls an hour through one link and 60 from one requester (`record#Attempt`), and 1000 files and 2 GiB through a link in all, counted on the share (`ResourceShare.uploadCount`, `uploadBytes`), whether or not the drive has a quota. A drive with no quota still takes an upload link: the link's own cap holds instead. Nothing scans the content, as before. Test `files_tests::a_link_upload_takes_a_files_last_name_and_is_capped`.
- **SEC-FIL-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Three `FileServices` parameters name a server path and are not checked in the service.** `create#Drive` refuses an explicit `rootLocation` to a non-administrator but takes `localPath` from anyone and stores it on the `GitRepository` row, where an absolute path or one with `..` is used as given. `export#Drive` writes a zip to any `targetLocation`, and passes `targetPath` to `GitServices.export#GitRepository`, for any drive manager. The check lives in the wrapper's parameter list, not in the service, so any new caller that forwards its parameters exposes a path write and a drive over any bare repository on disk. Where: `FileServices.xml:307,341`; `:680-681,695-696,719`. Fix: In `create#Drive` and `export#Drive`, take `localPath`, `targetPath` and `targetLocation` only from `ADMIN` or with authorization off, as `rootLocation` is. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 8f9411702): `create#Drive` refuses a `localPath` from anyone but an administrator or a caller with authorization off, as it does an explicit `rootLocation`; test `files_tests::a_drive_is_reached_by_its_owner_and_members_by_permission_and_not_by_a_stranger`. The `export#Drive` parameters were held earlier the same day: `targetLocation` under `runtime/drive-export` with authorization on, `targetPath` under `runtime/git-export` (`underExportDir`).
- **SEC-FIL-62 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The admin shell's `service`, `entity`, `cache` and `js` commands leave no audit record.** `js <code>` runs any JavaScript with `ec`, and `service <name>` any service, as that user under the Tools screen. Neither `shell.rs` nor the exec path in `server.rs` writes a log line or an audit row for the line that ran; only the login is logged. `sh` and `attach` are logged and `sh` has a `TerminalSession` audit row, so the stronger command (`js`) is the one without a trace. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/command/shell.rs:87 ; crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:1208. Fix: Log each shell line's command word and service or entity name at INFO, and write an audit row (who, from where, a digest of the `js` text) as `sh` does. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit b20d40a76): each `service`, `entity`, `cache` and `js` line that runs at the `moqui>` prompt (SSH exec and shell, the browser terminal's command line) writes an INFO line and a `moqui.terminal.TerminalSession` row of kind `command` (user, client address, the command word with the service or entity name, for `js` a SHA-256 digest of the code, the exit status; never the code or a call's parameters; `command::shell::record`). Test `ssh::sftp_and_the_admin_shell_run_on_the_built_in_server`.
- **SEC-FIL-63 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). Admin shell error text and C1 controls reach the administrator's terminal unescaped.** A failed `service` or `js` prints the message facade's errors as plain text; `with_newlines` only changes `\n`. An error that quotes stored text carrying ESC sequences (a title change, an OSC 52 clipboard write) is written to the terminal as is. Values are printed with `serde_json::to_string_pretty`, which escapes U+0000 to U+001F but not DEL or the C1 controls (U+0080 to U+009F). Where: crates/moqui-host/src/command/shell.rs:185 ; crates/moqui-host/src/command/mod.rs:130. Fix: Pass shell output through the TUI's `clean_text` (keeping tab and newline) before it is written to a pty channel. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): `with_newlines` (`crates/moqui-host/src/command/mod.rs`), which every shell outcome and the SSH exec answer go through, drops C0 controls other than tab and newline, DEL, the C1 controls and the bidirectional overrides and isolates, as the TUI's `clean_text` does; the shell's `error:` line does too. Test `command::tests::shell_output_drops_terminal_controls_but_keeps_tab_and_newlines`.
- **SEC-FIL-67 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). Calendar and contact objects have no parser limit of their own; the bound is the 256 MiB DAV body.** The only limits found in `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim` are the recurrence counts (100000 instances, 2000000 periods). An object of up to `max-file` is held as a string, passed to `put#DavObject` (`data`, `allow-html="any"`) and parsed whole, with every event or card in it. One request can cost memory and time well past the body size. Where: crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/rrule.rs:35 ; runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/work/CalendarDavServices.xml:359. Fix: Cap a calendar or contact object (1 MiB is generous) in `dav/pim.rs` before the adapter is called, and cap components per object in the parser. Fixed 2026-11-16: a calendar or contact object a client PUTs is at most 1 MiB (`dav/pim.rs` `MAX_OBJECT`), 413 before it is parsed; test `dav::pim::tests::an_object_past_its_limit_is_refused_before_it_is_parsed`.
- **SEC-FIL-09 (Info; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `PublicShare.xml` `open` and `upload` name no method.** Both transitions have `require-session-token="false"` and no `method="post"`. The visitor has no session, so the token is not what protects them; the link token and the grant are. A GET with `?password=` would put the password in a logged URL, but the framework's rule for a transition with actions that is not `read-only` refuses URL parameters. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/PublicShare.xml:28,56`. Fix: Add `method="post"` to both so the rule is stated on the transition. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-boss 43165868, with the unsubscribe page's changes): both transitions are `method="post"`; found fixed by the security proofs, phase P7, and recorded 2026-11-19.
- **SEC-FIL-10 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A link or share stays valid after its maker loses access.** `check#PublicShareToken` tests only `revokedDate` and `expireDate`. Nothing ends a link when `remove#DriveMember` or `transfer#DriveOwner` takes away the access of `createdByUserId` (`transfer#DriveOwner` ends only the new owner's member row). The map says "the former owner keeps no access of their own"; a link the former owner made still serves the files. Where: `FileServices.xml:2649-2655`. Fix: On member removal and owner transfer, revoke the shares that user made on the drive, or list them for the manager.
- **SEC-FIL-11 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). SSH permissions are fixed for the life of a connection.** The three flags are read at login and stored on the connection; a later `subsystem_request`, `exec_request` or `shell_request` reads the stored flags. A revoked permission keeps working on an open connection until it ends. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:746-763`, `:1073`, `:1196`. Fix: Re-read the permission when a channel request arrives.
- **SEC-FIL-64 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A terminal ticket is reusable for 300 seconds and travels in the `/termws` query string.** `confirm#TerminalUser` answers a ticket that stands for a recent sign-in. `ticket_holds` does not remove it, so it is good for every open and attach of that user for 300 seconds, and `/termws` takes it from the URL, where an access log or a proxy can record it. It is bound to the username, and a socket still needs that user's login key or session. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/term/registry.rs:96 ; crates/moqui-host/src/termws.rs:169. Fix: Use a ticket up on its first successful attach or open, or send it in the first socket message instead of the URL.
- **SEC-FIL-65 (Info; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `terminal` `command-line` and `host-sessions` are on in production for any value but `dev` or `false`.** The map and the docs describe both as dev-only. The switch reads unset and `dev` as "dev instances only" and every other value except `false` as on, so a typo or `true` opens the command line or host sessions whatever `instance_purpose` is. The permission, origin and place checks still apply. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/term/access.rs:247. Fix: Accept only `dev`, `true` and `false`, treat anything else as off with an error, and log a WARN at start when either is `true` on a production instance. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 2a53b8a29): only `dev` (or unset), `true` and `false` are read; any other value, empty included, is off with an error logged once, and `true` on a production instance is a warning logged once (`term::access` `switched_on`, `switch_on`). An empty value was documented as on for any purpose and is now off. Test `term::access::tests::a_switch_is_on_only_for_true_or_dev_on_a_dev_instance`.
- **SEC-FIL-66 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The `term-hold` socket checks file modes only; a connection starts any program.** The directory is `0700` and the socket `0600`; the holder reads no peer credential. One JSON line with `op` `open` starts the `argv` and `env` it names through a `helper` it names, and other operations list and attach to held sessions. This gives nothing the same OS user lacks, but the holder outlives the server, so the socket stays after a stop. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/term/hold.rs:139 ; crates/moqui-host/src/term/hold.rs:283. Fix: Check `SO_PEERCRED` against the holder's own uid on accept, as a second check behind the modes.
- **SEC-FIL-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Connections with no user from one address hold every SSH session.** With `login-dialog` true, keyboard-interactive is accepted at once and takes a session as `Who::Dialog`, counted against the total and the source's share. The default share of one source (8) is the default total (8), and such a connection needs no channel and is closed only after `LOGIN_WAIT` (120 seconds). Eight connections from one address, opened again every two minutes, keep `max-sessions` full; every key or password login is then refused (`SessionRefused::Total`), and so is a cluster peer on the SSH transport. Sessions already open stay. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:63-68`, `:425`, `:442`, `:1057-1081`, `:493-499`; `MoquiDefaultConf.xml:1323-1326`. Fix: Give connections with no user a share of their own below the total (for example half of `max-sessions`, and 2 a source), lower the default `max-sessions-per-source` under `max-sessions`, and close a dialog connection that has opened no `apps` within the pre-auth time.
- **SEC-FIL-88 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A connection's per-channel maps are never cleared, so repeated channels grow the server's memory.** `pty_request` stores the client's `TERM` string, of any length a packet holds, in `self.ptys` under the channel id; `env_request` stores `GIT_PROTOCOL` (no length limit) and the terminal variables. The handler has no `channel_close`, nothing removes an entry of `ptys` or `terminal_env`, russh has no limit on channels over a connection's life, and the handler's own limit counts only channels still waiting for a request. A client that loops (open a channel, `pty-req` with a `TERM` of about 250 KB, an exec the server ends at once, close) leaves each `TERM` in memory until the connection ends. Reached by any account logged in over SSH, and by a peer with no credentials for the 120 seconds a dialog connection lives (8 at a time). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:1108-1128`, `:1321-1341`, `:670-680`. Fix: Implement `channel_close` to remove the channel from `channels`, `ptys`, `terminal_env` and `git_protocol`; cap `term` and `GIT_PROTOCOL` at 256 bytes as the terminal variables are.
- **SEC-FIL-89 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An SSH key login does not check `ipAllowed` and writes no login history.** The password login runs inside `with_client_ip`, so `finish_login` checks the account's and its groups' `ipAllowed` against the SSH peer. The key login runs `login_ssh_key_user` with no `with_client_ip`; `client_ip()` is empty on the pool worker and `check_ip_allowed` returns at once. An account (or a group such as `ADMIN_ADV`) held to an address range logs in by key from anywhere and gets the admin shell, SFTP and git its permissions give; the commands after the login use `internal_login_user` the same way. `login_ssh_key_user` also drops the user id `internal_login` returns for the login history, so a key login leaves a log line and no `UserLoginHistory` row. Not confirmed: where the history row is written (`recorded_login` was read only by its callers). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:1035-1045`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:527-529`, `:1641`, `:1717-1720`. Fix: Run the key login inside `with_client_ip(&self.peer.ip().to_string(), ...)` and through the recorded form of the internal login, as `login_verified_app_password` does for XMPP.
- **SEC-FIL-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The prompt's history stores whole command lines, with service parameters and `js` code, as a user preference.** Every line ended at the prompt on a pty (SSH shell, the browser command line), up to 4096 bytes, goes into the history: a `service <name> {json}` with its parameters, a one-line `js`, each line of a `js` block. After each new line the last 500 are written as JSON to the user's `TERMINAL_HISTORY` preference in plain text, where whoever reads `UserPreference` rows (the database, a backup, the entity tools, another holder of the shell) reads them. SEC-FIL-62's audit keeps the service name and a digest of `js` code only, because parameters can carry secrets; the history keeps exactly those. A line recalled with Up is written back to the terminal as stored. Not confirmed: whether `UserPreference.preferenceValue` is an encrypted field, and which screens let a user read another user's preferences. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/command/line.rs:142-147`; `crates/moqui-host/src/command/mod.rs:143-178`, `:368-371`. Fix: Keep in the history what the audit keeps for `service` (the name) and nothing of `js`, or keep the history in the session's memory only.
- **SEC-FIL-91 (Low; reported (likely)). `agent-apps <id>` waits on a blocking lock on the async runtime while another command runs in that session.** A one-shot command runs in `spawn_blocking` holding the session's `std::sync::Mutex` for the whole command. Another `agent-apps <id> ...` exec for the same session calls `AgentSessions::get` in the async task, which locks that mutex to read the username and so blocks a worker thread of the runtime until the first command ends. Exec channels on one connection are not limited once they have their request, so one user (any account logged in by key or account password) starts a slow command and then enough further execs to block every worker of the runtime the SSH server shares, for as long as the slow command runs. Not confirmed: how long one `Agent::run` can take, and how many worker threads the edge runtime has. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/agent.rs:79-84`, `:194-204`. Fix: Keep the username beside the `Arc` outside the mutex, or take the lock inside `spawn_blocking` only.
- **SEC-FIL-92 (Low; reported (likely)). The SSH login lines write the client's username to the log as sent.** The username of an SSH auth request is any bytes of UTF-8 a packet holds, and a failed password or key login logs it with `{user}`, not `{user:?}`. The console writer splits a message at newlines and passes every other character, ESC included, so a terminal that tails the log carries the client's bytes. The dialog line at `server.rs:1075-1077` uses `{user:?}` and is escaped. Not confirmed: the file sink's format was not read; the TUI's log pane writes a space for a control character, so a terminal showing the log is the risk, not the pane. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:979-983`, `:1049`, `:789-793`; `crates/moqui-host/src/logging.rs:787-796`. Fix: Log `{user:?}` (as line 1076 does) cut to a length, or strip control characters in the log layer for every message.
- **SEC-FIL-93 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The calendar parsers panic on three malformed values.** `BYDAY=€` (any item whose last two bytes are not a character boundary) panics in `item.split_at(item.len() - 2)`, at expansion and inside `ICalendar.parse` for a VTIMEZONE's RRULE. `INTERVAL` is any positive `i64`, and `index.days()`, `.months()`, `.hours()`, `.minutes()` and `.seconds()` panic past jiff's span limits (`FREQ=DAILY;INTERVAL=99999999`); `index * 7` and `index += rule.interval` can also overflow. An all-day event with `DURATION:P99999999D` and no DTEND panics in `ICalendar.parse` (`days.days()`). A panic in a host function becomes a thrown script error (`engine.rs:1310`), and the callers of `expand` catch it (`EventServices.xml:518-524`). Where: `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/rrule.rs:227`, `:197-203`, `:268-270`, `:477`, `:496-498`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/ics.rs:296-297`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/zones.rs:324`. Fix: Split with `item.len().checked_sub(2)` and `str::get`; bound INTERVAL; use `Span::try_days` and the other `try_` constructors.
- **SEC-FIL-94 (Low; reported (likely)). The iCalendar writer writes RRULE and URL values as stored, so a stored line break adds content lines.** `parse_line` decodes quoted-printable for any property, so a DAV PUT with `RRULE;ENCODING=QUOTED-PRINTABLE:FREQ=DAILY;X=1=0D=0AATTENDEE:mailto:...` gives a value that holds a real CR LF. `store#CalendarFeedEvents` stores the trimmed value, `Rule::parse` ignores the unknown `X` part, and `write` puts `RRULE:{rule}` and `URL:{url}` out without escaping; `fold` keeps line breaks. The added lines appear in what other users of the calendar get over CalDAV, in an export, and in the iMIP mail the server sends for the event (`EventServices.xml:1783`). Not confirmed: whether the entity layer or the database changes a CR LF in `WorkEffortRecurrence.rrule` or `WorkEffort.infoUrl`, and which headers of the iMIP mail an added line could contradict. The contact card's stored `davUid` is written into the card the same way (`ContactDavServices.xml:223`) and reaches only the book's own owner. Where: `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/ics.rs:893-896`, `:928-930`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/line.rs:160-165`, `:366-385`. Fix: Refuse or strip CR and LF in `rrule` and `url` on write, and store a rule only if `Rule::parse` takes it.
- **SEC-FIL-95 (Low; reported (likely)). Two parses of a 1 MiB DAV body are quadratic.** `resolve` in the DAV XML reader copies the whole inherited namespace scope for every element, so a root with tens of thousands of `xmlns:` attributes and a hundred thousand children costs their product in string copies. `Card::label_of` scans every property of a card for each grouped EMAIL, TEL and ADR, so a card of 80,000 `item1.TEL:1` lines costs 80,000 squared comparisons, and `put#DavObject` parses the card twice (`ContactDavServices.xml:263`, `:266`). Any DAV user reaches both after login; the times are estimated, not measured. Where: `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/xml.rs:73-84`, `:98`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/vcard.rs:134-143`. Fix: Share the scope (push and pop, or an `Rc` chain); index X-ABLABEL by group once; cap the properties of one card and the events of one object.
- **SEC-FIL-96 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The SFTP client reads a remote file whole, with no size bound.** `poll#SystemMessageSftp` lists the remote directory and reads every matching file with `get_text`, which is `inner.sftp.read(path)`: the whole file in memory, then decoded, compared in a query and stored. Without `fileCountLimit` every listed file is read. The server a `SystemMessageRemote` row names (a trading partner) decides the sizes. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/client.rs:508-511`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/sftp.rs:303-312`, `:358-359`. Fix: `stat` first and refuse a file over a set size; default `fileCountLimit`.
- **SEC-FIL-97 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Over SSH a loopback peer is the server's own machine, and a recent sign-in is never asked.** For HTTP the place rule refuses "local" when a forwarding header is there. SSH has no such signal: a peer on loopback is `Place::Local`, so `sh` needs no `terminal` `remote`, and `initial_admin_local()` is true, so the login dialog asks no setup secret (on a system with no accounts and `initial_admin_enabled` true). A TCP forwarder on the same machine (a tunnel tool, an OpenSSH `-L` or jump through the host's own sshd, a port-forward) makes its clients loopback peers; an edge tunnel is handled (`via_edge`). Separately, `sh` and `attach` pass `&|_| true` for "the sign-in is recent", so a remote SSH connection of any age passes where the browser asks again after 60 minutes. Not confirmed: `crate::http::local_peer` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/term/access.rs:179-189`; `crates/moqui-host/src/command/host.rs:51`; `crates/moqui-host/src/command/attach.rs:81`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/setup.rs:46-53`; `crates/moqui-host/src/term/apps.rs:331`. Fix: Give `ssh-server` a setting that treats every SSH peer as remote (for a host that publishes the port through a forwarder), and compare the connection's login time with `remote-reauth-minutes` when `sh` or `attach` is typed.
- **SEC-FIL-98 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A drive's `.trash` is guarded in four services only.** `list#Folder`, `get#ResourceInfo`, `upload#Resource` and `trash#Resource` refuse the hidden trash by its path, as "the owner's only". `copy#Resource` takes `.trash` or `.trash/<id>/<name>` as a source from any member with view level and copies it to a folder they may write; `move#Resource`, `rename#Resource` and `save#Text` take such a path from a member with edit level and leave the `ResourceTrash` row behind. This is reached through the Files screens, not through DAV or SFTP, whose `info` call goes through `get#ResourceInfo` first. A member already had view of those files before they were trashed, so no boundary of the drive is crossed. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/FileServices.xml:1545-1551` (`copy#Resource`), `:1476-1481` (`move#Resource`), `:1416-1422` (`rename#Resource`), `:1321-1325` (`save#Text`), `:1352-1360` (`create#Folder`). Fix: One helper that refuses a `.trash` path for anyone but the owner, called by every service that takes a path.
- **SEC-FIL-101 (Low; likely (second check)). A drive mirror's URL may be plain `http://`, and the mirror's stored token is offered to it.** `create#Drive` (an administrator's, for a mirror) refuses a URL with a user or password in it but checks no scheme, and `update#Drive` (any manager of the drive) writes a new `remoteUrl` as given, keeping the stored `accessToken`. The mirror's fetch answers every credential request of the remote with the stored user name and token, whatever the URL's scheme, so over `http://` the token goes in clear to whoever is on the path, at each fetch interval. SEC-BOS-90 records the manager's repointing to another host; this is the scheme, which an administrator's own mirror has too. Not confirmed: that gix sends the credentials over plain http without a further check (its credential and transport code was not read). Report: the security proofs, phase P7 (ASVS v5.0.0-12.3.1); lines read again at mr-moqui `1d1594480`. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/FileServices.xml:328-333`, `:390-420`; `crates/moqui-git/src/fs.rs:394-455`. Fix: In both services refuse a mirror URL that is not `https://` or `ssh://` (a `git://` or `http://` one only without a token, or only off production), and clear the stored token when the URL's scheme or host changes.

## Calls, chat and messaging

- **SEC-COM-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Chat files have no size limit or quota of their own.** `upload#Attachment` writes the uploaded part to `dbresource://chat/...` whatever its size and counts it against nothing. The only bound is the request's (`webapp_max_upload`, 2 GiB by default). Call recordings count against the owner's drive quota and chunked uploads stop at 25 MB and 100 MB open, but a plain multipart upload to chat does neither, so one user can fill the database-backed store; unsent files go after a day, sent ones stay (a room has no retention unless `retentionDays` is set). Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:3143-3192`. Fix: Give `upload#Attachment` a size limit (a property, with the chunked path's 25 MB as the default) and count chat files toward a per-user or per-room quota. Fixed 2026-11-16: `upload#Attachment` refuses a file over `chat_attachment_max_bytes` (25 MB) and a user's uploads over `chat_attachment_daily_bytes` in 24 hours (1 GB), a per-user daily bound rather than the drive quota; test `chat_tests::files_go_with_their_message_and_go_away_with_it`.
- **SEC-COM-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A few anonymous connections keep every guest out of `/callws`.** A socket that has not yet sent its credential holds one of 64 places on the node for up to 10 seconds. A client that opens 64 sockets and sends nothing, and opens them again every 10 seconds, has every other guest's upgrade answered 503, so no guest gets media signaling on that node. The places are not per source, and a wrong or missing credential is not counted for web protection. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/rtc.rs:670-688,691-713,737`. Fix: Hold the waiting places per source (as `rtc/tcp.rs` does with `SourceSlots`), and take the credential in the upgrade request's first message with a shorter wait. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 6fff4a61e): `/callws` holds its waiting places in a `SourceSlots` (`notws/rtc.rs` `guest_waiting`: 64 on a node, 4 from one client address), a socket has 5 seconds (was 10) to send its credential, and a socket refused for its first frame counts as an `AuthFailure` of its client for web protection. Test `rtc::a_guest_by_link_joins_over_its_own_socket_and_revoking_the_link_ends_it`. The credential still comes in the first frame, not in the upgrade request (a change of the guest page and the client).
- **SEC-COM-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code for the count; likely for the rate limit); fixed). Fifty guest joins fill a link's meeting until it ends, and on the default conf nothing slows them.** Each join makes a `CallGuest`, a `CallParticipant` and a `CallLeg`, starts the meeting when none is going, and pushes the chosen name to the link's owner and the people in the call. The service refuses a join once 50 guest participants of the meeting are `CasWaiting` or `CasAdmitted`. A guest who leaves, whose credential runs out or whose offered leg times out keeps that admission status (only a removal or a denial changes it), so 50 joins from one holder of the link answer every later guest `callFull` for as long as that meeting runs, unless someone in the call denies the waiting names one by one. The declared limit (10 a minute a client) is a per-client rule, which is off unless per-client web protection is on; with it on, the 50 take five minutes from one address. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Calls.js:1514-1561` (`guestJoin`), `:1414-1438` (`revokeGuest`); `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/MoquiConf.xml:14`. Fix: Count only guests with an open leg, limit joins per link in the service (a few a minute, whatever the client), and take a waiting guest out when its page's socket is gone. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 88d1777f7): `Calls.js` `guestJoin` counts a meeting's guest places by open guest legs (offered or answered), so a guest who left, timed out or lost its credential frees its place, and a link takes at most `call_guest_joins_per_minute` (20) joins a minute whatever the client (`tooManyJoins`). Test `Calls.spec.js` "a link's places are held by guests with an open leg, and a link takes only so many joins a minute".
- **SEC-COM-06 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An invisible user's last activity and call state are still answered.** For a user whose status is invisible the presence is answered `offline`, but the same item carries `lastActiveDate` (the user's last activity, to the millisecond) and `inCall`, so a caller sees that the user is active now and whether they are in a call. The status text and emoji are answered too. The same item goes to room co-members on `userStatus` pushes (without `lastActiveDate`). Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:4086-4099`. Fix: For an invisible user (and for an id other than the caller's, when offline) answer no `lastActiveDate` and `inCall` false; consider limiting other users' status to those the caller shares a room with. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit e991f254b): `get#UserStatuses` answers another user who is offline (invisible included) with `lastActiveDate` null and `inCall` false, and `publish#UserStatus` pushes `inCall` false to co-members while the user is offline; the user's own item keeps both. Test `chat_tests::presence_follows_the_heartbeat_the_status_and_its_clear_at_time`. Not done: limiting statuses to users the caller shares a room with (presence is on every avatar, a product decision).
- **SEC-COM-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code that it is not checked; no request path that sets it was found); fixed). A room member's `agentRef` is any service name a moderator gives, and each message then calls it.** A moderator or owner of a room (anyone who makes a room is its owner) may give an agent member any text as `agentRef`. The role itself is limited to an `AgentDefinition`'s chat user, but the service name is not compared with that agent's. `send#Message` then runs `deliver#AgentMessage` asynchronously for each message, which calls the named service with the message's fields. Which user and authorization that asynchronous call runs with was not traced. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:1596,1623,1668,1673` (`add#Member`), `:1774,1799` (`set#MemberRole`), `:4023-4033` (`deliver#AgentMessage`). Fix: Take the service from the `AgentDefinition` of the member's chat user (or an allow-list of agent entry services), not from a parameter. Fixed 2026-11-16: `add#Member` and `set#MemberRole` take an `agentRef` only from `chat_agent_entry_services`; test `chat_tests::an_agent_member_gets_each_message_through_its_entry_point`.
- **SEC-COM-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Relayed call frames have no rate limit and reach every leg, a guest's included.** The media node copies the whole frame (up to 128 KiB, any fields) to every other leg of the room, with only `id` removed and `from` set. Nothing counts frames per leg, so one leg can push megabytes a second to up to 49 others' signaling sockets; a socket that falls behind is closed ("the client fell behind"), which drops those people's signaling. The frame's own fields other than `from` are the sender's, so a client that trusts a field of a relayed frame trusts the sender. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/rtc/driver.rs:435-449`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/rtc.rs:141-155,855-866`. Fix: Keep only the fields each of these frames needs (a short reaction, a boolean), cap their size at a few hundred bytes, and rate-limit them per leg. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 6fff4a61e): the media node relays a leg's `mute`, `hand`, `reaction` and `unpublish` as a new frame of only that frame's own short fields, with `from` and `callLegId` the sender's (`rtc/driver.rs` `relayed_frame`), and a leg relays at most 20 at once and 5 a second after (`RelayBucket`), the rest answered `rateLimited`. Tests `rtc::tests::a_relayed_frame_keeps_only_its_own_short_fields_and_names_its_sender`, `rtc::tests::a_leg_relays_a_burst_and_then_a_few_frames_a_second`.
- **SEC-COM-15 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). An XMPP session outlives the account's `terminateDate` and keeps receiving room messages.** `check#Credential` reads `disabled` and the app password only, so the session is not ended. Its service calls fail, but chat pushes are delivered by user id from the notification bus, and `event_out` builds the message stanza from the push and the session's cached room and username maps with no service call. The session goes on receiving the messages of the rooms it had cached (direct rooms, joined rooms) until it disconnects. A web session of such an account is ended (`account_closed`, `login.rs:1530-1533`, which tests `terminated(account, now)`). Where: `XmppServices.xml:82-90` (`check#Credential`); `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/mod.rs:660-666`; `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/registry.rs:147-180`. Fix: Make `check#Credential` use the same test as the sign-in gate (`account_closed` plus the lock), and end the session when `internal_login_user` refuses the user. Fixed 2026-11-16: `XmppServices.get#Account` and `check#Credential` refuse an account past its `terminateDate`, so a session ends at its next minute check; test `framework/test/XmppServices.spec.js`.
- **SEC-COM-16 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An upload URL with its token is written to the log.** `attach#Upload` refuses an upload that is not the caller's ("No such upload of yours"), and the session logs the whole URL at WARN. The token in the URL is the only thing needed to read the file, so a reader of the server log can fetch other users' files. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/chat.rs:303-310`. Fix: Log the upload's file name or the token's hash, not the URL. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit f5181b5a2): the XMPP session logs an upload it could not attach by its file name (`session/chat.rs` `upload_file_name`), never the URL whose token reads the file. Test `moqui-xmpp` `session::tests::an_upload_is_logged_by_its_file_name_not_its_token`.
- **SEC-COM-17 (Low; reported (likely); fixed). TURN allocation quotas are per username, and a leg or user can get many usernames.** The two-allocation quota counts allocations whose username equals the request's. A username is `<expiry>:<leg>`, and the expiry is the second the credential was made plus the lifetime, so each new request for ICE servers gives a new username with a fresh quota. One caller can hold `turn_max_allocations` (1,000) relays and their UDP ports, after which every other allocation is refused. An XMPP user gets 30 credentials an hour, each good for 4 hours: up to 240 allocations. Where: `crates/moqui-rtc/src/turn/mod.rs:495-503`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call_media.rs:424-429`; `XmppServices.xml:808-819`. Fix: Count allocations by leg (`a.leg`), not by username. Fixed 2026-11-16: the two-allocation quota counts the allocations of the request's leg, not of its username; test `turn::tests::a_leg_holds_its_quota_over_all_of_its_usernames`.
- **SEC-COM-18 (Low; reported (likely)). An XMPP user's TURN credential can relay to the host's own public addresses.** For an `xmpp.<userId>` leg a peer is allowed when it is public, is not an `allowed_peers` address, and is not the address a listener is bound to. The listeners' addresses are the bound ones (`turn_host`, by default `rtc_media_host`), which are a wildcard or a private address on a host behind NAT. A public address of the same host that is not the media node's public address then passes, and UDP sent to it comes from the host itself, past a firewall rule that only keeps outside sources off a UDP port (SIP on UDP, another node's ports). Where: `crates/moqui-rtc/src/turn/mod.rs:340-358`; `crates/moqui-host/src/rtc/turn.rs:341-343, 383`. Fix: Also refuse `public_ip`, the HTTP and SIP public addresses and every address of the host's interfaces (read at start) for an XMPP leg.
- **SEC-COM-19 (Low; reported (likely); deferred). One user can close another user's XMPP sessions with routed stanzas.** Routed stanzas go straight into the target session's queue of 512. When the queue is full the target session is failed with `policy-violation` and closed; a detached session past 1,000 unacknowledged stanzas loses resumption and ends. There is no per-sender rate limit, and the target's block list is applied when the stanza is taken from the queue, so blocking the sender does not stop the queue from filling. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/registry.rs:182-190`; `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/conn.rs:281`; `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/iq.rs:138-170`. Fix: Check the target's block list before queueing, and hold routed stanzas to a rate per sender and target. Deferred 2026-11-16: a design of its own.
- **SEC-COM-20 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Per-user storage over XMPP has no total bound.** A PEP item may be 262,144 characters, with 256 items a node and 200 nodes a user: about 13 GiB of `XmppPepItem.payloadXml` for one user. `block#Jids` takes 500 JIDs a call with no total. `enable#Push` replaces only the row of the same `pushJid` and `node`, so other names add rows, each with an options form of up to a stanza. Uploads are limited by the hour (ten times `xmpp_upload_max_bytes`, 200 MB by default) and a completed upload is never removed, so a user can add that much to the database every hour; nothing counts toward a drive quota. Where: `XmppServices.xml:507-514` (PEP), `592-598` (block list), `611-626` (push), `660-667` and `738-750` (uploads). Fix: A total byte bound for a user's PEP, a row cap for blocked JIDs and push registrations, and count XMPP uploads against the user's drive quota or expire unattached ones.
