Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit c967951cc), the owner's call: the proposed caps, each a conf setting, refused past the cap, and unattached uploads expired after 24 hours. `publish#PepItem` keeps an item only while the user's PEP items stay within `xmpp_pep_max_bytes` (20 MiB) of UTF-8 (`XmppPepItem.payloadBytes`, summed by the view `XmppPepItemBytes`, the replaced item not counted); `block#Jids` blocks nothing past `xmpp_block_max_jids` (1,000) and `enable#Push` keeps no registration past `xmpp_push_max_registrations` (50, a replacement not counted); past a cap each answers `resourceConstraint` and the session refuses the IQ with `resource-constraint` (type wait); the daily `clean#Uploads` deletes a completed upload no chat file has `xmpp_upload_unattached_hours` (24) after it was put; 0 is no cap for each. Tests `XmppServices.spec.js` "PEP bytes, blocked JIDs and push registrations stop at their caps; an unattached upload goes after a day", `moqui-xmpp` `session::tests::a_request_past_a_users_cap_is_a_resource_constraint`.
- **SEC-COM-21 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Chat apps makes a long-lived, unscoped credential with no recent sign-in.** `createXmppPassword` makes an app password that never expires, skips the second factor by design, and is taken by XMPP, DAV, SSH and SFTP, git over HTTP, the LLM gateway and A2A, though the dialog calls it "for XMPP". The service asks for no sign-in in the last five minutes, which the chat key services do ask for (`reauthRequired`). The new password is held in the web session (`newXmppPassword`) until the screen renders again. Where: `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Settings/ChatApps.xml:22-27`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/user_keys.rs:382-409`. Fix: Require a recent sign-in for `create#UserAppPassword`, and give the row a scope (the protocols it may be used with) that each taker checks. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part: `create#UserAppPassword` needs a sign-in in the last five minutes; left: a scope on `UserAppPassword` that each taker checks needs a design. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 6c112f7e4, mr-boss fe1db9e0), the owner's call: a row made before scopes keeps working everywhere. `UserAppPassword.scope` names the one protocol that takes it: `dav`, `ssh` (SSH, SFTP, git over SSH), `git` (git over HTTP), `xmpp` or `ai` (the LLM gateway, A2A, MCP; `user::APP_PASSWORD_SCOPES`); `create#UserAppPassword` requires it. `login_app_password` and `credential_user` take the taker's scope and refuse a row made for another (`login.rs` `scope_allows`): DAV and its pre-body check `dav`, the SSH server `ssh`, git over HTTP `git`, XMPP PLAIN `xmpp`, the gateway and `a2a::log_in` `ai`; the upload size check before a route's login takes any. XMPP's SCRAM credentials (`get#ScramCredentials`) and `login_verified_app_password` take only `xmpp` or unscoped rows. Chat apps makes `xmpp` passwords and lists only those XMPP takes; Commons Sync (mr-boss) makes `dav` ones, or `ssh` for SFTP by the dialog's choice, and shows each one's use. Tests `user::login::tests::an_app_password_is_taken_only_by_the_protocol_it_was_made_for`, mr-boss `SyncSettings.spec.js`, and the host's DAV, SSH, XMPP, gateway and A2A tests with their scopes.
- **SEC-COM-22 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). SCRAM and FAST sign-ins leave no login history and no last-used date.** Only the PLAIN path calls `login_app_password`, which writes `UserLoginHistory` and `UserAppPassword.lastUsedDate`. A SCRAM or FAST sign-in writes neither, and a failed one is a WARN line with no username. Settings, Chat apps shows "Not used yet" for an app password that is used every day over SCRAM, so a user cannot see that a stolen one is in use; `use#FastToken` stamps only the token row. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/auth.rs:411-421`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/user_keys.rs:462-523`. Fix: On every XMPP sign-in write the history row and the app password's `lastUsedDate` (at most once a minute), with the source address. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-COM-11: SCRAM and FAST ask `Backend::sign_in` before success, which writes the login history and the app password's last use (at most once a minute); a failed attempt still writes only the warning line.
- **SEC-COM-26 (Low; reported (likely); deferred). Unauthenticated connections can hold every SIP TCP and TLS slot.** The caps are 500 connections in all and 100 a source. A connection that never sends a valid request is kept until it is silent for 300 seconds (rsipstack's idle timeout), and a CRLF keepalive resets that. Five sources that each hold 100 connections fill the 500, and each new connection, a registered phone's or a trunk's, is then closed. A source blocked for failed digests keeps its connections. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:492-499`, `crates/moqui-lib/src/source_guard.rs:214-215`. Fix: Close a stream connection that has not completed an authenticated REGISTER, or is not a trunk's, within a short deadline, and lower the per-source cap for sources with no binding. Deferred 2026-11-16: a design of its own.
- **SEC-COM-27 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). RTP is sent to whatever address an SDP names, with no address check.** A leg sends its RTP to the SDP's connection address and port until a packet from an allowed source latches it. Nothing refuses loopback, private, link-local or the server's own addresses, so a caller can aim 50 UDP packets a second at an internal host and port for as long as the call lasts. The TURN server refuses such peers; the SIP media path has no equivalent. A phone behind NAT legitimately names a private address, so the check cannot simply refuse private ranges. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/media.rs:149-150,264-266`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/leg.rs:334`. Fix: Send no RTP until the first packet arrives from the signaling address or the SDP's address (latch first), or refuse loopback, link-local, multicast and the server's own listener addresses as media targets. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit e761bdc24), the address-check half: a SIP leg sends no RTP to an address no phone can be (`moqui-sip` `media::media_target_allowed`, used by the host's `sip/leg.rs`): an unspecified, multicast or broadcast address, port 0, the leg socket's own address, and loopback or link-local unless the socket is bound in that scope; a private address stays allowed (a phone behind NAT names one), and the first refusal per target is logged. Test `media::tests::rtp_goes_to_no_address_a_phone_cannot_be`. Latch-first was not done: a far end on hold, or one that waits for media first, would get none.
- **SEC-COM-28 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The digest answer's `uri` is not compared with the request.** HA2 is computed from the `uri` parameter inside the Authorization header, and no code compares that parameter with the Request-URI of the INVITE or REGISTER. A valid answer can be moved onto a request for another number before it reaches the server (the original must be kept from arriving, since a nonce count is taken once). RFC 7616 section 3.4.6 asks the server to check that the two match. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/digest.rs:472-486`. Fix: Refuse an answer whose `uri` is not the request's Request-URI. Fixed 2026-11-16: an answer whose `uri` is not the Request-URI (`uri_matches`) is refused with 403 and not counted; test `ua::tests::failed_digests_block_the_source_and_never_the_trunk`.
- **SEC-COM-09 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). "Authorization disabled" doubles as "trusted caller" in the chat and call services.** The services take `userId`, `senderUserId` and `senderActorId` (act as another user or a bridge's actor) and skip the record check of `create#Room` whenever `ec.artifactExecution.authzDisabled()` is true. The root transitions (`/setUserStatus`, `/chatTyping`, `/chatUnread`) and `/notws` call them with authorization disabled because no app's authorization covers them. Today each of those callers builds its parameter map from named fields and passes no `userId`, so nothing is reached; a transition that later passes a request's map through would let a user act as any other. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:192,2225,2231,3125,4164`, `:1367`; callers `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot.xml:143,161,173,185,197,222`, `basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Chat.xml:462,475,485,497,509`, `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/rtc.rs:186,203,220`. Fix: Give the "as another user" parameters their own internal services (`allow-remote="false"`, refusing unless called from the bridge, an agent or a job), so that turning authorization off for a screen's convenience does not also grant impersonation.
- **SEC-COM-10 (Info; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). An SMS bridge's API base may be `http://`, and one setting lifts the address guard.** `start` accepts an `apiBaseUrl` that begins with `http://`, and each send posts `Authorization: Bearer <apiKey>` there, so a mistyped or downgraded address sends the provider key in the clear. `allowInternalAddresses` (documented for tests) makes the adapter's client take loopback and private addresses for the API and for the media a text names, so with it an inbound text can make the server fetch an internal URL. Where: `crates/moqui-channels/src/telnyx.rs:97-100,122-124,150-161,242-244,388-391`. Fix: Refuse `http://` unless `allowInternalAddresses` is set, and refuse `allowInternalAddresses` unless `instance_purpose` is `dev` or `test`. Fixed 2026-11-16: Telnyx `start` takes `http://` only with `allowInternalAddresses`, which a production instance ignores; test moqui-channels `a_bridge_sends_its_key_over_https_and_reaches_no_internal_address_on_production`.
- **SEC-COM-23 (Info; reported (to check); fixed). `is_public` leaves out some non-public IPv6 ranges.** The IPv6 arm refuses unspecified, loopback, multicast, `::/96`, `fc00::/7`, `fe80::/10`, `2001:db8::/32`, `2001::/32`, `64::/16`, and maps `::ffff:a.b.c.d` and `2002::/16` to their IPv4 address. It does not refuse site-local `fec0::/10`, the IPv4-translated form `::ffff:0:a.b.c.d` (RFC 2765), `2001:10::/28`, `2001:2::/48` or `3fff::/20`. A network that still routes one of these inside would be reachable through a relay bound to an IPv6 address. Where: `crates/moqui-rtc/src/turn/mod.rs:84-122`. Fix: Allow only `2000::/3` for IPv6 peers, minus the ranges already listed. Fixed 2026-11-16: TURN's `is_public` takes the same IPv6 rule as `internal_address` and still refuses NAT64 whole; test `an_xmpp_users_credential_relays_to_public_addresses_only`.
- **SEC-COM-24 (Info; reported (to check)). The SCRAM decoy differs from a real user by one service call.** For a name that is no account, `credentials()` returns after `get#Account`. For an account it then calls `get#ScramCredentials` (two more queries) before the challenge is sent. The answers are the same in form (same iteration count, a stable salt), so only the time differs. The same holds for FAST (`get#FastToken` stops after `get#Account` for an unknown name). Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/auth.rs:242-248`. Fix: Call `get#ScramCredentials` for every name (it answers nothing for an unknown one), or fold both lookups into one service.
- **SEC-COM-29 (Info; reported (likely)). The phone-access answers tell an existing box from a number with none.** Failures for a number with a box count on the box (`box:<id>`), whatever suffix of the number named it; failures for digits with no box count on those digits (`number:<digits>`). The engine ends the call at the first refusal when the answer says `blocked`, and asks again when it does not. After five failures on one suffix of a number, a longer suffix of the same number is refused at once when the box exists and asked again when it does not. The map and `docs/interfaces/Voicemail.md` say the answers tell nothing of which boxes exist. Where: `call/Voicemail.js:625-637`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/voicemail.rs:371-374`. Fix: End the call after the same number of refusals whether or not the answer is `blocked`.
- **SEC-COM-30 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The emergency call email has no subject.** `notifyEmergency` builds the email to the location's `notifyEmailAddress` from a variable `title` that the function never declares. An unset name is null in these scripts, so the `EmailMessage` is created with a null subject and a body that begins with the text "null". The notification to the voice administrators uses its own title and is not affected. This is a defect in a safety notice, not an access weakness. Where: `call/Voice.js:302-309`. Fix: Build the subject from `who` and `place` with `ec.resource.expand` and use it for the subject and the first body line.
- **SEC-COM-83 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A SIP request with a made-up To tag passes the request rate limit and the transaction cap.** The transport inspector applies `sip_request_rate`, `sip_request_burst` and `sip_max_transactions` only to what `is_new_request` calls new, and that is decided from the text of the To header (`!to.value().contains(";tag=")`), not from whether a dialog exists. An INVITE, REGISTER or OPTIONS with `;tag=x` and no dialog is not counted, rsipstack makes a server transaction of it, and `incoming` then treats it as a new request: it is challenged with a fresh nonce, or taken as a trunk's call when the source is a trunk's. A blocked source is still dropped first and a failed digest still counts. The gate is confirmed in code; how long rsipstack keeps a server transaction that was challenged or left unanswered, so how far memory grows, was not measured. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:447-458`, `:472-490`, `:1744-1764`. Fix: Count a request as in a dialog only when the endpoint has that dialog (Call-ID and both tags), else treat it as new; keep the To-tag test only as a fast path before the lookup.
- **SEC-COM-84 (Low; reported (likely)). A UDP phone's or PBX's Contact decides where the server sends its ACK, BYE and re-INVITE, a new TCP connection included.** Only the INVITE to a phone names the binding's flow. rsipstack keeps a dialog's later requests on the flow only for a TCP or TLS connection; for UDP it routes by the dialog's remote target, the Contact of the peer's INVITE or 200 (and a Record-Route, for a call this side placed), resolves a name and dials a new TCP connection to a `;transport=tcp` target it has none for. So a holder of a SIP device credential (any signed-in user gets one for their own account), or a PBX trunk's digest user, over UDP makes the server send an ACK, and at hangup or hold a BYE or re-INVITE, to a host and port of their choice: loopback, a private address or a third party. Nothing is read back, and `media_target_allowed` guards RTP only. The rsipstack path was read and the call was not run; that the server dialog's remote target comes from the INVITE's Contact (`get_or_create_server_invite`) was not read, nor whether a forged-source INVITE from a trunk's UDP address that is answered and never ACKed makes the server send a BYE to its Contact. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua_phones.rs:574-588`, `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:884`, `:920-925`, `:691-701`; rsipstack 0.8.5 `dialog/dialog.rs:1208-1215`, `:1262-1281`, `transaction/transaction.rs:426-438`, `:790-812`, `transport/transport_layer.rs:545-555`. Fix: For a device or trunk dialog send every in-dialog request to the flow (the binding's source for a phone, the trunk's resolved target for a trunk), with a target locator (`endpoint_inner.locator`) that answers the dialog's recorded flow and refuses anything else; refuse a Contact whose transport is not the flow's.
- **SEC-COM-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Stored captions are kept for ever and nothing reads them.** With `call_captions_store` set to `true`, each caption of every captioned call is written as a `CallTranscript` with the call and the leg and no `callRecordingId`. The only deletes of `CallTranscript` are by `callRecordingId` and by `voicemailId`, so a live caption's row is never deleted: not when the call ends, not by a job, not by anyone in the call. No service or screen reads the rows, so the people of the call cannot see or remove what was kept of them, while a reader of the entity (the database, entity REST for `ADMIN`) can; the entity's description says a transcript has its source's access policy. Whether a deployment's own job removes old `CallSession` rows with their transcripts was not confirmed. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/CallCaptionServices.xml:116-118`, `CallRecordings.js:738`, `framework/entity/CallEntities.xml:575-580`. Fix: Give stored captions a retention (delete with the call's rows after `call_recording_retention_days` or a setting of their own), and either a reader for those who were in the call or a line in the notice that the text is kept.
- **SEC-COM-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Each DTMF packet of a phone leg becomes a task and a service call, with no rate.** A telephone-event packet with a timestamp other than the last one is a new digit. The leg's worker reads every waiting datagram each tick and sends each digit to the engine over an unbounded channel, and the engine spawns a task and calls `note#PhoneLeg` for each. The far end of a phone leg whose media is accepted (a registered device, any signed-in user's own, or a trunk's media source) that sends event packets with changing timestamps as fast as it can makes that many service calls; a digit `1` during a recording that asks for acknowledgements also locks the call's row (`CallRecordings.js:471`). The path is confirmed in code; what bounds `call_service` (its pool) was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/dtmf.rs:176-186`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/leg.rs:255`, `:305-312`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/mod.rs:1575-1587`, `:1043-1057`. Fix: Take a new digit only when the last one ended (the end bit) or 40 ms passed, cap digits a leg a second, and read at most a fixed number of datagrams a tick.
- **SEC-COM-87 (Low; reported (to check)). Hostile Opus packets are decoded by a library that indexes without bounds checks, on threads that do not catch a panic.** Any leg of a call that publishes audio, a guest's included, while the call has a mixed recording, captions or a phone leg, and a phone or trunk that negotiated Opus, has its frames given to `opus_rs::OpusDecoder::decode` and `decode_fec` as they came, with no length check of ours. opus-rs 0.1.42 is a Rust port of libopus with no C code, but with 123 `get_unchecked`, 21 `get_unchecked_mut`, 29 `from_raw_parts(_mut)` and 14 `transmute` in `src`, the range decoder among them, and its own comments record panics fixed for a wrong frame size. A packet that reaches an unchecked index out of range is undefined behavior in the server process; one that panics ends the thread. The recorder catches a panic and fails the recording; the captions thread and the phone leg's worker do not, so captions stay "on" with nothing transcribing and a phone leg goes silent until the call ends. No faulty input was found and the library was not read: that any input reaches an unchecked index out of range is not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-rtc/src/audio/opus.rs:82-85`, `:103`, `crates/moqui-rtc/src/audio/jitter.rs:153-154`, `:186`, `crates/moqui-host/src/rtc/captions.rs:719-721`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/leg.rs:218-220`. Fix: Refuse a packet over 1,275 bytes a frame before decoding (`opus_packet_samples` already parses the TOC); wrap the captions thread and the leg worker in `catch_unwind` and end captions or the leg cleanly; fuzz `OpusDecoder::decode` and `decode_fec`, or put the decoders in a process of their own.
- **SEC-COM-88 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An SMS bridge whose `start` fails still sends, so an `http://` API address still gets the provider key (the fix of SEC-COM-10 does not hold on the send path).** SEC-COM-10 was fixed by refusing `http://` in `start`. The delivery worker calls `start` once a round and, when it fails, only sets the bridge's status to not connected; `deliver_head` then holds a row only for a disabled bridge or a missing adapter and never asks whether the bridge started. `deliver` and `send` have no scheme check of their own and the HTTP client takes `http`, so each text of a bridge saved with `apiBaseUrl` (or Sinch's `authBaseUrl`) as `http://` is posted there with `Authorization: Bearer <apiKey>` (Telnyx), Basic auth (Twilio) or the token call's `keyId:keySecret` (Sinch). `send#BridgeDirect` (the system's sign-in code) calls `adapter.deliver` without `start` at all, and `store#Bridge` checks only that `configJson` is JSON. The fix's test asserts `start` only (`crates/moqui-channels/src/tests.rs:1590`). Not run. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge.rs:1033-1035`, `:1159-1164`, `:1324`, `crates/moqui-channels/src/sms.rs:849-859`, `:1067-1070`, `providers/telnyx.rs:142-145`, `providers/twilio.rs:271-275`, `providers/sinch.rs:202-203`, `:361-365`, `chat_bridge_web.rs:733`. Fix: Check the scheme where the request is built (`Settings::of` answering an error, or `SmsAdapter::send` and `Sinch::token` refusing a non-https address unless `allow_internal`), and have `deliver_head` hold a row while the bridge's last `start` failed.
- **SEC-COM-89 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). On an edge node an anonymous caller spends a Twilio or Sinch bridge's webhook allowance, learns which bridge ids exist and fills its kept refusals.** An edge node (or an all-in-one node for a paired node's bridge) cannot check the signatures of `sms-twilio`, `sms-sinch`, `rcs-twilio` or `rcs-sinch`, so with `channels_webhooks_enabled` it queues any `POST /channels/{chatBridgeId}/webhook` that has the signature header, whatever its value, within 120 a minute a client address and 600 a minute a bridge. Five addresses use the whole bridge allowance and the provider's own requests are then answered 429, which Twilio does not send again, so texts that come in are lost; the client bucket is taken before the bridge's and a new key is refused when 10,000 buckets are live, so many source addresses refuse every new client. The answers tell a bridge that exists (200, 400, 429) from one that does not (401) and a disabled one (503) without a valid signature, and none counts for web protection. On the local node each forged item is kept as a `ChatBridgeWebhookRefused` row until 1,000 are kept for the bridge, after which a real request refused for a wrong secret is no longer kept for `replay#RefusedWebhooks`. Whether web protection's general request limits bound this first, and the edge queue's own limits, were not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge_edge.rs:384-442`, `:531-583`, `:705`, `:787-822`. Fix: Give each such bridge a random path secret that the edge node's replicated copy holds, checked in constant time before anything else and answered 401 without it; answer a disabled bridge as an unknown one until then; count the bridge's bucket before making a client bucket.
- **SEC-COM-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A STOP or START is remembered for 30 days only, so a signed Twilio text sent again later changes the opt-out again.** Twilio's signature has no time, so the replay cache (300 s) is the only replay check, and after it the ids dedupe. A text is deduped for good by its `ChatEventExternal` row, but its keyword is applied before that, and the keyword's once-only mark is a `ChatBridgeInbound` row with status done, which the worker deletes after 30 days. Someone who holds a whole signed webhook request of a Twilio bridge (the edge node, a reader of `EdgeQueueItem` or `ChatBridgeWebhookRefused.requestJson`, a TLS-terminating proxy's log) and posts it again opts a person back in with an old START after their later STOP, or out with an old STOP; through an edge node the time checked is the one the edge node wrote, so Sinch's and Telnyx's timestamps do not stop a copy the edge node itself sends again. The path is confirmed in code; that an attacker holds a signed request is the precondition, and whether room retention removes `ChatEventExternal` rows (an old text would then also appear as a new message) was not confirmed. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Sms.js:553-561`, `:644-646`, `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge.rs:1080-1087`, `crates/moqui-channels/src/providers/twilio.rs:327-347`, `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge_edge.rs:678`. Fix: Refuse a keyword whose message time (`originMillis`, signed by the provider) is older than the pair's `SmsOptOut.changedDate`; keep `signal:` marks as long as the opt-out row.
- **SEC-COM-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Inbound texts and MMS files have no cap: any outside sender makes rooms for every user of the number and stores files without a quota.** Only new group rooms are counted (5 a sender, 50 a number a day). A text from a number not seen before always makes a pair room, a member row for each user the number reaches, and a joined push unless the spam filter hid it; the filter hides a room and does not stop the room, the rows or the files. Each message's files (up to 10, each up to `maxInboundMediaBytes`, 5 MiB by default and at most 20 MiB) are fetched and written under `chat_attachment_root` with no uploader, so the per-user daily quota of SEC-COM-03 does not count them, and nothing else does. What the provider charges or filters upstream was not confirmed. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Sms.js:616-633`, `:657-658`, `:182-225`, `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge_web.rs:594-597`, `:627-645`, `ChatBridgeServices.xml:385`, `:482-490`, `crates/moqui-channels/src/sms.rs:64`, `:117-121`. Fix: A day's cap of new pair rooms for one of our numbers (as `GROUP_ROOMS_PER_NUMBER`), and a day's byte quota of inbound files for a bridge, past which a file is named as not received.
- **SEC-COM-92 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A chat push that waits is still sent after its message is deleted, the user leaves the room, or the account is disabled.** The worker writes the payload (room name, sender, snippet) into `PushOutbox` when it works the job, and a deleted message is left out only then. At the send it checks the time to live (4 weeks), the read marker, that the device is enabled and still the user's, and a native device's key; it does not check that the event is not redacted, that the user is still a member or may read the room, or that the account may sign in. A push held by do not disturb, or waiting on its push service, goes out hours later as it was written, and redaction, retention and member removal do not name `PushOutbox`. A browser subscription is removed at logout and by nothing else that was found, so a disabled account's browser keeps getting pushes of its rooms. That no check is made is confirmed in code; that it shows on a device is likely, and whether ending a session through `revoke#AccountAccess` reaches `forget_visit` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_push.rs:1166-1216`, `:877`, `:1024-1066`, `:1235-1237`, `web_push.rs:609-626`. Fix: In `deliver_one`, before the claim, read the event and the member row and drop the push (`PosDropped`) when the event is redacted, the member left (not a direct room) or the account may not sign in; delete a user's browser subscriptions when the account is disabled or its access is revoked.
- **SEC-COM-93 (Low; reported (likely)). Stream management keeps up to 1,000 unacknowledged stanzas a session with no byte bound.** For a signed-in XMPP user who turns stream management on and never acknowledges, every stanza the server sends is cloned into `unacked`, and only the count is bounded. A stanza the user can make the server send to them is large: a PEP notification of their own 262,144 character item, a MAM page of 65,536 character bodies. A thousand of them is on the order of 250 MB a session, and a user may hold 20 connections from one address and 10 detached sessions. `WRITE_STALL` closes a connection that takes nothing for 60 seconds but not one that reads slowly. The sizes the services really return were not confirmed; not run. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/sm.rs:30`, `:57-66`, `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/mod.rs:295-297`. Fix: Bound `unacked` by bytes as well (a few MiB), and mark the session `lost` past it.
- **SEC-COM-94 (Info; reported (to check)). Whoever names a dialog's Call-ID and tags ends the call, moves its media or asks a transfer, from any address.** A request that matches a dialog is handed to it before any source or digest check: rsipstack matches by Call-ID and the two tags and checks that the CSeq does not go back, and no code compares the source with the dialog's peer or the binding's flow. A BYE ends the leg, a re-INVITE with an offer is answered from the leg's local media and moves its RTP to the new address (with new SRTP keys when the offer has them), and a REFER is passed to the host as the leg's transfer request. The ids are not guessable (this side's Call-ID is 20 random characters), so this needs a network peer that sees the call's signaling over UDP or TCP; over TLS the ids are not seen. This is how SIP dialogs work everywhere; what else `InviteDialog::handle` checks was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:1744-1749`, `:1634-1651`, `:1666-1686`, `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/mod.rs:1309-1315`, `crates/moqui-sip/src/media.rs:221-224`. Fix: For a device's dialog take in-dialog requests only over the binding's flow; for a trunk taken by address, only from the trunk's addresses.
- **SEC-COM-95 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `pin` is not a secret parameter name, and `setPin` takes it from a query.** The one rule for what request details, the HTTP log's query and telemetry leave out (`secret_parameter`) lists `password`, `secret`, `token`, `credential` and a few names, not `pin`, `ha1` or `response`. The client posts the voicemail PIN in a body, where only `ec.web.getRequestDetails` would list it, but the `setPin` transition declares no `method`, so a `GET .../setPin?pin=1234` works too and its query is kept in the HTTP log. What is exposed is the user's own PIN to readers of that log. `ha1` and `response` are parameters of in-server services only, and no place that records service parameters was found (whether a span or an `ArtifactHit` records them was not confirmed). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:4-11`, `:14-22`, `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Settings/Phone.xml:51`. Fix: Add `pin` and `ha1` to the names, and `method="post"` to `setPin`.
- **SEC-COM-96 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The Twilio API credentials go to whatever host a signed webhook's `MediaUrl` names, and a next-page address is checked by prefix only.** Every inbound file fetch of a Twilio bridge carries `Authorization: Basic` with the API key or the account's Auth Token to the URL in `MediaUrl{N}`, whatever its host. The paging of `ParticipantConversations` follows `next_page_url` when it starts with `conversationsBaseUrl`, which has no trailing `/`, so `https://conversations.twilio.com.example.org/...` passes. Only Twilio itself (a signed webhook, or its API's answer) reaches this while the signature holds; the client drops the header on a redirect to another host and the address guard refuses internal hosts. Where: `crates/moqui-channels/src/sms.rs:1245-1253`, `providers/twilio.rs:445-447`, `:686-691`, `providers/twilio/group.rs:546-550`. Fix: Add the header only when the URL's origin is `apiBaseUrl`'s or `mediaContentBaseUrl`'s; compare the next page's origin, not a string prefix.
- **SEC-COM-97 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `store#EdgeMedia` does not check that the edge queue calls it, and reads any `contentLocation`.** `apply#EdgeWebhook` refuses any caller but the queue (`called_by_queue`). Its sibling on the edge node does not: it takes `contentLocation` (any resource location), reads it, and serves the bytes at `/channels/{chatBridgeId}/media/{token}` for a `tokenHash` and `chatBridgeId` the caller names. No route to it from outside was found: only the queue kind `chatMedia` names it, so the caller would be one inside the server (a service runner of an administrator on an edge node). That the queue always sets `contentLocation` to its own received copy was seen by grep only. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge_edge.rs:1276-1305`, against `:845-854`. Fix: The same `called_by_queue` check, and refuse a `contentLocation` outside the queue's received root.
- **SEC-COM-98 (Info; reported (likely)). A failed delivery's error shows a room's readers the provider's API address and account id.** A send that could not reach the provider keeps the client's error as `lastErrorText`, and `get#EventDeliveries` answers it as `error` to any user who can read the bridged room (`get#Timeline` gives each message's `delivery`). The client's message is `HTTP POST <scheme://host/path> failed`, and the path of a Twilio or Sinch send holds the account SID or project id, with the bridge's `apiBaseUrl`. What `Chat.qvue` shows of `error` was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-channels/src/sms.rs:771-779`, `ChatBridgeServices.xml:715-717`, `crates/moqui-lib/src/http.rs:407`, `:760-766`. Fix: Answer the reason code to readers and the full text only to `ChatBridgeAdmin` (`find#Bridges`).
- **SEC-COM-99 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `open#PairRoom` has no limit: a user with one text number makes a room, with every user of the number in it, for each number typed.** Each call with a new number by a signed-in Comms user granted a number with messaging makes a room and member rows for all the number's users and pushes "joined" to them. It also makes the number one "of a phone room the user is in", which is all `find#PhoneNames` asks before it runs the apps' `call-context` providers for it (600 an hour), so "cannot read an app's contacts by guessing numbers" holds only as far as the providers' own answer for that user. No count or rate was found in `openPairRoom`, `ensurePairRoom` or the two transitions. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Sms.js:300-332`, `:775`, `basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Chat.xml:477-483` (`textRoom`), `Calls/History.xml:97-102` (`textNumber`). Fix: A per-user hourly cap of new phone rooms; make the room's other members join at the first text sent or received, not when it is opened.
- **SEC-COM-100 (Info; reported (to check)). Two accounts whose usernames differ only by case, or by spaces at the ends, have one JID.** A session's own address is the lower-case, trimmed username, and `get#Account` finds the exact name first, then any name equal without case. With accounts `Bob` and `bob`, both sessions send as `bob@domain`, and a stanza to `bob@domain` goes to `bob`; the stored sender is the right user, what an XMPP client shows is not. This needs a deployment that allows two usernames that differ by case; whether `create#UserAccount` compares usernames without case was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/jids.rs` (`escape_node`), `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/mod.rs:273`, `XmppServices.xml:43-48`. Fix: Refuse a sign-in when more than one account matches the name without case, or enforce it at account creation.
- **SEC-COM-101 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A FAST token is taken by either mechanism, and the count that stops a replay is not covered by the proof.** `issue#FastToken` stores the mechanism the client asked for; `get#FastToken` and `use#FastToken` never read it, so a token asked for as `HT-SHA-256-EXPR` also signs in as `HT-SHA-256-NONE`. For `NONE` the proof is `HMAC(token, "Initiator")` with nothing else in it, the same for every sign-in, and `count` is an attribute beside it, so a captured proof signs in again with a larger count. This is the mechanism as XEP-0484 has it, and reading the exchange needs the TLS session, so it is written down, not a fault of this code. Where: `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/auth.rs:356-392`, `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/fast.rs` (`mac`), `XmppServices.xml:104-108`, `:146-153`. Fix: Take a token only by the mechanism it was issued for; offer `HT-SHA-256-NONE` only where the connection cannot bind.

## Cluster and edge node

- **SEC-CLU-01 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The address an edge node names passes the member's loopback rules.** The member makes the named address the request's TCP peer. `/health` and `/metrics` answer a loopback peer, git and DAV take credentials from a loopback peer as if over TLS, and an SSH session from a loopback peer is `Place::Local`, which skips `terminal` `remote` and the recent sign-in for a command or host session. Only the browser terminal's rule excludes a request that came through the edge (`self.via == RequestVia::Direct`); the others do not look at `RequestVia`. The same address feeds web protection's exempt rules and limits. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/down.rs:223`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:957`-`961`, `:977`-`985`, `:1679`; `crates/moqui-host/src/git_http.rs:296`; `crates/moqui-host/src/dav_http.rs:74`; `crates/moqui-host/src/term/access.rs:176`-`177`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:800`. Fix: Keep `RequestVia::Edge` (and a mark on tunneled SSH and XMPP connections) where the peer is checked, and treat a peer named by a link as never loopback in `status_allowed`, `secure()` and `Facts::place`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit acf76c086): one rule, `http::local_peer(peer, via)`: a loopback peer is this machine only when the request did not come through an edge link; `/health` and `/metrics`, git's and DAV's credentials over plain HTTP and the terminal's place use it, and an SSH connection an edge tunnel hands over is marked (`Connection.via_edge`) and placed remote (`Facts::over_ssh`). Tests `http_tests::status_routes_allow_loopback_only`, `term::access_tests::an_ssh_tunnel_from_the_edge_naming_loopback_is_remote`. Web protection still takes the address the edge node names, as it must to limit the edge's clients.
- **SEC-CLU-02 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A paired node can fill the other end's database through Replicate and PutContent.** `apply_batch_rows` writes one `EdgeReplicaValue` row per change with the set and entity names the sender gives; it checks neither against the edge node's `<replicate>` conf, nor the number of changes, nor a total per pairing. `PutContent` stores up to 64 MiB a call under a new name each time and keeps it until a delivery is answered or the 14-day retention pass; nothing counts it against the pairing's queue cap. On an edge node that serves several pairings one pairing can exhaust the shared database; a compromised edge node can do the same to its local node's content store. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:2910`-`2923`, `:2893`; `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/replica.rs:716`-`812`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/queue.rs:163`-`218`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/dial.rs:483`. Fix: On the receiver refuse a set or entity the conf does not replicate, cap the changes of a batch and the rows and bytes of a pairing, and count received content against the pairing's queue cap. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 9dc9b1b4c): the edge node refuses a `Replicate` batch of more than 500 records, of a set its own `<replicate>` conf does not name, or with an entity that set does not have (`edge::replica::batch_refused`; test `the_edge_node_takes_only_the_sets_and_entities_of_its_conf_in_batches_a_sender_makes`). Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commits 9189aec42 and aae2d54b9), the owner's call: `<edge replica-max-rows>` of 1,000,000 by default and received content counted against the pairing's queue cap; at a cap the pairing pauses with an administrator alert and nothing is dropped. An edge node keeps at most `replica-max-rows` replicated records per pairing (all its sets; or the pairing's `EdgeClient.replicaMaxRows`), and the content a pairing's link sends before its deliveries (`PutContent`, under `CONTENT_IN<node>/`) counts against `queue-max-megabytes` or the pairing's `EdgeClient.queueMaxMegabytes` (`edge::caps::admit_batch`, `admit_content`; near the cap an update of a kept record adds none and a delete makes room). At either cap the pairing pauses (`EdgeClient.capPausedDate`, `capPausedReason`): its `Replicate` and `PutContent` are refused as unavailable marked `moqui-edge-paused`, which the replication sender retries with its backoff and a queue delivery takes as an uncounted pause, the edge node's ADMIN group gets one `EdgePairingPaused` notification and an error is logged; it stays paused until an administrator presses Resume on System › Edge Clients (`resume#EdgeClient`) or saves new caps (`update#EdgeClient`, which now takes `replicaMaxRows`). A local node refuses content its edge node sends past its own queue cap, logged, with no pause of its own. Tests `edge::caps::tests::a_pairing_at_its_record_cap_pauses_with_a_notice_until_an_administrator_resumes_it`, `content_kept_for_a_pairing_counts_against_its_queue_cap`, `edge_link::tests::a_paused_pairing_s_refusal_is_a_pause_and_not_a_counted_failure`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (what was outside the call; commit 6c112f7e4), the owner's call: a notice on the local node, with no stored pause. The content files kept for a pairing count against the new `<edge queue-max-content-files>` (10,000, empty ones too; 0 for none; `EdgeSettings.max_content_files`), at which an edge node pauses the pairing as at its byte cap (`caps::admit_content`). A local node that starts refusing its edge node's content tells its administrators once (`EdgeContentRefused`, `caps::local_refusing`) and once more when it takes content again; the edge node keeps the content and tries again. Tests `edge::caps::tests::content_files_kept_for_a_pairing_count_against_their_own_cap`, `content_kept_for_a_pairing_counts_against_its_queue_cap` (the local notices).
- **SEC-CLU-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The cluster login is accepted on the SSH port whatever the transport.** The docs and the map confine the cluster to port 8444 "between the members only". The SSH server also accepts `moqui-cluster` with the key derived from `cluster_tls_key` and applies its frames on every topic, with no check that `cluster_transport` is `ssh`, that any member advertises `ssh://`, or that the source is a member. A leaked cluster key is then usable from the internet wherever SSH is reachable, including `component-deploy`, `session-end` and `web-ban` messages. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:939`-`952`, `:1099`-`1112`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/cluster.rs:52`-`61`. Fix: Accept the `moqui-cluster` login only when this member's transport is `ssh` or a live member row advertises `ssh://`, and only from the members' addresses; refuse it on a connection that came down an edge tunnel. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit acf76c086): the SSH server takes the `moqui-cluster` login only when this member takes the SSH channel (`cluster_transport=ssh` or an `ssh://` `cluster_base_url`), not through an edge tunnel, and from an address a member's `clusterBaseUrl` resolves to (`ssh::cluster::login_refused`, `cluster_edge::member_addresses`); a refusal is logged. Test `ssh::cluster::tests::the_cluster_login_is_taken_only_on_the_ssh_transport_from_a_member`.
- **SEC-CLU-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A `ClusterMember` row decides where cluster messages go and, for `ssh://`, which host key is trusted.** The sender posts every topic to each live row's `clusterBaseUrl` without comparing the row's `certFingerprint` or `sessionKeyFingerprint` to its own. For an `https://` row the pinned certificate still protects the message. For an `ssh://` row the host key it pins is `sshHostKeyFingerprint` of that same row, and the client's key proof gives nothing to the server, so a row naming another host and that host's key receives every cluster message (notification bodies, co-editing updates, call signaling). A `pg-notify:` row makes members send every message as `NOTIFY moqui_cluster`, readable by any session of the database. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/cluster/mod.rs:1327`-`1350`, `:1476`-`1483`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/cluster.rs:169`-`178`; `crates/moqui-host/src/cluster_notify.rs:118`, `:185`. Fix: Send only to rows whose `certFingerprint` equals this member's, and for `ssh://` peers authenticate the host with something derived from the cluster key rather than a value in the row. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commits 5040094ff, 104ca858d): the fan-out and `send_to` go only to a member row whose `sessionKeyFingerprint` and `certFingerprint` are this member's (`cluster::same_material`); a row with others is left out with a warning. Test `cluster::tests::messages_go_only_to_a_row_of_this_members_material`. This keeps out a row of another cluster's material, not a writer of the table, who can copy the fingerprints. Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 17b248269, its tests moved first in b54765bcf), the owner's call: the peer proves the cluster key by signing a challenge inside the `moqui-cluster` SSH channel, and the host key is unchanged. The proof runs both ways, since the connecting member's login already signs with a key derived from the cluster key and what the finding needs is the sender checking the host: each `cluster <topic>` channel starts with the server's `challenge <nonce>`, the member's `proof <nonce> <signature>` and, once that holds, the server's `proof <signature>` (else `403` and the channel closes), each signed with an Ed25519 key derived from `cluster_tls_key` under its own label over its role, the server's host key fingerprint and both nonces, so a proof cannot be relayed to another host or used again (`ssh::cluster::prove_server`, `prove_client`, `channel_proof_key`). The server reads no frame before the member's proof, and the sender sends none to a host that does not prove the key, each refusal logged; a member of an older version is refused both ways. Tests `ssh::cluster::tests::a_cluster_channel_proves_the_cluster_key_both_ways_before_any_frame`, `a_cluster_peer_that_sends_no_proof_is_refused`, it `ssh::sftp_and_the_admin_shell_run_on_the_built_in_server`. A `pg-notify:` row's messages are readable by any session of the database, by the notify transport's design.
- **SEC-CLU-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The PASS authority's wrong-secret limit is keyed by the exact TCP peer.** After 10 wrong secrets from one address every call from that address is refused for the rest of the 10 minutes, right secret or not, before the secret is checked. The address is the connection's peer, so behind a load balancer, a NAT or a front node all PASS servers share one key and one caller locks the others out. The key is the full address, so an IPv6 caller with a /64 never reaches the limit, and the table has no size cap (pairing counts sources by /64 and caps the table at 10,000). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/pass_authority/mod.rs:131`-`154`, `:189`-`213`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http/serve.rs:434`-`438`. Fix: Key by `source_key` of the believed client address, cap the table, and do not refuse a call whose secret is right. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit d40982257, with SEC-PSA-04): wrong secrets count by source (the peer, or the client a believed `client-ip-header` names; `source_guard::source_key`, an IPv6 /64 as one) in a table of at most 10,000 sources, and a source past its 10 in 10 minutes (or a new one while the table is full) is no longer refused unchecked: its calls are checked one at a time across all such sources, waiting at most 5 seconds before `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, so a right secret from the same address gets in (`Authority::admit`). Tests `pass_authority::tests::wrong_secrets_from_one_source_hold_it_until_the_window_ends`, `the_wrong_secret_table_is_bounded`, `a_right_secret_gets_in_from_a_source_past_its_wrong_secrets`.
- **SEC-CLU-06 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). A notification's `link` is followed with no scheme check.** `isExternalLink` treats any `scheme:` text as external, and `openLink` passes it to `window.open(text, "_blank", "noopener")`; a desktop notification's click calls `window.open(jsonObj.link)` with no `noopener`. A `javascript:` link is not refused in either place. Where: `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/MoquiLib.js:736`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootVue.qvt.js:349`-`364`, `:441`, `:9465`. Fix: Follow only `http:`, `https:` and paths; drop any other scheme in `openLink` and in the desktop notification's click. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit b547c4559): `moqui.followableLink` (MoquiLib.js) answers a path, a relative link or an `http`, `https`, `mailto` or `tel` URL, after taking out the tabs and line breaks and the leading control characters a browser ignores, and null for any other scheme (`javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`); `moqui.openLink` and the desktop notification's click follow only what it answers, the click with `noopener`. The desktop app already followed a notification's link only on the node's origin (`bridge.rs` `node_link`). Test `WebrootVue.qvt.page.js` "a message info with a link notifies with a button that follows it" (the unsafe-link cases).
- **SEC-CLU-07 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A cluster message makes a member load a commit the sender names.** A `component-deploy` body with `action` `update`, the name of a component kept in a database repository and a commit id in `to` makes each member write that commit's tree and reload the component. With `require-passed-run` false, the default, no attestation is asked, so a commit pushed to that repository but never deployed is loaded. This is the one topic that leads to code being run. Where: `crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/git.rs:1023`-`1066`, `:798`-`801`. Fix: On a member apply only the commit the shared `DeployedComponent` row names, not the one in the message.
- **SEC-CLU-81 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A node with only the pairing's shared key links as a listed member that has no key pinned, with no notice.** With members linked, a new member is admitted when a linked member's list has its id as live and its key is not another one (`other_key` is `pinned == Some(false)`). An id with no pin passes, so the Hello's own key is pinned for it and the link registers as an ordinary member, with no takeover, notice or hold; it then gets its share of the pairing's work (forwarded requests of terminate hosts with their cookies, tunnels, queue items, delegated calls), and the real member's later link is refused as another key. A member has no pin from when it joins the cluster's list until its first Hello. With none linked, a node naming the last member's id, or an id live in the kept list, with no pin is admitted with no notice too (`pool.rs:219`-`227`; `forgotten` is read only on that branch). The admission path is confirmed in code; not confirmed: that a live listed member that never linked exists in a real deployment (`hook.rs` and the dialer were not read), how long the window is at a member's start, and how an outsider learns an instance id. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/pool.rs:198`, `:209`-`217`, `:313`-`316`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:2438`, `:2477`-`2485`, `:2529`-`2531` (reports A12, A13). Fix: Carry each member's own key fingerprint in the member list (each member can publish it on its `ClusterMember` row) and admit a listed id only with that key; until then tell the administrators of a first pin made while other members are linked.
- **SEC-CLU-82 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A valid pairing code pairs any revoked pairing id with a new key, which then has what was kept for that id.** A code is not bound to an id, and `pair_client` refuses only an id that is active with another key. For a revoked id it sets the new key and `EdgeClientActive` on the same row; the row's `setupJson` (PASS sites, chat bridges, XMPP and SIP domain) is not cleared, and the id's `EdgeHost` and `EdgePort` rows, replicated copies and queued items are keyed by the id and kept for a re-pair. So the holder of a code made for node B who names revoked node A gets A's host names, ports and PASS sites, and any item still queued for A. The pairing is confirmed in code; what is inherited is likely (the kept rows were read in comments and `pair_client`, not row by row), and that a revoked id's queued items are still delivered after the re-pair was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/store.rs:156`-`193`, `:373`-`388`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:2705`-`2721`; `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/claims.rs:525`-`527`. Fix: Let `create#PairingCode` name the id the code is for (required for a re-pair of an existing id), and on a re-pair with another key clear `setupJson` and remove or hold the id's hosts, ports, copies and queued items until the operator confirms.
- **SEC-CLU-83 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `PutContent` holds up to 64 MiB a call in memory before the pairing's cap is checked, on a connection with no stream limit of its own.** The first chunk's `total_size` reserves that much at once (`content.reserve(total)`), the chunks are appended to one `Vec`, and `admit_content` is asked only when the stream ends; there is no deadline on the stream. The up connection's `Limits` have `streams: None`, and the down connection takes up to `down::MAX_STREAMS` (1024), so many calls held open each hold up to 64 MiB: a paired node against the edge node, a compromised edge node against the local node. SEC-CLU-02 capped what is kept in the database, not this. The number of calls at once is likely only: hyper's default of concurrent streams was not read, and `MAX_CONTENT_BYTES` was taken from the map. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/queue.rs:208`-`256`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:1623`-`1627`, `:1657`, `:2883`-`2894`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/dial.rs:483`-`496`. Fix: Take a per-pairing permit for a content call (a few at once), check the cap against `total_size` before reserving, and bound the call's time.
- **SEC-CLU-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An up connection with any certificate costs a database transaction and an info log line, with no rate limit.** An anonymous client that offers `moqui-edge/1` with any self-signed client certificate and the role byte makes the edge node run `store::link_up` on a request worker before it is refused: a transaction that reads `EdgeClient` for update by the fingerprint, then a log line at info. What bounds it is the listener's permits, web protection's open connections per client when web protection is on, and the handshake's time; nothing counts attempts by source as `pair_allowed` does for Pair, and web protection's request limits never see a link connection. An unknown key matches no row, so nothing is locked or written. Not confirmed: the request pool's queue bound. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:1567`-`1584`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/store.rs:79`-`94`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:727`; `crates/moqui-host/src/guard.rs:922`-`954`. Fix: Count refused up connections by source in the same table as Pair's (or a second one) and close before the lookup past a limit; log at debug after the first few.
- **SEC-CLU-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The PASS client id of the edge node's offer is written unseen at Apply, over an existing client of that id.** `apply#EdgeSetup` takes the client id from the offer, checks its syntax only (1 to 63 letters, digits, `_`, `-`, no `.`) and writes `pass_edge_client`, `pass_client_<id>_sites` and `pass_client_<id>_callback` to `.env` with no look at an existing client. A direct client of that id has its sites and callback replaced by the wizard's choices, and the link, which sends no secret, is then that client. The id is not shown before Apply; this code's own edge node always offers `edge`, so another id means an edge node that is not this code. The write is confirmed in code; the effect on that client's sessions is likely (the authority's scripts were read by a helper pass only). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/setup.rs:288`-`293`, `:476`-`482`, `:511`-`527`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/edge.rs:1619`-`1621`, `:1668`; `crates/moqui-core/src/pass_authority.rs:351`-`356`. Fix: Refuse at Apply an offered id for which `pass_client_<id>_secret` or `_sites` exists and that is not already `pass_edge_client`; show the id on the review step.
- **SEC-CLU-86 (Low; reported (likely)). The voicemail and recording queue handlers do not refuse the wrong role.** `receive#EdgeVoicemail` and `receive#EdgeRecording` check only that the user is `_edge_`, which is true on both ends of a link, so a paired local node's `Link.Deliver` of such an item runs them on an all-in-one node. For `voicemail` the box is the one this node's own `PhoneNumber` routes to, so an item naming one of the server's numbers is stored in that user's box, with a sender number and name of the paired node's choice; a caller of that number can leave the same. For a recording that exists the media node must be an edge-linked one, which refuses the server's own; a recording id that does not exist is left to `store#RecordingSegment`, which was not read, nor were `boxOf`, `store` and the owner scope of a queued item on an all-in-one node. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Voicemail.js:319`-`325`, `:343`-`366`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/CallRecordings.js:615`-`635`; `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/queue.rs:1060`-`1069`. Fix: Refuse both handlers on a node that takes links, as `note#EdgeMailStatus` does, or give each queue kind a direction in the conf and check it in `queue::apply`.
- **SEC-CLU-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A notification's title is drawn on a TUI user's terminal with its control characters.** The TUI link makes a `Push` from the notification body with no cleaning (`title: json_field(body, "title")`), the session sets the title as its message line, and the painter writes one cell for every character whose width is not zero, which a control character's is not; every other text the TUI draws goes through `clean_text`. Whoever sets the title of a notification routed to a user with a TUI open reaches it: a cluster peer on `notification-message`, and any sender whose title holds outside text, such as `record#AuthFailure`'s `sourceAddress` from an edge node. The path to the terminal write is confirmed in code; the effect on a real terminal is not (nothing was run): the terminal library moves the cursor after a zero-width character, so a whole escape sequence is probably not delivered while single-byte controls are. The remote TUI's path and each sender's cleaning were not read. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/tui.rs:667`-`673`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/session/shell.rs:810`-`814`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/draw.rs:451`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/paint.rs:195`-`213`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/layout.rs:1944`-`1953`. Fix: `clean_text` the title (and topic) in `Session::on_push`, or in `set_message` for every caller.
- **SEC-CLU-88 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The gRPC listener has no idle, keepalive or request timeout.** The server is built with tonic's defaults: no concurrency limit, request timeout, TCP or HTTP/2 keepalive or `max_concurrent_streams` of its own. A plain h2c connection is handed to tonic at once with no first-byte or preface timeout, and a TLS one has 10 seconds for its handshake and nothing after, so a connection that sends nothing, or opens streams and stops, keeps its web protection slot until the client closes it. This is before any credential, on the OTLP receiver's and the PASS authority's port. Not confirmed: web protection's per-client connection limit and whether it is on by default, and hyper's defaults at the locked version (the copy read was 1.5.2, `Cargo.lock` names 1.12.0). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/grpc_listen.rs:230`-`233`, `:264`-`267`. Fix: Set `http2_keepalive_interval` and `http2_keepalive_timeout`, `max_concurrent_streams` (64, as the cluster port), `timeout`, close a connection with no stream for a minute as `cluster_edge/listen.rs:324`-`343` does, and give h2c a first-byte timeout.
- **SEC-CLU-89 (Low; reported (likely)). A `ClusterMember` row is not among the entities the generic tools refuse, and one with a future heartbeat is live for ever.** `clustermember` is in none of the lists of `sensitive.rs`, though `instancedrain` of the same package is. Liveness is `now - heartbeat <= window`, so a heartbeat in the future is always live, and the cleanup deletes only rows whose heartbeat is before the cutoff. Such a row with another fingerprint makes every member refuse to start; with the fingerprints copied it is a live member wherever the code asks: a destination of cluster messages (a `pg-notify:` row makes the members send every message by `NOTIFY`), an address with member slots on the cluster port and allowed the SSH cluster login, a live member in the list a local node sends its edge node, and on an edge cluster an `edgeLinkUrl` every local node dials. Not confirmed: that Tools › Data or the entity REST accepts a write to this entity (`use="nontransactional"`), and who holds that authorization besides `ADMIN`; a session on the database can write it in any case. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sensitive.rs:47`-`119`; `crates/moqui-core/src/cluster/mod.rs:938`-`943`, `:945`-`965`, `:1591`-`1593`. Fix: Add `clustermember` (and `clustermessage`) to `SERVICE_WRITTEN_ENTITY_KEYS`; treat a heartbeat more than a window ahead of the database clock as not live.
- **SEC-CLU-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). On a dedicated edge node every pairing's greetings and prompts are kept at one location, with no cap and no removal.** A `callAudio` item names a location under the greeting or prompt root and the edge node stores its content at `kept_audio_location`: a path of the pairing on an all-in-one node, the location itself on a dedicated edge node, the same for every pairing. The SIP engine reads that location first for any pairing's call, so pairing B can store audio at a location pairing A's box or menu uses, which A's callers then hear; B must know or guess the name, which holds a box id or folder and a millisecond stamp. The handler has no size check and no pass that removes kept audio was found, so a paired node can also fill the edge node's store past SEC-CLU-02's caps. The shared location and the write are confirmed in code; the missing cap is likely. Not confirmed: that an edge node with several pairings runs as `front_edge` or `edge` in practice, and the sender side's limits on `callAudio` items. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/media.rs:357`-`362`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call_media.rs:262`-`314`; `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/voicemail.rs:584`-`585`; `crates/moqui-host/src/sip/edge.rs:496`-`507`. Fix: Keep a pairing's audio under `dbresource://moqui/edge-node/<node>/` on every node that takes links; cap an item's size (the 8 MiB of `MAX_CALL_AUDIO`) and the pairing's kept audio, and remove a file no fallback row names.
- **SEC-CLU-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). As the edge node's delegate user, `record#AuthFailure` blocks any source address and `note#TrunkStatus` marks any trunk, each with an alert.** Every other delegated SIP service checks `edgeAllowed` or `isEdgeCaller`; these two do not. `record#AuthFailure` with `blocked: true` creates or updates a `VoiceAuthFailure` row for any `sourceAddress` (up to 200 characters, one row a value, no limit on rows), blocks it for `authBlockSeconds` and alerts the voice administrators; `get#EngineConfig` hands the blocked list to the local cluster's own SIP engines. `note#TrunkStatus` sets any trunk's status and `lastErrorText` (4,000 characters) and alerts on each change to failed. The service bodies are confirmed in code; the effect on the local cluster's own SIP engines is likely: how `sip/mod.rs` applies the list (whether a trunk's `inboundAddresses` is exempt), and whether the local cluster runs engines of its own, was not read. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Voice.js:900`-`902`, `:924`-`937`, `:944`-`976`. Fix: As `_edge_`, keep the edge node's blocks apart from the local engines' (a node column, read only by that node), cap the rows and the alerts per hour, and take a trunk status only for a trunk the edge node's engine holds and only as that node.
- **SEC-CLU-92 (Low; reported (likely)). A pairing's replicated records are capped by count, not by size, though SEC-CLU-02's fix names bytes.** SEC-CLU-02's fix line says "cap the changes of a batch and the rows and bytes of a pairing"; only the rows are capped. `admit_batch` counts the pairing's `EdgeReplicaValue` rows against `replica-max-rows` (1,000,000) and `batch_refused` checks the number of records, the set and the entity; neither looks at the size of a record's `fieldsJson` or at its field names, so a record may be as large as the 16 MiB message allows. The count is also read before the batch is applied, outside its transaction, so batches sent at once each pass against the same count. Not confirmed: `apply_batch_rows` past `replica.rs:752` was not read, so a check there would have been missed, nor the database's own column limit for `fieldsJson`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/caps.rs:119`-`150`; `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/replica.rs:707`-`722`. Fix: Refuse a record over a size (64 KiB is far above any of the replicated fields), and keep only the fields the set's conf names.
- **SEC-CLU-93 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). An approval of a held node names its instance id, not its key.** With `holdNewMember=Y` a new node is held and shown by id and address, and `approve#EdgeMember` stores the id as `approvedMemberId`. The next link of the pairing's key that names that id, with any member key, is the takeover. The record of the held node (`RefusedMember`) does not keep the key it showed. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/edge.rs:813`-`826`; `crates/moqui-core/src/edge/pool.rs:231`-`236`; `crates/moqui-host/src/edge_link/accept.rs:2514`-`2526`. Fix: Keep the held node's member key fingerprint with the refusal, show it, and approve the id with that fingerprint.
- **SEC-CLU-94 (Info; reported (likely)). The pairing code is kept in clear in the session row while the wizard is open.** Session attributes are written to the session row as JSON, not encrypted (`HttpSession.attributes`). On the edge node the code is set and removed at the next render, so it is in the row only if a flush falls between. On the local node it is removed only when pairing succeeds, so after a failed or abandoned pairing it stays until the session ends; a reader of that table or of a backup within the code's life (60 minutes by default, a day at most, one use) has it. The session flush was read by a helper pass. Where: `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/EdgeNode/Pair/EdgeUrl.xml:35`, `Connect.xml:33`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/EdgeClients/Clients.xml:42`, `:129`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/session.rs:2729`-`2730`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/ServerEntities.xml:81`. Fix: Keep the code out of session attributes (pass it through the wizard's own posts), or remove it when a pairing fails too.
- **SEC-CLU-95 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The edge node acts as a phone's user with no fresh digest answer through `refer#Call` and a device leg's `answered`.** When a call rings a user whose phone has a binding at the edge node, the edge node's own `note#PhoneLeg` with `answered` makes the `CltSipDevice` leg the user's answered leg, and `refer#Call` on it signs in as that user (`internalLoginUser`) and blind-transfers the call to any number the user's own outbound permissions allow. The 60 second digest window (`edgeDigestFresh`) guards `store#SipRegistration` and `receive#DeviceCall` only, and the edge node can start the ringing call itself. What it gains over what the map grants is a call placed in a user's name and against that user's limits; the map's sentence that it cannot place a call as a user without a fresh answer does not hold. Not confirmed: `transfer` and `dialNumber` past `resolveTarget`, and that the signed-in user does not stay on the worker after the delegated call. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/CallTransfers.js:428`-`443`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Calls.js:2189`, `:2299`-`2330`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/SipDevices.js:304`, `:403`-`447`. Fix: Correct the map's sentence; if the statement is meant, ask in `refer` for a digest answer of that phone within the call's life, as `receive#DeviceCall` does.

## Data layer and stored secrets

- **SEC-DAT-05 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). `export#EntityDataSnapshot` writes to a path built from an unchecked `baseFilename`.** `baseFilename` goes into `runtime/db/snapshot/<baseFilename>.zip` with no path check, and the writer creates missing parent directories. A value with `../` writes a new `.zip` of every entity, decrypted secrets and password hashes included, anywhere the server can write, such as a directory a web route serves. The import, download and delete paths check `isSafeSinglePathSegment`; the export does not. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/EntityServices.xml:252`, `:276-284`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Snapshots.xml:19-30`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/data_writer.rs:354`, `:591-594`. Fix: Check `WebUtilities.isSafeSinglePathSegment(baseFilename)` in the service. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-TLS-07: a snapshot's `baseFilename` must be one safe path segment.
- **SEC-DAT-06 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). Files with secrets are created with the process umask.** `runtime/db/embedded/pgpass` holds the embedded superuser password and is `-rw-rw-r--` in this runtime. Snapshot and export files hold decrypted fields and are created with `File::create_new`. `moqui.log` is `-rw-rw-r--`. A `pg_dump` file is `0600` only after the dump ends. On Unix the socket is inside the `0700` data directory, so the password alone does not open the database; on Windows the cluster listens on `127.0.0.1` (`embedded.rs:837`). Where: `postgresql_embedded-0.22.0/src/postgresql.rs:256` (reached from `crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:769`); `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/data_writer.rs:594`; `crates/moqui-host/src/logging.rs:258`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/backup.rs:193-197`. Fix: Create `pgpass` with mode `0600` before `setup`, open export, snapshot, dump and log files with mode `0600`, and create their directories `0700`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit fd405248d): `moqui_lib::private_fs` (files 0600, the directories it makes 0700): the embedded `pgpass` is written 0600 before the setup or made 0600 when it is there, data exports and snapshots are 0600 with new directories 0700, log files 0600 and a new log directory 0700, and a `pg_dump` writes into a partial file made 0600 before it runs. Tests `private_fs::tests::files_and_directories_are_this_user_s_alone`, `data_writer::tests::an_export_file_and_its_new_directories_are_this_user_s_alone`, `backup::tests::a_dump_s_partial_file_is_this_user_s_alone_before_pg_dump_writes_it`, `embedded::cluster::tests::the_password_file_is_this_user_s_alone`. A log shipper that reads `runtime/log` as another OS user now needs a group or an ACL.
- **SEC-DAT-07 (Low; likely (second check); fixed). The fallback socket directory under the temp directory is accepted when it already exists.** The socket directory is `/tmp/moqui-pg-<FNV-1a of the data directory path>`, a name anyone can compute. `DirBuilder::recursive(true).mode(0o700).create` returns success for an existing directory and checks neither owner nor mode. A user who made that directory first can put their own listener on `.s.PGSQL.5432`; the client then sends the superuser password in clear, because the cluster's method is `password` and the client answers a cleartext request. Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:590-603`, `:906-927`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/pgwire.rs:577-581`. Fix: After creating the directory, check that its owner is the current user and its mode is `0700`, and fail otherwise; or put the socket under a `0700` directory in the runtime. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-OUT-06 (commit fd405248d): the socket directory is refused unless this user owns it with mode `0700` (`embedded.rs` `socket_dir_private`), so a directory another user made first stops the start with a message saying how to fix it. Test `embedded::cluster::tests::a_socket_dir_that_is_not_this_user_s_alone_is_refused`. Checked against the code 2026-11-17.
- **SEC-DAT-08 (Low; likely (second check); fixed). `get#EntitySyncData` reads any entity, and `put#EntitySyncData` takes `UserAccount` and job rows.** `get` returns the rows of any entity the caller names, with `UserAccount` password hashes, `UserLoginKey` rows and decrypted fields, as one in-memory XML string whose size the caller sets with `recordThreshold`. `put` refuses the restricted list, but the list leaves out `UserAccount` (so `currentPassword` can be set), `moqui.service.job.ServiceJob` (a job runs with authorization disabled) and every other entity. The code comment says "A sync partner is not an admin". Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/entity_sync.rs:246-299`, `:220-240`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sensitive.rs:113-117`. Fix: Apply the restricted-entity check and the password-field strip in `get_data`, cap `recordThreshold`, and add `UserAccount` password fields and the job entities to what `put_data` refuses. Fixed 2026-11-16: `get#EntitySyncData` skips a restricted entity, writes no password field and no restricted related record (`RecordGuard::Replica`) and caps `recordThreshold` at 10,000; `put#EntitySyncData` refuses a password field, the service-written entities and `moqui.service.job.*`; test `entity_sync_data_keeps_login_keys_and_password_hashes_from_a_partner`.
- **SEC-DAT-09 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). No upper bound on `pageSize`, and `pageNoLimit=true` is honored, for screens and Service REST.** The find reads every matching row into memory and the response renders them. Entity REST caps this (`rest-max-page-size`); these two paths do not. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/search_form.rs:98-113`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:731-737`. Fix: Clamp the page size in `search_form_map` to a conf maximum and ignore `pageNoLimit` unless the caller's code passes it. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part, with SEC-RTE-14: Service REST caps a page at `rest-max-page-size` and ignores `pageNoLimit`; left: a screen's `pageSize` from `search_form_map` has no cap (deferred by the lead). Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commit 6c112f7e4), the owner's call: a high capped limit for an export. `search_form_map` holds a request's `pageSize` to `rest-max-page-size` (1000), and `pageNoLimit=true` (a screen's CSV, XLSX, print or text export) reads from the first row at most the new `entity-facade` `screen-max-rows` (100,000; `query_rest::screen_max_rows`); a limit the caller's code set on the find is its own. The form's paginator holds its page to the same cap (`screen/statics.rs` `page_keys`). Tests `entity::search_form` `a_requests_page_size_is_held_to_the_rest_max_page_size`, `defaults_apply_only_without_input_and_order_and_paging_are_read` (the export's limit).
- **SEC-DAT-10 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). Stored text that is not ciphertext is read as a valid value.** A stored value with no colon that is not hex fails to decrypt and is then returned as it is, with no log line. A database writer can so set `SystemMessageRemote.sharedSecret`, `OauthFlow.secret` or `EmailServer.smtpPassword` to a value of their choice. `rewrite#EntityEncryptedFields` then seals that value under the current key. This is the same class as the map's "value sealed under the public default pass", by a shorter road. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/field_rw.rs:187-198`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/tools.rs:873-878`. Fix: Accept stored plaintext only behind a conf switch for migration, and log each such read. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 66f8c000d): stored text of an encrypted field with no colon that is not hex reads as `_DECRYPT_FAILED_` (logged; a write refuses it and `rewrite#EntityEncryptedFields` skips the row) unless `entity_ds_crypt_read_plain=true` (`entity-facade` `crypt-read-plain`), for a migration of values stored before their field was encrypted, when each such read is a logged warning (`field_rw.rs` `decrypt_fields`). Test `service::tools` `plain_text_in_an_encrypted_field_is_read_only_for_a_migration`. Release note: such values read as failed until the operator sets the property, runs `rewrite#EntityEncryptedFieldsAll` and turns it off.
- **SEC-DAT-11 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). Row filters fail open when the set has `allowMissingAlias="Y"` or its `applyCond` is false.** With `activeOrgId` unset the `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG` set is skipped, so every organization's rows are read (the seed comment says so). With `allowMissingAlias="Y"` a find of a view over `mantle.party.Party` that does not alias `privateOwnerUserId` is not filtered, so another user's personal contacts are read through that view. A find with authorization disabled, a write by primary key, and the search index are not filtered at all. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/filter.rs:155`, `:376-378`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/data/MantleSetupData.xml:491`, `:555`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/data/BossSetupData.xml:2160-2161`. Fix: Set `allowMissingAlias="N"` on `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT`, and log a find where a set was skipped for a missing alias. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (commit 28e2389c4): a find that a filter term is left out of, because the view has no alias for its field and the set has `allowMissingAlias="Y"`, is logged: a warning naming the view, the entity and the field the first time, then debug (`filter.rs` `note_missing_alias`; test `user::filter` `a_filter_term_left_out_for_a_missing_alias_is_logged`). Fixed 2026-11-16 (the application's part; mr-mantle bfaf6945, map commit 3f990e0a4), the owner's call: `allowMissingAlias="N"` on `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT`, with the alias added to the views over `Party` that need it. A find on a view over `Party` with no alias of `privateOwnerUserId` is now refused under the filter rather than read unfiltered; the eight views over `Party` that lacked the alias have it (FinancialAccountAndOwner, InvoicePaymentApplicationSummary from and to, AcctgTransAndEntrySummary, AssetLocationOriginDetail, AssetLocationOriginProductUomDetail, ProductStoreShipOptionDetail, CarrierShipmentMethodDetail, WorkEffortProductionRunDetailFind both owners), found by resolving every view of every component by its members (44 over `Party`, 36 with the alias). Test mr-mantle `PrivateContactFilter.spec.js`. Found while fixing, not fixed: with two `Party` members aliased (InvoiceFinance, PaymentFinance, InvoicePaymentApplicationSummary, WorkEffortProductionRunDetailFind) `filter.rs` `map_conditions` ANDs the two aliases inside each filter term, so a row with one shared party and one of the caller's own personal contacts is hidden from its owner too (fails closed); per-member conditions would be the exact rule.
