Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 6c112f7e4, mr-mantle 005e7cbd for its spec): on a view with two or more members of the filter's entity that alias a field of the map, each member is held to the whole map on its own and the members' conditions are ANDed (`filter.rs` `conditions_for`, `Relation::OneMember`), so `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT` is `(from null or from = me) and (to null or to = me)`; a field one member has no alias for, where another has, is that member's. Tests `user::filter` `a_view_with_two_members_of_the_filters_entity_holds_each_member_to_the_map`, mr-mantle `PrivateContactFilter.spec.js` (SalesAccountContact, a public contact of the owner's personal account).
- **SEC-DAT-12 (Info; confirmed (second check)). Regulated identifiers stored in clear.** `BankAccount.routingNumber` and `.accountNumber`, `TaxAuthorityParty.partyTaxId` and `Employee.taxAccountNumbers` are neither encrypted nor hashed, while `CreditCard.cardNumber` and `PartyIdentification.idValue` are encrypted. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/entity/AccountingAccountEntities.xml:1144-1145`; `AccountingOtherEntities.xml:249`; `HumanResourcesEntities.xml:386`, `:468`. Fix: Mark them `encrypt="true"` and run `rewrite#EntityEncryptedFields`.
- **SEC-DAT-13 (Info; not confirmed (second check)). The advisory check covers one target and the default features.** A crate linked only on Windows or macOS, or only by a non-default feature of a server package, is outside the graph `cargo deny check` reads, so an advisory on it does not fail the check. `RUSTSEC-2023-0071` is accepted for `rsa`. Where: `deny.toml:6-8`, `:10-18`. Fix: Run the advisories check with `--all-features` and without the target filter in CI.
- **SEC-DAT-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The search SQL has no time limit, and one wildcard word reads every row.** A contains or wildcard term on all fields compiles to `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM unnest(tsvector_to_array(all_text)) AS w WHERE w LIKE ?)`, which no index serves, run once for the count and once for the page. `bounded_query` sets `statement_timeout` only when a report's limit is active, so a search outside a report runs with none; `pageNoLimit=true` asks for 10,000 documents with their bodies, and a query string's length and clause count are bounded only by the request size (group nesting at 64). Reached by any signed-in user of a search box (`Commons/Search` `q`, Marble's searches, chat and mail search) with `*a*`, `a?b` or many of them. Confirmed for the statements; whether the datasource sets a `statement_timeout` of its own is to check (the replica's `read-statement-timeout` applies only to a read replica). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/search/postgres/mod.rs:670-683`; `crates/moqui-core/src/search/postgres/compile.rs:954-965`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/search/SearchServices.xml:127-129`; `crates/moqui-core/src/search/opensearch.rs:955`, `:988`. Fix: Run every search under a default `QueryLimits` (a few seconds), cap the clauses of a parsed query string, and hold `pageNoLimit` to the callers that need it.
- **SEC-DAT-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). An empty group in a query string matches every document, and Marble writes its organization limit as such a group.** Marble's product lookup and Catalog Search append the limit to the user's query as text, `' AND ownerPartyId:(' + filterOrgIds.join(' OR ') + ')'`, which for a user with no organization is `ownerPartyId:()`. The parser reads an empty group as no clauses, and no clauses is match-all, where the entity filter for the same user and set gives `1 = 0`. The user's own text cannot make the limit optional: a clause joined by `AND` is required. Reached by a Marble user whose authorization carries `MANTLE_USER_ORG` and who has no organization (`filterOrgIds` is `[]`), through `getProductList` or Catalog Search; confirmed for the parser, likely for the reach, since no shipped authorization was found that gives a Marble screen `MANTLE_USER_ORG` (the seed uses `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG` there). `search#Universal` and `PartyForms.xml:104` handle the empty list. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/search/parse.rs:308-311`, `:413-423`, `:509-512`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/template/product/ProductTransitions.xml:61-62`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Catalog/Search.xml:64-65`. Fix: Make `field:()` and `()` an error or match-none in `parse.rs`, and pass the organization limit as `filter` (an `in` clause, which is `FALSE` for no values), as `search#Party` does.
- **SEC-DAT-86 (Low; reported (to check)). The older search callers read the organization limit from the first filter set only.** `search#Party`, `get#ProjectParties`, `search#MantleFiltered` and the Marble lookups take `findFilters[0].entityFilterSetId` and add the organization limit only when it is `MANTLE_USER_ORG` or `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG`. The list is in the order the `ArtifactAuthzFilter` rows are read, with no order-by, and every mr-boss authorization carries two sets (`MANTLE_USER_ORG` and `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT`, which filters `Party`, `BossAccountingSetupData.xml:195-196`); when `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT` comes first the search has no organization limit. `search#Universal` looks at every set. The reviewer found these calls only in Marble and `/rest/s1/mantle`, whose seed authorizations have one set or none, and did not check the row order the database gives. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/party/PartyServices.xml:186-193`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/work/ProjectServices.xml:1333-1334`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/GeneralServices.xml:252-255`; `mr-marble/template/party/PartyForms.xml:93-94`; `mr-marble/template/product/ProductTransitions.xml:49-50`; `mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Catalog/Search.xml:60-61`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/filter.rs:220-223`. Fix: One helper in mr-mantle that answers the organization ids from all of the filter sets, used by all of these callers; delete `mr-boss/template/SearchTransitions.xml`, which no screen includes and which lacks the private-contact filter.
- **SEC-DAT-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). On OpenSearch a backslash carries query syntax past the parser.** The tokenizer removes a backslash and keeps the next character as plain text. A word with a wildcard that is not a lone prefix or contains form becomes `TextMatch::Wildcard`, whose text is escaped again only for `*`, `?` and `\`, and the OpenSearch compiler sends it as the `query` of a `query_string`. So `a\:b*c*` is sent as `a:b*c*`, and `\/.*x.*\/*?*` as a regular expression, though the parser refuses `/`. The clause stays inside the request's own indexes and under the trusted filter, so this is cost and unintended syntax, not a read of other data; confirmed for the text sent, to check for what OpenSearch then allows. Reached by any user of a search box when the backend is OpenSearch. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/search/parse.rs:234-247`, `:495-505`; `crates/moqui-core/src/search/opensearch.rs:291-292`, `:301-302`. Fix: In `opensearch.rs`, escape a wildcard term for every character but the unescaped `*` and `?` (or carry the per-character flags to the compiler), and send `"allow_leading_wildcard": false` where the caller is a search box.
- **SEC-DAT-88 (Low; reported (likely)). With `filterOrgIds` unset, `MANTLE_USER_ORG` filters read the rows that have no organization.** `setup#UserOrganizationInfo` returns without setting `filterOrgIds` when the account has no party. The filter maps name it bare (`({organizationPartyId: filterOrgIds})`); an unset name is null, and a null value is `IS NULL`. So `AcctgTrans`, `FinancialAccount`, `Facility`, `ProductStore`, `Agreement` and the others in that form show their rows with no organization, and `Invoice` and `Payment` (`joinOr="Y"`) the rows where either party is null; the maps written `filterOrgIds || []` give `1 = 0`. Reached by a user whose account has no `partyId`, or a find before `setup#UserOrganizationInfo` ran, under an authorization with `MANTLE_USER_ORG`. That an unset name is null in `eval_map` is taken from `docs/dev/JS-Globals.md:24`, not from the evaluator's code. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/user/filter.rs:541-544`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/data/MantleSetupData.xml:427-438`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/party/PartyServices.xml:533-537`. Fix: Write every map as `filterOrgIds || []`, or set `filterOrgIds = []` when the account has no party.
- **SEC-DAT-89 (Low; reported (likely)). A snapshot import takes restricted entities and runs service elements with authorization off.** `uploadSnapshot` stores any `.zip`, and `importSnapshot` loads it with no `restrictSensitiveEntities` and, by default, with entity ECA rules off. A record of any entity is written, and an element whose name is a service (or any `verb#noun`) is called with `disable_authz`. So an `ADMIN` who is not in `ADMIN_ADV` and holds no sealed permission adds a privileged membership or a sealed grant, or calls any service as if authorized. The map says the import is unrestricted, not that service elements run and the sealed checks are off; the reviewer rates it Low because Tools Snapshots is a designed privileged tool, and lists it because it reaches a sealed permission. Not run; whether the import job runs in the importing user's context was not traced. Reached by a holder of `update` (upload) and `all` (import) on `Tools/Data/Snapshots`. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/EntityServices.xml:311-316`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/loader.rs:740`, `:760-761`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/eca.rs:199`, `:207`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Snapshots.xml:60-70`, `:102-115`. Fix: Require a sealed permission (`ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD`, which a full backup needs, or a restore permission) for `importSnapshot` and `uploadSnapshot`, and refuse service elements in a snapshot.
- **SEC-DAT-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The HMAC replay cache is per process, so a signed message is accepted once on each cluster member.** The replay key of a signed system message (`/rest/sm/...`) or chat bridge webhook is kept in the conf cache `moqui.security.hmac.replay`, declared `type="distributed"`. `add_from_conf` does not read `type`, and every conf cache is a local `MCache`, so each member has its own set of seen signatures and the same message passes `first_use` once per member inside the 300 s window. The comment at `chat_bridge_web.rs:176` ("Taken by another request (on any node)") assumes a shared cache. Reached by a network peer that captured one validly signed message, on an install with more than one member behind a balancer; confirmed for the cache, likely for the effect. Whether the receive service's own once-only rule (`store#ReceivedSystemMessage`) stops the second copy for every message type was not traced. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/message.rs:548-561`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_bridge_web.rs:181-186`, `:220-233`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/cache.rs:44-73`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:736`. Fix: Keep replay keys in a table with a unique key (insert or fail), or state in the map that the window is per member.
- **SEC-DAT-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The data loader holds a whole document, a whole CSV record or nested zips in memory with no bound.** One top-level element of an `entity-facade-xml` file is held to 64 MiB, but a file whose root is anything else is read to its end and parsed as one tree, and a zip entry inflates as it streams, so a small `.xml` entry of that kind expands without limit. The CSV reader has no record or field limit. A nested zip is read whole (up to 256 MiB) at each of five levels, each held while its children load; `MAX_UNIT` is checked only in `Pull::drive`. Reached by whoever gives the loader a file: a snapshot uploader and importer, a Data Load `location`; an administrator's file is the only source found. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/xml.rs:53-55`; `load_feed/csv.rs:46-54`; `load_feed/zip.rs:31-33`, `:92-100`; `load_feed/pull.rs:44-48`. Fix: Cap `read_to_end` (the same 64 MiB, or a conf value) and give the CSV reader a record limit.
- **SEC-DAT-92 (Low; reported (likely)). Tools auto forms show an encrypted field decrypted and a `UserAccount` password hash.** `auto-fields-entity` makes a field for every field of the entity from an `EntityShape` that has no encrypt or secret flag, and picks the widget from the field type alone; the screen's `entity-find` result is not passed through `GenericAccess::conceal`, which only entity REST, Service REST and the data writer use. The Browse screens refuse the restricted entities, but `UserAccount`, `AppInstanceEnv`, `CreditCard`, `PartyIdentification`, `LlmEndpoint` and the other entities with an encrypted field are not restricted, while the data grid masks the same fields. The rendered output was not looked at, and the Tools map records the owner's call that the browse tools read every record whole, which may cover this. Reached by a holder of view on `Tools/Data/Browse` (`AutoFind`, `AutoEdit`, `Record`). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/form.rs:458-486`, `:954-970`, `:1155-1215`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Browse/AutoFind.xml:48-53`. Fix: Have `EntityShape` carry the hidden fields and render them as set or not set, as the grid does.
- **SEC-DAT-93 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `verify-ca` accepts a public certificate for any host name, and the client then hands the password to whoever asks.** On Postgres, `verify-ca` with no `sslrootcert` trusts the whole `webpki-roots` set, and the `AnyName` verifier turns a name mismatch into success, so a certificate any public authority issued to any domain (one an attacker gets from an ACME authority) passes; the attacker then asks for `AuthenticationCleartextPassword` and the client sends the password. With `require` the same holds with no certificate at all, as in libpq; libpq's `verify-ca` trusts only the root file and refuses to run without one. On MySQL the fork trusts the public roots beside `sslRootCert`, and `verify-ca` (or `require` with a root file) skips the name, so a private authority does not narrow trust, and `caching_sha2_password` full authentication over TLS writes the password itself (a MySQL datasource ignores the mode, SEC-DAT-97, so this is a conf connection's). The client answers every method it has (there is no `require_auth`) and uses no channel binding. Reached by a network attacker on the path to a connection whose mode is `verify-ca` or `require`: a datasource (the operator's mode), an external connection (its owner's, with `DATABASE_EXTERNAL`, off production) or an InstanceServices conf map; whether any deployment or the search client uses `verify-ca` was not checked. Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/pg_tls.rs:69-85`, `:111-133`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/pgwire.rs:572-632`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/mysql.rs:119-132`; the `mysql` fork `src/io/tls/rustls_io.rs:39-40`, `:143-163`, `src/conn/mod.rs:953-970`. Fix: Make `verify-ca` require `sslrootcert` and trust only that file, as libpq does (for MySQL, a store built from the file alone, a fork change); on a connection that is not `verify-full`, refuse a cleartext or MD5 request, or add a `require_auth` setting; use SCRAM channel binding (`tls-server-end-point`) over TLS; say in the Data map what `verify-ca` means here.
- **SEC-DAT-94 (Low; reported (likely)). A database server ends the whole process with one message header.** On Postgres, `Message::parse` reads the four-byte length and reserves the whole message before its bytes arrive: a length of `0xFFFFFFFF` reserves 4 GiB, and an allocation that fails aborts the process, whatever request it served. On MySQL the fork reads a result set's column count as a 64-bit length-encoded integer and calls `Vec::reserve` with it before it reads a column; 2^50 columns cannot be allocated on any host, a larger count panics with a capacity overflow, and since `connect_mysql` runs `SELECT VERSION()` first, testing a connection is enough. A server that sends `DataRow`s and never `ReadyForQuery` grows the rows until memory runs out: the read timeout bounds one read, and `SqlRunOptions.limit` is applied after the rows came. Reached by whoever answers as the database server: the server of an external connection (its owner names it, with `DATABASE_EXTERNAL`, and reaches it through `test#DatabaseConnection` or any `db=ext:` service, off production), a man in the middle of a connection that is not `verify-full`, or a compromised database server. Not run: Postgres's 4 GiB reservation may succeed on a host that has the memory, and a `catch_unwind` does not stop an abort. Where: `postgres-protocol-0.6.12` `src/message/backend.rs:120-133` (reached from `crates/moqui-drivers/src/pgwire.rs:816-825`); the `mysql` fork `src/conn/mod.rs:1207-1216` (from `crates/moqui-drivers/src/mysql.rs:259`, `:353`, `:370`); `pgwire.rs:845-862`, `:1209-1227`. Fix: In `pgwire`, read the five-byte header and refuse a length over a limit (say 1 GiB plus the header) before `Message::parse` reserves; in the fork, refuse a column count over MySQL's 4096 before `reserve`; give a query a row or byte limit, and stream the SQL tools' results.
- **SEC-DAT-95 (Low; reported (likely)). A database error's text, its DETAIL line included, reaches the caller on a production instance.** A Postgres `ErrorResponse` becomes `postgres <context>: <message>` with the `DbError` as its source, whose text is `db error: <severity>: <message>` with a `DETAIL:` and a `HINT:` line, and the library error's text appends its source's. When a service body fails, `record_failure` adds the error's text and every cause's to the message facade; `client_text` drops only lines that start with white space, which `DETAIL:` does not; Service REST answers the facade's errors as `errors` with 400 or 500, and `sendJsonResponse` with 500, on any instance purpose, since `error_text` answers an error id in production only for an error that leaves the request. The DETAIL is a key violation's `Key (col)=(value) already exists`, a foreign key's target table, or a not-null or check failure's `Failing row contains (...)`, every column of the row as written; MySQL's text goes the same way. Reached by any caller whose service, called through a screen transition or a Service REST resource, fails on a statement. A path where a low-privileged caller's failing row holds columns that caller cannot read was not found (only primary key columns are marked not null), and the entity layer was read only in `exec.rs:72-107`, where it passes the error through. Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/pgwire.rs:197-208`, `:236-238`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call.rs:656`, `:781-810`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/error.rs:222-234`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/dispatch.rs:159-167`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1147-1161`. Fix: Keep the `DETAIL` and `HINT` in the logged diagnostic only and give the client the message, or the SQLSTATE class alone; in production have `record_failure` add a fixed text with an error id for a failure whose chain holds a database error.
- **SEC-DAT-96 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A load keeps every service element with its parameters for the life of the process, and a wrapped load stores its whole text as job parameters.** `call_data_file_service` pushes each call (name, location, parameters) onto a vector on the facade that only tests read and nothing clears, so a seed service call with a password parameter stays in memory, and the vector grows with each load. With `wrapForeignKeys` (a `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder's Data Load), `xmlText`, `jsonText` or `csvText` is passed as a parameter of the `DatabaseLoadWrap` job, and `create_run` stores job parameters as JSON in `ServiceJobRun.parameters` (`text-long`, not encrypted) and sends them in the job's notification. Where: `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/loader.rs:760`; `load_feed/mod.rs:136-156`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Load.xml:74-78`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/jobs.rs:1372-1376`. Fix: Drop the `services` vector outside tests; pass a wrapped load its text through a file or a row that is deleted after the run.
- **SEC-DAT-97 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A MySQL datasource takes only on or off from `sslmode` and ignores `sslrootcert`, and a conf connection to MySQL with no mode is plain text.** `entity_db::mysql_driver` builds the driver with TLS on or off and never calls `.tls_options(...)`, so a MySQL datasource always verifies in full against the public roots: `require`, `verify-ca` and `sslrootcert` have no effect, and a server with a private authority connects only with `sslmode=disable`, which pushes an operator toward plain text. In `conf_driver` MySQL with an empty mode gets no TLS, where Postgres gets `verify-full`; over plain TCP, `caching_sha2_password` full authentication asks the server for its public key and encrypts the password to whatever key comes back, so a man in the middle reads it (libmysqlclient does not request the key unless asked). External connections are spared by `ssl_mode_of`; the InstanceServices conf map is not. Where: `crates/moqui-drivers/src/entity_db.rs:430-448`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/lib.rs:107-117`; the `mysql` fork `src/conn/mod.rs:958-970`. Fix: Call `.tls_options(conf.tls_verify, &conf.tls_root_cert)` in `entity_db::mysql_driver`; treat an empty MySQL mode in `conf_driver` as Postgres does (no TLS for loopback only); set a `server_key_path`, or refuse the key request over plain TCP.

## PASS frontend (mr-pass)

- **SEC-PSF-02 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed 2026-11-16 by the framework rule of SEC-WEB-04: a GET of a writing transition another site started is refused). Write transitions without `method="post"` run on a GET that carries no query string, with no session token.** The session token is compared only for POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE. What stops a GET to these transitions is the rule against URL parameters, which a GET with no query string passes; the resolver's path parameters (`token` of `held-mail/{token}/claim`, the topic id of `t/{id}/{slug}/read`) are not URL parameters to that rule. Today the reachable effects are small: `GET /forum/held-mail/{token}/claim` posts the held message as the signed-in account whose confirmed address sent it, `GET /shop/Checkout/begin` starts a checkout from the session's cart, `GET /forum/t/{id}/{slug}/read` calls `markRead`; the others need a parameter and fail. A transition added later that reads its input from the path or the session inherits the gap. Where: 57 transitions (list in A8), for example `screen/Forum/HeldMail.xml:13`, `screen/Shop/Checkout.xml:7,37,43`, `screen/Forum/Topics/TopicDetail.xml:37`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2667-2673`, `2076-2095`. Fix: Add `method="post"` to the 57 transitions (the pages already post to them), or have the framework compare the session token for any method on a transition that runs actions and is not read-only.
- **SEC-PSF-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Bearer values in PASS URLs that the log and Visit redaction do not cover.** Path redaction applies only when the first path segment is a framework token route, so `/forum/held-mail/{token}`, `.../-/forum/held-mail/{token}`, `.../-/held-report/{token}` and `/preview/{token}` are recorded whole. Query redaction goes by name: `token`, `orderToken`, `downloadToken`, `code` are left out, but `key` (a payment return key, which reads the attempt and, on Billing, its invoice), `r` (a one-time cart recovery token), `confirm` (an email-preferences confirmation link) and, on the authority, `returnKey` (`getPaymentResult` is a GET) do not match the rule and are recorded. A sign-in link's `returnTo` holding one of the path tokens is recorded too. Where: `service/pass/RouteServices.xml:46-49`; `service/pass/SmithyRoutes.js:179, 239`; `screen/Shop/ShopPreview.js:60-66`; `screen/Billing/InvoicePay.xml:39-43`; `screen/Shop/ShopCheckout.js:687-690`; `screen/Shop/ShopRecovery.js:48-52`; `screen/Account/Hub.js:34-37`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:24-41`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/token_route.rs:187-205`. Fix: Add the PASS token paths to what `redact_url` knows (a `public-site` could declare token path patterns), and rename the query parameters to names the secret rule matches (`returnKey` to `return_key` or `returnToken`, `r` to `recoveryToken`, `confirm` to `confirmToken`) or add them to the rule. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle 9f62d658, mr-pass a40fb83 and 886c8d6; framework commits 86a8bf84b, 6ecd8fd4a): the links carry their bearer values in names the log's secret rule redacts (`returnToken`, `recoveryToken`, `confirmToken`, with SEC-PSS-02 and SEC-PSS-27; the old names are still read for links sent before), `returnKey` is a secret name (SEC-PSA-09), and the path tokens are redacted: a component declares a `token-path` of the webroot webapp (`/preview/{token}`, `/**/held-mail/{token}` after any segments), and the request log's path, query values and Referer, log lines, a Visit's `initialRequest` and `initialReferrer` and browser telemetry record that segment as `:token` (`token_route::TokenPath`, `redact_path`); mr-pass declares `/**/held-mail/{token}`, `/**/-/held-report/{token}` and `/preview/{token}`. Tests `token_route_tests.rs` `a_token_paths_token_is_redacted_from_a_recorded_path_or_url`, `screen/tests/shell.rs` `a_visit_leaves_out_a_token_routes_token`, `moqui-host` `a_token_routes_token_stays_out_of_the_request_root`, and the specs of SEC-PSS-02. Upgrade note: a remote frontend of this version asks `getPaymentResult` with `returnToken`, so the authority is upgraded first.
- **SEC-PSF-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The shell's CSP has no `default-src` and allows `'unsafe-eval'` and every same-origin script.** The policy lists `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'`, `img-src`, `media-src`, `object-src`, `base-uri`, `form-action` and `frame-ancestors`. With no `default-src`, styles, frames, fonts and connections are unrestricted, so injected markup can load a frame from any origin and send data with CSS or `fetch`. `script-src 'self'` trusts every URL of the site's origin, which on these sites includes files visitors upload or push; whether one can be served with a script type is what the download headers decide (A5): Smithy's raw route cannot, a release file can (C8). Where: `screen/Shared/Headers.js:8-16`. Fix: Add `default-src 'self'` (with `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` if the pages need inline styles) and `connect-src 'self'`; move the islands to precompiled render functions and drop `'unsafe-eval'`. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (mr-pass 8724ff6): the shell's policy (`Shared/Headers.js` `CSP`) adds `default-src 'self'`, `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, `font-src 'self' data:` and `connect-src 'self'`, so frames, fonts, connections and workers come from the site alone; test `ShopAccess.spec.js` (the policy text). Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; commits 9b0f89a61, 9f40efe11, 044549eca and 511629342, mr-pass 75ae298), the owner's call: precompile the islands' templates on the server and drop `'unsafe-eval'`. A `.qvue` asked for as `<path>.qvue?_qvt=bundle` (a screen's `template="false"` qvue or a `.qvue` file under a screen) is answered compiled by the vendored Vue compiler (`vue_check::qvue_make`, `served_qvue`; `screen/qvue_bundle.rs`), with the screen's render and authorization as for the plain `.qvue`, as a script that registers it under the path the client asked for (the loader's `data-moqui-bundle`, else its own URL path); on the runtime build of Vue a page whose `qvt.handles` lists `bundled-qvue` loads each `.qvue` that way (`WebrootVue.qvt.js` `loadComponent`, `moqui.loadBundledQvue`), never through `parseVue` or `new Function`, and `WebrootPublic.qvt.js`'s roots and `moqui.NotFound`/`EmptyComponent` are render functions. PASS's islands (`ShopCart.js`, `SmithyIssues.js`, `Shared/Compose` and `TopicEnhance`) load `vue.runtime.global.prod.js` with `handles: ['static-template', 'bundled-qvue']`, and `Shared/Headers.js` sends `script-src 'self'`. Tests moqui-core `a_qvue_asked_for_as_its_bundle_is_compiled_and_named_by_its_path`, webroot `WebrootPublic.runtime.spec.js` (the island mounts with `eval` and `Function` stubbed to throw), mr-pass `ShopAccess.spec.js` (the policy text) and the client `ShopCheckoutWalk` (the purchase island mounted from its bundle); the Forum composer was opened once in a browser, and the acceptance `SmithyWalk` (2 of 2, 2026-11-17) mounts Smithy's issue composer island on the runtime build, switches its editor and renders its preview; Smithy's DiffReview, Editor and JobLog islands are walked by no browser spec.
- **SEC-PSF-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The REST transport sends the client secret and visitors' delegations to whatever scheme `pass_backend_url` names.** `authorityClient` builds the URL from the property and adds `x-pass-client-secret` and the visitor's `Authorization: Bearer` with no check that the scheme is `https`. `docs/Deployment.md:18` only advises "use TLS in production". The gRPC transport has the same choice (`grpc://` is clear text, `Deployment.md:42-44`); its client is framework code and was not read. Where: `service/pass/Transport.js:264-287`. Fix: Refuse an `http://` `pass_backend_url` whose host is not loopback unless a property such as `pass_backend_insecure=true` is set, as the authority's gRPC listener does with `pass_authority_grpc_insecure`. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-pass 17b0a35): a PASS frontend refuses an `http://` `pass_backend_url`, or a `grpc://` `pass_backend_grpc_url` (given or derived from an `http://` backend), whose host is not loopback, unless `pass_backend_insecure=true` (`Transport.js` `select`, `authorityClient`; each call Unavailable, naming the setting); test `Transport.spec.js` "a transport in clear text to another host is refused unless pass_backend_insecure says so". `addPropertyHeader` alone kept the secret to URLs under `pass_backend_url` whatever their scheme, and the visitor's Bearer went regardless.
- **SEC-PSF-08 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A Smithy release file is served from the site's origin with a script content type, so `script-src 'self'` stops no injected script.** A release file's name may end in any extension and its type is taken from the name, so `x.js` is answered as `application/javascript` at `/{repository}/-/release-assets/{id}/x.js`. The facade adds `nosniff` and `Content-Disposition: attachment`, which stop a navigation from showing the file but not a `<script src>` from loading it, and no sandboxing CSP. Alone it does nothing; with any markup injection on the site it turns the shell's `script-src 'self'` (C4) into no barrier. The sub-review reports the same for a CI report artifact in local mode (the engine's `send#RunArtifact` types the answer by the report's file name); I read only `file_content_type` and `file_name` of that path. Smithy's raw route does this correctly: it answers `text/plain`, four image types or `application/octet-stream`, with `default-src 'none'; sandbox`. Where: `screen/Smithy/SmithySite.js:392-398`; `service/pass/Facade.js:103-117`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/smithy/SmithySite.js:905, 1129, 1150`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/resource.rs:255-262`. Fix: In `Facade.downloadFile` answer `application/octet-stream` for any type outside a short list of inert types and add `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, as `Smithy.js` `raw` does. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-pass c773c88, 8873a9f; mr-mantle 83052b72, cb683f86; framework commit 067e58938): a PASS download is answered by its own type only when inert (images, fonts, audio and video, PDF, plain text and CSV, archives), else `application/octet-stream`, with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, an inline PDF aside (browsers do not show one in a sandbox): in local mode `Facade.js` `downloadFile` and `downloadRanged`, for the remote transports `PassAuthority.js` and `PassApi.js`; the framework does the same for a file the engine sends itself (`send#RunArtifact`, `web::send_file_response`) and for a download forwarded over gRPC (`script/host/pass.rs` `download_headers`; `web::is_inert_content_type`, `USER_FILE_SANDBOX`). Tests `Smithy.spec.js` "a release's file is answered as an inert type with a sandbox", `web/tests.rs` `a_users_download_has_an_inert_type_and_the_sandbox`, `script/host/tests.rs` `a_forwarded_pass_download_has_an_inert_type_and_the_sandbox`. A run artifact or report of another type (XML, JSON) now downloads as an octet stream.
- **SEC-PSF-06 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The resolver's preview-host check fails open.** `isPreviewHost` catches the error, logs it and returns false, so the resolver and `Pass.xml` treat the preview host as a live host of the site and serve the site's pages there, the Account pages included. The framework's own `host-check-service` call for the same host fails closed (`crates/moqui-core/src/screen/public_site.rs:180-186`), so only the site's pages are affected. Where: `screen/Shop/ShopPreview.js:35-57`; used at `service/pass/RouteServices.xml:19-20` and `screen/Pass.xml:19`. Fix: Answer Unavailable when the check throws.
- **SEC-PSF-07 (Info; reported (to check)). Bearer values sit in the frontend's session rows as written.** The delegation (a bearer for the visitor at the authority), the authority's own session cookie over REST, cart capabilities, order and pay tokens and, for two minutes, a newly made access token are session attributes. A copy of the frontend's database is then enough to act as its signed-in visitors at the authority until each delegation ends. Where: `service/pass/Account.js:85, 224`; `service/pass/Transport.js:412, 514`; `screen/Billing/InvoicePay.xml:26`; `screen/Smithy/SmithyForms.js:2060-2063`. Fix: If session attributes are stored in clear, encrypt these with the session cookie key, or keep only a digest-addressed handle.
- **SEC-PSF-09 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Authority HTML is written without the frontend's own cleaning (five Smithy fields, forum posts).** These five are passed through `raw(...)` or `Markup` after only a link rewrite, relying on the authority's `cleanHtml`. Issue and merge request text is cleaned a second time on the frontend (`SmithyIssues.js:34`), these are not, and the link rewrite on both sides runs after the cleaning. The authority cleans README, Docs and release notes (sub-review: `mr-mantle/service/mantle/smithy/Smithy.js:354`, `SmithySite.js:184, 641`); the landing page's cleaning was not traced. Where: `screen/Smithy/Files.html.fancy:22` (`view.readme.html`), `screen/Smithy/Blob.html.fancy:3` (`view.html`), `screen/Smithy/GroupOverview.html.fancy:3` (`overviewView.landingHtml`), `screen/Smithy/SmithySite.js:152` (a Docs page), `:182, 314` (release notes). Fix: Clean after the link rewrite, and pass these fields through the frontend's cleaner (`StringUtilities.cleanHtml` or `ec.resource.theme.sanitizeRichText`) as Smithy's posts and Marketing's pages are. Coverage note (my own pass over the other apps): the inline island configuration escapes `<` (`screen/Shared/Compose.html.fancy:3`, `TopicEnhance.html.fancy:8` `JSON.stringify(...).split('<').join('\\u003c')`); the four islands outside Smithy have no `v-html` or `innerHTML` (literal search), and the topic island shows a post through the framework's `m-html-view`, a frame with `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox"` (`runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootVue.qvt.js:8534-8560`). A site's `subscriptionUrl` is checked by the authority as https or a local path (`mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/Forum.js:344-345`). The page scripts of Shop, Account, Support, Sales and Marketing that build HTML by string concatenation were read only where this report cites them. Coverage note (sub-review of `screen/Smithy*`, re-checked by me only where C8 and A items cite code): `${...}` in `.html.fancy` escapes `& < > " '` (`crates/moqui-core/src/script/template.rs:638-644, 1197`) and `raw()` writes as is; Smithy pages build HTML with a tagged template that escapes every value not marked as markup (`screen/Smithy/SmithyForms.js:15-38`). No unescaped user value, no unvalidated redirect (`Smithy.js:1157-1161`, `SmithyForms.js:1996-1999`) and no island that compiles server text was found. `Smithy/redirect` is not reachable with a request's `location` (the Smithy grammar reads `/redirect` as a name). Not read there: `SmithyForms.js` 1330-1900 except for redirects, most of `Do.xml`. Two smaller points it left open: a group's `addons.xml` is answered inline as `text/xml` with the shell's CSP (`SmithySite.js:409-419`; whether Mantle escapes names in it was not traced), and four links prefix an authority path without the `siteLocal` check (`SmithyReview.js:317-319, 374, 388`, `Smithy.js:1072`).
- **SEC-PSF-81 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A same-site image in a member's bio or a forum post runs the site's GET writes as each reader (SEC-PSF-02's fix does not stop it), and a post's outside image goes to every list member's mail.** This is the cause SEC-CLI-82 records for rich text, on the PASS pages: `StringUtilities.cleanHtml` is ammonia's default set, which keeps `<img src>` of any `http`, `https` or relative address, and a profile's bio (`storeProfile`; shown at `/members/MemberDetail?handle=`, to anonymous visitors when public) and a forum post (`publishPost`, `updatePost`; shown to the topic's readers) are written raw after it. The shell's `img-src 'self' data:` lets a same-site image load, and its request carries the reader's cookie, `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` and no query string, which SEC-WEB-04's rule (SEC-PSF-02's fix) lets through, so a write transition with no `method` runs for every reader: `GET /shop/Checkout/begin` begins a checkout of the reader's cart where the Shop is on the same host, and `held-mail/{token}/claim` claims only the reader's own held message; `GET /forum/t/{id}/{slug}/read` writes nothing, as `markRead` gets a NaN ordinal and answers Not Found. In mail the cleaned post is the template's `body` for members who take every post, where no page policy applies, so a mail client that loads images fetches an outside `src` and tells the poster each list member's address and when they read. Confirmed in code up to the operator's mail template, which is not in the repository (it must write `body` unescaped to show it); not run; what `beginCheckout` changes beyond starting a checkout, Smithy's GET transitions and what Marketing's `?tc=` page load records were not read. Reports: round 3 `C1.md` (the bio), `C2.md` (posts and mail). Where: `screen/Shared/View.js:1529-1531`; `screen/Shared/Page.html.fancy:20`, `:52`; `screen/Shared/Headers.js:18`; `screen/Shop/Checkout.xml:7-12`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumCore.js:1405-1410`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/DeliveryWorker.js:567-570`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/safe_html.rs:30-37`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:663-666`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2768-2782`. Fix: With SEC-CLI-82's fix, keep in member HTML only an `img src` of the site's own attachment download path (drop a relative `src` that names an app route), and in `DeliveryWorker` replace any other image in mail with a link; give the remaining write transitions `method="post"`, or have `cross_site_write` also refuse a GET whose `Sec-Fetch-Dest` is not `document`.
- **SEC-PSF-82 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A link to `Checkout/return` puts another shopper's return key in the visitor's session, and the result page then offers that checkout's payment.** `Checkout/return` is a read-only GET, so neither the URL-parameter rule nor the cross-site rule applies to it, and `returned()` keeps the request's `ref` and `returnToken` (or `key`) in the session's `passShopCheckout:{site}` map with no check that they read a checkout, then redirects to `PaymentResult?ref=`. Every later checkout call for that reference adds the kept key, and the authority accepts any reference with its key (`via` "reference"), so a visitor sent the sender's own reference and key (from `submitCheckout`'s answer to the sender's cart, or the provider's return address) sees the sender's pending attempt with "Continue to the payment page", and `PaymentRelay` posts a fresh hosted form token from `getCheckoutPaymentForm`, which does not test `via`. A visitor who pays there pays the sender's order, delivered to the sender's address, with the receipt mailed to the sender; the visitor's confirmation page is the minimal one. Marketing's registration relay refuses the return key for its payment form; what this adds over a phishing page of the sender's own is a landing page on the store's domain. Not confirmed: what Authorize.Net's hosted page shows the payer; not run. Where: `screen/Shop/ShopCheckout.js:688-703`, `:79-92`, `:791-808`, `:892-919`; `screen/Shop/Checkout.xml:49`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/ShopCheckout.js:192-200`, `:214-221`, `:560-600`, `:1500-1533`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2173`, `:2775`. Fix: Answer the payment form and the provider link only to the cart's holder, the placed cart's session or the signed-in buyer (`via` "cart", "placedCart" or "account"), as Marketing does, and let the reference and key read the state alone; in `returned()`, keep a key only when the reference is this session's cart's checkout, or only after the authority accepted it.
- **SEC-PSF-83 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A theme still gives its links the `unsafe-url` referrer policy, by a `<meta>` that has a `charset` or `itemprop` or by a link's `referrerpolicy` (SEC-PSS-62's fix does not hold).** SEC-PSS-62's fix allows a `<meta>` only with a listed `name`, so that a theme cannot replace the shell's `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`. `meta_allowed` returns true as soon as the tag has a `charset` or `itemprop` attribute, before it looks at `name`, so `<meta itemprop="x" name="referrer" content="unsafe-url">` passes the source and output checks; and `referrerpolicy` is not in `DENIED_ATTRIBUTES`, so `<a href="https://elsewhere.example/" referrerpolicy="unsafe-url">` passes with no meta, and a link's own policy overrides the document's for that navigation. A visitor who follows such a link sends the page's full address to the linked host: on a live Shop page its path and query (the Shop addresses that carry a bearer value redirect before a theme renders), and on a preview `/preview/{token}` (whose header is `no-referrer`) a token that reads the whole draft for up to twenty minutes. Reached by a store's theme author (`COMMERCE_THEME_CODE`, or `COMMERCE_THEME_EDIT` by an import), published by a `COMMERCE_THEME_PUBLISH` holder. SEC-SCR-91 records the same weakness from the Scripts side; its evidence (`markup.rs:185` `"http-equiv"` as the only `meta` rule) is stale, as `meta_allowed` is the rule now, and its title holds through these two ways. Not run in a browser; the effect is the HTML standard's. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/markup.rs:175-187` (`DENIED_ATTRIBUTES`), `:207-225` (`meta_allowed`), `:510`; `screen/Shared/Headers.js:136`; `screen/Shop/ShopPreview.js:105`. Fix: In `meta_allowed`, refuse a `<meta>` whose `name` is not in `META_NAMES` whatever other attributes it has (or allow exactly one of `charset`, `name`, `property`, `itemprop`); add `referrerpolicy` (and `attributionsrc`, SEC-SCR-91) to `DENIED_ATTRIBUTES`, or force `rel="noreferrer"` on theme links to other origins.
- **SEC-PSF-84 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A return's tracking link and an event's join link are written to an `href` with no scheme check.** `Support/CustomerReturn.html.fancy` writes a return's `trackingUrl` (written by staff, or from a carrier integration's answer by `Shippo.js`) and `Marketing/Registration.html.fancy` a confirmed registrant's `joinUrl` (the occurrence's `WorkEffort.infoUrl`, which a calendar import also writes), escaped but with no scheme test; Sales passes the same tracking column through `httpsUrl`. The shell's `script-src 'self'` stops a `javascript:` link from running, so what is left is a link to any address on the organization's page, seen by a customer on a return's page and a confirmed registrant on the registration's page. Not confirmed: whether a calendar import writes the `WorkEffort` of a registration offer's occurrence. Where: `screen/Support/CustomerReturn.html.fancy:7`; `screen/Marketing/Registration.html.fancy:14`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/CustomerReturns.js:112`, `:403`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/marketing/MarketingPortal.js:1056`. Fix: Pass both through the `httpsUrl` test `SalesPortal.js:478` uses.
- **SEC-PSF-85 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A request parameter sets two names the shell reads: `?shopThemed=1` drops the PASS shell and `?canonicalUrl=` sets the canonical link.** Every request parameter is put in the screen context before the actions run. `shopThemed` is set by `Pass.xml` only on a preview host and by `Shop.xml` only under Shop, so on any Account, Members, Sites, Billing, Supplier, Support, Sales or Marketing page the request's value decides whether the shell's header and footer render, and the page goes out without its doctype, head, title, brand style and navigation. `canonicalUrl` is set only by a Forum topic; elsewhere the request's value, escaped, is the page's canonical link. Neither runs script; anyone with a link reaches it. Where: `screen/Pass.xml:20`, `:58`, `:66`; `screen/Shared/Header.html.fancy:2`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:856-860`. Fix: Set `shopThemed=false` and `canonicalUrl=null` in `Pass.xml`'s always-actions before the subscreens set them.
- **SEC-PSF-86 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Ids are written into attributes without escaping on the email preferences page and the invoice page.** `Account/Hub.js` escapes every text through `esc` but puts `'list_' + contactListId`, `'mkt_' + contactListId` and `'topic_' + topic + ':' + productStoreId` into `name="..."` as they are, and `Billing/InvoicePay.xml` writes the invoice id into a hidden input's `value` in inline text, which is not escaped. The ids come from Mantle: generated for forum categories and for the lists `create#ContactList` makes, and set by staff for a store's id; no path for another party to choose one was found. Reached by a list link's holder, a signed-in account or a receipt's confirmation link on `Account/EmailPreferences`, and a contact who may share an invoice on `Billing/InvoicePay`. Where: `screen/Account/Hub.js:41`, `:146-147`, `:150-159`, `:168`; `screen/Billing/InvoicePay.xml:151`; `mr-mantle/service/mantle/public/AccountHub.js:91`. Fix: `esc(name)` in `hidden`, `box` and `decline`; `${html(view.invoice.invoiceId)}` in the inline text.
- **SEC-PSF-87 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A `$'` or `` $` `` in a product's text copies the page into the head's structured data.** `withHead` inserts the product's JSON-LD with `html.replace('</head>', extras + '</head>')`; a string replacement reads `$&`, `$'`, `` $` `` and `$$`, and `extras` holds the product's name and SKU (`dataJson` escapes `<` only). A name with `$'` copies the whole body into the `<script type="application/ld+json">` block up to the body's first `</script>`, and the rest is parsed again in the head, so the page's own markup and same-origin scripts appear twice. Nothing from outside the page is added and the shell's `script-src 'self'` blocks inline script, so no injection was found: the page and its structured data break. Reached by a Commerce user who names a product, against every visitor of a themed product page. Not run. Where: `screen/Shop/Catalog.js:84-87`, `:40-59`, `:75-81`, `:155-159`. Fix: `html.replace('</head>', () => extras + '</head>')`.
- **SEC-PSF-88 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A shop theme reads the visiting shopper's order id, invoice id or subscription name on the account pages.** Every Shop account page renders through the release's checkout page kind with `themed(context, html, title)`, which puts `title` in `view.page.title` after the authority's `contract()`; on the account, order, invoice, review and subscription pages the title names the shopper's record (`'Order ' + id`). A theme may write it anywhere, a link to any `http` or `https` host included, so a shopper who follows such a link sends it there; the order id alone opens nothing. Reached by a holder of `COMMERCE_THEME_EDIT` and `COMMERCE_THEME_PUBLISH` in the store's scope. Not confirmed: whether a theme author may already read the store's orders. Where: `screen/Shop/ShopCheckout.js:449-471`; `screen/Shop/ShopAccount.js:82-87`, `:445`, `:463`; `screen/Shop/ShopReviews.js:210`; `screen/Shop/ShopSubscriptions.js:230`; `theme/starter/layouts/store.html.fancy-locked:6`. Fix: Give the theme a fixed title per page kind ("Your order", "Invoice") and keep the record's id in the component.
- **SEC-PSF-89 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The signed-in email preferences page shows and ends the account's list memberships of every organization.** In account mode `getEmailPreferences` lists every current `ContactListParty` row of the account's party, of any organization (only topic lists, mailboxes and archived lists are left out), with the list's name, description and state, and `storeEmailPreferences` unsubscribes from any of them; the rest of the page is held to the site (store topics to its Shop store, Marketing's topics to its Marketing organization). A delegation is issued for one client and site, so through this page that registered client reads the account's memberships of other organizations' lists and ends them. Not confirmed: whether a global preference center is meant (the service description says "its current lists"), and whether any deployment runs a remote frontend for a party other than the authority's operator. Where: `mr-mantle/service/mantle/public/AccountHub.js:236-255`, `:331-338`; `mr-mantle/script/PassRoutes.js:277-278`. Fix: If the page is a site's, keep the lists to the organizations the site's mounts serve (or to lists with no owner), and leave the rest to the business host's own preferences page.
- **SEC-PSF-90 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The shell's CSP allows `base-uri 'self'` where no PASS page needs a `<base>`.** `Headers.js` sends `base-uri 'self'`. No PASS page writes a `<base>`, a theme cannot have one (`DENIED_ELEMENTS`), and the theme preview already sends `base-uri 'none'`; with `'self'`, injected markup may still set a `<base>` on the site's own origin and move the page's relative script, style and form addresses to another path there. `test/Shop/ShopAccess.spec.js` asserts the whole policy string, so the spec changes with the policy. Report: the security proofs, PASS part of phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-3.4.3, a Gap row); lines read again at mr-pass `b0829a1`. Where: `screen/Shared/Headers.js:12-24`; `screen/Shop/ShopPreview.js:116`; `test/Shop/ShopAccess.spec.js:231`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/markup.rs:124-133`. Fix: `'base-uri': ["'none'"]`, with the spec's expected string.

## PASS authority and public accounts

- **SEC-PSA-02 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Failed logins by someone else end a PASS visitor's delegation.** The framework tells a tarpit lock (`disabled` Y with a `disabledDateTime`) from an account disabled for good, and keeps sessions through a tarpit lock (old map line 51: "so failed logins by someone else cannot sign the owner out"). `authenticateDelegation` tests `disabled === 'Y'` alone, so every call with that account's delegation answers 401 while the flag stands. Where: `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:339-344` (also `:473`, `:511`). Fix: Test "disabled with no `disabledDateTime`" in the three places, or call the framework's own closed-account test. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit b6619e448): `public/Account.js` `authenticateDelegation`, `completeSso` and `exchangeSso` refuse only a closed account (gone, disabled with no `disabledDateTime`, or past its `terminateDate`, the framework's own test), not a tarpit lock. Test `PassDelegationLock.spec.js`.
- **SEC-PSA-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A wrong client secret is not counted on the REST servlet.** The 10-wrong-secrets limit is in the gRPC service only. On REST a secret can be tried at the webapp's general rate, with no log line and no `auth-failure` event. Where: `MM/script/PassApi.js:215-219`; `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:38-51`. Fix: Count a present and wrong secret per address in `clientRegistered` (the `record#Attempt` buckets) and note an auth failure for the web protection. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle bbad4979, ff650805; framework commit 980c8fb55): the authority's REST servlet counts a present and wrong `x-pass-client-secret` per address, ten in ten minutes as the gRPC listener does (`PassApi.js` `checkSecret`, `record#Attempt` in its own transaction, a log line), and an address that has used them up is answered 429 for every call naming a secret until its window ends; each wrong secret also counts toward the web protection's auth-failure ban through a new script member, `ec.web.noteAuthFailure()` (`web::note_auth_failure`, taken by the host after the request). Tests `PassAuthority.spec.js` "the servlet counts wrong client secrets by address", `script/host/tests.rs` `a_script_notes_an_auth_failure_for_the_web_protection`.
- **SEC-PSA-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The gRPC wrong-secret limit is per connection address and stops right secrets too.** Ten calls with any client id and a wrong secret make `throttled` true for that address, and `checked` then refuses every call from it before the secret is looked at, for up to 10 minutes; it can be repeated. The address is the TCP peer, not the client address a trusted proxy names. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/pass_authority/mod.rs:135-147, 190-196`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http/serve.rs:376, 436`. Fix: Count only against calls that fail, and let a call with the right secret through (check the secret first, with the comparison's cost bounded by the count). Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit d40982257, with SEC-CLU-05): wrong secrets count by source (the peer, or the client a believed `client-ip-header` names; `source_guard::source_key`, an IPv6 /64 as one) in a table of at most 10,000 sources, and a source past its 10 in 10 minutes (or a new one while the table is full) is no longer refused unchecked: its calls are checked one at a time across all such sources, waiting at most 5 seconds before `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, so a right secret from the same address gets in (`Authority::admit`). Tests `pass_authority::tests::wrong_secrets_from_one_source_hold_it_until_the_window_ends`, `the_wrong_secret_table_is_bounded`, `a_right_secret_gets_in_from_a_source_past_its_wrong_secrets`.
- **SEC-PSA-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A registered client reads other sites as an anonymous caller.** The call is not refused; it runs as an unregistered caller, so every `GET`-routed operation of the other site answers its public data. Old map line 813 says the local node "serves only the sites" of the client. Over REST the same reads are open to anyone, so this matters where the authority is reached only by gRPC or the link. Where: `MM/script/PassAuthority.js:387-433`; `crates/moqui-core/src/pass_authority.rs:541-553`. Fix: In `run()`, when a client id is given (or `clientVerified`) and the site is not the client's, fail with NotFound. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle bbad4979, mr-pass 03334e5): a registered client's call (a right secret, or a client the transport verified) for a site it does not serve is Not Found, no longer an anonymous read of that site (`PassAuthority.js` `run`); test `PassAuthority.spec.js` "a registered client reads only its own sites". A frontend whose `pass-authority/client[<id>]@sites` misses a site it serves now gets Not Found for that site.
- **SEC-PSA-06 (Low; reported (likely)). Registration and recovery tell whether an account exists.** The answers' text is the same, the work is not. For a new address registration makes an account and sends the verification mail in the call (a synchronous `send#EmailTemplate`); for a claimed address it does nothing. Recovery for a known account locks the row and hashes a reset password (argon2) before answering; for an unknown one it returns at once. An address that is another account's user name but not its `emailAddress` makes `create#UserAccount` fail, answered as 503 "Registration is temporarily unavailable". Where: `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:128-198, 539-549`; `FW/service/org/moqui/impl/UserServices.xml:404-462`. Fix: Send the mail on a service worker after commit (as Marketing's subscription request does, old map line 179) and answer the neutral sentence when the account cannot be made.
- **SEC-PSA-07 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). A multipart POST to the servlet may be 2 GiB.** The gRPC upload stops at 10 MiB before the service runs (`pass_authority.rs:52`, whose comment calls it "the REST API's multipart limit"). On REST the only transport limit is the webapp's `max-upload`, 2 GiB by default; the 10 MiB rule is the forum service's own, applied after the file is spooled. A per-servlet `max-body` does not apply to multipart. Where: `MM/MoquiConf.xml:51-53`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1797-1806`; `FW/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:79`. Fix: Give the servlet a multipart limit of 10 MiB (a `max-upload` on the `servlet` element, or a check of `Content-Length` before the body is read). Fixed 2026-11-16: a `servlet` may say `max-upload` (`web/servlet.rs` `max_upload_for`), which takes the webapp's place for a multipart body, and mr-mantle's `PassApi` servlet says `11m`; a request with no session or framework credential is held to `webapp_max_body` (SEC-RTE-07).
- **SEC-PSA-08 (Low; reported (to check)). A REST download is read whole into the script.** Only a ranged Shop download is streamed in windows. Every other download is `stream.readAllBytes()` and one `response.write(bytes)`, where the gRPC transport sends 64 KiB chunks. A script's heap is 64 MiB (`docs/interfaces/Web-Scripts.md:206`), so a large archive fails or holds that much per request. Where: `MM/script/PassApi.js:153-182`. Fix: Stream every download in windows, as `streamRanged` does.
- **SEC-PSA-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `returnKey` is not a secret name for the request log.** `getPaymentResult` is a GET whose `returnKey` (40 random characters, kept only as a digest, reads a payment attempt's outcome) travels in the query over the REST transport. The secret-name rule redacts names holding `token`, `secret`, `password` and names ending `_key`, `-key`, `.key` or `loginkey`; `returnkey` matches none, so `url.query` keeps it. The 21 GET operations with `token` and `getShopDownload`'s `downloadToken` are redacted. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:3-41`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1591-1604`; `MM/script/PassRoutes.js` `['GET', 'billing/payments/{attemptId}', 'getPaymentResult']`. Fix: Add `returnkey` (or names ending `key` after a lower-case letter) to the rule, or make `getPaymentResult` a POST as the other capability reads are (F75). Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit e5eb4bae2): `returnkey` is a whole secret name (`context::identity` `SECRET_PARAMETER_NAMES`), so the request log's `url.query`, request details and telemetry leave it out. Test `context::tests::generated_secrets_are_32_random_bytes_and_secret_names_are_known`.
- **SEC-PSA-10 (Low; reported (to check)). A gRPC session handle may stay signed in after an account action sent with a delegation.** The bound session's login is cleared only when the answer has a `credential`. `account.security` with a delegation signs the bound session in and answers no credential, so the handle then names a signed-in session until it expires or `account.logout`; `manage#Account` reads `ec.user.userAccount` and would run with no delegation (the password change still asks the old password; `endVisit` ends another visit of the user). Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/pass_authority.rs:556-569, 618-639`; `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:386, 551-572`. Fix: Clear the session's login after every account action that did not leave a sign-in in progress, not only when a credential is answered.
- **SEC-PSA-11 (Low; reported (to check)). Public accounts are ordinary accounts on the business host.** Nothing marks a public account apart from a staff account with no group, so every `ALL_USERS` grant is a grant to the public. Five of them are `AUTHZT_ALWAYS` or reach data by the service's own check alone (`ConstitutionServices`, `DriveDeviceServices`, `XmppClientServices`, `MantleDavServices`, `mantle.party.IdentityServices.*`), `apps/Terminal.xml` with `TerminalServices` opens to every user, and `/mantle/my` answers `setup#UserOrganizationInfo` and the gateway payment paths by a `paymentId` of the request. Each was written with an internal user in mind ("for every signed-in user"). Where: `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:149-159, 255-264`; the `ALL_USERS` rows listed in A6. Fix: Give internal users a group of their own for these grants (BOSS already has `BOSS_USERS`), or refuse an account with no group but `ALL_USERS` at the business host's sign-in and header login outside the PASS servlet.
- **SEC-PSA-12 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `handle#Call` trusts `clientVerified` from its caller.** `clientVerified: true` with a `clientId` makes the caller that registered client with no secret: account actions and token writes with no session, `CLIENT_READS`, and the requester key of the rate limits. Where: `MM/service/mantle/pass/PassAuthorityServices.xml:8-33`; `MM/script/PassAuthority.js:387-396`. Fix: Take the verified client from a value the transport sets in the context (as `passVerifiedClient` is read by `Account.js`), not from a parameter.
- **SEC-PSA-13 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Account action names are looked up in plain objects.** `SSO_ACTIONS['constructor']` is truthy, so the call passes the action test and asks for a service named from a function's text; it fails as an unknown service (503, logged). No service is reached. Where: `MM/script/PassApi.js:318-327`; `MM/script/PassAuthority.js:476, 495`. Fix: `Object.hasOwn(SSO_ACTIONS, action)` and the same for `ACCOUNT_ACTIONS`.
- **SEC-PSA-81 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The authority accepts a client secret of `CHANGEME`, which the frontend treats as unset.** `check#Client` compares the presented secret with `pass-authority/client[<id>]@secret` through `System.propertyMatches`, which refuses only an empty value, so a client whose secret is configured as `CHANGEME`, the conf's placeholder, is accepted with that secret, over REST and gRPC alike. The frontend's `Transport.js` treats an empty or `CHANGEME` `pass@client-secret` as unset and calls no remote authority, and the conf schema marks the client secret `Kept`, not generated, so a `CHANGEME` left in a conf or `.env` is neither replaced at boot nor refused. No shipped conf has a client. Report: the security proofs, PASS part of phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-13.2.3); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:40-55`; `FW/service/org/moqui/public/AccountServices.xml:137-148`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/globals.rs:648-660`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/conf_schema.rs:371-378`; `crates/moqui-core/src/pass_authority.rs:667-686`. Fix: Treat a secret that ends in `CHANGEME` as unset in `propertyMatches`, as `isPropertySet` and `secretCodeHash` do, and log at boot a client configured so.

## PASS application services (Mantle)

- **SEC-PSS-02 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The checkout return key and the cart recovery token are query parameters the request log keeps.** The provider returns the browser to `/Checkout/return?ref=<checkoutId>&key=<return key>` and a recovery mail links `/Recover?r=<token>`. The log's one redaction rule hides a name that contains `token`, `secret`, `password`... or ends in `_key`, `-key`, `.key`; `key` and `r` match none, so both values are logged. The return key reads the checkout's state for a day and can ask for a hosted payment form; the recovery token still needs the cart owner's sign-in. Where: `M/order/ShopCheckout.js:1212`; `M/order/CartRecovery.js:235`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:24-41`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1597`. Fix: Name the parameters `returnToken` and `recoveryToken` (the rule then hides them), or register both routes for redaction. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle 9f62d658, mr-pass a40fb83): a checkout's return key rides as `returnToken` (`ShopCheckout.js` `returnUrl`) and a cart recovery link's token as `recoveryToken` (`CartRecovery.js` `shopLink`), names the log's secret rule redacts; PASS reads the new name first and the old one (`key`, `r`) for links already sent. Tests `mr-pass ShopCheckout.spec.js`, `mr-mantle CartRecovery.spec.js` and `ShopCheckout.spec.js`, client `ShopCheckoutWalk`.
- **SEC-PSS-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A ten-minute download capability is accepted as an order link and can issue its successor.** `issueDownload` makes a `SalesOrder` capability of scope `Download` for the grant's order. `reachDownloads` accepts any `SalesOrder` capability with the `Download` scope as `orderToken`, so the ten-minute token passes it and `issueDownload` then makes a new ten-minute token. The limit of ten minutes holds only if the holder does not renew. The capability also covers every grant of the order, not the one grant asked. Where: `M/product/ShopFulfillment.js:490-506`, `:573-588`. Fix: Give the short capability a scope of its own (`DownloadFile`) that `reachDownloads` does not accept, and bind it to the grant id. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle a8610d69, 7bdb1658): `issue#Download`'s capability has a scope of its own, `DownloadFile`, which `get#DownloadFile` checks and an order link's `reachDownloads` does not accept, so the ten-minute token lists no downloads and issues no successor; test `StoreFulfillment.spec.js` "a guest's digital line ...". Not bound to one grant: the capability row has no field for it, and the order link it comes from already reaches every grant of the order, so it widens nothing.
- **SEC-PSS-04 (Low; reported (to check)). A guest order with a typed email is merged into the account that verifies that email.** The guest's contact email is taken as typed and never verified. `claimGuestPurchases` merges the guest party into the account's party for every completed guest checkout of the store with that email. The account then owns an order it did not place, and whatever `merge#Parties` moves with the guest party (its postal addresses would then be offered as the account's saved addresses at a later checkout). Where: `M/party/ShopCustomer.js:114-160`; `M/order/ShopCheckout.js:677`. Fix: Claim a guest order only when its email was confirmed from the order's own receipt link, or attach the order to the account without merging the guest party's contact data.
- **SEC-PSS-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Anonymous cart creation is limited per requester only.** Each call with no cart makes a `Party`, a `Person`, a `PartyRole`, an `OrderHeader` with its part and item, and a `StoreCartAccess` row. The only limit is 30 a requester in ten minutes; there is no limit per site, as Marketing's anonymous writes have (1,000 an hour). A caller with many addresses fills the party and order tables until the cart expiry job (30 days for a guest) removes them. Where: `M/order/ShopCart.js:155-190`. Fix: Add a per-site hourly cap on cart creation, and make the guest party at checkout contact, not at the first add. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (mr-mantle 90c962f7): a guest's new cart also counts against its site's hour, 1,000 (`ShopCart.js` `createCart`, `record#Attempt` key `cart-site:<siteId>`), as Marketing's anonymous writes do; an account's cart is not counted; test `ShopCart.spec.js` "a site's new guest carts are bounded per hour". One requester over many addresses can now close new guest carts for the hour (the trade-off of any site limit). Fixed 2026-11-17 (the rest; mr-mantle 8ee8d279, docs mr-pass f12c729), the owner's call: the guest cart cap a ProductStore setting (1,000 an hour by default), and no guest Party until checkout. A guest's cart makes no Party, Person or PartyRole (`ShopCart.js` `createCart`: an order with no customer, `StoreCartAccess.guestPartyId` empty); `begin#Checkout` makes the guest party under the order's lock, sets it on the order part and `guestPartyId`, and applies the promotions again (`ShopCheckout.js` `guestParty`). A store's new guest carts an hour are its `PsstGuestCartsHourly` setting (1000; 0 stops them; `record#Attempt` key `cart-store:<store>`, all its sites together, in place of the per-site count). Tests mr-mantle `ShopCart.spec.js` "a guest's cart has no party until its checkout begins ..." and "a store's new guest carts an hour are its setting ...". The party made at begin is one more revision of the cart, so a cart page left open in another window gets STALE_REVISION once the checkout began.
- **SEC-PSS-06 (Low; reported (to check)). A zero-total guest checkout mails any typed address.** A total of zero or less skips the gateway and goes straight to placement. The order's receipt goes to the typed, unverified contact email. The cost of each mail is then one cart (30 a requester in ten minutes). Where: `M/order/ShopCheckout.js:677`, `:1174`. Fix: Limit zero-total guest placements per site and address, or require a verified account when nothing is due.
- **SEC-PSS-08 (Low; reported (to check)). Shop search and catalog reads have no limit at the authority.** Each search reads every published product row of the store (up to 2,000 candidates), runs an index query of 200 hits and matches names in memory. No `throttle` call exists in `ShopCatalog.js`. The query is at most 100 characters and its index syntax is escaped. Where: `M/product/ShopCatalog.js:560-600`. Fix: Count searches against the requester, as cart and review writes are.
- **SEC-PSS-22 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). `createCustomerCase` stages and scans the upload before it checks who asks.** after the per-requester throttle and the site check, the service writes the file (up to 10 MiB) under `pass_media_root/support`, reads it whole to sniff it and calls `pass_scanner_service`, and only then checks the token or the sign-in (`intakeCase` / `askers`). A caller with no credential costs the server a write and a scan per request, 30 in ten minutes per address. The other upload operations check first (`replyCustomerCase` `:947` then `:959`; `addReturnEvidence` `CustomerReturns.js:416` then `:427`; `submitSupplierBill` `Portal.js:486` then `:511`). Where: `request/SupportPortal.js:751-761`. Fix: resolve the form token or the asker before `stageGiven`. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-PSS-21: the upload is staged only after the form token or the asker is checked.
- **SEC-PSS-23 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). A guest's order link (`OrderView`, a read scope) authorizes cancel, return and case writes.** the only scope checked is `OrderView`. The holder can file a cancellation of the order's open lines, which applies at once when the organization's service policy sets `customerImmediateCancel` (off by default, per the comment at `OrderChanges.js:761`), request a return with a chosen remedy, and open cases in the customer's name. The comments record this as intended (Shop P6, F27); it is listed because the link is mailed, long-lived and described elsewhere as a view link. Where: `request/SupportPortal.js:149-166` (`askers`), `order/CustomerOrders.js:133-162`, `order/CustomerReturns.js:287-341`, `request/SupportPortal.js:806-809`. Fix: give the cancel and return writes a scope of their own (or require a sign-in when `customerImmediateCancel` is on), and state in the map that an order link is a write credential. Fixed 2026-11-17 (mr-mantle 9790641e, mr-pass 0652e10 and fb5acae, docs mr-pass f12c729 and mr-boss 785b065a), the owner's call: the guest order link reads, and its cancel, return and case need a one-time code mailed to the order's email. The three order-link writes (`SupportPortal.js` `askers` with `{write: true}`: `request#CustomerCancel`, `request#CustomerReturn`, `create#CustomerCase`) need a `changeCode` that the new `send#OrderChangeCode` mails through `queue#Delivery` to the order's address (its completed Shop checkout's `contactEmail`, else the customer's email); only the code's digest is kept, on a `SalesOrder` capability of the new `OrderChange` scope bound to the link (`unissued`, so no mailed link carries it): eight digits, 15 minutes, used once, the fifth wrong try ending it, a new code ending the earlier ones (`ExternalAccess.js` `openChangeCode`, `mintChangeCode`, `useChangeCode`). A code is sent at most 5 times a requester in ten minutes and 5 an order an hour, and the answer names no address. PASS's NewCustomerCase and RequestReturn ask for the code ("Email me a code" and a code field). Links already mailed keep reading; a signed-in account is unchanged. Test mr-mantle `SupportPortal.spec.js` "an order link's cancel, return and case need the code emailed to the order ..."; the acceptance `ShopOrderScenarios` asks for the code and uses it.
- **SEC-PSS-24 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). The BillUpload page shows the purchase order's current total to a superseded or not-yet-sent revision's link.** `SupplierPortal.js` answers a superseded link nothing of the new revision and, while a revision waits for approval, the order as last sent. `Portal.js` `checkOrder` checks only the `BillUpload` scope, with no revision test, and `orderView` answers the live `orderName` and `grandTotal`. The supplier reads the total of a revision the buyer has not sent. Where: `account/Portal.js:426-438`, `:467-475`. Fix: build the bill page's order summary from `SupplierPortal.js` `sentStateOf`, or drop the total for a link below the supplier's revision. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle a942c9a2): `Portal.js` `orderView` (the bill page's order, `get#Submission`) leaves the live total out for a link of an earlier revision and while a revision the buyer applied waits for its approval (`PurchaseRevisions.js` `awaitingApproval`); test `SupplierPortal.spec.js`.
- **SEC-PSS-25 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). `rateArticle`'s "nonce" is any text the client sends.** `getCustomerArticle` answers a random `nonce` but stores nothing. `rateArticle` accepts any `token` of 24 to 64 characters and dedupes only on the digest of that value and the page, so one visitor records as many ratings and 2,000-character comments as the per-requester limit allows (30 in ten minutes per address), with no limit per site or per article. The ratings feed the knowledge review (`ArticleFeedback`, source `AfsCustomer`). Where: `request/SupportPortal.js:466`, `:472-503`. Fix: sign the nonce (MAC over page, site and an expiry) or keep issued nonces, and add a per-site hourly limit as Marketing's anonymous writes have. Fixed 2026-11-16 (mr-mantle e9f347bf, c822ab91; framework commit e6c120563): `rate#Article` also counts against its site's hour, 500 (`article-rating-site:<siteId>`), beside the requester's 30 in ten minutes, and its nonce is signed: `getCustomerArticle` answers a random view id with `ec.user.serverGrant("article-rating", <site>:<page>:<view>, 3600)`, a new script member that makes `<expiry>.<HMAC-SHA256>` keyed by a key derived for the purpose from `session_cookie_key`, which no script reads, and `rateArticle` takes only a nonce `ec.user.checkServerGrant` accepts. Tests `SupportPortal.spec.js` "a site's article ratings are bounded per hour" and "an article's rating takes only the nonce the server signed ...", `script/host/tests.rs` `a_server_grant_checks_for_its_purpose_and_subject_until_it_expires`.
- **SEC-PSS-26 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). Billing's and the bill upload's writes have no post limit; a signed-in contact has none at all.** `SupplierPortal.js` and `SupportPortal.js` count every post against a per-requester limit; `Portal.js` has no such call. A contact can submit any number of bills, each a new bill number with a 10 MiB file, each making an incoming draft invoice and stored content for AP to review. Each `getInvoice` and `getPaymentResult` with a pending attempt calls the payment gateway. Where: `account/Portal.js:309-360` (`startInvoicePayment`), `:485-554` (`submitSupplierBill`), `:195-206` (`getInvoice`). Fix: call the shared post throttle in `startInvoicePayment`, `submitSupplierBill` and `issueInvoiceAccess`, and cap open submissions per order. Fixed 2026-11-16 in part (mr-mantle 99096aff): `Portal.js` `startInvoicePayment`, `submitSupplierBill` and a contact's `issueInvoiceAccess` count against the requester first (`billing-post`, 60 in ten minutes, Supplier's post limit); test `AccountingPortals.spec.js` "Billing's and the bill upload's posts count against the requester first". Fixed 2026-11-16 (the rest; mr-mantle 0a589dd5, mr-pass 18c1df9), the owner's call: at most five open (not approved or rejected) supplier bills per purchase order. `Portal.js` `submitSupplierBill` refuses (Conflict) a new bill while five of the supplier's submissions for the order are neither approved nor rejected (no invoice yet, or `InvoiceIncoming`/`InvoiceReceived`; `openBillCount`, `OPEN_BILL_LIMIT`); a repeat of an operation or bill number still answers the first, and a declined or accepted bill frees a place. Test mr-pass `Billing/Portals.spec.js` "a sixth bill for an order is refused while five wait for review, and taken once one of them is declined". Outside the call and not done: a minimum gap between gateway lookups of a pending attempt.
- **SEC-PSS-27 (Low; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed). The invoice payment's return key rides in URL parameters the request log does not redact.** the return address is `/InvoicePay/return?attemptId=…&key=…` and the page redirects to `?attemptId=…&key=…`; the authority's operation field is `returnKey` on a GET route. The log's secret-name rule redacts names containing `token` or ending `_key`, `-key`, `.key`; `key` and `returnKey` match neither, so `url.query` keeps the value. The key reads the attempt and the invoice (party names, lines, totals) while the attempt is pending (up to seven days) and for a day after it settles. The key lookup is not counted against any limit and compares digests with `!==`; at 40 random characters that is not a practical guess. Where: `account/Portal.js:336-340`, `:364-382`; `mr-pass/screen/Billing/InvoicePay.xml:39-43`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/identity.rs:24-41`. Fix: name the parameters so the rule redacts them (`returnToken`), or add `key` and `returnkey` to the secret names; answer the return key only the attempt's state, not the invoice.
