Fix: Count among a requirement's authors every user who changed a line or the quote at the revision (the audit log's `changedByUserId` on `OrderItem` since the previous revision).
- **SEC-BAP-114 (Low; reported (to check)). A warehouse receiver opens a customer return against another organization's sales order.** `newReturn` checks the facility, then calls `create#ReturnFromOrder` with the request's order part, which by D13's reading checks only the part's status and copies the part's customer, seller and ship-to address and the order's payment method onto a return at the user's facility. D10 read the same service's find as authorized and so held by the row filter on `OrderPart` (its Found sound: `MantleSetupData.xml:431-432`, `ReturnServices.xml:140-142`); the two readings differ and were not settled. What ReturnDetail then shows the receiver of the other organization's customer, address and payment method was not traced. Reached by a holder of `STOCK_RECEIVE` at a facility, through `/qapps/Warehouse/Returns/Returns/newReturn` with `orderPart=<any order>:<part>&facilityId=<own facility>`. Fix: Refuse an order whose seller is not the facility owner's organization, or not in the user's scope.
- **SEC-BAP-115 (Low; reported (likely)). Commons' id lookup answers a party by an identification number.** When no party has the text as its id or pseudo id, `lookup#ById` finds a `PartyIdentification` by `idValue` (an encrypted field, which a find matches through `encrypt_condition`) and returns its party, and the palette shows the party's name. A user who knows a tax or identity number learns whose it is, for a party their filters admit; another user's private contact is dropped. Reached by any `BOSS_USERS` member through the palette's id lookup (`lookup#Id`, `GET /shellSearch?text=`). The palette rendering is D2's reading. Report: round 3 `D14.md`. Fix: Drop the `PartyIdentification` branch from `lookup#ById`, or keep it for holders of People's or Accounting's identity permission.
- **SEC-BAP-116 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Any user of a facility's organization adds items to its stock requests.** RequestDetail's `addStockRequestItem` calls `add#InventoryRequestItem` with no Warehouse `check#Record` (the transitions around it have one), and `addItem` checks only that the request is a stock request of a facility of one of the user's organizations and is a draft or submitted, so any staff member changes what a submitted request asks for before it is approved. Reached by any `BOSS_USERS` member through `/qapps/Commons/Requests/RequestDetail/addStockRequestItem` (`Commons/Requests.xml:23` mounts the shared screen), and any self-service employee at a RequestDetail mount (`BOSS_PPL_CASES_SELF`). - **SEC-BAP-117 (Low; reported (likely)). An inbound 850 whose lines and charges come to zero or less is approved by the system as it is placed.** The 850 is applied with each line's price as sent (only a negative price is refused) and each SAC as an order item; SAC05 is read as a signed integer, so a charge code with a negative amount lowers the total as an allowance does, with no limit. `place#Order` sets the order Placed and `OrderPlacedAutoApprove` calls `autoApprove#Order`, which approves when the authorized payments reach the grand total; an EDI order has no payment, so a total of zero or less passes. With no `SalesPolicy` approval setting, no Sales rule, no credit policy and not the partner's first order, the order is approved by the system, the 855 records "AD" (accepted as ordered) to the partner and fulfilment follows, though the design says "approving it is the seller's decision" (`X12OrderServices.xml:23-24`). Each line is also stored with `isModifiedPrice` N though its price is the partner's. Not confirmed: that `update#OrderStatus` fires the SECA inside the apply transaction and recomputes the grand total first; the new-customer setting's default per store. Reached by a trading partner whose interchange its EDI remote accepts (`/rest/sm/EdiX12/{remote}` or the SFTP poll), or by anyone SEC-BAP-96 lets post on that remote. Report: round 3 `D15.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/edi/X12Documents.js:120-137`, `:226-236`, `:261-273`, `:297`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/edi/X12Lib.js:131-136`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/OrderReturn.secas.xml:50-58`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/OrderServices.xml:1535`, `:1549`, `:1564`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/OrderInfoServices.xml:922`, `:1009`. Fix: Refuse a sign in SAC05; never auto-approve an `ScEdi` order (or place it with a hold); flag a line whose price differs from the seller's price for the customer.
- **SEC-BAP-118 (Low; reported (likely)). The label host check reads the host with a pattern a backslash defeats, so SEC-BAP-06's host list is passed.** `check#LabelUrl` takes `hostOf(url)`, whose user-info part `[^\/?#@]*@` admits a backslash. For `https://attacker.example\@shippo-delivery.s3.amazonaws.com/l.pdf` it answers the Shippo host, so the URL passes on a production instance; the URL standard (and `reqwest::Url`) ends the authority at `\` in an `https` URL, so the request goes to `attacker.example`. `denyInternal()` still refuses internal addresses; what is lost is the allow-list: the server fetches any public host, prints what it answers or keeps it as the label, and Marble's `getShipmentLabel` streams it to the user's browser through `sendResourceResponse`. Not run; the script client's string reaching `reqwest::Url::parse` was read only at `crates/moqui-lib/src/http.rs:450`, `:505`. Reached by whoever writes `ShipmentPackageRouteSeg.labelUrl` or `returnLabelUrl`: a carrier's answer (`Shippo.js:695`, `:1113`), a Marble administrator through `update#ShipmentPackageAndRouteSeg` (`ShipmentServices.xml:340-357`), or the generic entity tools. Report: round 3 `D15.md`. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/admin/ConfigurationConnections.js:85-88`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/account/GatewayConnections.js:807-818`; callers `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/shipment/ShipmentServices.xml:1980-1986`, `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/ext/shipment/Shippo.js:217-226`, `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/template/shipment/ShipmentTransitions.xml:67-69`, `:87-89`. Fix: Refuse a label URL with `\`, `@` or `%` in the authority or any user info, or take the host from the same parser the client uses.
- **SEC-BAP-119 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Customer Service's drop-downs name any party by id, and another organization's queue agents and case types.** `selected` is labelled with the `partyName` of any party id; `agents` with any `queueId` lists the names of that queue's team; `caseTypes` with any `organizationPartyId` lists that organization's service-enabled types. Reached by any Customer Service user through the `choices` and `caseChoices` transitions. Report: round 3 `D8.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/customerservice/CustomerService.js:762-781`, `:782-797`, `:1031-1041`. Fix: Keep `queueId` and `organizationPartyId` to the user's, and label `selected` only when it is among them.
- **SEC-BAP-120 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A warranty goodwill decision, and covering a claim past its coverage end, need one approver, who may be the claim's own agent.** `WARRANTY_APPROVE` alone decides goodwill or an override, and the decision records the decider as its approver; nothing refuses the user who filed or works the claim. No amount moves: a covered or goodwill repair carries no charge (`:753-754`). Accounting and Sales refuse self-approval by default. Report: round 3 `D8.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/request/Warranty.js:743-747`, `:785-787`. Fix: none suggested by the reviewer.
- **SEC-BAP-121 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A reply's and a public forum reply's draft text travel in the preview redirect's query string.** Preview is a transition whose default response is the screen with the text as parameters, so a draft answer to a customer lands in the address, the browser history and the access logs. Report: round 3 `D8.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/template/CustomerServiceReplies.xml:37-44` (`replyBody`); `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/template/CustomerServiceChannels.xml:25-31` (`forumReplyBody`). Fix: none suggested by the reviewer.
- **SEC-BAP-122 (Info; reported (to check)). mr-marble's wiki expands a space's `publicPageUrl` and `publicAttachmentUrl` as script for every viewer.** D8 found, outside its unit, that mr-marble's wiki screen passes `WikiSpace.publicPageUrl` and `publicAttachmentUrl` to `ec.resource.expand` on each view, so `${...}` in them runs as script for whoever views a page or an attachment of the space, where Customer Service expands the first only on Send to customer (SEC-BAP-87). The field is written by a space's administrator, and a Commons user who creates a space administers it; Commons' `update#SpaceSettings` checks it only against `^https?://[^\s<>"']+$`. A lead for the unit that owns the Marble wiki: who reaches the Marble wiki screens and whether Marble writes the fields by another path were not read. Report: round 3 `D8.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Wiki/wiki.xml:214`, `:337`. Fix: Substitute the named values as text, as SEC-BAP-87's fix would, or refuse `${` in the two fields.
- **SEC-BAP-123 (Info; reported (likely)). An audience import makes each new contact a shared directory party, which every organization's users read.** An unmatched row is created through `import#ContactRow` with `asPersonal: false`, which passes no owner, so the party takes the entity default `ownerPartyId` `_NA_`, which the organization Party filter shows to every organization; one organization's prospect file becomes names and addresses in the shared directory. Reached by a Marketing audience importer; the contacts are then read by any `BOSS_USERS` member of any organization (Commons' read of a shared party was taken from the map). Report: round 3 `D9.md`. Fix: Decide whether imported marketing contacts are the organization's; if so, set `ownerPartyId` to the list's organization.
- **SEC-BAP-124 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A Marketing send's separation of duties counts only its creator and the maker of its last change as authors.** Each material change overwrites `revisedByUserId`, and the authors are `[createdByUserId, revisedByUserId]` and the requester, so a user who wrote the body at one revision and let a colleague make a later change (a schedule) approves or releases the content they wrote. Reached by a user who holds `MARKETING_SEND_EDIT` and `MARKETING_APPROVE` (or `MARKETING_DISPATCH`), which no seeded group holds together but a user in two groups does. Report: round 3 `D9.md`. Fix: Count every user who made a material change since the last approval (from the audit rows or a list kept on the send).
- **SEC-BAP-125 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A list unsubscribe is undone by a consent editor's acceptance dated now.** A subscription request is refused only when its evidence date is not later than the list's or the purpose's latest withdrawal, and a staff acceptance only when the purpose (not the list) is withdrawn. The evidence date defaults to now, so a person who left the list a minute ago is accepted again on the editor's word, with a new `PconGranted`. Reached by a holder of `MARKETING_CONSENT_EDIT` with `MARKETING_AUDIENCE_EDIT`, through `requestSubscriptions` with `accept=Y` or an import that accepts; whether the one-click page's choice reaches this was not traced. Report: round 3 `D9.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/marketing/MarketingAudiences.js:709-729`, `:770-781`, `:835-840`. Fix: Treat a list withdrawal as the purpose's: only the person's own confirmation renews it.
- **SEC-BAP-126 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The Warehouse label preview redirects the browser to a stored label URL with no check (SEC-BAP-06's fix guards the server's fetches only).** When the stored label image is binary or absent and a `labelUrl` (or `returnLabelUrl`) is set, the `labelPdf` transition answers a redirect to it, with neither `check#LabelUrl` nor a scheme test, so the application's origin sends its user to any address. The fetch paths ask `check#LabelUrl`, and mr-marble's `getShipmentLabel` answers 403 when it refuses. Reached by a holder of `SHIPMENT_SHIP` or `SHIPMENT_PACK` who opens a package's label ("View") for a shipment leaving their facility, on a URL written by a carrier's answer, a Marble administrator or the generic tools. Reports: round 3 `D10.md`, `D15.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Warehouse/Shipping/Ship.xml:36-46`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/warehouse/Shipping.js:295-330`. Fix: Ask `check#LabelUrl` before the redirect and answer 403 when it refuses, as `getShipmentLabel` does, or stream the label as the print path does.
- **SEC-BAP-127 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). An unstocked lot is open to, and correctable by, a warehouse user of any organization.** A lot has no owner. One with no stock is every Warehouse user's to open, and `lotScope` lets any adjuster correct it, so a lot another organization made ahead of its receipt (`create#Lot`) has its number, manufacturer or expiry changed by a user of another organization; once it has stock in two organizations' facilities it is refused (`LOT_SHARED`). Reached by a holder of `STOCK_ADJUST` (or `LOT_EXPIRY_EXTEND` to extend) through `Warehouse/Inventory/Lots/correctLot`. Not confirmed: whether a receipt reads an existing lot's expiry by its number. Report: round 3 `D10.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/warehouse/Warehouse.js:365-375`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Warehouse/Inventory/Lots.xml:42-48`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/shipment/StockAdjustments.js:265-277`. Fix: Record the organization that made a lot and limit an unstocked lot's correction to it, or state lots as shared masters in the design.
- **SEC-BAP-128 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A cost-plus price list shows a price reader without cost access the standard cost.** The resolver withholds the base and intermediate amounts of a `standard` rule from a reader without cost read (`hideCost`), but the list detail answers the rule's `percentChange`, `amountChange`, rounding and bounds, and the explanation and the effective price output answer the resulting price, from which the base follows within the rounding increment. Reached by a `PRICE_VIEW` holder without cost read (Commerce manager, merchandiser and operator, the Customer Service groups) reading a list whose rule's base is the approved standard estimate. Report: round 3 `D12.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/PriceResolver.js:565-602`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/PriceLists.js:163-176`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/products/Pricing.js:142-170`. Fix: Withhold the operands of a `standard` rule from a reader without cost read, or accept and document it.
- **SEC-BAP-129 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A promotion rule's numbers are unbounded, so a discount can exceed the line or become a surcharge.** A rule parameter of kind number is only checked to be finite. A `discountPercent` above 100 makes `NewCustomerDiscount` and `BuyGetDiscount` discount more than the line, and a negative percent or `discountAmount` makes the discount line positive; `FlatDiscount` caps a positive amount at the line but not a negative one. The effect on totals and payment was not traced. Report: round 3 `D12.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/StorePromotions.js:185-197`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/PromotionServices.xml:499`, `:614`, `:698-702`. Fix: Bound percents to 0-100 and amounts to zero or more in `store#PromotionParameters` (the rule's own parameter definition can carry the bounds).
- **SEC-BAP-130 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The record files panel streams a product content row's stored location with no location rule, a second reader of SEC-PSS-82's location.** The panel's `download` sends `get#RecordFile`'s `contentLocation` through `ec.web.sendResourceResponse` for a `ProductContent` row of a product the user sees, where Products' document screens apply Mantle's `owned` rule (`Content.js:434`). A row's location is written by Mantle's `create#ProductContent` (no permission check, refusing only a download's foreign location) and `update#ProductContent` (no permission or owner check, keeping a given location for every type but `PcntDownload`), both reached by mr-marble's Catalog screens, and BOSS's clone copies the base product's rows as they are; no BOSS path takes a location. D12 states that SEC-PSS-82's fix has to cover `get#RecordFile` too, and gave this no severity of its own. Reached by a signed-in BOSS user who sees the product, at any root's `RecordFiles` mount (Products' at `Products.xml:30`). Report: round 3 `D12.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Shared/RecordFiles.xml:138-147`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/shared/RecordFilesServices.xml:471`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/Content.js:156-209`, `:434`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/ProductServices.xml:932-966`. Fix: In `get#RecordFile` apply `Content.js`'s `owned` rule to a `ProductContent` row, as part of SEC-PSS-82's fix.
- **SEC-BAP-131 (Info; reported (to check)). A price change is not refused for a line on a draft invoice that is not a pack invoice.** A line billed on a draft invoice before shipment (Accounting's `invoiceFromSource`) takes the new price while the draft keeps the old one; the price branch does not read `billedInProcess`. Report: round 3 `D13.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/SalesOrderChanges.js:290-327`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/OrderChanges.js:288-294`. Fix: Refuse `OclPrice` when `billedInProcess` is above zero outside the released pack invoices.
- **SEC-BAP-132 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Purchasing and return exception decisions accept a missing revision.** `decide#PurchaseApproval` and a direct `decide#ReturnException` decide the current revision when none is given, and the bulk approval falls back to the current revision when the form names none; Sales' `decide#Quote` and `approve#SalesOrder` require it. The BOSS forms post it, so only a direct caller decides blind. Report: round 3 `D13.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/PurchaseOrders.js:545`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/purchasing/Orders.js:499`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/ReturnPolicies.js:1060-1063`. Fix: Require the revision, as Sales does.
- **SEC-BAP-133 (Info; reported (likely)). Sales' change filing keeps any request id as the change's case.** Any existing `Request` id is stored as the change's case; Customer Service's branch checks the case and Sales' does not. Nothing found reads across organizations through it (`find#OrderChanges` by case is still filtered by the order's organization). Report: round 3 `D13.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/OrderChanges.js:663-664`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Shared/OrderChangeDetail.xml:47-50`. Fix: Check that the case is a support case of the order's organization.
- **SEC-BAP-134 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The Google place search writes the API key to the log.** The key, from the `google.cloud.api.key` preference or process property, is a query parameter, and `get#MapsPlaceSearchText` logs the built URI at warn on every call (the other two services have the line commented out). No screen, REST resource, job, agent tool or service names the three Google services; a direct service call (Tools' service runner) or a future caller reaches them. Report: round 3 `D15.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/ext/party/GooglePartyServices.xml:94`, `:117-127`. Fix: Delete the log line; send the key as the `X-Goog-Api-Key` header.
- **SEC-BAP-135 (Info; reported (to check)). EDI profile rows name a script file and a service, and the generic entity tools write them.** `parseSet` loads `EdiProfileDocument.grammarLocation` with `EdiHandler.loadEnvelope`, which evaluates the file as a script in the calling engine, here inside `X12.js` with authorization disabled; the location may be any component path, a `file:` path or a plain path. `mappingServiceName` is called for each document with authorization disabled. `EdiProfileDocument` is in none of the restricted lists of `sensitive.rs`, unlike the ETL mappings and the instance host rows whose stored code the same rule keeps from the generic tools (SEC-TLS-02), and no `Security.eecas.xml` rule covers it (SEC-SCR-06's fix). What a writer can place on disk to name was not traced. Reached by a writer of `mantle.edi.EdiProfileDocument`: `ADMIN` through the generic entity tools or entity REST; no Mantle screen or service writes it. Report: round 3 `D15.md`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/entity/EdiEntities.xml:85-86`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/edi/X12Lib.js:118-122`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/edi.rs:105-110`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/locate.rs:104-119`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/edi/X12.js:972`, `:1499`. Fix: Add the EDI profile entities to the service-written list, or accept only a `component://.../edi/x12/` grammar and a mapping service that implements the profile's interface.
- **SEC-BAP-136 (Low; confirmed (second check)). PayTrace's cached token is sealed with a home-made keystream of one fast hash of the API password, so whoever reads the cache tests password guesses offline.** The adapter keeps its OAuth access token in the cache `mantle.ext.account.gateway.PayTrace.accessToken` XORed with a keystream whose blocks are `ec.user.singleUseCodeHash("paytrace-token|" + password + "|" + nonce + "|" + block)`: one SHA-256 (the conf's hash type) with the fixed salt of single-use codes, no key stretching and no MAC. The sealed hex and its nonce sit in the cache entry, which any server script reads through `ec.cache` and which System › Cache › Cache Elements shows to its users by the cache's name (the adapter's comment counts on that view showing only hex), and `unseal` takes a result as the token when every byte is printable. So a reader of the entry tests a guess of the remote's API password with one hash and a printable check, offline and at hash speed, and recovers the password, which signs in to PayTrace with the merchant's rights; a token's own life is short, the password's is not. Report: the security proofs, phase P7 (ASVS v5.0.0-11.2.1, 11.3.2, 11.3.3, 11.4.4); lines read again at mr-mantle `4314cc87`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/ext/account/gateway/PayTrace.js:181-208`, `:228-229`, `:264-265`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/factor.rs:32`, `:135-137`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/Cache/CacheElements.xml:41`, `:65-66`. Fix: Keep the token in clear in a cache the framework hides from the cache screens and from scripts other than the adapter, or seal it with the framework's field encryption (an AEAD under the instance key), never with a key derived from the password.
- **SEC-BAP-137 (Low; confirmed (second check)). Sales Summary loads Chart.js 2.9.3 from cdnjs with no `integrity` in its html and vuet modes.** mr-boss's `SalesSummaryStats.fancy` writes `<script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/Chart.js/2.9.3/Chart.min.js">` whenever the render mode is not qvt, and mr-marble's Accounting report does the same in html and vuet. The script has no `integrity` and BOSS pages send no CSP that names script sources, so a change at the CDN, or anyone who can serve that address, runs script in a signed-in page that shows the organization's sales figures. The 2.x line is no longer maintained, and 2.9.3 has the prototype pollution fixed in 2.9.4 (CVE-2020-7746), which needs chart options from outside and is not reached here; the framework ships Chart.js 4.5.1, which the qvt branch uses. Report: the security proofs, phase P7 (ASVS v5.0.0-3.7.1, 14.2.3); lines read again at mr-boss `c8c6e27e` and mr-marble `4c52d22`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Sales/Reports/SalesSummaryStats.fancy:16-18`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Accounting/Reports/SalesSummaryStats.html.fancy:2-4`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/libs/chartjs/chart.umd.min.js`. Fix: Load the framework's own Chart.js in every mode (and port the two charts to its 4.x API), or at least pin 2.9.4 with an `integrity` and `crossorigin`.
- **SEC-BAP-139 (Info; likely (second check)). The shipment load filter quotes category ids into SQL after refusing only `'`, so on MySQL a category id ending in a backslash breaks out of its quote.** `make#ShipmentLoadOrderConditions` splits the request's `inventoryCategoryId` on commas, refuses an id that contains `'` or that names no `ProductCategory`, and joins the rest as `'<id>'` into an `IN (...)` of a raw condition. On MySQL, where a backslash escapes in a string literal unless the server runs with `NO_BACKSLASH_ESCAPES` (the drivers set no `sql_mode`), an existing category id that ends in `\` escapes its closing quote and the next id's text becomes SQL. It needs a category with such an id, made by someone who may name category ids, and a Warehouse user to send it; on Postgres and the embedded database a backslash is a plain character. Not confirmed: whether the entity layer refuses a backslash in a primary key, and which screens pass `inventoryCategoryId`; not run. Report: the security proofs, phase P7 (ASVS v5.0.0-1.2.4); lines read again at mr-mantle `4314cc87`. Where: `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/work/ShipmentWorkServices.xml:176-210`. Fix: Pass the ids as bound parameters (an `econdition` with `operator="in"` on a view, or `makeConditionWhere` with parameters), or refuse any id that is not letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`.

## Clients

- **SEC-CLI-02 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). The service worker keeps any same-origin GET that has a `v` parameter, cache first, with no expiry.** `cacheable` takes `/libs/` paths and any URL with a `v` query parameter. An answer that is 200, same-origin and without `private` or `no-store` is put in `moqui-shell-v1` and served from there on every later request, to whoever uses that browser, signed in or not. Screen renders are marked `private, no-store` and are not kept; other signed-in answers (a REST or web script answer, a file download) were not checked. Where: runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/sw.js:28. Fix: Keep only paths the server marks as static (`/libs/`, and versioned files under known static prefixes) and require `Cache-Control: public`; never keep an answer to a request that carried credentials for a screen, REST or download path. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): the worker keeps a `GET` with no `Authorization` header only for a file under `/libs/`, or a static file by its extension (script, style, map, font, image, wasm) with a `v` parameter outside the signed-in paths (`/rest/`, `/rpc/`, `/apps/`, `/qapps/`, `/dav/`, `/git/`, `/xmpp-upload/`, `/s/`, `/u/`, `/r/`); it keeps only a whole 200 answer of type `basic` whose `Cache-Control` names `public` and none of `private`, `no-store` or `no-cache` (`sw.js` `cacheable`, `keepable`). The cache is now `moqui-shell-v2`, so activating the worker drops what v1 kept. A versioned `.qvue` bundle under `/apps/` is no longer the worker's; the browser's own cache still keeps it (`public, immutable`). Client spec `WebrootServiceWorker`.
- **SEC-CLI-03 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); deferred). The main window's navigation check in node modes passes every scheme but five.** `is_external` returns true only for foreign `http`/`https` and for `mailto`, `tel`, `sip`; every other scheme (`file:`, `data:`, `blob:`, `chrome:`, the app's own `moqui-shell`/`moqui-app`) is "not external" and `stays_in_app` allows the navigation. The client-mode windows use a stricter rule (`goes` answers `Nowhere`). In the in-process mode the `node-bridge` capability is granted to local pages of window `main`, not to an origin. Where: crates/moqui-desktop/src/lib.rs:234. Fix: Allow only the splash origin, the node's origin and `about:blank`; refuse every other scheme as the client windows do. Deferred 2026-11-16: `crates/moqui-desktop` and `crates/moqui-client-tauri` are outside the workspace and need the CEF runtime, so no change could be built or tested, and the fix changes which navigations the main window takes (a `blob:` or `data:` frame, a PDF view). Proposed: `is_external`/`stays_in_app` allow only the splash origin, the node's origin and `about:blank`, open `mailto:`, `tel:`, `sip:` and foreign `http(s)` outside and refuse every other scheme, as the client windows' `goes` answers `Nowhere` (a test in `splash_tests.rs`); question: build the desktop app on a machine with CEF to make it, or leave it for the desktop work?
- **SEC-CLI-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); deferred). In the apps the server's default CSP is dropped whole, `form-action` and `frame-ancestors` with it.** The default screen policy is `frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self';`. It has no script directive, so `has_script_policy` is false and the scheme handlers do not pass the header to the page. In the apps a server page therefore runs with no CSP, where an injected form could post elsewhere. On the web the same default policy names no `script-src`, so it never limits injected script; the sanitizers are the only barrier. Where: crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/scheme.rs:319, crates/moqui-desktop/src/node/scheme.rs:195, crates/moqui-core/src/context/factory.rs:93. Fix: Pass the server's other directives through and add only the script directive the runtime needs, or give server pages in the apps a fixed policy with `form-action 'self'`; consider a `script-src` for the web shell. Deferred 2026-11-16: `crates/moqui-desktop` and `crates/moqui-client-tauri` are outside the workspace and need the CEF runtime, so no change could be built or tested, and the fix changes the policy every server page runs under in the apps. Proposed: `moqui_client_tauri::scheme` passes the server's CSP through when it has no script directive and appends only the one the runtime needs, or gives server pages a fixed `form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'` when the server sends none; question: build the desktop app on a machine with CEF to make it, or leave it for the desktop work?
- **SEC-CLI-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); deferred). A node page can set where OS mail and call links open, and it is kept across runs.** `set_comms_root` accepts any `/qapps/<letters, digits, _, -, />` path and writes it to the file `comms-root`. From then on, and on later runs, every `mailto:`, `tel:` and `sip:` link the OS hands the app opens `<that root>/Inbox/Compose` or `/Calls/Dialer` with the link's parameters, on a screen path the script chose. Where: crates/moqui-desktop/src/bridge.rs:288, crates/moqui-desktop/src/os/mod.rs:214. Fix: Take the comms root from the node's configuration through the host (the in-process node has it; the sidecar can answer it on an authenticated route), not from a page. Deferred 2026-11-16: `crates/moqui-desktop` and `crates/moqui-client-tauri` are outside the workspace and need the CEF runtime, so no change could be built or tested, and the fix changes a new authenticated route on the sidecar. Proposed: the comms root comes from the node (the in-process node's conf through the host, the sidecar's from an authenticated route the host calls), `set_comms_root` goes, and `comms-root` is written only from that answer; question: build the desktop app on a machine with CEF to make it, or leave it for the desktop work?
- **SEC-CLI-06 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). On mobile `scheme_fetch` trusts the webview's URL and the caller's own headers.** The shell's commands need the load nonce, but `scheme_fetch` decides shell or app origin from `webview.url()` at call time and takes `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` and `Sec-Fetch-Dest` from the caller's `headers` map. A server page still running while the host navigates to the shell (`client_show_shell` is callable by any page) can have its request answered as the shell's, which includes the offline answer of the chat store passphrase. The proxy's cross-site refusal reads headers the page wrote. Where: crates/moqui-mobile/src/scheme.rs:173. Fix: Require the shell nonce for a `scheme_fetch` answered as the shell's origin, and have the host set `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-*` itself.
- **SEC-CLI-07 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The shell's commands in the desktop client are not bound to an account.** The desktop's `is_shell_page` is the default `true`; the capability `client-shell` on window `main` is the only gate. The shell shows data of the current account's server (chat, mail) and holds `client_switch_account`, `client_remove_account`, `client_sign_in` and forwarded requests with each account's key, so script injected through one server's data would reach the user's accounts on other servers. The shell's CSP (`script-src` of its own paths, no inline script, no `eval`) is the barrier. Where: crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/lib.rs:96, crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/windows.rs:95. Fix: Keep the CSP as is and add the mobile app's load nonce to the desktop shell commands, so a second line holds if a `data:` or `blob:` page is ever shown in the main window.
- **SEC-CLI-08 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A personal account's mail and chat stay on disk in plain SQLite after sign-out.** The offline store keeps mail threads and messages, chat events, drafts and outbox files in `data/<account>.db` and `data/<account>.files`, mode `0600` under a `0700` directory, not encrypted by the app. `signed_out` deletes the store only for a shared-mode account; a personal account keeps everything until "remove account". A development run keeps device keys in a `0600` file instead of the OS key store. Where: crates/moqui-client/src/store/mod.rs:21, crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/commands.rs:575. Fix: Say so at sign-out and offer "sign out and delete"; consider sealing the store with a key from the OS key store.
- **SEC-CLI-09 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The single-instance socket is restricted only after it is bound.** `UnixListener::bind(path)` makes the socket with the umask's mode, and the next line sets `0600`, ignoring an error. The directory is made with `create_dir_all` and the default mode. A process that connects can only send the four link schemes the OS can send anyway. Where: crates/moqui-desktop/src/os/instance.rs:137. Fix: Create the directory `0700` before binding (the client crate's `create_private_dir` does this).
- **SEC-CLI-85 (Low; reported (to check)). A server's answer on the shell's origin can carry `Clear-Site-Data`, which would wipe what the shell keeps for every account.** The proxy drops ten response headers and every `access-control-*` one; `Clear-Site-Data` is not among them, and `answer` copies the rest onto the shell's response. The shell's origin holds every account's page data, the chat crypto stores among them (prefixed per account in one origin, `WebrootCrypto.js:101-102`). A `Clear-Site-Data: "storage"` on one server's answer to a data request the shell forwards (`/shellData`, `/qapps/clientConfig`, the comms transitions) would delete the other accounts' crypto devices on this device, which then need their key backup to read old encrypted messages. Confirmed in code that the header reaches the page; whether CEF and Android's WebView act on it for an answer of the app's scheme handler is to check. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/proxy.rs:61-72`, `:232-247`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/scheme.rs:318-333`. Fix: Pass the server's response headers by an allow-list (content type, caching, range, `Location`, `Content-Disposition`, a CSP on the app origin), or at least drop `clear-site-data`, `service-worker-allowed`, `refresh` and `link` on the shell's origin.
- **SEC-CLI-86 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). One server's clock decides when another account's disappearing messages leave the device.** `learn_server_time` stores the server's time less the device's in one process-wide static, from whichever account's chat answer or chat changes feed page came last, and the sweep and the offline answers of every account use it. Server A sending `serverNow: 1` sets "now" to near 1970, so the user's account on server B, while B cannot be reached, keeps and shows its disappearing messages past their `expireDate` (`without_due` keeps every event due after now); a far-future value has B's local copies removed early. B's own answers reset it once B is reached; how long such a state lasts was not traced. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/offline.rs:213-216`, `:255-276`, `:285-297`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sync/feeds/chat.rs:93-95`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sync/push.rs:73`. Fix: Keep the offset per account (in `Client`, or in the account's store), and ignore a value far from the device's clock (more than a day either way) as the server's error.
- **SEC-CLI-87 (Low; reported (likely)). A download's file name keeps Unicode format characters such as a right-to-left override.** `attachment_name` strips directories, control characters (`char::is_control`, general category Cc only) and the characters Windows refuses, but keeps U+202E and the other bidirectional controls (category Cf), so `invoice\u{202E}fdp.exe` is saved and listed as `invoiceexe.pdf`. The server percent-encodes the whole UTF-8 name in `filename*` (`crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1426-1436`), which the client prefers and decodes, so the account's server, or another user of it whose uploaded file's name the server sends, sets the name. Not confirmed: how the OS shows the name; the desktop saves an attachment through the save dialog, to the path the user picks (`download.rs`, read by `B14.md`). Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/proxy.rs:308-353`. Fix: Map Unicode format characters (U+200E, U+200F, U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069) to `_` in `attachment_name`.
- **SEC-CLI-88 (Low; reported (likely)). Any app on the phone can make the app open a server path of the current account in its shell.** Any installed app can send an explicit `ACTION_MAIN` intent to the exported `org.moqui.mobile/.MainActivity` with an int `NotificationId` and the notification object extra `{"extra":{"link":"/…"}}`. The notification plugin treats any such intent as a tap on one of its notifications and fires `actionPerformed` with the JSON it was handed; the app's `on_action` takes `extra.link`, keeps it if it is a server path, and the shell opens it, bringing the app to the front. The room, call and dial links are signed or carry a nonce, so another app's link opens nothing; this one is neither. Not confirmed: not run; the plugin's Rust side that turns `notification` into `extra()`, and what the shell's `setUrl` does with a path outside its comms screens, so whether a GET with an effect can be reached this way. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/src/lib.rs:196-203`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/commands.rs:184-189`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/host.rs:321-331`; `tauri-plugin-notification` 3.0.0-alpha.3 `NotificationPlugin.kt:124-131`, `TauriNotificationManager.kt:58-81`. Fix: Put an HMAC of the link (the room links' key) in the `notify` extra and take a tap only when it checks, or ignore `on_action` and route taps through the signed room-link intent.
- **SEC-CLI-89 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A photo taken with the camera from a page stays in the app's external files folder.** A capture from a page's file input (the chat composer's camera input, `Chat.qvue:1379`) writes `JPEG_<time>_….jpg` to `getExternalFilesDir(DIRECTORY_PICTURES)` and hands its URI to the page; nothing deletes it, so every photo taken in the app, a view-once photo too, stays on shared storage, outside the app's private files that `allowBackup="false"` and the sealed store protect. Another app with storage permission reads it on Android 8 and 9 (minSdk 26), and anyone can over USB. The `FileProvider` is declared over the whole external root and the whole cache folder. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/kotlin/RustWebChromeClient.kt:366-386`, `:502-508`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/res/xml/file_paths.xml:3-4`. Fix: Write captures to `getCacheDir()/captures` (a `cache-path` of that folder only), delete each after the page took it or at the next start, and drop the `external-path` entry.
- **SEC-CLI-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). On the phone every page and delegated frame that asks gets the camera, microphone and location with no prompt.** The app sets no permission handler on its webview, so each requested resource is answered DEFAULT, and the patched wry's `filterKnownPermissions` grants audio, video, protected media id and MIDI sysex; geolocation is granted to any origin when the OS permission is held (`callback.invoke(origin, true, false)`). A server's page on `http://moqui-app.localhost`, or a cross-origin frame it embeds with `allow="camera; microphone; geolocation"`, gets them once the app holds `RECORD_AUDIO`, `CAMERA` or location (it asks for the microphone after the first sign-in, `MainActivity.kt:335-376`), so a third-party widget a server page frames gets the microphone silently. The desktop allows media only to the node's or the client's own origins (`media_allowed`). Not confirmed: not run; Chromium's Permissions Policy default (`self`) is what keeps an undelegated frame out. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/src/lib.rs:261-286`; `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/binding.rs:510-512`; `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/kotlin/RustWebChromeClient.kt:60-81`, `:250-288`, `:290-297`. Fix: Set a permission handler on the one webview that allows media and location only to the main frame of the two app origins (`request.origin`), as the desktop does.
- **SEC-CLI-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The desktop app runs the first runtime it finds in its working directory or beside its executable.** Before its bundled runtime, a release build takes `runtime` in the working directory, `runtime` beside the executable or AppImage, or that folder itself, as soon as it has a `components/base` directory; only `MOQUI_RUNTIME` and a development run come first. The node loads those components, whose services and scripts run as the user, with the node's database and keys. Another local user who can write the folder the app is started in (a shared `/tmp` as the working directory) or the folder holding the AppImage reaches it; nothing checks the owner or mode of the found folder. Not confirmed: the working directory a desktop launcher gives the app (on macOS it is `/`, and the executable is inside the signed bundle). Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/runtime_dir.rs:81-89`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/lib.rs:443-449`; `crates/moqui-host/src/lib.rs:947-953`. Fix: In a release build use only `MOQUI_RUNTIME` and the bundled runtime, or take a found runtime only when it and its parents are owned by the user and not group- or world-writable.
- **SEC-CLI-92 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Any signed-in user stores rich-text images with no count, owner or removal.** Each `POST /richTextImage` (multipart `contentFile`) decodes the image, stores its bytes under a new random id in `dbresource://moqui/rich-text/` and answers the URL. Nothing records who uploaded it, counts a user's images or their total, or removes one that no text uses; the only bounds are 8 MiB an image and the per-client rate limit (`<rate-limit name="all" requests="50" seconds="1" burst="500"/>`), so one account can fill the database. Not confirmed: whether a PASS public account can reach the root webroot's `/richTextImage`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/images.rs:268-289`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/webroot.xml:239-249`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:589`. Fix: Record the uploader and size, cap each user's images and bytes, and remove images no stored text names after a grace period.
- **SEC-CLI-93 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). Text from outside the screen model reaches the terminal with its control characters on every writer but the session encoder (SEC-CLU-87's statement that every other text goes through `clean_text` does not hold).** `Painter::put` and `set_message` clean nothing, and the painter skips only what its own width table calls zero-width; C0 and C1 controls are one column there, so each lands in a cell. The SSH and browser-terminal loop's encoder writes a space for it. In `moqui tui` and `moqui tui --server` Revue's writer prints it; Revue gives a control no width, so the next cell is preceded by a cursor move, which breaks an `ESC`-led sequence but still delivers single bytes (BEL, BS, a scrolling LF, SO and SI, ENQ, C1 code points). The text answer of `agent-apps`, `moqui tui --agent` and the LLM screen tool, and `--snapshot`, join cells without moves, so a whole sequence (an OSC 52 clipboard write, a title, an OSC 8) survives; the agent's JSON answer escapes C0 but not DEL or C1. The bidirectional overrides and isolates pass every writer. The sources are a drop-down's option labels, menu titles, a transition's messages, a notification title (SEC-CLU-87), palette search hits, a component's JSON (board cards, calendar events, report cells), server error text, the Assist thread, and with `--serve` any logged text, an anonymous SSH username included (SEC-FIL-92). Not confirmed: what each terminal does with a lone control byte, and whether agent consumers show the output on a terminal. Where: `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/paint.rs:195-204`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/layout.rs:1944-1953`; revue 3.15.0 `render/terminal/render.rs:84-92`, `:188-191`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/encode.rs:145`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/agent.rs:1533`; `crates/moqui-host/src/tui_cmd.rs:161`, `:263`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/agent.rs:275`. Fix: Clean once where text becomes a cell: drop the `is_unsafe` characters in `Painter::put`, so every writer, the agent text, the snapshot and the copy get cleaned cells; failing that, make the agent answer and the snapshot apply `clean_text` per line and the local loop write through `encode.rs`.
- **SEC-CLI-94 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A mouse selection copies the cells' control characters to the clipboard, a bracketed-paste end marker included.** The selection's text is read from the frame's cell characters; `copy_text` cuts it to 64 KiB and nothing else, and the loop writes it as OSC 52. Over SSH the user sees a space where `ESC` is, but the clipboard holds `ESC [ 201 ~`, which ends a shell's bracketed paste early, so a newline after it runs what follows when the user pastes into a shell. Reached by whoever writes text into one of the sources SEC-CLI-93 lists, against a user who drags over it (or double or triple clicks it). Record text cannot start a copy: OSC 52 is written only after a user's action. Not confirmed: which terminals filter control characters from a paste. Where: `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/mod.rs:262-268`, `:304-321`, `:436-452`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/session/mod.rs:1116-1146`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/encode.rs:195-200`. Fix: The painter-level cleaning of SEC-CLI-93 covers it; else `clean_text` the text in `copy_text`.
- **SEC-CLI-95 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Removing an account can leave its whole old store in `data/<account>.db.old`.** A store of an earlier schema revision is moved to `<db>.old` and copied; when a table does not copy, the old file stays (it is removed only when every table copied). `forget` removes the database, its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files and the files directory, not `.old`, so "remove account" leaves the old chat and mail on disk (mode `0600` in the `0700` data directory) for someone with the device's files. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/store/mod.rs:479-505`, `:565-623`. Fix: Have `forget` remove `<db>.old` and its `-wal` and `-shm` files too.
- **SEC-CLI-96 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A rich-text image is stored and served as uploaded, with its metadata.** The image is decoded only to check it; the original bytes are kept and sent ("The original bytes are kept", `ResourceServices.xml:46`), so a phone photo's EXIF (GPS position, device, time) reaches every reader of a message or text it was pasted or taken into. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/images.rs:271-288`. Fix: Re-encode the decoded image, which drops EXIF, or strip the metadata segments.
- **SEC-CLI-97 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Four mobile commands are checked by the last load's origin only, not the shell's nonce.** `chat_room_shown`, `push_check`, `push_check_fix` and `push_test` call `shell_only`, which asks only whether the last load begun is on the shell's origin; every other shell command takes the nonce of the shell's current load. A server page reaches them in the moment a navigation to the shell has begun (it can start one itself with `client_show_shell`), through IPC posted before its document goes. `push_check_fix("firebase")` unregisters every UnifiedPush instance and forgets the distributor; `chat_room_shown` hides a room's pushes. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/src/commands.rs:170`, `:197`, `:212`, `:238`, `:244-251`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/host.rs:652-654`. Fix: Take `webview` and `nonce` in these four and use `is_shell_page`, as `call_state` does.
- **SEC-CLI-98 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The updater does not require the signed version.** The feed's answer is not signed; the plugin checks the artifact's minisign signature with the built-in key and, by default, that the announced version is greater than the running one. The check that the signed version equals the announced one applies only when the signature carries a version, and `requireSignedVersion`, which refuses a signature without one, is off, so whoever answers the update feed could serve an artifact ever signed by a CLI that wrote no version under a newer version number (a downgrade). Where: `scripts/moq/desktop.sh:366-367`; `tauri-plugin-updater` 3.0.0-alpha.3 `src/config.rs:134-135`, `src/updater.rs:1932-1940`. Fix: Add `"requireSignedVersion": true` to the generated `plugins.updater`.
- **SEC-CLI-99 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A web page can switch the phone app's current account through the join link.** Any web page or app can open `org.moqui.mobile:/join?host=<host>&token=<16-64 characters>` (a `BROWSABLE` filter). With an account on that host that is not the current one, the app switches to it (a reload of the shell) and shows the join page of a token the page chose; the person must still press Join. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:83-88`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/host.rs:358-375`. Fix: Ask before switching (show the invite in the current account's shell with "Open in <account>").
- **SEC-CLI-100 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A signed-out account's chat notifications and conversation shortcuts stay on the phone.** At sign-out the push registration, the dial account and the UnifiedPush instance go, but the account's shown notifications (sender, snippet) are not cleared, and each room's long-lived dynamic shortcut (its name) is never removed, for the next person who uses a shared device. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/src/host.rs:741-750`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/ChatNotifications.kt:367-384`. Fix: At sign-out call `ChatNotifications.clearAll(account)` and remove the account's `chat-<account>-*` shortcuts.
- **SEC-CLI-101 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). In sidecar mode, or with the tray's ports on, any local user may create the desktop node's first administrator until the owner does (the rest of SEC-CLI-01).** The node sets `user-facade/login@initial-admin-enabled` true on every start, and `initial_admin_local` takes any request with a loopback peer and no forwarding header as this machine's, which every account of the computer is. SEC-CLI-01's fix (allowed hosts, and the first administrator only from this machine or with the setup secret) stops a remote caller only; on a machine with several users, another one who reaches the node's port before the owner finishes setup makes the administrator of the owner's node. The Clients map says so; no entry held it. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-AUTH-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/node/mod.rs:133-141`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/setup.rs:40-68`. Fix: In the node modes have the app make the first administrator from its first-run window, or ask every caller, local ones included, for the setup secret, which the app can read. Fixed 2026-11-19 in part (commit a4847bbce): `user-facade/login@initial-admin-local` `in-process` makes only a request of the server's own process this machine's, and work with no request only one with no client address (not a loopback SSH peer); the node in process sets it (`node::setup_properties`), so another account of the computer that reaches the tray's ports needs the setup secret, and the window, which comes through the scheme, is unchanged. Open: the sidecar, whose window is a loopback client like any other, until the app hands it the secret (in the environment, not as an argument). Tests `user::setup` `in_process_only_a_request_of_the_servers_own_process_is_local`; the desktop's `node::tests` setup properties of each mode. Fixed 2026-11-19, the rest (commit 0f95c41e6): the sidecar takes only its own process as this machine too, and the app makes the setup secret once a run (an app password's form), gives it to the sidecar as `user-facade/login@initial-admin-secret` (a secret of the conf) in its environment, never its arguments, and to its window's Login form through the bridge (`node_setup_secret`, which answers only for a sidecar; `bridge.js` fills the field); a secret the conf gives is the server's, and the log names where it is set, not the secret. Tests `user::setup` `a_setup_secret_the_conf_gives_is_the_one_taken`, the desktop's `node::tests` (the setup properties of each mode, one secret a run) and `node::sidecar::tests` (the secret in the environment, not the arguments), `bridge_tests` (the ACL grants `node_setup_secret` to the node's origin). Not run on a sidecar build.
- **SEC-CLI-102 (Low; likely (second check); fixed in part). The app schemes get no `Sec-Fetch-*` and the proxy passes no `Referer`, so a GET a cross-origin frame in a server page starts runs a writing transition as the user.** The phone's WebView and the desktop's CEF hand a custom scheme's handler no `Sec-Fetch-*` headers (the code says so for both). The proxy's `cross_site` then rests on `Origin` alone, which a navigation or a no-cors GET does not send, and `REQUEST_HEADERS` does not pass `Referer` on, so the server's `started_by_another_site` (SEC-WEB-04's rule) sees neither and lets the GET through; the proxy adds the device key. A frame of another origin inside a server page (the server's CSP, `frame-ancestors` and `frame-src` included, is dropped in the apps, SEC-CLI-04) can start a nested frame or an image whose URL is a transition that is not read-only, with no query string, on `http://moqui-app.localhost`, and it runs; `X-Frame-Options` stops only its display. On the desktop's in-process node, `navigation` lets any cross-site document GET through by design, the node's session cookie goes with it, and the frame can withhold `Referer`. Reached by the origin of any frame a server's page embeds. Not run on a build. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PLATFORM-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/proxy.rs:35-52`, `:201-206`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/scheme.rs:129-131`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/node/scheme.rs:105-116`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:658-675`. Fix: Refuse in the scheme a request that does not come from the main frame of an app origin where the runtime tells (Android's `isForMainFrame`, CEF's request frame), or pass `Referer` on and have the proxy refuse a GET of a transition with no same-origin `Referer`; with SEC-CLI-04's fix, a `frame-src` for server pages. Fixed 2026-11-19 in part, on the desktop (commit 0f95c41e6): CEF gives every request of a window's main frame the frame's origin and leaves a subframe's alone, so the client's scheme (`SchemeHooks::fetch_site`) and the node's (`node::scheme::forwarded`) derive a `Sec-Fetch-Site` from the `Origin` (`moqui_client::proxy::site_by_origin`: the app's own is `same-origin`, any other or none `cross-site`), the proxy forwards it (`Proxy::send_from`; the page's own `Sec-Fetch-*` never go on), and the server applies its GET-write rule to a key-signed request that carries fetch metadata (`screen/render.rs`, beside SEC-WEB-04's for a session). Open: the phone, whose WebView gives a scheme's handler neither `Referer` nor the asking frame; a fresh window's first navigation to a writing transition is refused on the desktop. Tests `proxy::tests` `a_request_with_the_apps_origin_is_the_pages_own_and_any_other_is_cross_site`, `the_hosts_fetch_site_goes_on_and_the_pages_does_not`; the node's `scheme::tests` `the_node_hears_whether_the_pages_main_frame_sent_a_request`; `SecurityScreens.spec.js` "a key-signed GET of a writing transition that says another site started it is refused" (it failed with the server change taken out). Not run on a CEF build.
- **SEC-CLI-103 (Low; confirmed (second check)). The desktop AppImage runs Chromium with no process sandbox where unprivileged user namespaces are restricted, and a Windows build would always.** An AppImage cannot carry the setuid `chrome-sandbox` helper (it is mounted `nosuid`), and where AppArmor restricts unprivileged user namespaces (Ubuntu 23.10 and later) `tauri-runtime-cef` starts CEF with `--no-sandbox`; the crate also states that Windows always runs unsandboxed, since it passes CEF no sandbox info. A renderer bug that a server's page or its data reaches then runs with the user's rights, beside the node's files and the key store, and the bundled Chromium is patched only by an update the user installs. The `.deb` keeps the sandbox; no Windows bundle is built today. The README and `desktop.sh` say so; the Clients map and the register did not. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PLATFORM-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: tauri-runtime-cef 3.0.0-alpha.6 `src/sandbox.rs:33-40`, `:84-92`; `crates/moqui-desktop/README.md:237-239`; `scripts/moq/desktop.sh:396-398`. Fix: Make the `.deb` the Linux download, or have the AppImage refuse to start without a sandbox (`SandboxPolicy::Required`) with a message saying why; build no Windows bundle until the runtime passes CEF its sandbox.
- **SEC-CLI-104 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed in part). The apps' dependencies are resolved anew at each build, checked by no advisory scan and no Gradle verification, and CEF's archive by a SHA-1 from its own index, so a build is not reproducible.** The desktop, mobile and Tauri glue crates are workspaces of their own whose `Cargo.lock` is git-ignored and replaced by a copy of the repository's whenever that is newer, and the repository's lock has none of Tauri, wry, CEF or the plugins, so each build resolves them afresh. `deny.toml` covers the root workspace for one Linux target (SEC-DAT-13), so none of the apps' crates, nor the Android target's, is checked against advisories. Gradle has no `verification-metadata.xml` or lock, the wrapper names no `distributionSha256Sum`, and `download-cef` checks the CEF archive against a SHA-1 from the same index it downloads from (the class of SEC-OUT-91). Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-CODE-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `.gitignore:118-125`; `scripts/moq/desktop.sh:50-54`; `deny.toml:6-7`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties:3`; download-cef 3.0.0 `src/lib.rs:256-262`, `:323`. Fix: Commit each app's lock (merged with the root's when that moves), run `cargo deny check` on each app's manifest for its targets, turn on Gradle dependency verification and the wrapper checksum, pin the CEF archive's SHA-256 in the repository, and record a release's toolchain. Fixed 2026-11-19 in part (commit 81997c827): the Gradle wrapper checks its distribution's SHA-256 (`distributionSha256Sum`, from services.gradle.org's `.sha256`, the same as gradle.org's release checksums); test `android_settings.rs` `the_android_apps_gradle_wrapper_checks_its_distribution_by_its_sha256`. Open: the apps' locks, `cargo deny` for each app's manifest and targets, Gradle dependency verification, a pinned SHA-256 of the CEF archive, and a recorded toolchain. Found while planning the fix and fixed apart: the desktop's workspace and the generated app crate took none of the repository's crate patches (SEC-CLI-126).
- **SEC-CLI-105 (Low; likely (second check); fixed). On the desktop a server page's `http://` subresources load in clear text, with no mixed-content blocking and no upgrade.** Server pages show on `http://moqui-app.localhost`, a potentially trustworthy origin whose `http://` subresources Chromium does not treat as mixed content, the server's CSP is not passed to them (SEC-CLI-04), and `cef()` sets no switch or policy; on the web the same page, over https, has such loads upgraded or blocked. So a network observer or attacker on the path to a host that a page or its users' content names by `http://` (an image in safe rich text such as a Chatter entry, an email after "Show images") sees and can replace it: a read receipt and an image swap, or a script only where a server page names an `http` one. The node's origins are the same. The phone's release build refuses such loads (`usesCleartextTraffic` false). Not run on a CEF build. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-NETWORK-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/lib.rs:62`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/scheme.rs:324-326`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/lib.rs:160-178`. Fix: Add `upgrade-insecure-requests` to every server document answered on the app and node origins, or refuse a page's `http` request to a host that is not loopback in the runtime's request handler. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit a4847bbce): a server's page in an app window, the client's server pages and the node's in process, gets `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="upgrade-insecure-requests">` at the start of its head (`moqui_client_tauri::scheme::upgraded_page`; a meta tag, as the CEF runtime rewrites a page's first CSP header to add its scripts' hashes). Chromium 156 was seen to upgrade an outside `http` image and leave `http://*.localhost` and loopback loads as they are; not run on a CEF build. Not covered: a sidecar's pages, which the server serves to the window over `http://127.0.0.1`. Test `scheme::tests` `a_servers_page_has_its_loads_of_other_hosts_upgraded_in_its_head`.
- **SEC-CLI-106 (Low; confirmed (second check); fixed). The desktop names its device for the computer's host name, and every user who shares a room or a call is told it.** `device_name` is "MR Moqui desktop on <hostname>". It names the device key at sign-in and in the SSO URL handed to the system browser, and the shell gives it to the pages as `moquiDesktop.deviceName`, which `WebrootCrypto.js` takes as the chat crypto device's display name; `publicKeys` answers that name as `unsigned.device_display_name` to every user who queries the keys of a room's or a call's members. A host name often carries the owner's name or an asset tag. The phone's name is its model. The user can rename the crypto device afterwards. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/mod.rs:251-261`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/windows.rs:396-414`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/bridge.js:249-250`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootCrypto.js:205-213`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatKeys.js:464-470`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sso.rs:112`. Fix: Name the device "MR Moqui desktop on macOS" or "on Linux" by default, and answer other users a neutral display name. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit a4847bbce): the desktop is "MR Moqui desktop on macOS" (Linux, Windows), by `std::env::consts::OS` (`client::named_device`); a device named before keeps its name until the user renames it or signs in again. Open, as a defence: a neutral name for other users in `publicKeys`. Test `client::tests` `the_device_is_named_for_its_os_not_its_host`.
- **SEC-CLI-107 (Info; likely (second check); fixed). In the apps a re-authentication the server asks for never counts, so every action behind it fails there, a lost device's key revocation among them.** The server notes a re-authentication in the session (`note_recent_auth_at`) and `require#RecentAuth` reads it there, but a page's requests in the apps carry no session: the client keeps no cookie and the proxy drops `Set-Cookie`, so each request is a new session signed in by the device key, which never counts as recent. `moqui.reauth.retry` asks for the password and sends the action again, and it is refused again; an SSO account's re-authentication goes to the system browser. So starting or deleting a chat key backup, a cross-signing reset, revoking a device's chat keys, adding or ending a factor, an app password or an SSH key, and changing the account's email fail closed in the apps, and the user needs a browser for them. Not run. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-AUTH-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/http.rs:26-38`; `crates/moqui-client/src/proxy.rs:61-72`, `:175-177`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1339-1393`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootReAuth.js:116`. Fix: Note a re-authentication made with a device key against that key (its `UserLoginKey` row) for the five minutes, or take the password with the guarded action in one request. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 0f95c41e6): a re-authentication in a request a device's key signed in is kept on the key (`UserLoginKey.lastAuthDate`, `reauthPasswordDate`, written in a transaction of their own) and restored into the session at the key's next sign-in (`user/login.rs` `note_on_device_key`, `restore_key_stamps`; the session's own later stamp stays), so `require#RecentAuth` passes in the apps; another key, a session's key and an old stamp carry nothing more than they did. Test `user::login::tests` `a_device_keys_reauthentication_counts_in_its_next_requests_and_no_others` (it failed without the restore). An SSO account's re-authentication in the system browser still stamps no key.
- **SEC-CLI-108 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). A sign-out the server does not hear of leaves the device key valid there for up to 90 days, with no retry.** `revoke` returns at once when the app cannot make requests and only logs a failed `/rest/logout`; the key is then forgotten on the device. The server keeps it until it expires (`device-expire-hours`, 2160) or the user revokes it from another device, and nothing tries again. Only a copy taken before the sign-out could use it. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-AUTH-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/commands.rs:407-423`; `crates/moqui-client/src/login.rs:46-67`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:637`. Fix: Keep a sealed list of keys to revoke and revoke them when the server is next reached, or tell the user at sign-out to remove the device from another one. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 0f95c41e6): a key whose sign-out the server did not hear (no network, no answer) is kept, sealed beside the keys (`Keys::add_pending_revoke`, the `_revoke` entry, at most 32), and the outbox's passes revoke it there (`moqui_client::login::revoke_pending` at the top of `flush_all`: gone on a 2xx or 401, tried again at most every five minutes, dropped after 90 days). Tests `keys_tests` `the_keys_waiting_to_be_revoked_are_kept_beside_the_keys`, `login_tests` `a_key_signed_out_unheard_is_revoked_at_a_later_pass`. The phone's push take-back at sign-out is not retried with it.
- **SEC-CLI-109 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). A release build takes plain `http://` to `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` as a server.** `base_url` allows loopback http for development (`adb reverse`) in every build. On a phone any app may listen on a loopback port, and on a desktop any local user, so a user persuaded to type such an address hands that listener the password and then the device key. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-NETWORK-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-client/src/account.rs:186-206`. Fix: Take loopback http only in a debug build, or behind a developer setting. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit a4847bbce): `base_url` takes loopback http only in a debug build (`base_url_with`); an account saved before keeps its server. Tests `account::tests` `plain_http_is_for_this_machine_only`, `a_release_build_takes_no_plain_http_even_to_this_machine`.
- **SEC-CLI-110 (Info; confirmed (second check)). A video captured from a page's file input is left where the camera app puts it.** `showVideoCapturePicker` starts `ACTION_VIDEO_CAPTURE` with no `EXTRA_OUTPUT`, so the camera app writes the video to its usual place for videos (shared storage, the gallery), readable by apps with media access and taken by a gallery's cloud backup, and the app never deletes it. No bundled screen asks for video today (Chat's camera input is `image/*`); any server page with `accept="video/*" capture` reaches it. The photo case is SEC-CLI-89. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-STORAGE-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/kotlin/RustWebChromeClient.kt:388-401`. Fix: Give the intent an `EXTRA_OUTPUT` under the app's cache, as SEC-CLI-89's fix would for photos, and delete the file once the page has it.
- **SEC-CLI-111 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). On the desktop a device key in the file fallback outlives the move to the OS key store, in plain text.** `open` picks one store per run: the OS key store when it answers, else `client/keys/<account>.key` (0600), which a development run, `MOQUI_DESKTOP_KEY_STORE=file` or a session with no Secret Service uses. A key written there is never read, moved or deleted once a later run has the OS store: the account shows signed out, sign-out and removal delete through the OS store only, and the file stays, valid on the server until it expires or is revoked in My devices. The phone seals and removes such keys. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-STORAGE-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/keystore.rs:112-132`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/commands.rs:500`, `:560`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/keystore.rs:121-146`. Fix: On a run with the OS store, move each `*.key` into it and delete the file (or revoke the key and delete it). Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit a4847bbce): a run with the OS key store moves each `*.key` of the file store into it and deletes the file, keeping the OS store's own key of an account it already holds (`client::keystore::move_file_keys`; a key so dropped stays good on the server until it expires or is revoked). Test `client::keystore::tests` `a_run_with_the_os_store_moves_the_file_keys_into_it`.
- **SEC-CLI-112 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). The phone's release build logs a failed request's whole URL and a refused navigation's URL to logcat.** Logging is at Info in every build. A forwarded request that fails is logged with its path and the transport error, whose text is `{url}: {reqwest error}`, the query included; a refused navigation logs its URL. A token in a path (a share link) or a query value so reaches logcat, which adb and bug reports read (other apps cannot). Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-STORAGE-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/src/lib.rs:158-165`, `:317`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/scheme.rs:258`; `crates/moqui-client/src/proxy.rs:184`. Fix: Strip the URL from transport errors (`reqwest::Error::without_url`, and the path's query), log a refused navigation's scheme and host only, or log at Warn in a release build. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commits a4847bbce, 81997c827): a transport error names the server and the reason with its causes, never the URL (`moqui_client::http::transport`, reqwest's `without_url`, in the proxy, sign-in and SSO); a failed page request logs the server and the path's first part; the phone logs a refused navigation's scheme and host, and a path that did not open without its query. Logging stays at Info (the smoke run waits for an Info line). Tests `proxy::tests` `a_failed_request_names_its_server_not_its_path_or_query` (it failed on the old text, which held the query twice), the mobile `a_logged_url_has_only_its_scheme_and_host_and_a_logged_path_no_query`.
- **SEC-CLI-113 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed in part). The phone's keys and data are not tied to a secure lock screen, and neither app has a lock of its own.** The Keystore key that seals the device keys has no unlocked-device, user-authentication or StrongBox setting, by a documented choice (the outbox and an incoming call need the keys while the phone is locked), and the app never checks `KeyguardManager.isDeviceSecure()`. So on a phone with no screen lock, whoever holds it opens every account's mail and chat in the app, and file-based encryption adds nothing beyond the device's own. Neither app asks for the device credential or a biometric before showing the shell and its offline store. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-CRYPTO-2, MASVS-PLATFORM-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/KeyVault.kt:22-33`; `docs/ui/Mobile-Client.md:68-72`. Fix: Tell the user in the shell when the phone has no secure lock (and refuse a shared-mode sign-in there); seal what no background work reads (the chat store passphrases) under a second Keystore key with `setUnlockedDeviceRequired(true)`; offer an optional app lock. Fixed 2026-11-19 in part (commits 0f95c41e6, f4e01b07b): the shell's state answers `deviceSecure` where the host tells (`ShellHost::device_secure`; the phone's `DeviceLock.isSecure`, `KeyguardManager.isDeviceSecure()`), and the account menu warns of a phone with no screen lock (`Root.qvue`). Open: a second Keystore key with `setUnlockedDeviceRequired` for what no background work reads, a refused shared-mode sign-in on such a phone, and an app lock. Test the glue's `the_shell_hears_whether_the_device_has_a_screen_lock`; not run on a device.
- **SEC-CLI-114 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The desktop dumps the whole node every night, in clear, into a Documents folder by default, without saying so, and the tray cannot turn it off.** `Settings::default` has `scheduled: true` and the folder `~/Documents/MR-Moqui Backups`, chosen so the user's own backup reaches it; macOS (iCloud Desktop and Documents), Windows (OneDrive) and some Linux desktops sync that folder to a cloud, which then holds every record and password hash of the node in an unencrypted `pg_dump` archive. The tray offers Back up now, choose, open and restore, and the last time, but no switch; only `scheduled=false` in the `backups` file stops it, and nothing tells the user where the dumps go. SEC-TLS-97 holds their file modes. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-STORAGE-2, MASVS-PRIVACY-4); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/backup.rs:18-23`, `:75-82`, `:140-148`, `:284-291`. Fix: Tell the user at the first backup what it holds and where it goes, add an on and off item to the menu, and offer a passphrase for an encrypted dump (or keep the default folder in the app's data directory).
- **SEC-CLI-115 (Info; likely (second check); fixed). A text's one-time code shows on a lock screen left at its default.** For a chat notification that carries a code, `ChatNotifications` sets `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE` with a public version that names only the sender, and its comment says the lock screen shows no code; Android shows the public version only when the user has set the lock screen to hide sensitive content, so under the default "show all content" the code shows. Newer Android releases may redact codes themselves. Not checked on a device. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PLATFORM-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/ChatNotifications.kt:246-256`. Fix: Leave the code out of the notification's text (the Copy action holds it), or post such a notification as `VISIBILITY_SECRET`. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 81997c827): such a notification is `VISIBILITY_SECRET` with no public version, so a secure lock screen shows nothing of it and Copy code needs the phone unlocked (a phone with no secure lock is SEC-CLI-113). Tests `android_settings.rs` `a_chat_notification_with_a_one_time_code_is_secret_on_the_lock_screen`; the device test `ChatNotificationsTest.aOneTimeCodeIsSecretOnTheLockScreen` (not run).
- **SEC-CLI-116 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). The phone app installs on Android 8 and 9, which receive no security updates, and has no overlay defence below Android 12.** `minSdk` is 26. Neither the app's activities nor wry's view set `filterTouchesWhenObscured` (or `setHideOverlayWindows`, API 31), and Android filters untrusted overlays itself only from 12, so on 8 to 11 an app with the overlay permission can cover the app's dialogs (a call's answer, a permission question) and pass taps through. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-CODE-1, MASVS-PLATFORM-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/build.gradle.kts:47`; `MainActivity.kt`, `IncomingCallActivity.kt`, `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/kotlin/` (none sets either). Fix: Raise `minSdk` to a supported release, or set `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on the activities' content views. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 81997c827), `minSdk` kept at 26: the activities' decor views, wry's view and every dialog of the app and of a page drop a touch through another app's window (`filterTouchesWhenObscured`: `ObscuredTouches.kt`, `RustWebView.refuseObscuredTouches`), so a screen dimmer or chat head over the app stops its taps. Test `android_settings.rs` `every_activity_webview_and_dialog_of_the_android_app_drops_touches_through_another_apps_window`; not run on a device. Raising `minSdk` stays the owner's call.
- **SEC-CLI-117 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed).
