The desktop has no screen protection for a view-once photo.** Chat asks `secureScreen` while a view-once photo shows; the phone's bridge has it (`FLAG_SECURE`), the desktop's bridge does not, so a view-once photo can be captured on the desktop with no sign to the sender. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PLATFORM-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/bridge.js:240-262`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/bridge.js:319`; `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Chat.qvue:3796`. Fix: A `secureScreen` that sets the window's content protection (`set_content_protected`) while the photo shows, answering false where the OS has none (most Linux desktops). Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 0f95c41e6): the desktop's `secure_screen` keeps the asking page's window out of screenshots and screen recording while any reason holds it (`set_content_protected`: macOS's sharing type, Windows' `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE`); a Linux desktop, which has none, offers no `secureScreen`, where the viewer says a screenshot cannot be stopped. Chat tells the phone by `dialAccount`, no longer by `secureScreen`. Tests `bridge_tests` `the_screen_protection_is_held_while_any_reason_holds_it` and the ACL test; not run on a desktop build (macOS 15's capture may not honour the sharing type).
- **SEC-CLI-118 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The desktop updater reads the feed and the whole artifact into memory with no size limit.** `tauri-plugin-updater` reads the feed's JSON whole and collects the download into one buffer, so a feed's host (or whoever answers for it) can make the app allocate without bound when the user checks for an update; the signature is checked after. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-CODE-4); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: tauri-plugin-updater 3.0.0-alpha.3 `src/updater.rs:625`, `:885-890`. Fix: Cap the feed (some KiB) and the artifact (its announced size, and a ceiling), or stream the artifact to a file.
- **SEC-CLI-119 (Info; confirmed (second check)). A server page's native dialogs and notifications on the phone carry no origin.** A page's `alert`, `confirm` and `prompt` are native dialogs in the app's own look with no title naming the page, and any page may show a native notification through `notify`. A page of one account's server can so ask, as the app, for another account's password. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PLATFORM-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/patches/wry/src/android/kotlin/RustWebChromeClient.kt:141-240`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/commands.rs:100-127`. Fix: Put the page's host in each dialog's title, and refuse `prompt` on the app origin.
- **SEC-CLI-120 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed in part). The phone asks for the microphone at the first start after sign-in with no reason of its own, and any page's notification raises the notifications question.** `askForCallsOnce` asks `RECORD_AUDIO` at the first resume after an account signs in: at once with no dialog when notifications are already allowed (or before Android 13), else behind a dialog whose text speaks of notifications only. The `notify` command, which any server page may call, asks Android's notifications question itself, with no reason. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/MainActivity.kt:335-376`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml:43-44`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/commands.rs:117-127`. Fix: Ask for the microphone at the first call, or say why first (answering a call on a locked phone); have `notify` fail rather than ask. Fixed 2026-11-19 in part (commit 81997c827): `notify` fails without the permission and never asks. Open: the microphone asked at the first start after sign-in with no reason of its own. Test `android_settings.rs` `no_page_notification_asks_for_the_notification_permission`.
- **SEC-CLI-121 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). A Firebase build reaches Google on every launch, before any account signs in.** `MainActivity` calls `Push.start` at every start, which asks FCM for a token, and the manifest does not turn FCM's auto-init off, so Firebase Installations and FCM reach Google from the first start of a build that has `google-services.json`, with nothing said in the app. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/MainActivity.kt:110-114`; `Push.kt:30-42`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/fcm/java/org/moqui/mobile/PushService.kt:29-35`. Fix: Start Firebase and register only once an account signs in, with auto-init off in the manifest. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 81997c827): FCM's auto-init is off in the Firebase build's manifest, and `Push.start` asks for a token only with an account signed in here; a sign-in starts it (`android::fcm_start`, `Push.startFcm`). Not run on a device; the last account's sign-out leaves the Firebase installation. Test `android_settings.rs` `a_firebase_build_asks_fcm_for_a_token_only_once_an_account_is_signed_in`.
- **SEC-CLI-122 (Info; confirmed (second check)). Neither app has a privacy notice, and the Play Data safety draft leaves out location, photos and videos, the device name and UnifiedPush.** The draft in `docs/Build-Test.md` names messages, mail, files, the device key and call audio, but not the precise and approximate location a chat share sends, the photos and videos the camera captures, the phone model in the device name, or the UnifiedPush distributor. There is no privacy policy in the repository or the shell, which Play asks of an app with the camera, microphone, contacts and location permissions. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-3); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `docs/Build-Test.md:541-546`. Fix: One notice per app (what stays on the device, what goes to the user's server, to Google with Firebase, to a distributor, to Google from Chromium or the WebView), linked from the account menu and the store listing, and the Data safety list taken from the merged manifest.
- **SEC-CLI-123 (Info; confirmed (second check); fixed). The apps leave Chromium's and the WebView's background requests to Google on and unmentioned.** The desktop's `cef()` sets none of `safe_browsing`, `component_updates` or `spell_checking`, which `tauri-runtime-cef` leaves on: Safe Browsing list updates, the component updater (`update.googleapis.com`) and a dictionary download per language. The phone's manifest sets no `android.webkit.WebView.MetricsOptOut`, so WebView metrics go to Google on a device whose owner allowed usage reports. Traffic not observed. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/lib.rs:160-178`; tauri-runtime-cef 3.0.0-alpha.6 `src/runtime.rs:1024-1068`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`. Fix: Decide each (keep the component updater, which carries the revocation and CT lists; turn the rest off where the windows show only the app's content), set `MetricsOptOut`, and name what stays in the notice (SEC-CLI-122). Fixed 2026-11-19 (commits a4847bbce, 81997c827): the desktop turns Chromium's Safe Browsing off, and keeps its component updater (the revocation and Certificate Transparency lists) and spell checking on, which the notice (SEC-CLI-122) is to name; the phone's manifest sets `android.webkit.WebView.MetricsOptOut`. Tests: the desktop's `chromium_runs_without_safe_browsing_and_keeps_its_component_updates`, `android_settings.rs` `the_android_apps_webview_sends_google_no_metrics`.
- **SEC-CLI-124 (Info; likely (second check); fixed). On the desktop a frame a page delegates the camera or microphone to gets them by the window's URL, with no prompt (the desktop half of SEC-CLI-90).** `on_permission` decides camera and microphone by `webview.url()`, and the runtime hands the app's handler the kind only: `on_request_media_access_permission` has the frame and the requesting origin and passes neither. A cross-origin frame with `allow="camera; microphone"` in a page on the node's or the shell's origin is so allowed; on Linux the OS never asks either. Not run. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-1); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-desktop/src/bridge.rs:412-439`; tauri-runtime-cef 3.0.0-alpha.6 `src/cef_impl/client/permission.rs:188-195`. Fix: Answer media only for the window's own origin, which needs the runtime to pass the requesting origin (or refuse when it differs from the window's). Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit a4847bbce): a server's page in an app window, the node's too, is answered with `Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), microphone=(self), geolocation=(self)` unless it sends its own (`moqui_client_tauri::scheme::PAGE_PERMISSIONS_POLICY`), so it cannot give a frame of another origin what the window may have; the runtime still asks the app by the window. On the phone the same policy keeps SEC-CLI-90's delegated frames out; the page itself still gets them with no prompt. Test `scheme::tests` `a_servers_page_keeps_its_media_to_its_own_origin`; not run on a CEF build.
- **SEC-CLI-125 (Info; likely (second check); fixed). Each account's UnifiedPush instance is its account id, a host and an unsalted hash of the server and username, given to the distributor app.** `UnifiedPushes` registers each account with `UnifiedPush.register(app, account, ...)` under the account id, which is the server's host and 12 hex digits of an unsalted SHA-256 of the base URL and username. The distributor so learns which servers the user has accounts on and can test usernames against the hash. Whether a distributor sends instances to its own server was not read. Report: the security proofs, phase P8 (MASVS-PRIVACY-2); lines read again at mr-moqui `a73f06673`. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/src/main/java/org/moqui/mobile/UnifiedPushes.kt:99-121`; `crates/moqui-client/src/account.rs:215-232`. Fix: Register a random instance per account, kept in `push.json`. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit f4e01b07b): each account registers under a random instance kept in `push.json` (`PushRecord.instances`, `Native.instanceOf`, `accountOf`), which the push service and the key manager map back to the account, ignoring one of no account's here; the old instances (the account ids) are taken back by `registerAll`, and the server's one registration a device key is replaced by the new endpoint's (a push sent to an old instance meanwhile is lost). Tests `calls_tests` `each_account_has_a_random_unified_push_instance_kept_until_it_signs_out`, `a_push_record_with_no_instances_still_loads`; the Kotlin was not compiled.
- **SEC-CLI-127 (Low; likely (lead); fixed). The phone's release build kept no R8 rule for `ScreenSecurity` and `CalendarIntent`, which Rust calls by name, so screen security could fail there.** The release build runs R8 optimization (`app/build.gradle.kts` `optimization { enable = true }`), and `proguard-rules.pro` keeps each class Rust calls through JNI but these two; neither `ScreenSecurity.set` nor `CalendarIntent.insert` has a Kotlin caller, so R8 may remove or rename them, and `secure_screen` then fails: a view-once photo, a secured room and "everywhere" go without `FLAG_SECURE` (Chat says screenshots cannot be stopped), and a calendar event does not open. The release smoke run's list of JNI names (`scripts/android-smoke.sh`) lacked them too. Not run on a release build. Report: found while planning the fixes of the security proofs' phase P8 findings. Where: `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/proguard-rules.pro`; `crates/moqui-mobile/src/android.rs:209-245`; `crates/moqui-mobile/gen/android/app/build.gradle.kts:76-78`. Fix: Keep both, and check that every name the Rust calls has a rule. Fixed 2026-11-19 (commit 81997c827): both kept, with `Push.startFcm`, which SEC-CLI-121's fix calls; the smoke run's list has them, `Push.startFcm` and `CallManager.busyNow`. Test `android_settings.rs` `every_class_and_method_the_mobile_apps_code_asks_for_by_name_has_a_keep_rule`, which reads the crate's Rust and the Kotlin's reflection and failed on `ScreenSecurity.set` without the rule.

## Script engine, templates and parsers

- **SEC-SCR-07 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). `ec.service.rest()` reaches internal addresses unless the script opts out.** `deny_internal` is true only when the script sets it, so the default permits loopback, link-local and private addresses. A script author who forgets the flag makes a server-side request forgery path. Where: crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:2720-2726 and the `RestClient` shim near :903. Fix: Make refusal of internal addresses the default and let trusted callers opt in to internal ones. Fixed 2026-11-16: on a production instance `ec.service.rest()` refuses an internal address unless the script says `allowInternal()`, in `call()` and `send()` alike; `denyInternal()` wins over it and now works with `send()` and `addPropertyHeader` (`script/host/web.rs` `rest_denies_internal`); `outbound::OpenRequest` gains `deny_internal`, and the client that follows no redirect refuses link-local addresses too. Callers of a configured address say `allowInternal()`: ProxyServlet.js, `check#MoquiServer`, Admin's connection probe, mr-pass Transport.js, Smithy's GitHub import, and the PayTrace, Authorize.Net and Shippo gateways when the remote's `allowPrivateNetwork` is Y. A guarded request ignores `HTTP(S)_PROXY`. Tests `on_production_rest_refuses_an_internal_address_unless_the_script_allows_it`, `open_refuses_internal_addresses_when_asked_and_link_local_always`, `web_scripts` ProxyServlet (production).
- **SEC-SCR-08 (Low; confirmed in code; fixed). The `editable` widget writes its text unescaped by default.** The text is written as it is unless the screen sets `encode="true"`, the reverse of the label and display default. Stored markup would run in the viewer's page. Where: runtime/components/base/framework/template/screen/ScreenHtml.js:849. Fix: Escape unless `encode="false"`, as the other widgets do. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 2a53b8a29): confirmed, mr-marble's TaskSummary showed a task's name and description through it. The text is encoded unless the screen sets `encode="false"` (the XSD's default is now true), in `ScreenHtml.js`, `ScreenQvt.js` and the server-static `ScreenQvtStatic.js`, which wraps the label in the new `$mq.enc` (`WebrootVue.qvt.js`), since `m-editable` shows its label as HTML; no shipped screen sets `encode="false"`. Test `screen::tests::qvt` `a_section_in_a_list_cell_keeps_the_list_and_link_styles_are_expanded` (a note with markup, its goldens).
- **SEC-SCR-09 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). A thumbnail decodes an image with no limits of its own.** `image::load_from_memory` is called directly, where `service/images.rs` builds an `ImageReader` with `Limits` and a 30,000 pixel side cap. A small file that declares a very large image costs the crate's default allocation limit per decode, on a request thread. Where: crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/files.rs:455. Fix: Decode through the same limited reader as `images.rs`. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit b547c4559): `ec.files` thumbnails decode through `convert#Image`'s limited reader (`service::decode_image`, `images.rs` `decode_limited`: no side over 30,000 pixels, the decoder's allocation limit), so an image claiming a larger side gets no thumbnail. Test `script::host::files::tests::a_thumbnail_is_not_made_of_an_image_with_a_side_over_the_decode_limit`.
- **SEC-SCR-10 (Low; confirmed in code; fixed). Chromium's DevTools port has no authentication on loopback.** The browser listens with `--remote-debugging-port=0` on 127.0.0.1. A local process that finds the port can list targets and read or drive the pages being printed, which hold the documents of any user. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/chromium.rs:312-327. Fix: Use `--remote-debugging-pipe` so there is no listening socket. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 8892e5e4e): on Unix the PDF engine's Chromium takes DevTools on a pipe (`--remote-debugging-pipe`), started through `/bin/sh -c 'exec ...'` that gives it the server's pipes as its descriptors 3 and 4, so no unsafe code; `pdf/cdp.rs` `Connection::over_pipe` speaks NUL-delimited JSON over them, and the start waits for `Browser.getVersion`. Windows keeps the loopback port, as does the test client's browser (`chromium::PORT_ARGS`). Tests `chromium::tests::devtools_is_a_pipe_unless_a_port_is_asked_for`, `tests/it/pdf.rs` (both PDF tests through the pipe).
- **SEC-SCR-12 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). A subscreen row's menu title is expanded as script, in menus and in the breadcrumb.** A `SubscreensItem` row's `menuTitle` becomes the item's menu title. `menu.rs` localizes and expands it for the menus, and the breadcrumb code added on 2026-11-15 (`level_title`) expands it again for each render whose path includes the screen. A title with `${...}` runs as server script in the viewing user's request. The same row's `subscreenLocation` names the screen file that is mounted. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/definition.rs:1694`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/menu.rs:168`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs` `level_title`. Fix: Expand a title from screen XML only; treat a title from a database row as plain text. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-SCR-06: writing an expression into `SubscreensItem.menuTitle` needs `DATABASE_ADMIN`.
- **SEC-SCR-11 (Info; reported (to check)). A screen definition loads from any resource scheme, `http` and `dbresource` included.** The screen XML is read through the resource facade, which serves database and remote locations. A screen holds actions, so such a location is code. Where: crates/moqui-core/src/screen/definition.rs:910; crates/moqui-core/src/context/resource.rs:496-521. Fix: Allow only `component://` (and `dbresource://` behind a setting) for screen locations.
- **SEC-SCR-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). `upload#RichTextImage` and `write#Image` decode a whole image outside the concurrency slot.** The rich-text upload is decoded in full only to learn its format, with a side limit of 30,000 and the `image` crate's default allocation limit of 512 MiB a decode, so an 8 MiB file can describe an image near that allocation. `image_slot` (`image-max-concurrent`, 2 by default) is taken only by `convert#Image`; the upload, `write#Image` and `thumbnail_png` decode without it, so requests at once multiply the 512 MiB. Any signed-in user reaches the upload with `POST /richTextImage`. The reach of `write#Image` was not traced, nor how far each format's decoder honors `max_alloc`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/images.rs:177-180`, `:279`, `:446`, `:487`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/files.rs:457`. Fix: Read the format and dimensions without decoding (`ImageReader::into_dimensions`), refuse past a pixel count, set `max_alloc` to what the pixel cap needs, and take `image_slot` for every full decode.
- **SEC-SCR-86 (Low; reported (likely)). The XML editing layer recurses with no depth limit, so a deeply nested text ends the process.** `moqui_lib::xml_edit`'s `Doc::parse` is a loop and accepts any depth; `outline` then calls `node_of` once a level and clones the path at each, and `indent_of` and `lay_out` recurse the same way. A `text` of some megabytes of nested `<a>` overflows the stack, and a stack overflow in Rust aborts the process (not run). `moqui_lib::node` stops at 256 levels; this reader has no such limit. A holder of the DevAide grant reaches it through the `DevAide/Workspace` transition `outline` and the service `org.moqui.dev.DevToolsServices.apply#XmlOps` (dev component). Where: `crates/moqui-lib/src/xml_edit/outline.rs:70`, `:110`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/xml_edit/mod.rs:431`, `:507`. Fix: Count open elements in `Scanner::run` and refuse past 256, as `node` does.
- **SEC-SCR-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). One XML start tag costs time quadratic in its attributes.** `moqui_lib::node` compares each attribute of a tag with every earlier one, and nothing caps the attributes of a tag. A 10 MiB body that is one tag with about a million short attributes is on the order of 10^11 comparisons on a request worker. Whoever sends XML to `moqui_lib::node` reaches it: a signed-in DAV client (a body up to the DAV body limit), a mail autodiscover or S3 peer, a job's JUnit report. Where: `crates/moqui-lib/src/node/parse.rs:554`. Fix: Refuse a tag with more than a bound of attributes (1000 is generous), or keep a set past a few dozen.
- **SEC-SCR-88 (Low; reported (likely)). The CSV render mode guards and quotes each widget's text on its own, so a field with two widgets can put a formula in a cell of its own.** `$csv` adds an apostrophe when a value starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a CR, and quotes a value with a comma, a quote or a line break; a cell's code is one `$sep()` and then each widget's `$csv(value)` with nothing between. With a first widget that writes `A12` and a second whose value is `x,=1+cmd|' /C calc'!A0,y`, the second gets no apostrophe and is quoted on its own, so the line holds `A12"x,=1+cmd|' /C calc'!A0,y"`; a CSV reader takes a quote inside an unquoted field as text and reads three fields, the middle one a formula, and the row's later columns shift. Whoever sets a value a form-list shows (a customer's name, a note) reaches the staff user who renders that list with `renderMode=csv` and opens the file in a spreadsheet. Which shipped form-lists have a field with two value widgets over outside text, and what Excel and LibreOffice do with a quote inside an unquoted field, were not confirmed. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/template/screen/ScreenCsv.js:35-39`, `:43`, `:92-93`; `runtime/components/base/framework/template/screen/ScreenSupport.js:243`. Fix: Collect the texts of a field's widgets and pass them through `$csv` once, joined as the xlsx mode joins them (`screen/xlsx.rs:109-120`).
- **SEC-SCR-89 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The data grid's CSV export and the entity CSV writers have no formula guard.** These writers quote for CSV and do nothing else, so a value that starts with `=` is a formula in the file; the screen csv mode, the report CSV and the BOSS report exports guard, and these do not. Whoever sets a text field of a record reaches a Tools user who exports that entity from the data grid as CSV (`grid_service.rs:195`), a holder of `EntityExport` who writes CSV, or a reader of an ETL import's error file, once the file is opened in a spreadsheet. For the entity writer that is the price of a file that loads back unchanged; the grid export is made for a person. Where: `crates/moqui-tools/src/grid.rs:1329-1334`, `:1606-1607`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/query_rest.rs:1846-1849`, `:2113-2121`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/transform.rs:95-110`. Fix: Guard the grid export as `moqui-report/src/schedule.rs` `csv_cell` does; leave the entity writer as a data format and say so in the Scripts map, and make the grid's xlsx the default for a person.
- **SEC-SCR-90 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A CSS comment that starts and ends inside strings hides a `url()` or an `@import` from the theme's CSS check.** `decode_css` removes `/* ... */` wherever the two marks stand, strings included, before `check_css` tests `url()` and `@import`, and a browser does not start a comment inside a string. So `background: url("/*"), url(https://evil.example/x), url("*/")` is checked as one relative `url(" ")` and loaded by a browser as three URLs, the second on an origin that was not approved; an `@import` between two such strings is hidden from the `DENIED_CSS` test the same way. The function also decodes every `\` escape before strings are split, so an escaped quote ends a URL early for the check (with one approved origin, `url("//cdn.approved.example\"@evil.example/x")` is checked as the approved origin and loaded from `evil.example`). A theme author reaches it through a stylesheet asset, a `<style>` element or `style` attribute of a template, and the `style` attribute of an SVG asset. The PASS shell's CSP (`default-src 'self'`, `img-src 'self' data:`, `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`) refuses the load in a browser, so what fails is the theme adapter's own rule; the CSP of a response that serves a theme's stylesheet asset, and of another application that uses the theme engine, was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/markup.rs:706-779`, `:784-805`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-pass/screen/Shared/Headers.js:13-18`. Fix: Tokenize CSS as the CSS syntax does (strings first, with no comment inside them, escapes decoded per token) and check each `url()` token and each at-rule; or refuse `/*` and `*/` inside a string and a backslash before a quote or `)`.
- **SEC-SCR-91 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A theme's markup may set the page's referrer policy, and the `attributionsrc` attribute is not denied.** `meta` is not a denied element and only its `http-equiv` attribute is denied, so `<meta name="referrer" content="unsafe-url">` passes the source and output checks and replaces the `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` the response header sets. A theme may link to any http or https site, so a visitor who follows a theme's link sends the page's full address, query included, to that site. `attributionsrc` (Chromium's attribution reporting, which sends a request to the URL it names) is neither denied nor one of the URL attributes the check reads. A theme author reaches it in a layout, section or block. Which Shop pages carry a secret in their address when a theme link is on them, and whether the CSP stops an `attributionsrc` request, were not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/markup.rs:124-144`, `:175-187`, `:482-485`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-pass/screen/Shared/Headers.js:136`. Fix: Allow `<meta>` only with a `name` in a short list (`viewport`, `description`, `robots`, `theme-color`) or deny `name="referrer"`; deny the `referrerpolicy` and `attributionsrc` attributes.
- **SEC-SCR-92 (Low; reported (likely)). Record fields that name a template or screen location are not held by SEC-SCR-06's rule.** SEC-SCR-06's fix puts the text fields that are evaluated as script under `DATABASE_ADMIN`; a field that names a location selects text that is evaluated, and is not held. `moqui.screen.ScreenDocument.docLocation` is rendered by the renderer of its extension for any user who opens that screen's document (its `screenDoc` transition), so `https://host/x.md.fancy` or a `dbresource://` file named `.fancy` runs in the live renderer, and a location with no renderer (`file:///...`) is answered as that file's text. No rule names `ScreenDocument`, the `SubscreensItem` rule covers only `menuTitle` and not `subscreenLocation` (a screen definition from any scheme, SEC-SCR-11), and `WikiSpace.rootPageLocation` and `decoratorScreenLocation` (included as a screen by `wiki.xml:270`) are not held either. Who can write these rows without `DATABASE_ADMIN`, and the permission each write path needs, was not traced. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/saved_finds.rs:1308-1312`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/Security.eecas.xml:142-146`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Wiki/wiki.xml:270`. Fix: Hold these location fields with the same eeca rule unless the value is a `component://` location; in `TemplateRegistry`, give the live renderer only to `component://`, `classpath://` and `runtime://` locations and render a `dbresource`, `http`, `https` or provider location with the locked renderer or as text.
- **SEC-SCR-93 (Info; reported (to check)). The tui link list refuses only a lower-case `javascript:` link.** In the tui render mode a link's URL goes to the client's link list unless it starts with `#` or `javascript:`, compared as written, so `JavaScript:`, a leading space, `data:` and `file:` pass. Whoever writes HTML that a screen shows in tui mode (mail, chat, wiki text) reaches it. What the terminal client does with a link (opens it in the system browser or not) was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/html_text.rs:238`. Fix: Keep only `http:`, `https:`, `mailto:` and site-relative URLs.

## Outbound requests, processes and packaging

- **SEC-OUT-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). MX delivery connects to any address a recipient domain's DNS names, internal ones included.** `hosts` takes the MX hosts (or the domain's own address) and their addresses as resolved, with no internal-address check, and the node opens SMTP to port 25 there and delivers the message. An internal mail server that trusts the node's address accepts it, and the reply text comes back in the delivery report. Where: crates/moqui-mail/src/mx.rs:978. Fix: Drop internal addresses from an MX host's list unless the domain is in `mail_mx_override`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `mx.rs` `hosts` drops an MX host's internal addresses and refuses a domain whose hosts have only internal ones, unless `mail_mx_override` names it; test `mx::tests::an_mx_host_with_only_internal_addresses_is_not_tried`.
- **SEC-OUT-05 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The deploy token is in the `git` child's argument list.** The request's user and token are passed as `-c http.extraHeader=Authorization: Basic <base64>`, so any local user can read and decode them while `git` runs. Where: crates/moqui-devkit/src/deploy/git.rs:1506. Fix: Pass it through the environment (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0`, `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0`) or an askpass helper. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 62cf97db7): the deploy token reaches `git` as `http.extraHeader` through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` in the child's environment, never in its arguments (`deploy::git::git_process`; git 2.31 or newer). Test `deploy::git::tests::the_deploy_token_is_in_git_s_environment_not_its_arguments`.
- **SEC-OUT-06 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The embedded Postgres socket directory under the temp directory is used without checking its owner or mode.** `DirBuilder::recursive(true).mode(0o700).create` succeeds on an existing directory and leaves its owner and mode. The owner of the directory can then replace the socket file and receive the server's connections, or reach the postmaster's socket. Where: crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:596. Fix: After creating, check the directory is owned by this user with mode 0700 and refuse to start otherwise. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit fd405248d): the start refuses a socket directory under temp that is not a directory of this user with mode 0700 (`embedded::socket_dir_private`; a symlink is refused). Test `embedded::cluster::tests::a_socket_dir_that_is_not_this_user_s_alone_is_refused`.
- **SEC-OUT-09 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Child processes inherit the server's whole environment.** Chromium is started with only `NOTIFY_SOCKET` removed; OpenSearch, `term-hold`, the token command and the external agent get the environment unchanged. Secrets given to the server as environment variables (`/etc/moqui/environment`, the compose `entity_ds_password`) are in each child. Where: crates/moqui-host/src/pdf.rs:684. Fix: `env_clear()` and pass a short allowlist (`PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, `TZ`) to Chromium, OpenSearch and the command children. Fixed 2026-11-16: `moqui_lib::env::limit_child_env` clears each child's environment and passes only a named list (`CHILD_ENV_NAMES` and the caller's own: Chromium's display and fonts, OpenSearch's `JAVA_HOME` and `OPENSEARCH_*`); `term` host sessions and the `git` version probe are not changed; test `env_tests.rs` `a_child_keeps_only_the_named_environment`.
- **SEC-OUT-10 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). Mail capture has no test-run gate.** With capture on every send is kept in memory (500 messages) and not sent, and the tool returns the bodies, which hold password-reset and sign-in links. `HttpFixtures` refuses to turn on outside `moqui test`; this does not. Where: crates/moqui-core/src/service/mail_capture.rs:86. Fix: Gate `set_enabled(Some(true))` and the property on the same `allow()` the test runner sets, or refuse on `instance_purpose=production`. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-DEV-05: `MailCapture` captures only in a `moqui test` run (`mail_capture::allowed()`).
- **SEC-OUT-11 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The LLM endpoint check reads only an address written in the URL.** `check_address` returns `Ok` for any host name without a lookup, and always allows loopback, so a name that resolves to a private or metadata address is called with the endpoint's key and prompt content. Where: crates/moqui-llm/src/provider/mod.rs:1669. Fix: Build the provider client with `deny_internal_addresses(true)` unless `allow-private-network` or `local`. Fixed 2026-11-16: `provider::check_address` also resolves a host name and refuses any internal address that is not loopback unless `allow-private-network` or `local`; test `provider::tests::a_private_address_needs_allow_private_network_and_loopback_is_allowed`.
- **SEC-OUT-12 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). `internal_address` leaves out some non-public ranges; proxy variables may take names past the guard.** `192.0.0.0/24`, `198.18.0.0/15`, 6to4 `2002::/16` and Teredo addresses that embed an internal IPv4, and `fec0::/10` are treated as public. Separately `RestClient::transport` never calls `no_proxy()`, so with `HTTPS_PROXY` set the proxy resolves the target name and `PublicOnly` sees only the proxy's. Where: crates/moqui-lib/src/net.rs:42. Fix: Add the ranges, and call `.no_proxy()` on a client that denies internal addresses (or check the proxy case explicitly). Fixed 2026-11-16: `moqui_lib::net::internal_address` names every range outside public unicast (the listed ones, 6to4 and NAT64 by the IPv4 inside, `fec0::/10`), and a client that denies internal addresses takes no proxy (`no_proxy()`); test `net::tests::internal_address_covers_every_range_that_is_not_public_unicast`.
- **SEC-OUT-13 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The systemd unit and the image leave the binary and confs writable by the service user.** `ReadWritePaths=/opt/moqui` covers `/opt/moqui/moqui` and the confs, and the Containerfile gives the directory `/opt/moqui` to `moqui`, so a file write becomes a persistent replacement of the binary or conf. The unit sets no `CapabilityBoundingSet`, `RestrictAddressFamilies`, `SystemCallFilter`, `ProtectKernel*`, `PrivateDevices`, `ProtectProc` or `UMask`. Where: systemd/moqui.service:23. Fix: Make only the data directories writable (`db`, `log`, `tmp`, `cert`, `txlog`, `.env`) and add the missing sandbox directives. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit aa6954cad), the owner's call: `ProtectSystem=strict` with the runtime and its components writable, and the binary and confs read-only. `systemd/moqui.service` has `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ReadWritePaths=/opt/moqui /opt/moqui/components` and `ReadOnlyPaths=/opt/moqui/moqui -/opt/moqui/MoquiConf.xml -/opt/moqui/conf`; the image has the binary and confs root's and not writable to uid 1000, `components/` uid 1000's, and `/opt/moqui` root's, group moqui, mode 1775 (sticky), which the entrypoint no longer chowns; `docs/Run-Deploy.md`'s install and upgrade steps make the binary and conf root's. Test `scripts/moq-test.sh` deploy-file checks. Outside the call and not done: the other sandbox directives (`CapabilityBoundingSet`, `RestrictAddressFamilies`, `SystemCallFilter`, `UMask`).
- **SEC-OUT-14 (Info; reported (to check); fixed). The default release build carries `devtools`; `moq` downloads Chromium and add-on zips with no checksum.** `release) echo maximal,devtools` puts the dev tools (file watcher, `dev-aide`, `example`) in the default production binary. `verify_published_sha512` is called only for OpenSearch, and that checksum comes from the same address as the archive. Where: moq:1476. Fix: Build production images with `--enterprise` or without `devtools`; pin SHA-256 values for the Chromium revision in the script. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit 8892e5e4e), the owner's call: the release package keeps the dev tools, which open only on a dev instance; the server warns at start when a build with them runs on a production instance, and Run-Deploy's production checklist says a production image leaves them out (`--enterprise`, or Maximal without `devtools`). A Chromium snapshot of a revision and an add-on's version archive have their SHA-256 recorded in `moqui/downloads.sha256` of the user's cache the first time `./moq` fetches them, and a later fetch must match or the task stops (`moq` `check_download_sum`); a branch's archive moves and is not checked. Test `scripts/moq-test.sh` (recorded, checked, a changed archive refused and not unpacked).
- **SEC-OUT-83 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A script sends `pass_client_secret` to any host it names, over plain `grpc://` too.** `ec.service.passAuthority(endpoint)` takes the endpoint text from the script, and for every `grpc://` or `grpcs://` endpoint the sender reads `pass_client_id` and `pass_client_secret` from the conf and adds them as the metadata `x-pass-client` and `x-pass-client-secret`; `pass.rs` `allowed` checks sim mode and the fence only. SEC-SCR-03's fix keeps a secret property from a script, and `addPropertyHeader` sends a property header only under its base URL; this binding has no such tie, so `ec.service.passAuthority('grpc://host.example:4317').call({operation: 'x'})` delivers the secret to that host in clear text. It needs a server script on a host built with gRPC (a component's author; other routes to a script were not traced), or an operator who sets a `grpc://` endpoint to another host: mr-pass's `Transport.js:154-181` refuses clear text to another host, but that test is in the script, not in the sender. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/pass_authority/client.rs:58-89`, `:98-123`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/pass.rs:65-68`, `:115-123`, `:146-156`. Fix: in the sender, send the secret only to the endpoint `pass_backend_grpc_url` names (or derive it there and take no endpoint from the script), and refuse `grpc://` to a host that is not loopback unless `pass_backend_insecure` is true.
- **SEC-OUT-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The address guards do not know two metadata addresses outside link-local space.** Azure's WireServer answers at `168.63.129.16`, a public-range address reachable only from inside a virtual machine; `internal_address` treats it as public, so both guards pass it. `192.0.0.192`, which SSRF lists give for Oracle Cloud's metadata, is in `192.0.0.0/24` and refused by the full guard, but `link_local_address` does not have it, so the shared client (`remote.rs:31`) and every request that the row rule allows the private network reach it. The lists are confirmed in code; the addresses' use is from the reviewer's memory, and whether a current host of those providers answers a guest process with anything of value was not confirmed. Where: `crates/moqui-lib/src/net.rs:66-99`, `:104-114`. Fix: add `168.63.129.16` to both functions and `192.0.0.192` to `link_local_address`.
- **SEC-OUT-85 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The certificate's key file gets mode 0600 only when the staging file is new, and its directory is made with the default mode.** `write_private` opens `.privkey.pem.new` with `create(true).truncate(true).mode(0o600)`; the mode applies only when the open creates the file, so a staging file already there (left by a stopped write, or planted) keeps its owner's mode, the key is written into it and it is renamed to `privkey.pem`, and the open follows a symbolic link. The name's directory is made with `std::fs::create_dir_all` and the process umask. The log files use `moqui_lib::private_fs` for the same purpose (SEC-DAT-06); this does not. It matters to a local user of the host when `runtime/cert` or the name's directory is writable or readable more widely than the service user; the mode `runtime/cert` itself is made with was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/tls.rs:685-699`; `crates/moqui-host/src/acme/store.rs:417`, `:427-431`. Fix: remove the staging file first and open it with `create_new(true)`, or write through `moqui_lib::private_fs`; make the name's directory with `private_fs::create_dir_all`.
- **SEC-OUT-86 (Low; reported (likely)). A voice administrator points a trunk at another host, and the stored trunk credentials answer there.** System › Voice › Trunks `storeTrunk` (`org.moqui.call.VoiceServices.store#VoiceTrunk`, for a holder of `VoiceAdmin`, which `ADMIN` holds in the seed) starts from the existing row's `authJson` (username, password, realm, algorithms) and replaces the password only when a new one is given, while `host`, `port`, `transportEnumId` and `providerProfileEnumId` change freely and the host is refused only for white space, `/` and `@`. The engines reload and register to the new host, which gets a digest answer with the stored password, for the configured realm and MD5 included when the algorithms allow it, to a nonce it chose: it can guess the provider password offline, or pass the provider's own challenge through and register or call on the provider account. The password is write-only on the screens (`find#VoiceTrunks` answers `hasPassword`), and `ext.rs:437-467` and `update#RelayServer` clear a stored password on such a change; SEC-MAIL-84 is the same pattern for mail accounts. The store side is confirmed in code; that the engine reloads with the changed row and registers to the new host was read only through `reloadEngines()` and `ua.rs:2076-2088`, and whether `ADMIN` already reads the plain `authJson` elsewhere (which would lower the gain for `ADMIN`, not for a group given only `VoiceAdmin`) was not settled. Where: `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/call/Voice.js:1052-1077`, `:1084-1101`; `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:2076-2088`; `crates/moqui-sip/src/resolve.rs:153-193`. Fix: as `ext.rs` does, clear `auth.password` when the host, port, transport, provider profile, username, authUsername or realm changes and no new password is given, and answer that it was cleared.
- **SEC-OUT-87 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A served `addons.xml` that `myaddons.xml` names is searched before `addons.xml`, so its names replace the official components, sets and repositories, and a component name it gives is used as a path.** `moq` searches `myaddons.xml`, then the served files, then `addons.xml`, and takes the first match, so a served `<component name="mr-mantle">`, `<component-set name="apps">` or `<repository name="github">` replaces the official one: `component-get apps`, or a group component's depends-on `mr-mantle`, clones the served file's repository, which is linked into the binary at the next build when it has a `Cargo.toml`. A served repository named `bitbucket-token` receives the `${token}` that `expand_url` fills from a `myaddons.xml` component element; a component name is not checked before `target=$base/$name`, so one with `../` puts a clone at any path outside `runtime/components/addon` that does not exist yet; a zip location goes to `curl -fL -o "$target.zip" "$url"` with no `--url` and no scheme limit. It is reached by whoever controls a served file a developer put in `myaddons.xml` (the hosting server's operator; on an MR PASS server the file lists the group's public repositories by slug, so also a group member who can make a public repository there, whose naming rules were not traced), when the developer runs `./moq component-get` or `component-depends`. Where: `scripts/moq/components.sh:329-371`, `:40-50`, `:70-96`, `:99-107`, `:160-164`, `:175-180`, `:245-255`, `:278-282`, `:399-404`; `addons.xml:42-47`, `:63`. Fix: take from a served file only components and repositories no other file defines (or only the components `myaddons.xml` names with that repository), check every component name with `template_name`, and pass zip URLs as `--proto '=https' --url "$url"`.
- **SEC-OUT-88 (Low; reported (likely)). The OpenSearch child the server starts listens on loopback with no sign-in, so any local user of the host reads and writes every search index.** When `search-facade@backend` is `opensearch` with a loopback cluster URL and the server (or `./moq opensearch-start`) starts `runtime/opensearch`, the child gets only `-Epath.data` and `-Epath.logs`; its network and security settings are the install's, which `moq` downloads as `opensearch-min`, the distribution without the security plugin, binding loopback by default, and the cluster user and password default to empty. The server applies its own authorization to what it searches; a local user's or process's direct request to port 9200 has none, and can also rewrite or delete indexes. The server side was read; the install's `config/opensearch.yml` and what the indexes hold were not. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/search.rs:323-366` (`:335-346`); `scripts/moq/opensearch.sh:133-134`. Fix: say so in Run-Deploy and the Process map (a shared host keeps the Postgres backend, or an OpenSearch with the security plugin); when the server starts its own, run it as its own user and require credentials from `.env`.
- **SEC-OUT-89 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). A data load's CSV or JSON text is written to `runtime/tmp` with the process umask.** While an administrator runs Tools › Data › Load with CSV or JSON text, the text is written whole to `runtime/tmp/data-load-<uuid>.csv` or `.json` with `std::fs::write` (0644 under a 022 umask), in a directory made with `std::fs::create_dir_all`, loaded, then removed, so another local user of the host reads it where `runtime/tmp` and its parents can be traversed. SEC-DAT-06 made exports, dumps and logs 0600 and did not cover this copy, and the systemd unit sets no `UMask` (SEC-OUT-13's open part); the PDF browser's profile and the test client's outputs are made the same way. The mode of `/opt/moqui` in a systemd install was not read. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1973-1982`. Fix: write it with `private_fs::file_options().write(true).create_new(true)` in a directory made with `private_fs::create_dir_all`, or load the text from memory.
- **SEC-OUT-90 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The image script builds the release package with the dev tools, which Run-Deploy says a production image leaves out.** `podman/build.sh` runs `./moq build --release`, which builds `maximal,devtools`, where Run-Deploy's production checklist says "A production image leaves them out". The dev tools stay off on a production purpose and the server warns at start, as SEC-OUT-14's owner's call has it, so this is the script and the checklist disagreeing. Where: `podman/build.sh:34-35`; `scripts/moq/build.sh:160`; `docs/Run-Deploy.md:207-210`; `crates/moqui-host/src/lib.rs:1502`. Fix: build the image with `--enterprise`, or Maximal without `devtools`, or say in Run-Deploy that the shipped image script does not.
- **SEC-OUT-91 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). Some downloads that are run or embedded are checked only against a sum from the same place, or not at all.** OpenSearch is compared with the SHA-512 at the same address and used unchecked when that file cannot be read (`warn "could not read $url; the archive is not checked"`); the Postgres archive that the `embedded-postgres` feature embeds is compared with the `.sha256` of the same GitHub release; `podman/Containerfile.build` fetches `rustup-init.sh` and runs it with no sum; `scripts/moq/desktop.sh` runs `~/.cache/tauri/quick-sharun.sh`, which tauri downloaded, with no check. SEC-OUT-14's fix checks a Chromium snapshot and a release or binary add-on zip against their first download's SHA-256, and NASM is pinned; these are not. Each is fetched over https, so it is reached by whoever can change the file at its host. Where: `scripts/moq/opensearch.sh:186-189`; `scripts/pg-payload.sh:63-80`; `podman/Containerfile.build:49-50`; `scripts/moq/desktop.sh:401-408`. Fix: pin the SHA-256 of each version in the repository, as `scripts/nasm.sh` does, and stop when no sum is read.
- **SEC-OUT-92 (Info; confirmed (second check)). A remote-rest service's transport failure, a TLS refusal included, is logged without its reason.** `RestClient` makes a failed send `HTTP POST <url> failed` with the transport error (an unknown issuer, a name that does not match, an expired certificate, a refused connection) as its source. The service runner maps a `remote-rest` service's error with `LibError::new(err.message())`, which keeps the message and drops the source, and `outbound::send`'s span records `error.message()` too, so a remote service whose certificate fails logs only that the request failed, and an operator cannot tell a certificate refusal (an interception, or a certificate that expired) from a timeout. Report: the security proofs, phase P5 (ASVS v5.0.0-16.3.4); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call.rs:1492-1504`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/http.rs:404-407`; `crates/moqui-core/src/outbound.rs:136-145`. Fix: Keep the source (`with_source`) and write the error's chain to the log and the span; look for the same loss at the other `map_err(|err| LibError::new(err.message()))` sites.
- **SEC-OUT-93 (Info; confirmed (second check)). The PASS authority client trusts only the WebPKI roots over `grpcs://`, with no setting for a private CA.** `dial` builds the TLS client of a `grpcs://` endpoint from `webpki_roots::TLS_SERVER_ROOTS` alone, with no conf attribute for another root or a pinned certificate, so an authority whose certificate a private or internal CA issued cannot be reached over TLS. That is safe as it stands (no check is turned off), but it leaves such a deployment `grpc://`, which `Transport.js` allows to another host only with `pass@backend-insecure`, and which sends `pass@client-secret` in clear text (SEC-OUT-83). Report: the security proofs, PASS part (ASVS v5.0.0-12.3.2); lines read again at mr-moqui `507e93425`. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/pass_authority/client.rs:15-19`, `:205-219`; mr-pass `service/pass/Transport.js:157-183`. Fix: A `pass` attribute naming a CA file whose certificates are added to the `grpcs://` client's roots.

## Development tools

- **SEC-DEV-02 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). eval#Condition runs a caller-supplied expression at the view tier.** `condition` is placed into `<if condition="...">` (or as a condition element) and run with `run_actions` in the sim. The read tier is described as browse, search and read files, but this is expression evaluation in the server with the user's own grants; what it can reach depends on what the sim fences. Where: runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/services.rs:87-117 and 58-82. Fix: Require the extend tier for eval#Condition, as try#Snippet does. Fixed 2026-11-17 (commit ab82a7bbd): `eval#Condition` needs the extend tier, as `try#Snippet` does (`src/services.rs`). Spec `ServiceWriter` "a rule's condition is not tried at the view tier".
- **SEC-DEV-03 (Low; reported (to check); fixed). State-changing Dev AIDE transitions have no method attribute, so a parameterless GET runs them.** None of the 117 Workspace transitions, nor those of Home, Repositories, Tasks and Settings, has `method="post"`. By the confirmed rule a transition with no method runs on a GET with no query string, with no session token check, and the cookie is SameSite=Lax. restart#Server reads no parameter, so one cross-site navigation restarts the server; pull#All would pull every repository. Where: runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/screen/DevAide/Workspace.xml (transition `restart`, last transition of the file); runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/screen/DevAide/Home.xml:31; runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/screen/DevAide/Repositories.xml:23-28. Fix: Put `method="post"` on every Dev AIDE transition that is not `read-only`. Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-04 (commit 65c92a57a): a GET another site started is refused for a transition that runs actions and is not `read-only`, unless it carries the session token (`render.rs` `cross_site_write`). Dev AIDE's writing transitions (`restart`, `pull`, and every one not marked `read-only`) are of that kind, so a cross-site link no longer runs them; the app's own links and a typed URL still run a GET of one, as in every app, and `method="post"` was not added. Checked against the code 2026-11-17.
- **SEC-DEV-04 (Low; reported (confirmed in code); fixed). The file deny list covers six runtime names; conf, save, sftp and install are readable at the view tier, and store#Root accepts any other host directory.** `DENIED_RUNTIME` is `db`, `log`, `txlog`, `tmp`, `.env`, `cert`. A root is the git repository that holds the loaded components, which for this repository contains `runtime/`, so `runtime/conf/*.xml` (datasource passwords, crypt pass), `runtime/save` (database copies), `runtime/sftp` and `.env.*` copies beside `.env` resolve. store#Root checks only the same six names, so `/`, a home directory or `~/.ssh` can be made a root that every tier then reads. Where: runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/confine.rs:28; runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/workspace.rs:1238-1259. Fix: Add `save`, `sftp`, `install`, `opensearch` and `.env*` to the deny list, hide conf files from tiers below full, and limit store#Root to ADMIN or to directories under the runtime and the repositories. Fixed 2026-11-16 (commit 75de1a115): `confine::DENIED_RUNTIME` adds `save`, `sftp`, `install` and `opensearch`, a name at the top of the runtime that starts with `.env` is refused, `runtime/conf` is refused below the full tier (and `search#Files` leaves out what an open would refuse), and `store#Root` of a location outside the runtime and the current roots needs `ADMIN`. Tests `confine::tests::a_runtime_root_does_not_reach_the_denied_directories`, `confine::tests::conf_files_are_the_full_tier_s_and_a_non_admin_s_root_stays_near`. Left: a View-tier user still sees `runtime/conf` changes in the git views (diff, show, blame) of a repository that tracks them.
- **SEC-DEV-07 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). The extend tier is code execution as the server, wider than its description.** DEV_AIDE_EXTEND is described as "write site components, publish". It also runs any service for real (try#Service with real and confirm), runs JavaScript in the sim inside the DEV_AIDE_APP inheritable ALWAYS frame, runs specs, loads seed files, and starts a `sh` script from `ci.xml` in a host terminal session. An operator who gives the group to a less trusted person gives them the server. Where: runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/services.rs:338-447; runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/local_run.rs:336-377; runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/data/DevAideSecurityData.xml:23. Fix: Say so in the permission's description and in the lock-down advice; consider the full tier for a real run and for start#LocalRun.
- **SEC-DEV-08 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). A demo archive needs --with-dev, which ships Dev AIDE beside the demo component.** The demo conf names `components/dev/demo`, which a default archive leaves out with the rest of the dev set, so a demo server is built with `--with-dev` and carries dev-aide, example and template. Dev AIDE stays off only because the purpose is production; one `instance_purpose=dev` line in `.env` loads it for the shared ADMIN login, which is the full tier. Where: moq:5206-5208; runtime/conf/MoquiDemoConf.xml:17-19. Fix: Move the demo component out of the dev set (its own set or `addon`), or let archive keep one named dev component.
- **SEC-DEV-81 (Low; reported (likely)). Text the dev agent reads can restart the server: `try_service` runs `restart#Server` in the sim with no approval, and nothing on the way consults the fence.** `try_service` is a Read tool, so a sim run asks nobody. `sim_refusal` refuses only the Terminal's services and dev-aide's own services that are not read-only, and `restart#Server` is neither. The body runs in the sim as the user, and the sim's fence refuses an effect only where the code asks it (`check_effect`); `restart_soon` checks the dev instance and the permission and spawns a thread that restarts the server 200 ms later, so the sim records no violation and answers success. `DEV_AIDE_APP`'s `ADMIN` `AUTHZT_ALWAYS` grant on `restart#Server` authorizes the frameless sim call. Reached by the dev agent of a full-tier user (`DEV_AIDE` or `ADMIN`) on a dev instance, steered by text it reads (a workspace file, a merge request, issue or job log the `hosting` tool returns, a record), calling `try_service` with `service: org.moqui.dev.DevToolsServices.restart#Server` and no `real`. Not confirmed: `restart::request_restart` and `can_restart` were not read, nor that a model follows such text. Rust services that write the host with `std::fs` (`save#ConfFile` at `deploy/conf_edit.rs:317`, `remove#Component` at `deploy/load.rs:699`) have the same gap, but in a frameless sim they need a service grant for `ADMIN` that was not found in the base seed, so they are likely refused by authorization. Where: `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/agent/try_service.rs:55`, `:95-120`, `:141-198`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/service.rs:637-685`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/approval/preview.rs:180-215`; `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/data/DevAideSecurityData.xml:36-38`. Fix: call `fence::check_effect(Effect::Process, "restart#Server")` in `restart_soon`; in `sim_refusal`, refuse a Rust service (`type="rust"`) that is not read-only unless it is listed as overlay-safe, rather than only dev-aide's and the Terminal's.
- **SEC-DEV-82 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The `project` argument reads the index of any Moqui project on the host at the view tier (SEC-DEV-04's rule for roots does not cover this path).** `requested_dir` takes any absolute directory with no `.` or `..` part, and `detect` needs only marker paths (`runtime/components/base/framework/component.xml`, or a Java `framework/src/main/java`); the project's conf is booted in this process (artifacts only) and its components indexed. The answers give artifact ids, names, file paths and lines, data record primary keys and up to 160 characters of a record's `description`, service parameters, and for `check` the problems of any XML file in the project's components. `store#Root` of a directory outside the runtime and the roots needs `ADMIN` since SEC-DEV-04 (`workspace.rs:1272-1274`); this path has only the production refusal, with no `ADMIN` check, no deny list and no tier above view (`project_file` uses `ProjectIndex::file`, not `confine`). Reached by a holder of any dev-aide permission (`DEV_AIDE_VIEW` and up) on a dev instance, through the agent tools `artifact` and `check` (also over MCP with an API key) and `GET /rest/s1/moqui/aide/...?project=<dir>`. Not confirmed: what booting another runtime's conf in this process does beyond the index (whether `conf_env::setting`, which falls back to the server factory this process booted last, can then read the other project's conf), and whether a `check` problem message quotes file text. Where: `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/agent/artifact.rs:124-130`; `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/agent/check.rs:74-76`, `:102-123`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/project/cache.rs:532-559`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/project/mod.rs:243-254`, `:274-299`; `crates/moqui-devkit/src/service.rs:146-185`, `:256-271`. Fix: for a `project` outside the runtime and the current roots, require `ADMIN` as `store#Root` does, or answer only `DevArtifactProject` rows an admin allowed.
- **SEC-DEV-83 (Low; reported (likely)). The demo lock-down leaves `update#UserAccount` free to set `terminateDate` or `ipAllowed` on the shared login, so one visitor locks every other out.** mr-mantle `update#PartyUserAccount` declares the `UserAccount` non-key fields (`terminateDate`, `ipAllowed`, `externalAuthFlowId` among them) and passes its context to `update#UserAccount`. The demo's pre-service SECA calls `refuse#SharedAccountChange` with `accountUpdate: true`, which refuses only a new username, `disabled == 'Y'` and `requirePasswordChange == 'Y'`, and `check#AccountAction` allows the user's own account (a recent sign-in only for `emailAddress` or `externalUserId`). With `terminateDate` in the past, or `ipAllowed` set to the visitor's own address, every later sign-in of the shared login fails until an operator clears the field, which is what the SECA's description says it prevents. Reached by any visitor of a public demo server signed in with the Test Login button as the shared `john.doe` (`ADMIN`), through mr-marble Party › EditParty transition `updateUserAccount` (also Customer › EditCustomer, and `PATCH /rest/s1/mantle/parties/{partyId}/user`) with `userId=EX_JOHN_DOE`; the mr-marble My Account path calls `update#Account`, which does not take these fields. Not confirmed: that a demo server loads mr-marble; the URL of EditParty under the marble app and whether the `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG` filter of the grant stops the find of john.doe's party; the authorization of the mantle REST resource on a demo. Where: `runtime/components/dev/demo/service/demo/DemoServices.xml:20-22`, `:53`; `runtime/components/dev/demo/service/Demo.secas.xml:26-29`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/UserServices.xml:121-128`, `:136-148`, `:165`, `:660`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/party/PartyServices.xml:930-955`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Party/EditParty.xml:61`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1722`, `:1787`. Fix: with `accountUpdate`, allow only `userFullName`, `emailAddress`, `locale`, `timeZone` and `currencyUomId` to change for a shared login and refuse any other non-empty field that differs from the row (an allow-list, not a deny-list); add a case to `demo/test/Demo.spec.js`.
- **SEC-DEV-84 (Low; reported (confirmed in code)). The test client's browser profile and `--serve` ready file have predictable names in the shared temp directory, and a file planted there is taken as the browser's DevTools address or the test server's URL.** The profile is `<temp>/moqui-test-chromium-<pid>-<n>` (the pid is in `/proc`, and the browser starts at the first `newPage`); a failed `remove_dir_all` of another user's directory is ignored and `create_dir_all` succeeds on it, and `devtools_url` reads `DevToolsActivePort` there before it asks whether the browser has started. A planted file sends the client's WebSocket to the other user's loopback port, so every CDP command goes there, including the expression that fills the sign-in form with the user name and password (default `moqui`, or one the spec passes) and `Network.setCookies` with the kept sign-in cookies, and that endpoint answers the page checks as it likes. For `--serve` the ready file is `<temp>/moqui-serve-<pid>-<n>.url`; a failed `remove_file` is ignored, and `ready()` takes the first line of whatever file is there as the server, plain http allowed, and runs the group's specs against it. Reached by another local user of a Linux machine with a shared `/tmp` (a shared dev box or CI host) where `moqui test-client` runs a spec with a browser page, or runs `--serve`; on macOS `temp_dir()` is the per-user `$TMPDIR`. The open DevTools port itself is SEC-SCR-10's recorded exception. Nothing was run; how Chromium behaves with a profile it cannot write was not checked (the planted file is read first). Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/test_client/cdp.rs:165-174`, `:202`, `:213`; `crates/moqui-host/src/chromium.rs:344-353`; `crates/moqui-host/src/test_client/browser.rs:433-437`, `:914-917`; `crates/moqui-host/src/test_client/serve.rs:106`, `:216-217`, `:263-267`. Fix: make the profile and the ready file inside a fresh directory of this user with mode 0700 (`tempfile` or mkdtemp, or under `runtime/tmp` made with `private_fs`), refuse an existing one, and drive the test browser over `--remote-debugging-pipe` as the PDF engine does, which also closes its port.
- **SEC-DEV-85 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). An approved `try_service` real run does not ask the tier or the dev instance again.** `run` checks the extend tier before it asks (`try_service.rs:157-163`); `resume` calls `real` with the bound arguments, which lifts the fence and calls the service with no `context(Tier::Extend)`, where `propose_edit`'s accept asks the tier again (`propose.rs:365`). A user who lost `DEV_AIDE_EXTEND`, or an instance that stopped being dev, within the approval's time to live still gets the real run on approval; the service's own authorization still applies. Where: `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/agent/try_service.rs:76-88`, `:218-234`. Fix: call `context(Tier::Extend)?` at the start of `real`.
- **SEC-DEV-86 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `moqui check`, `refs` and `lsp` with `--server` send the login key over plain http to any host name that starts with `127.`.** `loopback` treats any host beginning with `127.` as this machine, so with `--server http://127.example.net` (a typo or a pasted URL) the stored login key goes as a bearer header in the clear to a DNS name. `devtools_cmd::safe_server` parses an IP literal for the same purpose (`devtools_cmd.rs:317-327`). Reached by the local user's own `--server` argument. Where: `crates/moqui-host/src/devtools_remote.rs:71-76`, `:176`, `:390-398`. Fix: parse the host as an `IpAddr` and test `is_loopback`, as `safe_server` does.
- **SEC-DEV-87 (Info; reported (confirmed in code)). `store#TaskSource` keeps a stored token when the source's address changes, and `get#Tasks` sends it over plain http to any host.** For a source Connect made, a new address clears the token; for a source with a typed token, a change of `baseUrl` with an empty `token` keeps it, and the next read sends it as the `api_key` header to the new `baseUrl`, which may be `http://` to any host. Reached by a view-tier session of the token's owner (`store#TaskSource`, then `get#Tasks`), for example a borrowed or stolen session, which can point the source at a host it names and receive the token for the other instance. Where: `runtime/components/dev/dev-aide/src/tasks.rs:444-487`, `:180-205`. Fix: clear the stored token when `baseUrl` changes, as for a Connect-made source, and refuse `http://` but to a loopback IP literal, as the hosting client does (`hosting/client.rs:38-47`).
